## Original: 2026-10-15-PLAYER-TEST-FOLLOWUP.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Player test follow-up, 15 October 2026

## Bedside awakening and image proportions — 0.3.29

Native keeper wake-up previously restored the counter position rather than starting beside the bed (`work/BedWake-red.log`). Generated sleeper routes could start on the mattress (`work/BedWake-generated-red.log`). A shared transition now finds a supported, clear floor beside the current bed, restores the standing body there and writes that floor position before automatic escape planning. Native response views preserve the original service position for later recovery. Existing saved response and route fields suffice; no schema change is required. If no bedside position is clear, the transition waits rather than placing the NPC through geometry.

`work/BedWake-native-0329-pass.log` verifies an actual sleeping native keeper stands near the bed, automatically plans escape without test reanchoring, retains waking/journey state through SAVE47 reload, physically reports to a finite guard and receives neutral investigation/arrival without false Murder. `work/BedWake-generated-0329-pass.log` verifies male/female sleepers in homes259/10 start outside the mattress footprint and retain waking identity and original journey timing after actual reload. The generated test injects a controlled saved noise observation, so it verifies the live wake/escape consumer rather than natural weapon-noise detection. `work/KeeperResponse-0329-pass.log` preserves actual native weapon death, family witnesses, service blocking, sleeping death/collider restoration and reload. Broader layouts, normal walking-to-bed/wake-to-work routines, blocked-bedroom acceptance and nonlethal/step/theft noise remain open.

The player's stretched-face screenshot exposed independent width/length scaling of a foreshortened frame. Rendering now preserves source pixel proportions within mattress bounds. A single true overhead composite is being refined for native182/0; the other three currently supported appearances retain aspect-correct, fixed legacy broadside composites. `work/SleepAspect-red.log` and `work/SleepSingleView-red.log` reproduce distortion and absent single-view support. Native build checks pass pixel proportions, fixed bed axes, transparent elongated source decoding and single-image support; male/female live sleeper lifecycle and proportion checks pass. Asset refinement and the wider artwork backlog do not complete the visual milestone.

The player approved overhead revision v4: the shorter v2 with only a modest blanket extension and retained stylized head/pillow/width, rather than the rejected anatomy-driven tall version. `art/sleep/topdown-flat-182-0-v4.png` is copied unchanged into the native182/0 runtime asset. Final native keeper lifecycle regression passes with that asset; `work/SleepTopDown-0329.png` records the actual in-game rendering. Installed0.3.29 SHA-256 `F5D6BE3A390BE4A20B45F11FB85B2087388C313BE63FEC19379EF8A8D028EE56`. Core244/244, native build and diff checks pass; tracked base-game scripts remain unchanged. Read-only review found no confirmed important defect; all-layout/ordinary bed-travel and wider artwork acceptance remain open.

## Sleeping alignment

Three screenshots and the eight-second recording show a sleeper rotating and sliding across the mattress. `SleepingPose.LateUpdate` faced the camera and rescaled the drawing. The old regression checked visibility, not bed alignment. The player explicitly requests no camera-facing rotation or view swapping.

The new drawing uses one existing complete face/pillow/green-blanket composite on a fixed horizontal bed-aligned plane. This is an interim flat representation; volumetric artwork is not complete. Editor regression failed before the change (`work/SleepAlignment-red.log`) and passes afterward. Both male and female live tests pass fixed alignment from multiple camera positions, physical mattress bounds, wake/toggle/collider restoration, raycast interaction, death and real save/load (`work/SleepAlignment-runtime-pass.log`). Native keeper response, reserved-bed sleep, service blocking, wake/collider restoration, death and reload also pass (`work/SleepAlignment-keeper-pass.log`). Core tests pass 227/227. The first female run exposed an obsolete assertion requiring the whole mattress to fit the screen even at close range; it is replaced by physical mattress bounds.

## Indoor hearing and waking — runtime checkpoint 0.3.27

The runtime now calls the saved hearing policy for active living generated residents and registered native keepers in the actual attack building, including sleepers. Native Stealth and distance affect the once-only wake roll. Reactive native sleepers wake and cannot open dialogue or trade. Direct evidence requires an already-awake observer, a visible player, and line of sight to both player and victim. Hearing-only recipients flee without spreading the two-person outdoor relay. Their physical contact with a posted guard dispatches an unidentified investigation; actual guard arrival controls discovery and preserves the one-hour occupation.

`work/IndoorNoise-runtime-red.log` reproduces missing live awakening before integration. `work/IndoorNoise-0327-pass.log` verifies a real native sleeping keeper awakened by a controlled blocked sound, service blocking, no false Murder accusation, actual SAVE47 reload, a controlled awake-origin exit/report route, neutral finite dispatch and actual arrival. Core244/244 and the Unity/native build pass. This probe creates a controlled stimulus and later a controlled death record; it does not claim natural weapon damage or bed-to-stair travel. The existing interior floor graph cannot traverse overlapping floors reliably, and native waking restores the original counter position. Full bed-to-floor/stair escape remains unfinished. Quiet movement, theft and nonlethal swing stimuli are not yet hooked.

Earlier source-only checkpoints below are historical and superseded by this runtime checkpoint.

Regression evidence: `work/KeeperResponse-0327-pass.log` passes actual native WeaponDamage, family eyewitnesses, persistent keeper death, no instant guard report, service restrictions, sleep/collider restoration and real reload. The stricter attacker-sight requirement exposed an old controlled fixture that left the player elsewhere; the fixture now explicitly places a visible attacker. `work/CrimeScene-0327-pass.log` passes confirmed death, prior crime retention, departure dispatch, arrival discovery, re-entry custody, real save/reload and occupation expiry. The installed bundle SHA-256 is `DED4B2A0ECBE870FCAD0F2607761AF60A7E9D86CB836164E476C5FEFED6E4140`. Tracked base-game scripts remain unchanged.

Next source stage provides neutral physical reporting: hearing-only escape plans survive saves and mature into report candidates, and a hearer must actually reach a living posted guard before recording contact. A reported scene can be investigated before the player departs. Actual guard arrival still controls discovery, corpse cleanup and one-hour occupation. Visiting victims can receive this investigation when an indoor hearer supplies evidence, without changing their home ownership. Direct eyewitnesses cannot be submitted as unidentified hearers. Core243/243 pass. Live actor stimuli and adapter dispatch remain pending; this does not yet make sleeping NPCs wake in the installed player.

Source foundation now implements `NoiseAwareness.WakeChance` and saved `HearViolence` checks, with explicit hearing/awakening flags separate from direct visual witnessing. Violence has the highest wake chance; higher Stealth and distance reduce it. Negative checks persist without reroll, observers arriving more than five game seconds later cannot hear an old attack, and legacy incidents receive no invented hearing. Hearing-only recipients flee and cannot relay to more NPCs. Core237/237 and DFU native FullSerializer/legacy-field checks pass (`work/IndoorNoise-pass.log`, `work/IndoorNoise-native-pass.log`). This is not connected to live actor noise or guard reporting yet; installed0.3.26 remains unchanged. The next integration must extend scene ownership: current reporting assumes direct witnesses and charges Murder, while unidentified scene registration currently requires zero recipients. Do not wire hearing into that path without neutral investigation support. Plan: `superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-indoor-hearing.md`.

The player can kill occupants in separate rooms without alerting the others. Confirmed source cause: `CivilianResponses.Murder`, `BeginKeeperHit` and `ObserveKeeperViolence` require visual line of sight and skip sleepers. Add an audible violence stimulus within the same building. Awake occupants should investigate, flee to report, or fight according to capability. Keep physical exit/report journeys and finite guard travel time; never instantly spawn guards downstairs.

Sleeping occupants have a chance to wake from noise and subsequently report. Use native **Stealth** for quiet movement and theft noise. Weapon hits, struggles and shouting should greatly increase wake chance. Account for noise intensity, distance and sleep state. Roll once per discrete event, not every frame, and preserve waking/reaction state through save/load. Exact probabilities remain to be implemented and tested.

Hearing a disturbance does not identify an unseen attacker. Preserve the agreed crime-scene policy, lawful self-defense and bounded outdoor two-person relay. Newly awakened occupants should investigate, with sight establishing direct attacker identification.

## Interior stairs — runtime checkpoint 0.3.28

The original graph fails all124 clear upper bedroom samples in native shop131343 (`work/InteriorStairs-red.log`). Mesh-face inspection shows discrete treads approximately0.3125 metres high/deep. An x/z-only graph merges storeys; coarse0.4-metre sampling and a low capsule sweep reject these narrow treads. The graph now uses distinct height cells,0.2-metre horizontal sampling, native-sized0.5-metre step limits and body clearance above that step band. Every height-changing edge contains a checked vertical/horizontal corner, preventing foot interpolation through solid treads. Walls, head clearance, endpoint floor support, locked/scripted doors and actual exit ownership remain required. Survey work is capped at12000 nodes and logs truncation rather than silently claiming full coverage.

`work/InteriorStairs-feet-red.log` catches tread penetration before explicit corners. `work/InteriorStairs-0328-pass.log` verifies all124 upper samples route to the actual exit without feet cutting treads, plus a controlled awake native keeper physically descending and leaving through its saved path. One survey of this shop measured84ms; this is not a large-city performance result. These tests do not certify all house layouts, natural sleeping-bed transitions, or ordinary routines walking to beds. Bed-to-floor awakening still needs implementation. M2/M3 remain open.

`work/InteriorEscape-0328-pass.log` passes overlapping-obstacle rejection, generated resident movement, physical exit, exterior identity, delayed finite response and actual SAVE32 reload. Its controlled attacker placement now explicitly supplies direct sight, as required by0.3.27's witness policy. Native actor movement advances the transform along the validated saved route; these checks do not certify CharacterController-driven collision resolution. Read-only review found no confirmed important defect after the explicit corner fix.

Final native build, Core244/244, `work/KeeperResponse-0328-pass.log` and `work/IndoorNoise-0328-pass.log` pass. These preserve actual native weapon/death/family/service/sleep/reload and controlled hearing/wake/neutral-report/arrival/reload behavior. Installed0.3.28 SHA-256 `05808C6114AE074C0F547B1CE84367D166FE43C1E6903DD987705CBE60E136F4`; tracked base-game scripts remain unchanged.

## Shop occupants and unkillable elder — investigation pending

Reported shop: woman named Kinghart, another person named Hearthfield, and a service keeper whose name is not visible. Inspect saved ownership, activity, current building and native hash before deciding whether the differently named person is a shopper or an incorrectly housed resident. Keeper families share the keeper's surname; resident capacity remains one per actual bed. Expose the keeper's full identity while retaining native services.

The screenshot shows an elderly green-robed native flat that cannot be killed. Current registration only includes merchant-social-group NPCs in shops. Damage handling requires a registered identity and excludes native quest/action ownership or actors with their own entity. Unregistered generic static flats retain vanilla damage behavior. The exact screenshot actor is not yet identified; do not claim its specific exclusion is proven. Add a live identity/damage regression before widening coverage. Ordinary stationary NPCs need identity and mortality; preserve main-quest/castle placement.

## Daggerfall city — requested extension, pending

Add Daggerfall city alongside Gothway Garden, with distinct settlement state and disposable test saves. Native curse quest `S0000977` uses global variable **LiftedCurse**, table ID **0**, and ghost danger hours **19:00–05:00**. Unresolved haunting suppresses night patrols and late civilian activity; exposed guards flee toward verified reachable refuges rather than fighting ghosts. Solving the curse restores supported normal behavior. Full tavern nightlife and traveler lodging remain separate unfinished systems. See `DAGGERFALL-HAUNTING.md`.

## Original: ACTIVITY-AND-GATE-FOLLOWUP.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Activity, crowd spacing and gate-post requirements

## 16 October additional live feedback

- Interior walking should use clear straight segments between meaningful corners, rather than trace every survey-grid step around furniture. Browsing anchors must have an unobstructed view of their shelf; nearby shelves across walls are not valid facing targets.
- All 24 mobile appearances need proper standing poses for every viewing direction. Native record 5 provides front-facing idle art only. Reuse that art; missing directional views must preserve the original identity, outfit, proportions and pixel style. A frozen walking frame remains a temporary fallback, not finished idle artwork.

16 October route checkpoint: bounded route simplification and cached activity visibility are built and installed. Shortcuts require capsule clearance and sampled floor support; no per-frame path search is added. Core tests pass 539/539. Unity integration checks pass, including blocked-wall, target-collider and transient-pedestrian visibility cases (`work/InteriorRouteVisibility-build.log`). Native interior acceptance remains pending because the live test refused to interrupt an existing player session. Generated idle pilots were rejected for changing identity and style; no completed directional idle set is claimed.

- Visible outdoor walkers must reach their actual doorway before an interior schedule phase removes their body. Schedule time alone must not make a delayed walker disappear. Failed reconstruction must preserve an already placed body. Legitimate distance culling remains separate.
- Stopped or socializing NPCs must not freeze in a wide walking stride. Inspect original directional frames before changing poses; a narrower walking frame is only a fallback until directional idle art exists.
- Counter conversations currently look too close; street conversations too far apart. Aim for comparable personal space, constrained by reachable geometry and physical clearance.
- Overnight inn guests must physically reach their assigned rooms, rather than remain in hallways while their scheduled room position advances. A common-room patron test does not verify bedroom arrival.
- Walkers should anticipate nearby people using a wider comfort zone, steering earlier and more smoothly. Keep close avoidance as fallback where space is limited. Bound neighbour work and measure cost before claiming performance is acceptable.

These reports are accepted requirements under implementation, not completed acceptance results.

16 October comfort-avoidance verification: rendered opposing walkers now retain a rate-limited lateral offset instead of being pulled back to every interpolated schedule waypoint. The native test begins lateral correction at 1.761 m separation and maintains at least 1.088 m while passing. The inserted-wall collision check also passes (`work/ComfortAvoidance-native-pass.log`). Core tests pass 529/529 and the Unity build is installed (`work/ComfortAvoidance-build.log`). The soft step reuses the nearby-body list and falls back to existing hard separation when blocked; it does not launch a path search. This verifies one opposing-pair scenario and wall clearance, not dense-city performance or every crowd layout. Inn room travel and other reports above remain separate work.

16 October public-shelter implementation checkpoint: generated street civilians without a guard-report escape can now select an accessible home, shop, tavern, guild hall or palace entrance. Candidate routes reject segments that approach the nearby attacker, locked public buildings, player-owned homes and active crime scenes. Selection uses surveyed route length with deterministic ties; an actor-owned cache and bounded surveys limit repeated path work. Final arrival uses the existing saved one-hour shelter lock. Core 399/399 and Unity build pass (`work/PublicShelter-core.log`, `work/PublicShelter-build.log`). Live public-shelter selection and complete arrival remain unverified; native keepers and offscreen street flight remain pending. This supersedes the earlier statement that no alternative shelter selection is implemented, but does not establish live acceptance.

User requirements recorded 15 October 2026. All unchecked items below are pending implementation and verification; recording them does not establish working gameplay.

16 October gate checkpoint: the full Daggerfall test passes paired inner/outer sentries at all four native gates, actual observed actor facing, patrol exclusion, and all outer sentries returning before closure. The extended test also passes a dusk transition while the player stays inside a native inn. The survey uses actual doorway dimensions rather than full wall-mesh width. See [gate verification and remaining scope](HISTORY.md#source-guard-posts-and-doorways-md). The broad checklist remains open for wall-quarter sleeping, native save/load coverage, other settlements and replacement meshes.

16 October verified checkpoint: the archive385 running preview passes actual-player movement, all eight displayed directions, all four frames and restoration after talking/stopping/sleep/death (`work/RunningPilot-runtime-first.log`; side screenshot `work/RunningPilot-side-ingame.png`). This validates integration, not the known arm/leg art defects. The actual inn probe confirms a native witnessed murder alerts an occupant in a separate room and produces an escape plan when a posted-guard route exists (`work/InnAlarm-runtime-pass.log`). It does not verify every occupant reaching the street, moving-patrol contact, absence of posted guards, or the newly requested arriving-customer corpse discovery.

Checkpoint: managed adult resident task-facing, stationary directional frames, movement-facing during approach and dialogue-facing/restoration pass the actual shop test; see [SHOP-VISITS.md](HISTORY.md#source-shop-visits-md). The broad requirements below also cover future social activities and native stationary appearances, so they are not all marked complete by that one-shop result.

Second checkpoint: variable browsing itineraries and the stable50% buyer/non-buyer split are implemented. The native fixture verifies both outcomes, first browsing stop, dialogue and exit; all-stop continuous motion and broader shelf-side/layout acceptance remain open. The running pilot remains unconnected while Astra corrects the user-reported repeated leading arm in some angles.

## Shops and task-facing

Comfort avoidance performance sample: three one-minute Daggerfall City windows pass, with frame p99 values 21.38, 21.95 and 23.80 ms; maxima 96.46, 43.11 and 79.96 ms. The same run confirms 545 active itineraries, 16 gate posts and 8 wall plans, and passes the follow-on itinerary/witness checks (`work/ComfortAvoidance-city-performance-pass.log`). This is a single current-build sample, not a controlled before/after benchmark or a guarantee for every settlement. Periodic stalls remain.

Latest 16 October checkpoint: newly dispatched witnessed corpse reports now carry separate `CrimeScene` occupation while preserving accusation/pursuit policy. Managed visitor rejection and inn admission checks include this occupation. Actual inn eyewitness/cleanup verification and the standing-anchor navigation fix are documented in `CRIME-SCENE-DISCOVERY.md`; old dispatched reports are not retroactively upgraded yet.

16 October inn extension: loaded admitted travelers now discover corpses and flee; actual registered traveler doorway departure passes in `work/InnCrimeArrival-runtime-pass.log`. Guarded investigation admissions refuse travelers without reserving beds, verified by core tests including restore and reopening after occupation. See `TAVERN-LODGING.md` for limits, including retained paid bookings and pending live guarded-refusal coverage.

16 October guarded-scene rejection: managed scheduled visitors entering an occupied investigation leave along a surveyed interior route and their reversed routine route home. `VisitDeparture` saves the departure and cancellation until the next day (or arrival if travel extends beyond midnight), so scene reload does not restore the aborted visit. At home, normal sleeping still takes precedence. The response creates no new crime/alarm report, uses walking speed, and requires a living assigned guard physically inside the occupied building. Old saves with no departure list remain valid; one latest departure is retained per resident. Core 370/370 and Unity build pass; the complete native keeper suite passes a new arrival after neutral corpse cleanup, actual departure and snapshot restore (`work/VisitDeparture-runtime-pass.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_GUARDED_VISITOR_PASS`; `work/VisitDeparture-build-editor.log`). This verifies one shop, not every building layout. Traveler inn admission, witnessed-accusation occupancy, unreachable exits and exterior full-journey rendering remain pending. The current routine returns the rejected visitor home; future multi-appointment routines should select the next valid appointment.

Latest 16 October checkpoint: the neutral-prefix cleanup gap below is now resolved for the tested shop. The complete native keeper suite verifies neutral guard entry, physical corpse approach, inspection and cleanup (`work/NeutralInspection-runtime-pass.log`). Core 369/369 and Unity/native build pass; see `CRIME-SCENE-DISCOVERY.md` for test conditions and remaining scope.

16 October neutral-contact verification: the native keeper suite now advances the shopper's saved street route while the player stays indoors. No neutral report exists on shop exit; after the scheduled guard contact, the roster creates a neutral investigation with four responders, leaves the prior player crime unchanged and does not immediately mark guards arrived. Evidence: `work/CorpseContact-runtime-pass.log`, marker `LIVING_CITIES_CORPSE_VISITOR_CONTACT_PASS`; build: `work/CorpseContact-build-editor.log`. This proves unloaded route/contact dispatch, not a rendered street journey or completed guard entry/cleanup. The runner now requires the contact marker. The remaining cleanup gap is concrete: `CrimeSceneInterior.UpdateCrimeSceneInterior` only resolves `discovery:` reports, while this neutral dispatch uses `inn-alarm:`.

Final build and ordinary buyer/non-buyer shopping regression pass after the corpse-observer change (`work/CorpseVisitor-final-build-editor.log`, `work/CorpseVisitor-shop-regression-pass.log`). Normal shelf/counter routes, activity facing, dialogue facing and scheduled departure remain verified.

16 October corpse-arrival implementation supersedes the diagnostic shopping behavior below: managed scheduled visitors in a loaded interior discover confirmed uncleared murder corpses, enter a neutral saved flee response, and use the existing surveyed exit/posted-guard route instead of browsing or haggling. The native keeper suite passes actual shopper departure without keeper resurrection (`work/CorpseVisitor-runtime-pass.log`). The additive observer timestamp survives restore and allows discoveries after the initial violence alarm expires without reactivating expired witnesses; repeat frames do not restart flight, while a later return can discover the remaining corpse again. Core tests: 368/368 (`work/CorpseVisitor-core.log`). This does not yet cover traveler admission, rejection after the corpse was cleared but guards remain, absent/unreachable posted guards, moving patrol interception, completed neutral guard contact/arrival, or cleanup for neutral visitor reports. The current live probe proves doorway departure, not completed exterior contact. Existing body position/arrival logic is used; this is building-level awareness, not a new line-of-sight system.

16 October keeper-arrival regression: the live Gothway shop fixture kills the native keeper, saves and reloads, then injects a scheduled non-native purchase visitor. The visitor physically reaches browsing and counter stops; the keeper stays dead/inactive, with one matching native identity and one unchanged service flat. Evidence: `work/KeeperVisitor-runtime-pass.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_KEEPER_VISITOR_PASS`; build evidence: `work/KeeperVisitor-build-editor.log`. This does not reproduce the reported resurrection and does not prove inn coverage. It confirms the separate incorrect behavior that customers still browse and haggle at a dead keeper's shop. The next production change must supersede this diagnostic expectation with crime-scene rejection/discovery and physical departure/reporting. The initial red probe used reversed purchase-seed parity; corrected to match `ShopVisitGeometry` and reacquired the loaded interior after save reload. `TownTest.ps1` now requires the visitor marker so this regression cannot silently be skipped.

- [ ] Visitor arrival must never respawn a dead shopkeeper or innkeeper. Persistent native death remains authoritative across visitor activity, scene reconstruction and reload.
- [ ] Arriving customers and inn guests immediately leave a murder scene that is already occupied by guards. If an uncleared corpse is present without guards, they flee and physically report to the nearest reachable guard.
- [ ] In an inn, once any occupant hears or witnesses a murder, the alarm reaches the other occupants throughout the building. Patrons and staff escape while avoiding the player, then seek guards if responders have not already arrived. Preserve the bounded outdoor warning policy; this is an indoor inn-wide exception.

- [ ] Fix the reported rapid left/right sprite flicker while browsing shelves. Check both logical facing and directional sprite selection under crowd movement.
- [ ] Preoccupied NPCs face their activity target, rather than automatically facing the player. Browsers face the shelf; customers at the counter face the keeper; conversation partners face each other.
- [ ] Starting dialogue immediately turns the addressed NPC toward the player, including walkers, browsers and conversation participants. Keep this override while dialogue is open, then restore the activity-facing direction and behavior.
- [ ] Browse multiple reachable shelves, with small steps to inspect different sections of the same shelf and varied dwell times. Use both sides only where physically accessible. Do not route through furniture or stack shoppers at the same position.
- [x] Each shop visit has a 50% chance of a purchase interaction after browsing. The customer walks to the keeper, faces the keeper for a few seconds, then exits toward the next scheduled destination. Other visitors finish browsing and leave without a mandatory counter stop.
- [x] Make the purchase choice stable for a visit across reconstruction/reload; do not reroll each update. Actual item/money transfers remain a separate inventory concern and must not be claimed from a visual transaction alone.

## Street activity and spacing

- [ ] NPCs occasionally meet for deliberate conversations, keep enough personal space, face one another, then resume their schedules after a varied duration.
- [ ] Distinguish actual conversation groups from accidental bunching or blocked movement in diagnostics and tests.
- [ ] Spread pedestrians across available street width rather than one narrow shared line. Keep paths collision-safe, respect narrow passages, and preserve deterministic destinations and timing.

## Castle and gate guards

16 October user correction: exterior gate sentries end their shift before closure, walk through the gate and enter city-wall quarters, then sleep in individually assigned beds. They must not remain standing at extra inner posts beside the on-duty inner pair. Keep the same finite guard identities, health and effects; reserve available physical beds and preserve the journey/sleep state across interior transitions and saves. Morning duty resumes from quarters. The current separate inner return points are staging points only, not the final approved nighttime behavior. Quarters/bed assignment and sleep rendering remain pending.

- [ ] Allocate more suitable posts around city castles within the finite garrison. Avoid blocking entrances or facing doors.
- [ ] At each guarded city gate, place guards to the left and right of the opening, clear of passing traffic and facing away from the wall.
- [ ] While gates are open, also staff the exterior left/right positions facing outward from the wall. Interior positions face inward toward the city, away from their side of the wall.
- [ ] Exterior guards physically move inside shortly before the actual gate closing time. Use gate-aware schedules and reachable routes, not teleportation or a new guard roster. Keep identities, health and effects across movement.
- [ ] Preserve existing Daggerfall haunting restrictions, combat and emergency response priorities; routine gate duty must not override them.

## Additional flight and pursuit requirements

16 October running-pilot decision: the user approves an in-game preview of the current imperfect atlas. Known defects include repeated arm lead and missing side-view leg-passing poses (legs remain spread across the cycle). Preview permission does not accept the art as finished. Preserve the rejection evidence in `art/run/pilot-385/ARM-CYCLE-REVIEW.md`; do not expand this draft to other appearances.

16 October additional crime-scene requirement: guards must reach a corpse and inspect it for five unpaused real seconds before cleanup, then wander inside for the remainder of the original one-game-hour occupation. See [CRIME-SCENE-DISCOVERY.md](HISTORY.md#source-crime-scene-discovery-md).

- [ ] Running-art pilot: the user explicitly requested an Astra agent at medium reasoning for one mobile adult's running cycle covering all native walking view angles. The agent is inspecting archive385's five authored walking directions (eight displayed directions through mirroring). Generate source-matched running art and verify a fleeing preview in game before expanding the catalogue. This is a pilot, not evidence of full running-art coverage.

- [ ] Fleeing civilians prioritize increasing distance from the attacking player over reaching the closest safe interior. Reject shelter approaches that lead them toward the threat when a safer escape direction is available.

16 October partial implementation: loaded generated street civilians without a saved guard-report escape now reject homeward segments that reduce close threat clearance. A bounded seven-direction search uses existing collision-surveyed routes and an actor-owned 0.75-second cache. Whole segments are checked, including paths whose endpoint is farther away but whose middle crosses the attacker. Beyond 12 metres the citizen can resume homeward travel while keeping that clearance. Creature alarms supply the live hostile position or a same-scene saved sighting, rather than fleeing the innocent player. Current saved position remains authoritative. Native keepers, already planned witness-report journeys, alternative shelter selection, offscreen street-flight progression and one-hour shelter locks remain pending.

Evidence: 390/390 core tests, Unity build and full native citizen-threat suite pass. The added live case blocks the route home with a hostile, places the innocent player on the opposite side, and verifies physical flight increases hostile distance without a player crime (`work/FlightSafety-core.log`, `work/FlightSafety-runtime-pass.log`, `work/FlightSafety-build-editor.log`). This is not proof of all civilian flight cases.
- [ ] After reaching a safe interior, fleeing civilians lock its exterior door for one game hour. Persist the expiry and reconcile it with ordinary locks and access rules.

16 October home-shelter checkpoint: generated street residents reaching their existing home refuge now create a saved `ShelterLock`. It survives the shorter alarm lifetime, expires one game hour after arrival, and does not renew on repeated updates or reload. A later distinct arrival can extend it; expired entries are pruned when another lock is recorded. The native BuildingIsUnlocked hook applies the temporary restriction, preserves player-owned houses and the existing household-lock toggle, and falls back to ordinary access after expiry. Additive fields preserve old saves. The current callback covers the existing home-refuge path only; alternative shop/guild/castle shelters and native keeper arrivals still need integration.

Core 393/393 and the full native citizen-threat suite pass (`work/ShelterLock-core.log`, `work/ShelterLock-runtime-pass.log`, `work/ShelterLock-build-editor.log`). The live lock case places the witness at the final home approach, then exercises the normal arrival callback and native locked-door query, native JSON round-trip and one-hour expiry. It does not prove an uninterrupted flight from a remote crime scene or a disk reload.

This run also caught a street-flight stall: the global route can start at a nearby navigation cell toward the attacker, so all retreat candidates were rejected. Short local retreat now first checks a complete body corridor and ground support, then falls back to street routes. Native physics checks accept supported floor and reject walls and drops; hostile-blocking-home flight passes again. Failure evidence is retained in `work/ShelterLock-grid-red.log`.
- [ ] Guards actively chasing the player may periodically shout "Halt!". Nearby guards who hear this can join the same pursuit, using the finite roster and their actual positions. Bound alert propagation and shout frequency; quiet patrols and creature combat remain quiet.
- [ ] A guard shouting "Halt!" at the player triggers a local bystander reaction. Civilians within approximately 10 metres back away to avoid trouble. Those approximately 10–20 metres away stop and face the player; if the player approaches, they retreat to maintain distance. Beyond approximately 20 metres they resume their previous activity or schedule. Slight, stable per-NPC variation prevents simultaneous artificial-looking thresholds. Use hysteresis to avoid rapid switching near boundaries; active danger, crime flight and emergency responses take priority.

## Acceptance and remaining work

16 October witness clock persistence: `AlarmPerson.EscapeAdvancedAt` now saves the last authoritative escape update. Runtime safety planning uses this timestamp instead of a transient dictionary, preserving the starting time across reload before a subsequent movement jump. Lookahead calls to `CivilianEscape.Advance` do not change it; only WorldStateStore advancement does. Zero remains the legacy default and conservatively starts checking at current time without inventing historical timing. Copy/restore rejects nonfinite, pre-plan/pre-redirect and orphan timestamps; expired corpse-response renewal clears it with the discarded journey. Core 397/397 passes, including restore before redirecting a large elapsed step, atomic corrupt-save rejection, legacy acceptance, and response renewal. Unity build passes and is installed (`work/WitnessClock-core.log`, `work/WitnessClock-build.log`). Native probe now asserts the timestamp in JSON and real disk reload, but this revision's live run was refused because the user's game is running (`work/WitnessClock-runtime.log`). Do not treat the previous moving-attacker pass as native proof of this new field.

16 October moving-attacker acceptance checkpoint: the native InteriorEscapeProbe now places the attacker two metres ahead of an escaped witness, then switches to the opposite side. It checks every frame for 0.65 seconds per phase that saved position and rendered actor preserve clearance, the actor remains active, and both actually move when a safe route exists. At least one phase must offer movement, so two stationary holds cannot pass. After the attacker moves aside, the same witness reaches a real guard and reports the original building. The complete run includes actual disk save/load before the exterior check. `TownTest.ps1` requires the new marker. Core 397/397, Unity build and the strengthened native probe pass (`work/WitnessMoving-core.log`, `work/WitnessMoving-build.log`, `work/WitnessMoving-runtime-pass.log`). This supersedes the missing direct live test below. It covers controlled attacker repositioning, not every street layout or continuous long chase. Initial restore/time-jump boundaries and escape into open space beyond surveyed segments remain pending. Probe fix: reacquire the current building directory after save/load rather than retaining the destroyed scene object.

16 October witness retreat integration supersedes the foundation-only status below. Runtime supplies an exterior player threat or a live registered hostile, never interior player coordinates. When no safe guard route exists, the surveyed graph returns a retreat with no GuardId, or a stationary two-point route if trapped. Reporting still requires actual finite-guard proximity. Escape preparation now runs before elapsed movement; a per-person prior simulation time anchors redirects, and an unsafe upcoming step overrides the six-game-second replan cadence. The initial after-advance implementation was rejected in review because time jumps could consume an unsafe route first.

Verification: 397/397 core tests and Unity build pass; native InteriorEscapeProbe passes real save/load, doorway identity, exterior movement, delayed finite guard dispatch (`work/WitnessRetreat-core.log`, `work/WitnessRetreat-build.log`, `work/WitnessRetreat-runtime-pass.log`). Its old co-located victim/witness fixture conflicted with crowd separation and could choose a sleeping witness. The fixture now selects awake, separated visible positions. It also moves the attacker aside for its onward-report timing check; holding at a blocked doorway is now valid. This is regression evidence, not a complete live attacker-blocking-path acceptance test. Remaining: directly observe and assert retreat around a moving attacker, wider street connectivity when all surveyed edges are blocked, and initial restore/time-jump boundaries.

16 October witness-routing foundation: `GuardRoster.PlanWitnessRoute` accepts an optional threat position in settlement coordinates. Dijkstra rejects directed street segments that reduce close clearance or cross the threat, choosing the nearest safely reachable guard instead. A blocked graph returns no plan; it does not dispatch guards. Existing callers without a threat retain their behavior. Core 395/395 passes, including a farther safe post, a complete surveyed detour, all safe targets unavailable, saved roster copying and invalid coordinates (`work/WitnessSafety-core.log`). This is not yet wired into live witness movement: runtime must resolve same-scene threat coordinates and safely retreat or wait when no safe guard route exists, rather than retaining the previous unsafe route. No new live avoidance claim is made.

16 October latest gate-shift decision supersedes bed sleeping: outer guards enter wall quarters and patrol inside until morning. Inner pair remains on duty. Beds are deferred; see [GUARD-QUARTERS.md](HISTORY.md#source-guard-quarters-md) for implementation and validation status.

Verify browsing from multiple camera angles, stable facing during avoidance, varied shelf stops, purchase and non-purchase visits, conversations ending without deadlock, wide/narrow street traffic, and gate duty before/after closing. Use Daggerfall City for wall/gate checks. The user has allowed live tests again after closing the game; scripts must still avoid interrupting a new user session or capturing the mouse.

Related implementation records: [shop visits](HISTORY.md#source-shop-visits-md), [local avoidance](HISTORY.md#source-npc-local-avoidance-md), [guard posts](HISTORY.md#source-guard-posts-and-doorways-md), [milestones](HISTORY.md#source-milestones-md).

## Original: BED-CENSUS.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Vanilla settlement bed census — 14 October 2026

> Current amendment, 14 October 2026: one NPC per bed; maximum household residents equals actual bed count, with no separate five-person cap. Preserve old saves and use a fresh compliant test population. Shared-bed rendering, kill-all-bed logic and separate head/blanket layers are superseded. Keep complete green-blanket/white-pillow single-sleeper sprites. Children may inherit either parent's race; keep existing child sprites now. Gameplay takes priority over art. See [current citizen behavior and next guard work](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md). Earlier checkpoint descriptions below remain historical evidence.

Read-only scan of the installed original game assets through pinned DFU 1.1.1 readers. `BedCensus.Run` visited 3,444 cities, hamlets and villages, using 491 distinct RMB blocks and 5,780 block/interior record combinations. The editor's model catalog labels models 41000, 41001 and 41002 as beds. These counts describe model instances, not sleeping capacity or navigable sleep anchors.

| Internal building type | Distinct block/interior records used | Bed counts observed |
|---|---:|---|
| House1 | 11 | 1 |
| House2 | 2,847 | 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9 |
| House3 | 253 | 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 |
| House4 | 776 | 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9 |
| House5 | 1,307 | 0 |
| House6 | 14 | 0, 4 |
| HouseForSale | 66 | 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 |
| Alchemist | 28 | 1, 3 |
| PawnShop | 13 | 2 |
| Tavern | 196 | 0, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 13, 14, 15 |

House1–House6 are engine classifications, not bedroom or family-size tiers. Block/interior combinations can repeat the same layout, and their reuse across settlements is recorded separately in the CSV. Zero bed models is not evidence that a building is a suitable empty home: geometry, access, current NPCs, ownership and quest use still need inspection. Rest markers (texture 199, record 4) are counted separately; DFU notes that these sometimes substitute for entrance markers, so marker count is not a reliable bed count.

Gothway Garden contains 143 House2 candidates (0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 or 9 beds), six House3 candidates (0 or 1), and thirteen House5 candidates (zero). Its three taverns have 3, 4 and 6 beds. The earlier 162-house population census therefore cannot yet be treated as 162 valid residential households.

The scanned vanilla alchemist/pawn-shop interiors do contain bed models. This does not invalidate the requirement: any shop whose effective layout lacks suitable living quarters, including modded layouts, sends its owner to a separate assigned home after closing. No visual or accessibility audit of every shop is claimed.

Allocation follows the user's clarified policy: beds and floor/room size are weak preferences; couples and children can share beds; larger homes favor larger families; maximum household size is five. Do not enforce one NPC per bed or use this table alone to allocate residents.

Reproduce with `tools/Editor/BedCensus.cs` deployed to an ignored Unity Editor folder and Unity `-batchmode -nographics -quit -executeMethod BedCensus.Run`. Evidence: `docs/data/bed-census.csv`, local `../work/bed-census.log`. Original game archives remain unchanged. City-overhaul replacement layouts need their own live census.

## Original: CIVILIAN-RESPONSES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Citizen response checkpoint

## Persistent murder witnesses, 16 October 2026

Schema 19 distinguishes confirmed player-caused innocent deaths, issued accusations and resolved crimes. Ordinary direct witnesses and their bounded warned contacts flee and refuse dialogue until resolution, including after the temporary alarm expires. A native crime value of None before a witness reports does not clear this state. Partner/child grudges remain independent and lasting. Native keeper responses can clear immediately after resolution rather than waiting for the old alarm deadline.

Attribution occurs after the weapon-hit callback confirms death. Exterior murder dispatch requires a corpse and is retried after confirmation. Vanilla mobile-civilian hits already kill instantly; this dispatch gate is defensive, not evidence that vanilla supports nonlethal civilian attacks. Nonlethal escaped violence uses Assault without replacing an existing charge.

Core suite: 518/518. Unity build and native isolated-store adapter/FullSerializer checks pass (`work/WitnessPersistence-performance-pass.log`). The actual weapon-hit/nearby-guard reporting regression also passes (`work/WitnessPersistence-guard-pass.log`), including corpse-less dispatch rejection, no automatic distant guards, bounded report dispatch and native restoration. Its fixture now waits for complete asynchronous approach surveying and selects points outside the actual 20 m report radius; the old partial-cache/35 m fixture failed before reaching gameplay assertions. Resolution uses the existing native crime-state signal after game readiness, not a new courtroom event; regional crime transitions and complete courtroom playthroughs remain unverified.

## Ordinary stationary people, 15 October 2026

Registration now covers ordinary building `StaticNPC` identities outside shops as well: tavern staff/patrons, guild staff, temple staff and bankers receive the existing native weapon-hit, corpse, awareness and persisted-death behavior. Only actual shop merchants allocate shop-family beds. Flats already replaced by a residential family are excluded, avoiding duplicate identities. QuestResourceBehaviour, DaggerfallAction and named Individual-faction actors remain separately owned; this checkpoint does not remove quest-specific protection or implement all their schedules.

Native test `TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -StationaryNpcProbe` kills28 ordinary people across5 real building categories and confirms no revival on interior re-entry. The red case was an unregistered tavern NPC182/1, faction510. Evidence: `work/Stationary-runtime-red.log`, `work/Stationary-runtime-pass.log`. Keeper/ordinary book-holder real save-load and report/sleep regressions pass (`work/Stationary-keeper-pass.log`);342 core tests and Unity build pass. Test kills remain in the isolated test process; this probe does not overwrite normal user saves.

Successful human witness reports now show: “A witness has reported your crime to the guards.” The notice occurs only after responders are dispatched and a living direct witness exists. Existing report deduplication prevents repeat messages; hearing-only investigations and enemy-defense reports do not accuse the player. Native delayed-escape regression records one notice at the successful report (`work/Stationary-keeper-pass.log`).

## Flight speed amendment, 14 October 2026

New flight uses a stable per-resident speed between 2.5 and 3.5 times the normal 1.3 m/s walking baseline, or 3.25–4.55 m/s. The multiplier derives from the resident's persisted appearance seed, so it does not change on each flight, scene transition or reload. Physical retreat and new planned indoor/exterior escape journeys use this speed. Planned escape speed is saved with the route and controls both interpolation and guard-report arrival time. Existing active escape saves with no speed field retain their historical 2.8 m/s timing. Four-decimal physical values survive DFU's decimal JSON conversion exactly; a live test initially showed 4.2341000000000006 becoming 4.2341 while arrival time remained unchanged.

Validation: 182 core tests and native build pass. The actual-player InteriorEscapeProbe checks the resident-specific speed, native serialization, real SAVE32 reload, physical indoor/exterior movement, delayed reporting and finite building response. Evidence: work/FleeSpeed-pass.log. Current walking artwork is reused; distinct running frames remain pending. This supersedes the fixed 2.8 m/s description below for new flight.

## Current 0.3.9 indoor escape

Supported direct fleeing witnesses now follow saved interior exit paths and surveyed streets to a posted guard. The same actor identity appears outside; progress survives real save/load and scene unloading. These planned escapes delay the native indoor alarm until reporting. No-route and unwitnessed indoor cases retain native alarm behavior pending discovery work. Long journeys extend the incident through arrival plus five game minutes. See [indoor witness implementation and limits](HISTORY.md#source-indoor-witnesses-md); earlier scope statements below are historical.

## Current 0.3.8 public reporting

With civilian reactions and experimental guards enabled, an outdoor weapon kill of an owned generated resident no longer automatically dispatches guards. A posted guard must see the crime within 20 metres, or a fleeing direct witness must reach a posted guard within 20 metres and unobstructed sight. Guards receive the immutable crime location, not the player's current position. Dispatch uses the existing finite roster and collision-validated routes; repeated reports cannot replenish the response. Guard reporting does not consume the witness's two-citizen warning allowance. Secondhand witnesses still cannot relay.

The original crime location and spent report survive native serialization. The weapon interception scope restores correctly after normal hits and exceptions. This applies only to supported outdoor generated residents. Indoor native alarms remain active; unwitnessed indoor murder discovery, actual witness exit traversal, stationary NPCs and autonomous offscreen reporting remain unfinished. Earlier checkpoint paragraphs below are historical.

Validation: 151 core tests and native build pass. `TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -GuardWitnessProbe` exercises an actual native weapon kill away from guard posts, a direct witness, local reporting, bounded dispatch to the original crime location, repeated-report suppression and native serialization. The probe deliberately moves the witness to a guard; it does not claim autonomous refuge navigation coverage. Its warning call waits for the real quarter-second polling cadence.

Final 0.3.8 player regressions also pass `-ResponseProbe` (native hit, retreat, counterattack, identity and SAVE29 reload) and `-GuardBuildingProbe` (native indoor alarm, delayed entry, wounded SAVE31 reload and return). Run these switches separately. Logs: `work/guard-witness-runtime-pass.log`, `work/civilian-response-038-pass.log`, `work/guard-building-038-pass.log`. Installed bundle SHA-256: `F9414866227E4950DA47C8F7C8237A845F40A05063D18D8ED1A357FA015892EB`. Upstream tracked source remains unchanged. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed important defect; native mobile-civilian weapon hits are lethal at this interception boundary.

## Current scope

The 0.3.3 implementation starts the approved fight-or-flight behavior with generated residents in Gothway Garden. It does not complete M3 or M4. The user explicitly prioritized behavior over further sprite production on 14 October 2026.

The mod observes the existing native weapon-hit boundary before a mobile civilian is killed. Awake, nearby generated residents with an unobstructed view become direct witnesses. Children flee; an initial stable adult capability policy selects flight or an unarmed counterattack. Existing appearance, name, household and actor identity remain intact. Flight runs at 2.8 metres per real second using existing art. Separate running and battle frames are deferred.

Exterior fleeing residents retrace their own previously validated commute toward their home. Indoors, the initial response retreats through unobstructed space in the current room. Fighters approach only while they can see the player, stop chasing through blocked sight, and counterattack in melee range. This is a basic response, not the final combat, courage, equipment or pursuit system.

Each fleeing direct witness can warn at most two nearby, previously uninformed residents. Secondhand recipients cannot relay. The incident ID, direct/secondhand status, spent warning budget, reaction and local position persist in schema 5. Repeated reports of the same victim do not renew the 15-game-minute local alarm. Actor unload does not erase the incident. Movement while the actor is unloaded still needs catch-up integration.

`General/CivilianReactions` toggles this pilot without deleting its ledger. Existing native crime accounting, racial effects and guard spawning remain untouched in this slice.

## Next gameplay work

1. Implement a saved finite guard roster and replace native instant spawn paths inside supported settlements. Nearby posts and patrols must physically respond; no available guard means no invented squad.
2. Add shared indoor/exterior movement through real doors, witness guard reports and discovery of unwitnessed household murders.
3. Add guard sight loss, last-seen search, escape and return to duty, then patrol pairs, torches and hostile-creature defense.
4. Extend reactions to original stationary citizens while preserving native service and quest identity.
5. Add corpse persistence, one-hour crime-scene occupation, recruitment and household recovery.

Native static NPCs, spells, sleep detection, theft dispatch, guard warning radius, full indoor exit pathfinding, additional safe refuges and offscreen emergency movement are not covered by this first slice. Core and actual-player results are recorded below only after execution.

## Authoritative housing and art rules

Household capacity equals the actual number of beds. One resident per bed. There is no independent five-person cap; bedless or unknown-bed homes receive no new generated residents. Old saves retain their existing population; new test populations enforce the new rule. Additional residents beyond the two-parent/three-child family pattern are adults.

Use complete single-sleeper images, green blanket and white pillow. Separate head/blanket layers and shared-bed images are rejected. Earlier shared-bed kill-all requirements are therefore superseded. Children inherit either parent's race when race modeling expands; existing child sprites remain in use now. No further art generation belongs to this checkpoint.

## Verified evidence, 14 October 2026

- Core: `dotnet run --project tests/LivingCities.Tests` passes 127/127. This includes two unique warnings per witness, no secondhand relay, repeated-report/expiry behavior, persisted budgets, child flight, schema migration, nine-bed occupancy and transactional rejection.
- Native build: `tools/Build.ps1` passes full DFU serialization of incidents and spent warning budgets, settings defaults, save adapter and bundle checks.
- Actual player: `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -ResponseProbe` passes native weapon interception, three eyewitnesses, room retreat, controlled melee counterattack, talk refusal, retained appearance/name and real SAVE29 reconstruction. Both adult reaction branches are forced on the disposable witness for repeatability; stable default selection has core coverage. Melee uses verified floor positions and briefly freezes the test player motor to separate target placement from native physics.
- Actual beds: `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -BedProbe` passes all fresh household capacities, four people in four distinct beds, reconstructed claims, three classic mattress models, ordinary street animation, door continuity, death and save/load. The fresh town has 181 residents including eight children.
- Distribution: built/installed bundle SHA-256 is `CC34F4C41C65088B66DF2A0F04A2E2CE084835087CB7BDFE78AA91BB54E5D06F`. All 598 official player files remain identical to the release archive; upstream tracked source remains unchanged. A subsequent comparison of 309 old save files found no differences.

Evidence logs: `work/civilian-alarm-final.log`, `work/civilian-response-final-pass.log`, `work/single-bed-runtime-pass.log`, `work/player-integrity-033.json`, and sibling `../work/livingcities-build.log`. Luna/high read-only review found no confirmed important issue in the reviewed scope. Exterior emergency arrival, dynamic warning encounters and sight-loss behavior still need dedicated actual-player scenarios; this checkpoint does not imply full guard or city-wide behavior coverage.
