## Original: CRIME-SCENE-DISCOVERY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Unwitnessed household murder discovery

## Replacement inspectors verified, 16 October 2026

If an assigned inspector dies, leaves, returns from duty or no longer belongs to the incident, another living arrived responder inside the same building can take over during the original occupation window. A healthy eligible inspector retains its assignment and progress. A replacement starts its own five-second inspection; no eligible replacement leaves the corpse uncleared. Neither replacement nor repeated assignment extends the first-arrival one-hour deadline. Switching from wandering to inspection discards the old wandering route so the replacement approaches the body.

Core 373/373 passes, including partial inspection, native roster death, state restore, replacement, fresh five seconds, unchanged deadline, no surviving responder and expired occupation. The installed Unity build and complete live inn probe pass: the first native guard is killed mid-inspection, another responder physically reaches/inspects/clears the upstairs body, Murder remains recorded, and occupation does not restart. Evidence: `work/ReplacementInspector-red.log`, `work/ReplacementInspector-core.log`, `work/ReplacementInspector-runtime-pass.log`, `work/ReplacementInspector-build-editor.log`. The runner requires `LIVING_CITIES_REPLACEMENT_INSPECTOR_PASS`. Unloaded physical inspection and exhaustive building-layout coverage remain open.

## Retained-accusation recovery verified, 16 October 2026

The household fixture now uses the same sustained native concealment approach. Its full live regression passes physical corpse inspection/cleanup, visible countdown progress before Trespassing, native serialization of the remaining warning, actual save/reload, refusal retained across frames and additional guards, acceptance into native court, and one-hour occupation expiry (`work/SceneCountdownEffect-runtime-pass.log`, `work/SceneCountdownEffect-build-editor.log`). This supersedes the household failure recorded below. No navigation relaxation or guard teleport was required.

The inn failure below was caused by the test's temporary concealment flag, not an unreachable route. Diagnostics found a clear inspector position, 193 exit-route points, and 177 reachable/visible approach candidates. At failure the player was visible again, the guard was hostile, and a dialogue had paused gameplay. DFU clears constant effect flags through `ClearConstantEffects`; setting `MagicalConcealmentFlags` once does not provide sustained invisibility.

The probe now assigns a named native `Invisibility-True` spell bundle, verifies concealment remains active throughout inspection, and removes test-added bundles afterward. A name is required because HUD spell icons call `bundle.name.TrimStart`. With that controlled loss of sight, the real native guard leaves combat after the separate five-minute pursuit timeout, reaches the upstairs corpse, inspects for five seconds, and clears it without clearing Murder. The original one-hour occupation remains independent. Core 372/372 and Unity build pass; the complete inn alarm/traveler-arrival/retained-Murder cleanup probe passes (`work/InspectionEffect-runtime-pass.log`, `work/InspectionEffect-build-editor.log`, `work/InspectionEffect-core.log`). This supersedes the inn routing diagnosis below. It proves sustained magical concealment recovery in this layout, not every physical hiding location, unloaded inspection, or replacement inspectors.

## Current verification limitation, 16 October 2026

The follow-up pursuit timer separates five-minute lost-sight combat from one-hour scene occupation and passes saved-state core checks (372/372). The live inn test retains Murder and confirms native combat expires after controlled loss of sight, but the inspector then has no route from its post-combat position to the upstairs body (`work/ScenePursuit-runtime-red.log`). Full cleanup under retained accusation is therefore not verified. A subsequent household scene regression also failed to reach its body before the countdown stage. Do not treat the older passing isolated-cleanup fixtures below as proof that these paths now pass. The previously verified ten-second visible warning remains implemented; the latest household rerun did not reach that assertion.

## Witnessed murder cleanup verified, 16 October 2026

The full native keeper regression also passes after the witnessed-report and approach-point changes, including neutral cleanup and guarded visitor rejection (`work/WitnessedInspection-shop-regression-pass.log`).

New witnessed corpse reports retain their accusation policy (`Investigation=false`) and carry a separate saved `CrimeScene` flag. They record responder arrivals, keep the one-hour scene occupation, and permit the same five-second inspection without converting a known Murder accusation into a neutral investigation. Occupied-scene visitor rejection and traveler admission now recognize both report kinds. Core 372/372 verifies accusation preservation, combat-paused inspection, cleanup and restore. Unity build and the actual inn attack/report/inspection probe pass (`work/WitnessedInspection-runtime-pass.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_WITNESSED_INSPECTION_PASS`; `work/WitnessedInspection-build-editor.log`). The probe first asserts Murder from a real eyewitness report, then temporarily clears crime and conceals the player to isolate cleanup, restoring both afterward. Natural pursuit loss and post-combat recovery are not proved by that controlled step. Historical already-dispatched reports default to no new crime-scene flag; automatic retrofitting remains pending.

The first live attempts exposed a navigation defect: an assigned inspector stayed at the entrance with no route while the corpse was upstairs. Inspection had selected targets only from 80 general standing anchors, which intentionally avoid NPC locations and can omit upper rooms. `InteriorGeometry.ApproachPoints` now selects from the surveyed walkable floor near the corpse, retaining route, height, distance and line-of-sight checks. The same inn fixture then reached and cleared the body. Red evidence: `work/WitnessedInspection-route-red.log`. Unloaded physical inspection, replacement inspectors, all-layout coverage and natural combat recovery remain open.

## Neutral-report inspection verified, 16 October 2026

Neutral `inn-alarm:` reports from corpse observers now enter the same physical inspection flow as `discovery:` reports. The core selects an arrived, living guard inside the correct building and validates progress against the matching report's arrival evidence; an unwitnessed household record is no longer required. Five seconds of inspection, pause/combat gating and save/restore progress remain intact. The native shop fixture verifies shopper departure, unloaded street contact, neutral dispatch without a new Murder charge, delayed guard entry, approach to the body and completed inspection/cleanup. The player is concealed and crime cleared only during this test's inspection phase, then both prior values are restored, to avoid testing arrest interruption instead of inspection. Evidence: `work/NeutralInspection-runtime-pass.log` (`LIVING_CITIES_NEUTRAL_INSPECTION_PASS`), `work/NeutralInspection-build-editor.log`, 369/369 core tests in `work/NeutralInspection-core.log`. The full native keeper suite passes. This supersedes the earlier neutral-prefix integration gap. Witnessed accusation reports using `civilian:`, unloaded inspection progress, inspector replacement, broader interiors and guarded-scene visitor rejection remain separate work.

## Verified live checkpoint, 16 October 2026

The household investigation fixture now passes physical approach to the corpse, inspection progress, five-second cleanup, native serialization of remaining inspection time, visible warning countdown, Trespassing after refusal, vanilla surrender before damage, real scene save/reload and occupation expiry. Evidence: `work/CrimeInspection-runtime-pass.log` and `work/Parallel-seventh-build-editor.log` (subsequent successful build includes the incident-prefix correction). The first live attempt exposed an incorrect hardcoded `discovery:` prefix length; the adapter now uses the prefix string length. The wider integration limitations below still apply. Random wandering is connected but this fixture does not independently assert every wander route or interior layout.

## Required replacement: physical inspection and cleanup, 16 October 2026

This requirement supersedes the historical immediate-cleanup-on-arrival behavior below. Unity compilation and the household live fixture passed; broader scene coverage remains pending as described above. The installed verification build is recorded in `work/Parallel-eighth-build-editor.log`. The subsequent shop activity regression also passed for both buyers and non-buyers (`work/Parallel-shop-regression-pass.log`).

- Arrival starts the existing one-game-hour occupation deadline, without renewing it on reload or for later guards.
- A guard walks through the interior to the actual corpse, faces it, and inspects it for five unpaused real seconds before clearing it. Entering the building alone must not remove the corpse.
- After cleanup, guards wander among reachable positions inside the building for the remainder of that hour, then leave normally.
- Persist inspection/cleanup progress and occupation timing. Pause and interruption by combat must not silently complete the inspection. Coordinate multiple guards and corpses without duplicate cleanup; unreachable bodies must not be cleared merely because a route failed.
- Preserve the separate outdoor cleanup policy, warning/arrest rules, and priority of active threats.

Acceptance must cover visible arrival/path/inspection/removal, multiple bodies, stairs, interrupted inspection, save/reload, unloaded transitions and departure at the original occupation deadline.

Additional 16 October warning requirement: show the remaining ten-second leave deadline as a visible countdown, updated once per whole unpaused second. Leaving cancels the warning; save/load retains the remaining time. At zero, charge Trespassing and preserve the normal surrender/arrest choice, with combat following refusal. Do not count paused dialogue/menu time or repeatedly restart the warning.

Implementation checkpoint, 16 October 2026: a scene report stores one fixed occupation deadline at earliest responder arrival plus one game hour, and all assigned responders share it. Scene discovery now assigns one deterministic earliest-arrival guard as corpse inspector without clearing the body. The core inspection ledger stores that guard and five seconds remaining; only that living guard, inside the target building, can advance it. Paused time, combat, and time before reaching the body do not decrement it. Completion clears and releases lodging; ordinary indoor cleanup remains one day while unattended, and exterior discovery/cleanup remains separate. The runtime routes the assigned inspector to a reachable interior anchor beside the saved body position, faces the body, then starts inspection. After cleanup, each responder follows reachable interior anchors until the fixed occupation deadline and exits through the normal return path. Runtime warning text displays 10 through 1, once per whole unpaused second; saved remaining time is displayed when an active warning resumes. The full core suite passes 366/366. Native/Unity compilation and live scene verification have not yet run.

Known integration limits: this path currently runs only for discovered `discovery:` reports backed by an unwitnessed scene. Witnessed shop/inn murders and other corpse reports without `UnwitnessedScene` do not yet receive this inspector behavior. Interior movement paths are rebuilt from loaded building geometry after scene reload; inspection progress and guard position are persisted, while the transient route is not. A body without a saved position, without a nearby reachable anchor, or in a different building is deliberately not cleared by this helper. Unloaded buildings cannot physically inspect the corpse or accrue inspection progress; their saved body remains until the route runs in a loaded interior. This change does not add neutral arriving-shopper reporting for an indoor corpse after the original violence alarm has expired; that discovery hook remains a separate pending dependency and must not identify the player as a witness.

Latest approved policy: returning to a scene does not prove Murder. An unidentified indoor visitor receives a ten-second unpaused-real-time order to leave. Refusal triggers Trespassing and normal surrender/arrest. Already witnessed murder remains Murder. This supersedes the immediate-Murder re-entry statements in the historical checkpoints below. Save/load must preserve an active warning's remaining time; leaving ends that visit's warning.

## Corpse runtime checkpoint, 14 October 2026

Generated residents killed through the native weapon-damage hook now retain corpse state and a native human corpse placeholder (texture 380, record 1). The saved death position is independent of the first assault position, so a victim who moves before a later lethal hit does not leave a body at the original alarm location. Views are keyed by incident, limited by distance outdoors, rebuilt from saved state, and removed on scene changes. Confirmed investigator arrival clears the body permanently; ordinary corpses use the existing one-day/absence cleanup rule. Legacy deaths without confirmed corpse state do not gain invented bodies.

The actual-player CrimeSceneProbe passes body creation after a native kill, native serialization of its death position, removal after guard arrival, cleared state after real SAVE33 reload, and existing Murder/surrender/one-hour occupation behavior. Evidence: work/CorpseProbe-pass.log. Core suite: 162/162. Native serializer checks include all saved corpse coordinates. The escaping-witness regression also passes. These checks do not establish final visual fidelity: the temporary sprite is not matched to each resident, and specialized bed corpses, loot and non-weapon death paths remain unfinished. Older pending-runtime statements below describe prior checkpoints.

The approved rule is departure-triggered discovery: an unobserved household killing does not immediately identify the player or summon guards. Once the player leaves, finite guards investigate the house. Their arrival clears the corpse and starts one game hour of scene occupation. Returning during that window triggers custody behavior without claiming that guards witnessed the murder. Repeated reports and reloads must not renew the timer.

Implementation order:

1. Persist a scene only for a confirmed dead generated resident in their home with no murder witnesses. Persist first departure and first confirmed guard arrival independently of the short civilian alarm window.
2. Add investigation reports to the finite roster with one-hour occupation deadlines, immutable report type and normal physical approach/return routes.
3. Extend the native weapon finalizer to preserve the previous known crime for successfully concealed murders. Suppress native instant dispatch only while the complete supported replacement is active.
4. Observe real departure, dispatch existing guards against the saved approach, record arrival, clear corpse presentation and connect re-entry to native custody. Validate actual save/load, no early response, occupation expiry and prior-crime preservation.

The 0.3.10 adapter connects confirmed generated-resident home deaths to finite investigation. The scoped completion preserves previously known crime. Confirmed transition events record departure immediately; off-screen travel advances independently of actor ownership. Investigators remain virtually inside their destination after arrival and appear through the surveyed entry on re-entry. Seeing the player there charges Murder and starts native guard engagement, following the latest user correction.

Current occupation policy is one game hour, overriding the earlier one-hour request on 14 October 2026. Existing preview saves with an exact one-hour active occupation floor are normalized in memory; files are not rewritten by loading. Active pursuit deadlines and completed history are retained. Corpse visuals/cleanup, stationary victims, broader layouts and household recovery remain unfinished.

## Verified core foundation

The incident ledger now optionally owns an `UnwitnessedScene`. Registration requires a confirmed dead resident, a known crime position in that resident's own home and no murder witnesses. An explicit location observer records first departure once, even after the normal civilian alarm has expired. Explicit guard arrival records first discovery once; `Occupied(now)` lasts exactly one game hour from that arrival. Malformed chronology, living victims and witnessed murders are rejected, and failed restore leaves prior state intact. Legacy incidents do not acquire scenes retroactively.

Finite building reports optionally use investigation policy. They keep normal physical travel deadlines, occupy for one hour from arrival, and retain report type and responder identities through repeated calls and reload. Ordinary response deadlines remain five minutes. An indoor suspect sighting cannot shorten an investigation's one-hour floor, though active combat can extend the usual search beyond it. Guards eventually retrace their normal return path.

Validation: 159/159 core tests and `tools/VerifyGuards.ps1` pass. DFU's native FullSerializer retains scene departure/discovery chronology, investigation type and one-hour deadlines. Evidence: sibling `../work/guards-persistence.log`, markers `LIVING_CITIES_SCENE_PERSISTENCE_PASS` and `LIVING_CITIES_GUARDS_PERSISTENCE_PASS`. This core prerequisite originally left installed 0.3.9 unchanged; runtime verification is recorded below.

Review hardening: null location means unknown and cannot trigger departure; explicit -1 means exterior. Investigation reports retain first actual arrival when a living responder transitions from Responding to Searching. Discovery requires that evidence, uses its original timestamp and survives late catch-up after guards have returned. Dead en-route responders cannot discover the scene. Save restoration cross-checks discovered scenes against the matching investigation report. Historical responder IDs remain in old reports after return so repeated reports cannot replenish guards; membership alone is never proof of arrival.

Native integration constraints: native WeaponDamage assigns Murder and calls SpawnCityGuards before SetHealth(0). A before-hit hook alone cannot confirm death or restore the prior known crime. Use a scoped finalizer and verify normal/exception/nested restoration. Detect departure before clearing town state. Call discovery only on an actual living responder's arrival; an empty roster must never fabricate arrival. Keep investigation guards virtually inside while their building is unloaded, materialize them through the surveyed entry on re-entry, and preserve the original absolute occupation deadline. Arrest interaction and corpse presentation require actual-player probes before publishing the feature.

Arrival validation also retains a per-responder scheduled-arrival ledger. Restore rejects invented arrival flags that contradict active travel or deaths before arrival, while preserving completed evidence after guards return or take later assignments.

## Native damage completion prerequisite

`NativeCivilianHooks` now accepts a per-hit begin callback returning an `Action<Exception>` completion. The completion runs after native WeaponDamage, with its own target still available. Nested hits restore the enclosing target; native and prefix failures also restore scope. Completion errors propagate when native damage succeeded, but cannot replace an existing native exception. The original before-hit constructor remains compatible.

`tools/VerifyGuards.ps1` exercises the actual Harmony-patched native method with inert targets, nested hits, a null-target native exception, prefix failure, completion failure and the legacy constructor. Marker: `LIVING_CITIES_CIVILIAN_SCOPES_PASS`. This validates the hook contract, not actual murder concealment: the runtime adapter still must capture the prior crime, confirm the generated victim died, register the scene, and suppress immediate response only for a fully supported investigation.

## Runtime verification

`TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -CrimeSceneProbe` exercises a native kill in a surveyed one-resident home, no premature report, preservation of an existing Theft crime, synchronous departure observation, finite physical dispatch, arrival discovery, re-entry custody trigger, real SAVE33 reload and occupation expiry. SAVE33 is `Living Cities - crime scene investigation`. The player test accelerates later travel and occupation deadlines. It validates the custody trigger, not every arrest-dialogue outcome. Generated dead actors still disappear immediately; persistent corpse rendering and timed visual cleanup are the next missing part of this requirement.

Verified 0.3.10 bundle: SHA-256 `A7FC5B219225F4F0EA1E88B1E1371E34355BE4400EDF1EB9437E44A426D24BDE`. Core suite: 159/159. Actual-player crime-scene and indoor-witness regression logs are `work/CrimeSceneProbe-0310-pass.log` and `work/InteriorEscapeProbe-0310-pass.log`.

Corpse persistence prerequisite: opt-in confirmed-death state now records death and irreversible cleanup timestamps. Scene corpses clear on confirmed discovery; ordinary corpses require a full day and absence from the same interior. Unknown location does not authorize timed cleanup. Old deaths do not gain invented corpse state. Core tests pass (161 total); runtime confirmation/rendering remains pending.

<a id="source-crime-scenes-md"></a>

## Original: CRIME-SCENES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Crime reports, physical guard response and crime scenes

User refinements, 14 October 2026. These distinguish eyewitness alarm, body discovery and a known murderer.

## Shop theft and delayed guard arrival

When detected shop theft calls guards, dispatch available members of the existing finite roster from nearby exterior posts, patrols or actual guard quarters, including reachable city-wall headquarters entrances. Guards must travel from their current locations to the shop and enter through its door as they arrive. Do not spawn a squad inside, teleport guards to the entrance, or treat every wall door as a guard source. Headquarters can supply only guards already assigned there.

Arrival time follows the actual reachable route, guard speed and door transitions. A distant garrison means a longer wait; no available guard means no invented immediate response. The player can leave during this delay and attempt escape. Keep the response advancing while the player remains inside, leaves, or reloads; crossing the shop threshold must neither summon responders instantly nor reset their journey. Once outside, pursuit uses actual sightings, last-known position and the planned search/escape rules rather than continuous knowledge of the player's position.

The shopkeeper can attack or remain passive after reporting. Select a stable response from capability, disposition and courage, keeping reporting separate from combat. Preserve the shopkeeper's identity and use the required appearance-specific combat animation when active; passivity does not cancel the report. This choice is separate from the indoor-murder fight-or-flight rules below.

Acceptance cases: nearby patrol versus distant post with measurably different arrival times; real headquarters exit; staggered responders entering as they arrive; player leaves before the first arrival and escapes unseen; player remains inside while exterior travel progresses; shopkeeper attacks versus stays passive; no available guards; duplicate theft reports do not duplicate guards; save/load preserves responder identity, location and journey progress. This refinement is planned for M4; vanilla theft guard spawning remains in the current preview.

## Indoor murder and discovery

### Sleeping deaths (latest decision, 14 October 2026)

One person occupies each bed, and household capacity equals actual bed count with no independent five-person cap. This supersedes the earlier shared-bed kill-all decision. A lethal hit kills that bed's sole occupant. Old saves retain their identities, but excess legacy residents receive no shared-bed assignments. Fresh compliant test populations are separate from preserved old saves.

Slain-in-bed sprites are still missing. Add covered corpse poses with the same green blanket and white pillows, distinguishable from living sleepers and retaining appearance. Bodies must remain until the established discovery/cleanup rule removes them; save/reload must preserve that state. The current preview removes deceased actors immediately and does not yet implement corpse persistence or crime-scene cleanup.

If a house has one occupant and the player kills that person in one blow without another witness, do not send an immediate guard alarm. On the player's departure, schedule guards from the finite roster to travel to and enter the house. They discover the body and clear it upon arrival. This specific discovery cleanup supersedes the generic one-day corpse-cleanup delay for this case. No teleporting reinforcements and no attribution of an unwitnessed murder to the player merely because a corpse exists.

From the guards' arrival, hold the crime scene for one game hour. If the player returns during that interval, guards attempt to take the player into custody as for a crime. Track this explicitly as crime-scene suspicion/custody behavior, separately from a witnessed murderer identity. After one hour the occupation expires and guards resume normal duty; the house remains vacant until its independent recovery/sale deadline. Do not use an unobserved corpse cleanup event to invent a witness.

Where other occupants are present, survivors react immediately. Stationary indoor NPCs must become active actors rather than remaining inert flats: those capable of fighting can become hostile and attack, while others flee outside and inform guards. A successful fleeing witness raises the alarm; responding guards pursue the player as hostile. The final clarification permits fight-or-flight, superseding the preceding blanket instruction that all survivors flee. Preserve persistent identity, faction, relationships, service ownership and quest bindings when changing actor mode. Do not replace a quest NPC with an unrelated generic enemy.

Keep witness knowledge and readiness to fight separate. Household members absent elsewhere do not witness the killing automatically. Partner-killing hostility and bereavement remain applicable, with flight to obtain help a possible immediate response. A murder that is not silent/instant, outside witnesses, failed escape or guards already present can follow the broader perception system; this one-blow special case is not blanket impunity for every indoor attack.

Use separate persisted events for killing, witnessing, fleeing/alarm, player departure, guard dispatch/arrival, corpse removal, custody suspicion and one-hour occupation expiry. Timers use game time and survive save/load/absence. Dispatch only available guards; tiny hamlets with no garrison cannot conjure responders. Record a pending report/discovery and respect later external response capacity rather than violating finite rosters.

## Public incidents and bounded warnings

Nearby NPCs who perceive a public killing flee toward home or a reachable safe interior such as a guild, castle or shop. Refuge selection respects access, opening hours and actual routes. While fleeing, each direct witness alerts guards within a configurable local radius and warns at most the next two eligible NPCs encountered nearby. Those recipients may flee or fight according to their capabilities and disposition, but do not relay secondhand warnings further. This one-hop rule bounds propagation from each witness and prevents an unlimited town-wide chain reaction.

Give each incident a stable ID. Deduplicate reports and recipients, retain each witness's two-recipient budget across actor unloading/save-load, and expire local alarm opportunities. A recipient already aware of this incident is not a new warning recipient. Multiple direct witnesses can each warn two people; genuinely new witnessed violence can create a new incident. Guard alerts update the same finite response rather than spawning a fresh squad for every report. Exposure to the same corpse or repeated reporting must not reset budgets.

## Player-caused deaths and quest compatibility

The user explicitly superseded blanket quest-NPC invulnerability: deliberate player kills may permanently kill important NPCs and break their quests. Preserve the NPC's technical identity, quest bindings and save integrity; do not silently resurrect or substitute another actor to repair story consequences. Keep protection from random background events as the default, separately from player-caused death policy. The current foundation's `IsQuestProtected` death restriction remains implemented until a versioned policy migration separates these cases.

Acceptance cases: silent single-occupant kill while player stays; exit then travel delay; corpse removal only after arrival; return before/after one-hour expiry; multiple occupants fighting/fleeing; stationary-to-active identity continuity; absent household member; no available guards; save/load during each phase; deliberate player kill of a quest NPC; ambient-event protection; owned-property handling; two warnings per direct witness; no secondhand relay; duplicate reports; multiple witnesses; save/load of warning budgets. This behavior is planned; current vanilla combat still spawns guards and the temporary hook probe does not implement these crime rules.

Latest charge correction (14 October 2026): when investigators find the player in the murder house during the scene response, charge Murder, not Trespassing. Preserve vanilla surrender/arrest before applying guard damage. Unobserved death still does not immediately dispatch guards before departure.

# Daggerfall nightlife and the Lysandus haunting

## 16 October: quest-owned dawn expiry

Stock `S0000977.txt` stops its spawn task during 05:00–19:00, but does not explicitly hide or destroy existing foes. `Task.Clear()` only clears the triggered flag. Correction to the initial investigation: native scene-cache serialization contains doors and loot, not enemies. Existing exterior enemies remain alive under the disabled exterior parent while the player is indoors. These native sources are unchanged; Living Cities' enemy-defense dictionary only holds actor references. The native probe reproduced a genuinely quest-owned ghost surviving dawn (`work/HauntingDawn-red-native.log`). The old city fixture lacks an active haunting quest, so the probe starts the stock quest in its disposable session without rewriting the fixture.

`HauntingLifetime` now tracks only S0000977-owned Ghost/Wraith actors and expires them at their next 05:00 boundary or curse resolution. Despawn avoids damage, death and loot events. It watches spawn/load/exterior transitions and includes inactive exterior actors. A reviewer found that destroying an unplaced native spawn could invalidate CreateFoe's pending array; a failing native regression reproduced that edge. The adapter now retains pending inactive-self actors until placement and uses OnEnable to suppress expired activation synchronously. Existing hidden exterior actors have activeSelf=true and still expire indoors.

Unity build and `TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -HauntingDawnProbe` pass: outdoor dawn, inactive exterior expiry while inside an inn, unrelated ghost preservation, and delayed placement across dawn (`work/HauntingPending-green-build.log`, `work/HauntingPending-green-native.log`). Actual disk reload and curse-resolution regression remain to be added. No engine or quest script changes were made.

## Settlement coverage and live regional verification (0.3.31)

StreetPopulation and GuardPopulation now admit native city/town/village categories0/1/2 rather than two named map IDs. Unsupported location types retain native behavior; the Daggerfall-specific test isolation remains internal.269/269 core tests pass. `TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -RegionalProbe` passes with470 schedules/38 guards in Berbaaqnia and435 schedules/35 guards in Bubogureg. Each city validates every generated name/race and constructs two adult actors to check race, gender, identity and billboard asset. This is not a visual portrait audit or continuous movement acceptance for all layouts.

The native RegionRaces table only supplies Breton/Redguard names; Nord appearance comes from mountain climate independently. Live generated names contain no missing-localization placeholder. Probe evidence: `work/WorldCoverage-regional-pass.log`. A dedicated command-line probe avoids rerunning the entire refuge suite for regional diagnostics; no menu option was added. The initial combined probe failed because gameplay had not resumed after travel; returning the disposable session to HUD and waiting for population initialization resolves that fixture issue. The full combined refuge suite still needs rerunning after this expansion; its prior city checks passed before regional failure. Regional fixtures are not saved.

## Single-name regional households

Native NameHelper.Surname(Redguard) returns an empty string. The previous initializer rejected it and would interrupt initialization in Redguard regions. Missing surnames now use a separately seeded regional first name as the shared household surname, retaining the requested family naming convention without borrowing Breton names. Existing identities remain unchanged.

268/268 core tests pass, including a previously failing single-name culture case. Native editor verification exercises Breton, Redguard and Nord banks (`work/SingleName-native.log`); Redguard produces C'ozom Caogur and Stirt-e Caogur. The headless Nord output contains a LocaleText-NotFound suffix because localization is not initialized there; this is not proof of valid in-game Nord display. Live regional appearance/localization verification remains pending. Unity build passed and installed bundle SHA-256 is `3F7E16886B492438264C840784E43131CFD8167C644F5408A9AE7F2D9B1569A4` (development 0.3.30).

## Persistent regional household identity

RegionalIdentityAdapter now initializes missing household surnames, resident full names and race before schedule setup, using native MapsFile.RegionRaces for the name bank and ClimateSettings.People for supported display race. A native household head's explicit race takes precedence for missing family race. Existing explicit names/races/surnames remain unchanged. Name generation retains appearance/surname seeds and restores DFRandom state; assigned names and race use existing save fields, so later actor recreation does not depend on the player's current region. Ordinary initialized actors therefore no longer depend on the old Breton fallback.

267/267 core tests pass after the expected missing-implementation failure. Unity build and actual city save/reload/movement suite pass (`work/RegionalIdentity-{red,green,live-pass}.log`). Read-only inspection of SAVE50 confirms611/611 city residents have FullName and nonnegative Race. Installed0.3.30 SHA-256 `148036724361B18A737B6D3BC82203A81FD4BA19E1A818CBECB0F41877C2BBC0`. Live Redguard/Nord region appearance checks and wider settlement admission remain pending. Generic actor fallback remains for independently created/uninitialized fixtures.

## Settlement scale follows native category

GuardPopulation now reads LocationData.MapTableData.LocationType rather than identifying Daggerfall by map ID to select a garrison scale. Original DFRegion values0/1/2 map to City/Town/Hamlet; other location types reject classification instead of silently treating dungeons, temples or isolated buildings as towns. Existing8% quota/minimums remain unchanged.266/266 core tests, native build and combined city/save/movement suite pass (`work/SettlementScale-{red,green,live-pass}.log`). Installed0.3.30 hash `7D10A90513D912F659622C797B908D45E2A8063B1FE699C566B283DE811BA6CD`.

Wider settlement admission remains pending. Inspection found generated ResidentActor fallback appearance and first/surnames are hardcoded Breton; these must follow native regional identity rules before expanding to Redguard/Nord regions. Native PopulationManager uses PlayerGPS.ClimateSettings.People for display race, while MobilePersonNPC uses MapsFile.RegionRaces[CurrentRegionIndex] for the name bank. Persist identity choices independently of transient actors and preserve explicit native identities.

## Initial rendered movement sample

After the real city save/reload, the fixture advances to a daytime journey, positions the test player near its analytical location, and requires that resident's real ResidentActor to appear and remain active while moving for three seconds. Resident s:1291010263:h:65796:r:0 moved1.053m net displacement;11 nearby actors were active, below the80 actor cap. The195 sampled frames measured median13.383ms and p95 28.774ms. This is one hidden automated run with the whole game active, not isolated mod CPU cost, a crowded-market benchmark, continuous collision validation or a player-visible FPS guarantee.

Evidence: `work/CityMovement-pass.log`, marker `LIVING_CITIES_CITY_MOVEMENT_PASS`. Unity build and combined city/refuge/save suite pass. Installed0.3.30 SHA-256 `0867F31ADFEE80EE4BB5E1FC944F0B99727175D3232E4813D8C9356EF3C45D2C`. Broader crowd, movement and performance acceptance remains open.

## Automatic city coverage in0.3.30

Daggerfall City now runs automatically alongside Gothway Garden. The proposed city-preview menu option was removed at the user's request; no temporary settlement enablement settings should be added. The final scope remains the whole game world. Test-only suppression isolates synthetic refuge fixtures and is restored in finally; normal sessions never enable suppression. The full city fixture and actual SAVE50 reload pass with this admission path (`work/CityAutomatic-pass.log`). Historical gated/opt-in descriptions below are superseded. Broader world integration and remaining gameplay/performance work are not complete.

## Actual city save/reload

DaggerfallSaveProbe now writes and reloads the dedicated test character's `Living Cities - Daggerfall city preview` save (SAVE50). It requires a LivingCitiesTest- character name. Native load reconstructs the full city population and guard controller; checks compare serialized household identities/attributes and saved routine routes, all49 guard IDs, daily haunting policy, and all611 managed residents' activities/buildings/route positions sampled at the same noon timestamp. All comparisons passed. The probe does not yet verify continuous rendered movement or steady-state city performance. Normal play still requires the forthcoming city-preview admission setting; loading this fixture alone does not bypass that gate.

Evidence: `work/CitySave-pass.log`, marker `LIVING_CITIES_CITY_SAVE_PASS`. Unity build and the combined refuge/city suite pass. Installed development0.3.29 SHA-256 `0A8806CE92C988F75EDC37C651796D63D11F380BD94A3C4079B0420D9E3182FF`. Earlier no-save fixture descriptions below are historical: the suite now deliberately saves only its dedicated test character.

## Full city population fixture

An opt-in DaggerfallPopulationProbe now temporarily permits actual StreetPopulation and GuardPopulation admission in map1291010263, after the refuge suite. The first run failed because the Gothway-only gates prevented setup. Those gates now accept Daggerfall only while the disposable probe is active; normal player sessions remain gated. Guard target sizing uses SettlementScale.City for Daggerfall. Quest-binding scope is unchanged.

The live fixture built611 resident schedules and49 guards (ceil611×8%) in11.61 seconds, then checked every managed resident's20:00 schedule was at home under the unresolved haunting. The fixture uses the actual population created by HousingSurvey, not the prior one-guard synthetic rosters. It restores its quest flag and clears city views afterward without writing a save. This measures setup completion, not worst-frame latency or steady-state FPS; full native city save/reload and actual route/actor continuity remain pending before normal city admission.

Evidence: `work/CityPopulation-red.log`, `work/CityPopulation-pass.log`, `work/CityPopulation-core.log` (265/265). Unity build passes. Installed development0.3.29 SHA-256 `8E9B0A10E7312B7A74C8BC6A065ED2D5327564F5DCF79261270CC27F288CD389`.

## Saved daily civilian policy and runtime wiring

WorldState now has an optional HauntingDay (day number and restricted flag). First observation of each Daggerfall day chooses from live LiftedCurse; later observations retain that decision until the next day, including after save restore. Other settlements do not create this state. Legacy saves initialize it lazily, and malformed day numbers reject restore transactionally. The native quest offers no historical completion timestamp; first observation is authoritative for previously unseen days.

StreetPopulation builds ordinary and safe-window schedules and routes all schedule evaluations through the saved daily policy. It uses actual world time to commit the day, not forward animation samples. Restricted trips depart no earlier than05:00 and reach home by19:00, preserving normal bedtimes. Lifting the curse releases guards immediately but civilian appointments resume the next day to avoid repositioning an underway trip. This covers generated DayVisit plans; native keepers and broader future tavern/work schedules still need their own integration.

265/265 core tests pass, including same-day resolution/save continuity, next-day recovery, legacy-null and invalid-day restore cases. Native DFU WorldState serialization passes (`LIVING_CITIES_HAUNTING_DAY_SAVE_PASS` in `../work/guards-persistence.log`). Evidence `work/HauntingRoutineDay-{red,green}.log`. Build installed development0.3.29 SHA-256 `D7D0BECD99FC15312711FA12F9231C8D484FF0AFBA069B59D330DCD97A3415BB`. City admission remains disabled; actual city schedule population/save/load validation is pending. This supersedes the source-only limitation below.

## Civilian travel-window foundation

DailySchedule.DayVisit now accepts an optional exterior travel window. Both the outbound departure and return arrival must fit that window, independently of destination opening hours and the resident's actual sleep hours. Unchanged defaults preserve existing schedules. A 05:00–19:00 window supports the unresolved Daggerfall haunting without putting residents to bed at sunset. Trips whose walking duration and dwell cannot fit become home days rather than teleporting home at the deadline.

Two tests reproduced travel outside safe hours and impossible-trip admission before implementation. All 263 core tests pass; the new coverage checks 800 deterministic seeds on a two-hour commute, bedtime preservation and rejection of invalid windows. Evidence `work/HauntedVisits-{red,green}.log`. This is source-only foundation; runtime StreetPopulation has not yet supplied the window, and the installed bundle is unchanged. Integration must persist a daily policy choice so resolving the curse cannot suddenly reposition an ongoing trip. Daggerfall city admission remains disabled.

## Native combat-to-refuge flight verified

The automatic flight probe now also starts a real GuardActor in native defense combat against a stationary rat, waits for CombatReady and enabled attacks, then introduces a hostile Ghost. Normal controller Update admits refuge and clears CombatReady, DefenseTarget, motor, attacks and sensed target. The guard then passes the existing exterior/interior shelter and curse-release/post-return checks, retaining the existing report. This verifies combat shutdown and subsequent actor phases, not every continuous collision step or response to an actively attacking opponent. All prior refuge checks pass in the same run. No production behavior changed in this verification step.

Evidence: `work/HauntingCombat-flight-pass.log`, marker `LIVING_CITIES_HAUNTING_COMBAT_PASS`. Unity build passes; installed development 0.3.29 SHA-256 `988F4BE8BAB88037D627FA106F166B46B141ADC0E52E75A16712B8733CEF7E1A`. The native combat transition limitation below is superseded. Other response duties, interior evacuation, city admission, actual city save/load and scaling remain open.

## Exterior combat interruption

An exterior Engaged guard can now take refuge after a visible haunting threat. The observer captures the existing actor's health/position before planning. Admission requires a surveyed current-position-to-post route, appends it after the reversed refuge approach, then clears active engagement and response fields while retaining health and historical reports. Missing/invalid routes reject admission before roster mutation. Existing refuge actor handling ends native combat before placement. This does not implement interior combat evacuation or interruption of Responding/Searching/Returning duties.

The new core case reproduced rejected combat admission, then passed route-required rejection, health/report retention, copy validation and physical return. All 261 core tests pass. The live controlled Wraith adapter now starts with an engaged roster member at a moved position and 40% health, verifies successful refuge admission and preserved prior crime report, then catches up release/return. Automatic at-post and moving-patrol flight regressions also pass. This is not yet a live native-combat-to-refuge actor transition test. Ordinary GuardDefenseProbe passes separately. Evidence: `work/RefugeCombat-{red,green,live-pass,defense-regression}.log`. Unity build installed development 0.3.29, SHA-256 `11A9F576789333FBCB4D473A3DD0E77FCE5ACF51CED999E459FD7D7754A1B159`. City admission remains disabled.

## Live patrol refuge verification

The live probe now surveys a real Daggerfall circuit (105.6m in the fixture), starts a pair, advances into movement, introduces a native hostile Ghost and enables normal controller updates with the curse unresolved. The leader detaches into refuge without jumping to its post. DFU's native serializer retains the detached assignment and explicit return path; lifting the curse and advancing time returns the guard to its original post. This uses a disposable roster and serialization round-trip, not a disk save/load of an enabled city. Night-policy updates can stop the patrol before the observer runs; this also exercises refuge admission during its physical return. The separate actor-flight and wall-interior checks pass in the same run.

Evidence: `work/HauntingPatrol-pass.log`, marker `LIVING_CITIES_HAUNTING_PATROL_PASS`. Unity build passes. Installed development 0.3.29 SHA-256: `89740329C251A96A2BCC2363991C570526CF57152AABC4AB2BD2BAF72C3BB4EB`. Combat interruption, full city admission, actual city save/reload and scaling remain pending.

## Patrol interruption

Posted-duty guards may now flee from their current patrol position. Admission saves an explicit exterior return route: reverse the surveyed refuge approach, then follow the patrol's existing return path to the original post. The pair is interrupted, while the fleeing member is detached from that assignment so completing refuge travel cannot snap it onto the partner's unfinished return. The partner continues its physical return and can independently flee if it sees the haunting. Invalid admission is checked before mutation. Existing saves without ReturnExterior retain the old reverse-approach behavior.

The new core test first failed admission, then exposed retained patrol membership. It now verifies current-position departure, partner interruption, JSON round-trip, intermediate return positions and both final posts. All 260 tests pass. Native serializer checks and the existing live automatic at-post flight regression also pass; live flight from an actual moving patrol and combat interruption remain unverified/unimplemented respectively. Evidence: `work/RefugePatrol-red.log`, `work/RefugePatrol-detach-red.log`, `work/RefugePatrol-green.log`, `work/RefugePatrol-flight-regression.log`, `../work/guards-persistence.log`. Installed development 0.3.29 SHA-256: `1C89AD6A6CDCB97D48445ECC7DA7B1DC8CB4A634F5AAEF0DC6142F6AF645CDDA`. City admission remains disabled.

## Automatic guard flight verification

The normal GuardPopulation.Update loop now has a passing live acceptance probe: a native hostile Ghost triggers refuge allocation, the exterior guard is visible during approach and hidden after entry, the same guard is rendered inside the selected wall refuge, LiftedCurse releases it, and it disappears from the interior before returning alive to its post without a crime report. The probe uses a disposable one-guard roster in the real city and advances game time between phases. It does not prove continuous collision traversal, city-wide performance, or a full city save/reload. The outer probe restores the quest flag, and no player save is written.

Evidence: `work/HauntingFlight-pass.log`, marker `LIVING_CITIES_HAUNTING_FLIGHT_PASS`. All 259 core tests and the Unity build pass. Installed development 0.3.29 SHA-256: `162C4B86D7872557D3E768A83964BC8FEAD252F0AAC6C02E0759D7A0BD8C7E70`. This supersedes the automatic-flight limitation below; interrupted patrol/combat duties, full city admission and its save/reload/performance checks remain pending.

## Dawn catch-up

Refuge release now uses the first safe05:00 after departure, even when the next evaluation occurs on a later haunted night. This prevents guards remaining sheltered indefinitely while the town is unloaded. When LiftedCurse is first observed before that dawn, release uses the observation time: native global state does not expose its historical completion timestamp. GuardRefugeJourney still clamps release until physical shelter arrival and evaluates the complete reverse journey.

Two core scenarios failed before implementation;259/259 now pass. The live Ghost/Wraith probe also invokes the real release adapter at22:00 three days later, verifies first-dawn release and advances the roster back to Posted. It remains a controlled adapter call, not an actual city save/reload. Full refuge probe and Unity build pass. Evidence: `work/HauntingDawn-red.log`, `work/HauntingDawn-green.log`, `work/HauntingDawn-live-pass.log`. Latest installed development0.3.29 hash `68F3FB86197DB6B1659750B79D4E3396AC117465C10F776509F8C1557891AC6F` supersedes earlier hashes. Automatic full actor flight, interrupted patrol/combat duties and city admission remain pending.

## Visible haunting detection and at-post assignment

GuardHaunting now checks live LiftedCurse, the city/time policy, living active hostile Ghost/Wraith entities, and20m line of sight from each eligible guard post. It searches up to8 nearest original Town23 wall entrances (models444/445/446), checks the exterior route, lazily surveys interior routes and tries unoccupied destinations. The selected journey uses3.8m/s and12 game seconds per real second. The enemy-defense survey calls this before normal attackable-target filtering; classified haunting threats never receive defense dispatch even if no refuge route exists. ReleaseSafeRefuges requests return when the policy becomes safe. Caches clear with settlement ownership.

The controlled live test creates actual native Ghost/Wraith bodies beside a real Daggerfall route and invokes the production observer on a disposable at-post roster. Both select building257 with exterior/interior routes and no crime reports; setting LiftedCurse makes classification false. Early fixture failures were caused by disabling native motor startup and failing to activate the native-created foe, not production detection. Evidence: `work/HauntingThreat-red.log`, `work/HauntingThreat-passive-fixture.log`, `work/HauntingThreat-pass.log`. Ordinary GuardDefenseProbe passes (`work/HauntingThreat-defense-regression.log`);257/257 core tests and Unity build pass. Latest installed development0.3.29 hash `8899C3CB2520571B470A77C1D07E8ED55DE734B3CB0BBF54AAD6E52E37678197` supersedes earlier hashes.

Still unverified/incomplete: automatic Update-to-actor end-to-end flight, safe-release timing through unloaded dawn/quest resolution, patrol/combat interruption, route avoidance, corpse claim cleanup, survey frame cost and full city admission. Test invokes the observer directly on a disabled controller with controlled time and restores the quest flag; it does not save the city roster. The declaration that at-post assignment works must not imply Daggerfall is enabled for normal play yet.

## Unloaded refuge geometry

`RefugeGeometry.Survey` synchronously creates a temporary native automap model-only interior100m above its normal position, disables renderers, synchronizes physics and extracts local entry-to-standing routes. It does not call normal DoLayout/AddPeople/AddFlats/AddActionDoors; the temporary object is deactivated and destroyed in finally. This is verified for the original wall models, not arbitrary interiors or overhaul models whose omitted flats/action doors can affect navigation.

Initial unloaded survey destinations failed real-interior RouteToExit validation near an upper floor edge. Reducing the10000m isolation offset to100m did not fix it; no body-overlap collider was present. Requiring level support within a4cm neighborhood rejected unstable stair/ledge destinations. With that conservative filter, every retained destination validates in the actual entered representative interiors: model444 has71 routes,445 has80,446 has21. These are candidate positions, not simultaneous occupancy capacity. The test proves destination reachability, not equality of every isolated route segment to a native traversal. Existing actor/controller and100 exterior-route probe checks also pass in the same run.

Evidence: `work/RefugeGeometry-red.log`, `work/RefugeGeometry-coordinate-mismatch.log`, `work/RefugeGeometry-edge-mismatch.log`, `work/RefugeGeometry-pass.log`. Unity build passed; latest installed development0.3.29 hash `A8CF354094A5809F0CEDBEFB4459A0AE9026DC85117278222644438B224FE033` supersedes earlier hashes. Survey still runs only from the opt-in probe; automatic refuge allocation and ghost detection remain pending.

## Refuge destination reservations

At-post refuge admission now rejects a destination within0.8m horizontally and1.8m vertically of another same-building reservation. The claim starts before departure and remains through indoor exit; it frees when the owner reaches the exterior return phase. Other buildings do not conflict. A dead guard blocks its actual frozen indoor position; a guard killed outside releases the intended shelter. This does not yet remove indoor corpse claims after corpse cleanup, reserve paths, or provide moving-actor avoidance.

Three new scenarios failed before implementation;257/257 core tests now pass. Native DFU serializer verification also confirms a sheltered reservation still blocks a second guard after reload into a roster copy. Evidence: `work/RefugeClaims-red.log`, `work/RefugeClaims-green.log`, `work/RefugeClaims-serializer.log`. Installed player bundle unchanged. Runtime allocation must try other checked anchors when admission rejects a crowded destination.

Additional verified quest detail: shipped S0000977 spawns both Ghost (`_F.00_`, every31 minutes with50% success) and Wraith (`_wraith_`, every21 minutes with50% success) during the haunting task. Detection must account for both, not only a single named Lysandus actor. Current GuardEnemyDefense still treats eligible hostile ghosts as enemies; city admission must not precede the fear/defense override.

## Controller refuge views

GuardPopulation now selects refuge indoor views only for the matching building and phase, hides sheltered guards outside, bypasses normal crime/combat handling during refuge travel, and replaces a view whose indoor/exterior ownership changed. Expired views deactivate immediately before deferred destruction, avoiding an extra active body in the same frame.

A live controlled test initially failed because UpdateInterior ignored refuge state. It now shows exactly one actor while exiting and zero after departure for all three representative wall models. The test invokes the disabled controller's UpdateInterior with synthetic roster/time, so it does not prove normal Update sequencing, ghost-triggered flight or a full city save. Evidence `work/RefugeController-red.log` and `work/RefugeController-green.log`. Existing GuardBuildingProbe passes native alarm, delayed finite arrival, doorway entry, wounded interior save/load and return (`work/RefugeController-building-regression.log`). Core254/254 and Unity build pass. Latest installed development0.3.29 SHA-256: `CDC91C12C2DBC8D2474DDCBCB089F3F8C96DFF47DC1E549B2A4BEAB4ECEDD7EE`. Next integrate refuge allocation/ghost detection and interrupted-duty handling; city admission remains disabled.

## Refuge actor placement foundation

GuardActor now recognizes refuge interior phases, places the native guard body at the saved local floor position with its controller ground offset, uses idle while sheltered and movement while exiting, and deactivates a view when its journey crosses into the other scene. Refuge Capture preserves health/time without invoking crime-search observation. This does not yet wire GuardPopulation view selection or ghost-triggered admission.

Live red test reproduced `ArgumentNullException: geometry` when creating an indoor refuge actor. After the adapter change, controlled actors in original wall models444/445/446 pass destination placement and initial release movement checks. The synthetic roster uses a dummy exterior leg; the separate real exterior survey still checks100 wall connections. Do not describe this as an end-to-end flight from a ghost or complete exterior/interior transition. Evidence: `work/RefugeActor-red.log`, `work/RefugeActor-green.log`. Ordinary guard actor regression passes native body, timed route, walking animation, death/no resurrection and fixture loads (`work/RefugeActor-regression-pass.log`);254/254 core pass. Unity build passes. Latest installed development0.3.29 hash: `8EA69C290B8EA3DB79E8071065D1B69ED45BA54D7DE0DD9E8CF05DA373D5F2FC`. This supersedes earlier hashes.

## Roster persistence foundation

`GuardRecord.Refuge` and appended `GuardDuty.Refuge` now retain validated journeys in roster copies and native DFU serialization. Legacy absent fields remain null; existing enum numbers are unchanged. At-post admission checks the actual origin transactionally, excludes patrol/incident/interior actors, and marks the guard unavailable to dispatch. Advance keeps the guard sheltered until release and restores Posted only after the reverse route reaches the original post. Death preserves the journey and frozen exterior death position; indoor corpse rendering must use the journey evaluated at DiedAt, not the exterior door coordinate.

Tests:254/254 core pass after three expected missing-implementation failures. Native `VerifyGuards.ps1` passes, including approach/entry/shelter/release serialization and return; evidence `work/GuardRefuge-red.log`, `work/GuardRefuge-green.log`, `work/GuardRefuge-serializer.log`, and the `LIVING_CITIES_REFUGE_PERSISTENCE_PASS` marker in `../work/guards-persistence.log`. Installed player bundle is unchanged; these are source/editor checks. Runtime consumers still need explicit refuge phase handling before enabling this state. Patrol/combat interruption, spatial room claims, actual guard actors and city activation remain required; at-post admission is an intermediate stage, not the complete requested behavior.

## Refuge journey source foundation

`GuardRefugeJourney` now models separate exterior and interior routes, doorway phase transitions, indefinite shelter, explicit release and a physical reverse journey. Exterior coordinates remain settlement-local and interior coordinates remain building-local; the adapter must switch scenes at the doorway rather than interpolate between unrelated coordinate spaces. Early release waits until shelter arrival; repeated release does not reset the departure clock. Route copies validate coordinates, bounds, rates and deadlines.

Core tests first failed on all three unimplemented journey scenarios, then passed; the final suite including corrupt-state checks is251/251. Evidence: `work/RefugeJourney-red.log` and `work/RefugeJourney-green.log`. This class is not yet attached to GuardRecord, native save serialization, ghost detection, actor movement or room occupancy allocation. It must not be described as working guard flight. Its return endpoint is the original departure point; the runtime must resume the interrupted post/patrol journey after completion. Installed bundle remains the prior interior-probe build. Next attach validated journey state to the roster, preserve deaths and interrupted duties, reserve non-overlapping refuge positions, then connect and test live actors before city activation.

## Verified content census, 15 October 2026

`DaggerfallCityAudit.Run` reads the installed original data through DFU's ContentReader without spawning actors or changing saves. Unity completed with exit 0; evidence: `work/DaggerfallCity-census.log`. Daggerfall city has map ID **1291010263**, location type **TownCity**, and 8×8 exterior blocks. There are 484 House1–House6 buildings containing 714 beds, plus 20 taverns containing 134 beds. These are content counts, not an allocated resident population: quest reservations, ownership and shop households still affect allocation. One separate HouseForSale is excluded from the house total. The audit does not prove usable wall refuges or route connectivity.

Current runtime admission gates remain in `StreetPopulation.SetTown` and `GuardPopulation.SetTown`; both admit only Gothway Garden. Guard allocation also hardcodes `SettlementScale.Town`. The narrow `KnownQuestBindings` Gothway quest gate must remain independent when city simulation is enabled. Read the authoritative curse flag through `GameManager.Instance.PlayerEntity.GlobalVars.GetGlobalVar(0)` after player globals are initialized; do not cache it across loads or infer it from `LysandusSatisfied`.

Next acceptance work: city admission and scale classification; actual wall refuge survey; dedicated city save/reload and unresolved/resolved nighttime tests.

## Exterior wall refuge survey, 15 October 2026

`TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -RefugeProbe` loads only the dedicated exterior fixture, teleports that disposable session to Daggerfall, and performs the existing collision-checked TownGeometry route search. It does not save, enable city actors or modify quest globals. The live player completed successfully: 100 accepted ground-level Town23 entrances, all100 connected from another entrance within100 metres;59 other doors were rejected by the general entrance survey. This does not mean59 wall doors failed, nor does it prove paths from every city post. Evidence: `work/DaggerfallRefuge-exterior-pass.log`.

The original-content wall census records Town23 models444/445/446 with real interior records; the detailed candidate keys/models are in `work/DaggerfallCity-walls.log`. These model IDs and the Town23 type are evidence for this original layout, not a universal classification for city-overhaul mods. Next verify representative actual interiors, safe standing positions and physical guard arrival/retreat before using them as refuges.

The probe is opt-in and retains disabled test input/mouse capture. Owned test process34228 finished and was stopped by TownTest; no user session was interrupted. Unity build passed; latest development0.3.29 bundle hash is `E11E69AA2685B2E47AAC85461CCC164C5D6C68AB175D97CA5B5ECA4C7393B674`, superseding the policy-only build hash below. City admission remains disabled.

## Curse policy evidence

### Representative interior verification

The expanded `-RefugeProbe` physically enters and exits one connected original building per wall model. Live checks pass for model444/key1792 (80 checked anchors with exit routes), model445/key256 (80), and model446/key1536 (22). These are sampled standing points, not guard capacity: nearby anchors can overlap a guard's footprint. No survey-limit warning occurred. All100 exterior connections still pass in the same run. Evidence: `work/DaggerfallRefuge-interior-pass.log`; owned player32788 completed and stopped normally. No fixture saves were changed.

Unity build passed; latest development0.3.29 bundle hash is `46567F4E2B8BD4942D2B8B80BED2CF6FEFF610FC407FCF493496C99AC97EF369`, superseding the earlier hashes. This verifies the three entered interiors, not every wall interior or actor retreat. Next implement persistent refuge travel/occupancy/return and ghost-triggered guard flight, then activate city simulation with dedicated gameplay saves.

`SettlementHaunting.StreetsDangerous` now evaluates the verified city ID, current LiftedCurse flag and absolute game seconds. Tests cover exact 19:00/05:00 boundaries, midnight on distant days, immediate curse resolution, other settlements and invalid timestamps. Red run: 244/247 passed with three expected missing-implementation failures; green run: 247/247 passed. `GuardNightPatrols` reads the live player global each update and applies the policy before starting patrols; active patrols follow the existing physical return behavior when disabled. This return is to their existing posts, **not yet a wall refuge**.

Unity build/integration checks passed and installed a development rebuild retaining version0.3.29, SHA-256 `1549B2A6189E0A80B21E766ECBDA59722BA464F5AB31F576A453D4A712AEEC71`. Evidence: `work/Haunting-red.log`, `work/Haunting-green.log`, `work/Haunting-build.log`. Daggerfall remains excluded by runtime admission gates, so this is policy wiring, not verified city gameplay or completed haunting behavior. No live city quest-toggle/save test has run. Base-game tracked Assets/Scripts remains unchanged.

Verified shipped source: `Player/DaggerfallUnity_Data/StreamingAssets/Quests/S0000977.txt` (Curse of Daggerfall) ends on global `LiftedCurse`, table ID0 in `Tables/Quests-GlobalVars.txt`. Its daytime task is 05:00–19:00; haunting danger is 19:00–05:00. `LysandusSatisfied` is a different global and must not substitute for the curse gate. The player requests Daggerfall city as a second supported test settlement on 14 October2026; runtime enablement and disposable city saves remain pending.

Latest user decision,14 October2026: while the main quest's Lysandus haunting remains unresolved, Daggerfall city disables night guard patrols by default. This supersedes the earlier plan to keep regular night patrols running under fear. Existing exposed guards must flee a visible haunting threat into reachable city-wall refuges rather than fight the ghosts and accumulate corpses. Refuges require actual entrances and safe physical routes; never teleport a guard into a wall or invented building. Stationary duty, gates and indoor staffing remain separate from patrol enablement.

Town locals may use taverns in daylight and early evening, but visits and returns must finish before the actual nightly ghost activation boundary. No ordinary late-night party crowd or tavern-to-home traffic runs during the haunting. Existing paid traveler beds and indoor tavern services are not canceled merely because streets are dangerous. The actual main-quest state that resolves the haunting re-enables normal nightlife and guard patrols; do not infer resolution from date, player level, unrelated quest completion or temporary absence of ghosts.

City gameplay integration remains pending under M5/M7. Current runtime support is Gothway Garden only. Before enabling Daggerfall-city simulation, survey real wall refuges and test unresolved/resolved states, exact onset, family/traveler safety, guard flight, unloaded time and save/reload. The policy above is not an implemented visible-ghost detector.
