## Original: DEVELOPMENT-CHECKPOINT-HISTORY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# People of the Iliac Bay for Daggerfall Unity

Renamed from Living Cities on 16 October 2026. The bundle filename `livingcities.dfmod`, GUID, namespaces and `mod_livingcities.txt` save data remain unchanged for compatibility. Historical checkpoints and fixture save names below retain their original names. Current external-mod compatibility findings are in [the installation audit](HISTORY.md#source-external-mod-audit-md).

Integrated shop welfare acceptance now passes: keeper reports a threat, a finite guard arrives and fights, the player kills the witnessed attacker, welfare occurs, then the native surrender prompt handles prior Theft. A separate two-responder regression also verifies that an uninformed companion preserves courtesy before prior-crime handling, and releases it when the player recovers above the health threshold. See `work/WelfareChain-pass.log`, `work/WelfareCompanion-pass.log` and [scope](HISTORY.md#source-legitimate-defense-md). Outdoor, broader multi-guard layouts and mid-approach reload coverage remain open.

Guard welfare now has a first native runtime implementation: matching threat responders physically approach an injured player, offer a one-time exchange and sometimes a healing potion, then resume existing crime handling. Health must be above zero and strictly below 66%; departure or five game minutes ends the opportunity. Core310/310 and a controlled indoor movement/dialogue/inventory fixture pass. Natural encounter acceptance, outdoor approach coverage and save/load during the approach remain open. See [current welfare evidence](HISTORY.md#source-legitimate-defense-md). Older checkpoint paragraphs below are historical.

Lawful-defense work adds a schema7 ledger with persistent provenance, loot rights and bounded welfare/gift state.305 core tests pass. Native weapon, sourced-damage, Disintegrate, attacker-activation and corpse-binding probes pass. Actual corpse pickup and real save/load preserve loot rights and prior crimes. Broader spell/mod coverage, cross-scene tracking and welfare dialogue remain incomplete. See [scope and evidence](HISTORY.md#source-legitimate-defense-md).

Indoor defense follow-up preserves native attacker knowledge when the player assaults a defending guard. Live tests verify Assault takeover and return after the enemy dies, alongside effect persistence and citizen flight.289 core tests pass; broader encounter coverage remains open. See [verification](HISTORY.md#source-threat-response-md).

Latest development0.3.34 checkpoint connects indoor enemy defense: an escaped keeper contacts a finite guard, which travels to the shop, enters, searches around shelves and fights the reported attacker without blaming the player. Native shop defense and separate doorway pursuit/effect/save regression pass;289 core tests pass. Broader layouts and moving-target transitions remain unfinished. See [threat response evidence](HISTORY.md#source-threat-response-md).

Latest development0.3.34 rebuild preserves guard spell bundles across body recreation, actual shop exit and game save/load, with elapsed native effect ticks.289 core tests and targeted native checks pass. See [effect persistence evidence and limits](HISTORY.md#source-guard-effects-md).

**0.3.34 moves guard posts to public junctions, paved squares and gate approaches**, clear of building entrances and facing open streets. Saved guards keep their identities and walk to new posts when available. A guard pursuing the player inside a shop now follows through the doorway and physically resumes pursuit outside.286 core tests, native shop/save/pursuit checks and Daggerfall/city/refuge regression pass. Elevated wall posts and broader layout acceptance remain unfinished. See [post and doorway evidence](HISTORY.md#source-guard-posts-and-doorways-md).

**0.3.33 development checkpoint:** guards defend against existing hostile quest-tagged enemies; awake generated residents and registered native merchants flee. No separate random-incursion spawner. Core279/279 and native guard damage/citizen flight checks pass. Interior enemy defense, complete stationary coverage and dynamic blocked-route replanning remain unfinished. See [threat response evidence](HISTORY.md#source-threat-response-md).

**0.3.32 adds timed shop browsing and haggling:** visitors walk between surveyed shelf/merchant stops and leave when their appointment ends.272 core tests, an actual shop activity probe and indoor hearing/save/guard-arrival regression pass. Busy-shop crowd movement, complete idle art and actual NPC purchases remain unfinished. See [shop activities](HISTORY.md#source-shop-visits-md).

**0.3.31 enables population and finite guards across native cities, towns and villages**, replacing the two-map restriction without adding settings. Live regional checks pass in Berbaaqnia (470 resident schedules,38 guards, Redguard appearance) and Bubogureg (435 schedules,35 guards, Nord appearance). Names follow native regional banks; appearance follows native climate population. This is broader activation, not exhaustive layout, quest or performance validation. Isolated farms/taverns, wilderness travelers, richer social routines and complete artwork remain unfinished.

**0.3.30 includes Daggerfall City automatically alongside Gothway Garden.** No city-specific toggle is required. Dedicated test character `LivingCitiesTest-f238d317` has `Living Cities - Daggerfall city preview` (SAVE50). Its611 residents and49 guards survive actual save/reload. Generated civilian travel respects the unresolved haunting; guards flee visible ghosts/wraiths into surveyed wall refuges. Initial city setup measured about12 seconds. Whole-world coverage remains the intended scope; broader settlement integration, movement coverage, native keeper schedules, nightlife and performance remain unfinished. See [city verification](HISTORY.md#source-daggerfall-haunting-md).

An in-development standard DFU mod for persistent residents, daily routines, civilian awareness, finite guards, nightlife, and optional local dialogue.

The user approved the scope and recommended architecture on 13 October 2026 and requested continuous implementation, testing, and improvement. The working name is Living Cities.

Target: DFU 1.1.1, Unity 2019.4.40f1, C# 7.3. The simulation core has no Unity dependency. The canonical source is this repository, not the ignored copy deployed under DFU's Assets/Game/Mods directory.

See [milestones](HISTORY.md#source-milestones-md), [approved design](HISTORY.md#source-superpowers-specs-2026-10-13-living-cities-design-md), and [foundation plan](HISTORY.md#source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-13-population-foundation-md).

Historical 0.3.28 checkpoint adds separate floor nodes and collision-checked stair steps to interior escape routes. In native shop131343, all124 clear upper bedroom samples reach the exit without cutting stair treads; a controlled awake keeper follows its saved path downstairs and exits. Survey measured84ms in this fixture. Existing generated escape, delayed finite reporting and real reload regression pass. Core244/244 pass. Bed-to-floor awakening, broader interior coverage, nonlethal/step/theft waking, stationary mortality and Daggerfall-city support remain unfinished. Earlier0.3.27 added saved Stealth/distance-sensitive waking and neutral hearing reports. See [15 October player follow-up](HISTORY.md#source-2026-10-15-player-test-followup-md).

0.3.29 adds reactive bedside-floor awakening for native keepers and generated residents, automatic escape from that position, and preserved wake/journey state through actual reload. It also preserves sleeping images' pixel proportions and supports one overhead composite. Core244/244, native build and native/generated wake, sleep and keeper regressions pass in the documented fixtures. Normal bed travel, wake-to-work routines, broad interior coverage and full appearance coverage remain unfinished; see the follow-up above.

This is experimental software. Use the isolated portable player and dedicated test saves. Individual milestone completion is recorded only after its acceptance checks pass.

M1 baseline probes are complete. The opt-in `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -QuestProbe` verifies native quest activation, in-place original-NPC movement, hide/show and actual save/load reconstruction. See [quest identity evidence](HISTORY.md#source-quest-identity-md) for boundaries; broad quest/service compatibility remains part of later milestones.

## Current checkpoint

Version 0.3.25 adds exterior enemy defense in Gothway Garden. Existing finite guards detect visible living hostile enemies, select up to two closest reachable responders and use native enemy combat even with enemy infighting disabled. Defense does not accuse an innocent player or clear an existing crime. Saved enemy identities and wounded guard state survive real reload; dead, pacified, missing or disabled targets cause physical return. Player assault transfers a defender back to native criminal response. The guard flame now uses the actual upright native torch210/17 rather than brazier210/0. SAVE46, `Living Cities - guard defense`, contains the native hostile-enemy encounter. Indoor enemy defense, lawful-defense loot/welfare dialogue, broader quest/terrain acceptance and profiling remain unfinished.

Version 0.3.24 connects finite two-guard night patrols to surveyed street circuits, animated walking and existing night lights. The mod menu enables patrols and controls the maximum pair count (0–4, default 2). At least four available guards remain at posts before a pair starts. Saved plans preserve shared circuit phase; dawn, disabling patrols, crime and partner death produce physical returns or responses from current positions. SAVE45, `Living Cities - night patrol`, is the paired patrol fixture. See [guard settings and acceptance](HISTORY.md#source-guards-md).

Shopkeepers retain full native identities and head their on-site families, sharing surnames within actual bed capacity. One bed houses only the keeper; the native service actor is not duplicated. SAVE35 contains the daytime Kinghart couple, SAVE38 the keeper death and SAVE39 the living sleeper. Bedless commuting, merchant combat/spells, bereavement, travel to bed and broader sleep artwork remain unfinished. See [merchant integration](HISTORY.md#source-shopping-and-stationary-identities-md).

The standard `.dfmod` now packages the runtime DLL compiled by DFU's official mod assembly builder. Equivalent source-text compilation failed during native navigation; the precompiled package passes the live geometry/patrol checks. No tracked base-game scripts are changed. The precise runtime compiler failure remains unproven. Core216/216 pass; further acceptance and remaining scope are recorded in [paired patrol plan](HISTORY.md#source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-14-paired-guard-patrols-md).

Household capacity equals actual bed count, with one resident per bed and no separate five-person cap. Old saves remain unchanged. Fixture `LivingCitiesTest-f238d317` starts with 181 generated residents and eight children, with inside/outside saves at SAVE24/SAVE25. Native keeper families are added when registered; the original keeper occupies the head slot without a duplicate actor. Larger families occupy larger homes; bedless homes receive no generated residents.

The citizen response pilot observes weapon kills, checks nearby awake witnesses and line of sight, and selects stable adult fight-or-flight behavior. Children flee. Fleeing direct witnesses warn at most two people; secondhand warnings do not cascade. Incidents, spent budgets and reacting identities persist. Interior witnesses follow surveyed exits and report after traveling to a finite guard; missing routes retry without creating guard knowledge. Full stair navigation and individual movement/battle artwork remain incomplete. See [behavior and limitations](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md).

Finite guards default on in supported Gothway Garden, with physical building responses, native surrender, timed scene investigations and paired street patrols at night. Broader settlement coverage, city-wall duties, enemy defense and nightlife remain unfinished. Further sprite generation is deferred. Existing complete single-sleeper green-blanket/white-pillow assets remain available for two adult appearances.

Native mod settings expose population, awareness, theft/house locks, guards, sleep, schedules and diagnostics. The obsolete household-limit slider is removed. Schema 6 preserves existing identities, deaths, routes and quest contracts, plus bed capacity, native identity, civilian incidents and finite guard records. Broad quest compatibility remains open; the reviewed unchanged delivery pilot passes only within its documented scope.

Use the fresh fixture for current tests; preserve older test characters as historical snapshots. Run `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -ResponseProbe` for the civilian response test. Full milestones and broad quest compatibility remain incomplete.

Canonical source lives here; `tools/Build.ps1` deploys it into the upstream project's ignored mod folder, runs Unity's `LivingCitiesBuild.VerifyAndBuild`, waits for its exit and requires the success marker plus exit code 0. Run `tools/SmokeTest.ps1` for isolated headless startup checks; it stops only its owned process.

Evidence: `work/unity-build-result.json`, `work/player-smoke.log`, and sibling `../work/livingcities-build.log`. Build artifacts and machine-local logs are ignored by Git. `docs/INTEGRATION.md` records known engine limitations and deferred checks.

<a id="source-directional-civilian-animation-md"></a>

## Original: DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Directional civilian animation

> Current amendment, 14 October 2026: one NPC per bed; maximum household residents equals actual bed count, with no separate five-person cap. Preserve old saves and use a fresh compliant test population. Shared-bed rendering, kill-all-bed logic and separate head/blanket layers are superseded. Keep complete green-blanket/white-pillow single-sleeper sprites. Children may inherit either parent's race; keep existing child sprites now. Gameplay takes priority over art. See [current citizen behavior and next guard work](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md). Earlier checkpoint descriptions below remain historical evidence.

User amendments, 14 October 2026, including the expanded all-human coverage requirement. This is an essential part of the current mod, not only future visual polish. No generated sets are implemented or validated yet.

## Required behavior

- Every stationary and mobile adult human NPC appearance needs walking, running, weapon-fighting and spellcasting animations from all viewing angles, plus a sleeping sprite lying in bed. This includes appearances that already have walking animations: walking faster does not satisfy running. Asset coverage is not limited to shopkeepers, noncombatants or the first pilot appearances.
- Children need walking and running from all viewing angles and a sleeping sprite lying in bed, but no battle or spellcasting sets. They move between home and local play groups; adult shopping, work, tavern and inter-settlement travel restrictions still apply.
- Additional child diversity is required: at least three female and three male peasant appearances, plus at least three female and three male noble appearances (at least 12 distinct child appearances total). Each needs its standard flat, idle animations from every viewing angle, directional walking/running and the previously required sleeping pose. Match Daggerfall's pixel resolution, palette, proportions and style across the complete set. These are distinct source appearances, not merely different frames of the same child.
- Any NPC mobilized or made hostile by the simulation keeps the same recognizable appearance. A shopkeeper fleeing a murder visibly runs toward the exit; a civilian fighting the player uses its own weapon or spellcasting animations. Having spellcasting assets does not by itself grant every civilian spellcasting abilities; behavior selects an action consistent with the NPC's capabilities.
- Preserve identity, clothing, face, proportions, scale and palette across idle, movement and combat, indoor/outdoor transitions, hostility changes and save/load. Do not replace the person with a generic fighter or render a moving static flat as the completed behavior.
- Animation changes must not sever service, relationship, inventory-extension or quest bindings. Child mobility does not imply adult combat or adult activities.

## Delivery sequence

Priority reaffirmed after the initial quest pilot: resume this work with wider sleeping coverage and shared beds, then functional child/adult directional sets. Quest compatibility expansion must not indefinitely displace these gameplay deliverables. Required coverage remains every stationary/mobile adult appearance and at least twelve child appearances; the existing single male sleeping prototype is only the starting point.

Sleeping-art refinement (14 October): use a thick green blanket matching the original bed cover, with a white pillow. This later color decision supersedes the earlier white blanket requirement for all current and future sleeping art. The blanket covers the body up to the neck, with only the face/head visible. Reuse blanket poses where possible and preserve identity through the visible face, head silhouette and appropriate adult/child proportions. Sleeping does not require drawing every clothing combination under the blanket; waking still restores the original appearance. Use side and angled views appropriate to a player standing beside a bed, including head/foot viewpoints. A top-down cutout placed flat on the mattress is not the finished solution. Shared-bed variants must accommodate partners/children without stacking complete single-person blankets in the same position. Normal room views, mattress anchoring and camera changes need in-engine inspection.

User-selected production order: start with sleeping sprites for all NPC flats when implementing actual sleeping in homes and taverns. Validate bed alignment and waking transitions at that milestone, then expand into the full directional sets below. This changes sequencing, not the required movement/combat/child-diversity coverage. Do not delay the current family foundation to generate all animation sets upfront.

1. Export and inspect actual source flats from the installed game. Record archive/record, dimensions, pivot and palette references in an asset manifest.
2. Produce one child standard-flat/directional-idle/walk/run/sleep pilot and one adult walking/running/weapon-combat/spellcasting/sleep pilot from these references. Use generation followed by visual review and frame repair; generation alone does not establish usable animation. Low source resolution is not evidence of fast generation or consistent output. Validate native pixel scale and in-engine appearance before scaling to the 12 child variants and all existing human appearances.
3. Package frames behind an appearance-specific animation mapping. The standard DFU mobile billboard convention uses five drawn views with mirroring for eight directions; check asymmetric clothing/equipment before allowing mirrored views.
4. Verify frame order, transparency, feet/pivot alignment, speed, direction selection and state transitions in the actual player. Review facing-away and diagonal motion as well as frontal motion.
5. Exercise the actual behavior: child goes to a local play group and returns; shopkeeper flees through a real door; a fighting civilian retains its own visual identity. Save/reload and streaming must preserve the chosen appearance.
6. Inventory every stationary and mobile human appearance in the installed game. Track source archive/record or mobile variant, identity, age group, each action's required views/frames, sleeping pose, generation status, visual review and actual-player validation. Expand until every applicable appearance has the required sets. The full requirement is not complete after the pilots or only the appearances currently spawned by the prototype.

Home-only static child prototypes may support family/schema development but must remain labeled incomplete. Spellcasting and sleeping are now explicitly required; the earlier wording treating casting as optional is superseded. Additional corpse sprites and the later inventory mod remain separate recorded scope. Curved path polish remains deferred; functional animated movement is required. Sleeping acceptance requires alignment with a real bed, including a persistent appearance when a sleeper wakes, flees or fights.

## Original: EXTERNAL-MOD-AUDIT.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# External mod installation and compatibility audit

## NPC and quest inventory — 17 October

The current cross-category inventory, housing gaps, quest ownership risks and animation coverage are recorded in [NPC integration audit](NPC-SYSTEMS.md#inventory). This source audit supersedes assumptions that every stationary NPC is already adopted or housed. Runtime census and generic adoption remain pending.

Quest Actions Extension 2.0.0 and Quest Pack 1 2025.11.20b are installed separately in player/editor, enabled before People of the Iliac Bay, and pass startup plus the existing live dialogue regression. See [quest-pack audit](COMPATIBILITY.md#quests) for the 302-script inventory, minimum dependency version, representative difficult NPCs and outstanding quest acceptance. No external bundle was modified.

## Pending: external stationary NPC integration — 17 October

The user reports added outdoor NPCs ignoring murders, enemies, bedtime and households. Lively Cities 1.3.4 is a confirmed installed source of static street NPCs, and the live log confirms its initializer runs. Its manifest explicitly describes this purpose and includes WorldData building replacements. Beautiful Cities 0.5.0 and Beautiful Villages also replace settlement layouts; the exact provenance of each reported actor still needs an in-game inventory. Do not attribute every external NPC to Lively Cities without identifying the actor and effective override.

Inspection of the installed Lively Cities assembly (`work/lively-cities-inspected.cs`) shows `LivelyCitiesMod.InitMod` registering NPC flat archives 1200 and 10015, then subscribing to town entry, weather, dawn and dusk. `UpdateExteriorNPCs` enumerates all exterior billboards including inactive objects, finds StaticNPC components with nonzero faction IDs, and toggles their GameObjects using flags 1 (hide by day), 2 (hide by night), and 4 (hide in rain). It does not implement walking home or persistent sleeping. Its filter is broad, so it could also toggle adopted actors unless ownership is coordinated.

Our `KeeperIdentities.SurveyKeeperIdentities` only scans the current interior and requires `StaticNPC.Context.Building` plus the current entry building key. It registers ordinary indoor flats while excluding quest resources, actions and individual factions. `HouseholdOccupants.ReconcileHouseFlats` likewise only reconciles residential interior decoration. Neither path adopts Lively Cities' exterior population. This is a registration/lifecycle integration gap, not merely missing fear settings.

### TODO — not implemented

1. Inventory active and inactive external NPCs after world-data loading: mod/override provenance, map/block/building/record identity, faction, native hash, appearance and quest/service ownership. Verify stable collision-free IDs across reloads; never use transient instance IDs for persistence. Include non-StaticNPC decorative figures in the audit without assuming every humanoid prop is an interactable citizen.
2. Adopt eligible ordinary exterior NPCs into the existing resident/native-person ledger and physical actor system. Preserve original services, faction/name/appearance and protected quest actors. Ensure one identity and one live representation, persistent deaths, dispositions, corpse loot and save/load state; do not duplicate them as both generated household residents and external actors.
3. Connect crime witnessing, hostile-creature awareness, flee/combat decisions, physical reporting, dialogue and indoor/outdoor continuity. Test murder witnesses and creature attacks explicitly.
4. Reserve real available beds and household roles before allocating additional generated residents. Route adopted citizens to their actual home and sleep at night. Define a capacity-aware fallback for genuinely unhoused workers/visitors; never invent unlimited beds or silently assign everyone to a nearby house.
5. Coordinate Lively Cities day/night/weather visibility only for adopted NPCs. Our schedules must own their lifecycle so weather/dusk callbacks cannot hide, revive or duplicate them. Preserve behavior for non-adopted decorations and protected actors; do not disable the dependency globally.
6. Audit added flat archives and Elves/Beastfolk replacements for directional movement, idle, fleeing, corpse and sleeping art. Preserve recognizable identity; a static pose is not automatically a full mobile animation set. Document gaps before generating assets.
7. Acceptance: reload/revisit, dawn/dusk/rain, death persistence, threats, guards receiving reports, home/bed ownership, navigation around added structures, quest/service compatibility and large-city frame-time checks. Use incremental scans rather than a new full-scene scan every frame.

Implementation is deferred at the user's request; this inspection and backlog capture are complete. The precise actor provenance and broad runtime coverage remain to be verified during integration.

## Rosy's Resources delayed entry correction — 17 October

The installed Rosy's Resources assembly contains `FlowerDeActivatorMod.FlowerDeActivator.TeleportPlayerToEnterDoorOrMarkerAfterDelay`. Inspection confirms it yields nine frames, then reads each entry marker's `gameObject.transform` without checking whether the player has left or the marker still exists. Its exterior handler only restores exterior renderers/colliders; it does not cancel the pending entry correction. The final rapid inn entry/exit in `work/final-disposition-native-check.log` destroys those markers before resumption and produces `NullReferenceException`. A stale routine can also reposition the player in the wrong scene if its old markers survive.

The compatibility fix lives in our optional `RosyInteriorCompatibilityHooks`, not in the external archive or game assemblies. It wraps only that coroutine, retains its original yields and valid entry behavior, and checks the captured transition generation, current active interior and live entry markers before every resume. New entry/exit invalidates earlier routines. Other external-mod exceptions are not swallowed. The dialogue regression now waits 25 frames after its final exit and requires both a completed normal correction and a cancelled stale correction when the dependency is installed. Hook/runtime compilation and Unity build pass. Live acceptance passes with two normal corrections and one stale cancellation, followed by a clean strict-runner exit 0 (`work/rosy-interior-lifetime-pass.log`). This supersedes the earlier unresolved compatibility status.

17 October rendered inn retest: the replacement inn 131078 binds successfully, finds 80 anchors, and passes keeper-first route generation, but its rendered patron stalls behind another occupant before reaching the keeper. The full movement test fails with counter=False, zero social stops and only 1.52 m travelled. This is a confirmed remaining runtime movement issue, not a missing doorway binding. `work/ModdedInnMovement-red.log` records the failure; `work/ModdedInnMovement-diagnostic.log` includes the blocked waypoint, nearby bodies and remaining route. The diagnostic run ended before completing the probe. No detour behavior change has been made yet. Work was redirected to the user's conversation/flirting additions; resume this movement investigation afterward.

17 October day-rest performance follow-up: when an external city-wall layout has no usable bed, guard rest preparation now waits four real seconds before another geometry survey. This prevents a full wall scan every update while preserving eventual retries after streaming/layout changes. Runtime compile and the 585/585 core suite pass; no Unity/runtime measurement is available until activation succeeds.

17 October rest interruption follow-up: dispatching a guard to an emergency clears its pending daytime-rest assignment, so a guard cannot resume sleeping after handling a crime. The regression passes with 585/585 core checks and the runtime compile remains clean. Unity packaging and live behavior remain blocked by editor activation.

17 October patrol rest source checkpoint: a paired night patrol now records a daylight rest intent at dawn, completes its physical return to its original post, then surveys reachable existing wall beds and walks to a distinct free bed until dusk. The intent is saved, restores during the return, and expires after an unassigned shift. Gate guards remain excluded. The runtime source compile is clean and core 584/584 passes. Unity still cannot package or exercise this because its license activation fails. This does not add a lying-down visual; guards use the existing sheltered idle state at the bedside.

Guard bed save validation now rejects duplicate live claims both before recall and during active wall rest. The regression reproduced the missing rejection before the fix. Core 582/582 and runtime compilation pass; this is still source-only, not a Unity gameplay result.

17 October per-bed follow-up supersedes the whole-building reservation limitation below. Gate shift preparation and authoritative scheduling now allow different bed records in the same wall building, while rejecting duplicate beds. Legacy patrol/refuge journeys without a bed remain exclusive. A regression first failed on the previous whole-building exclusion, then passed with two distinct beds, rejection of a third guard's duplicate claim and restored assignments. Core 582/582 passes; runtime compilation has zero warnings/errors. Unity packaging, sleep rendering and full shift coverage remain pending.

17 October wall-rest foundation: outside-gate shift recall now surveys actual beds in replacement wall interiors and routes a guard to a reachable bedside. `RestBedRecord` survives serialization, holds position until the exact next-shift release, and preserves legacy indoor patrol behavior when absent. Survey caches clear with the settlement. Core tests pass 581/581 and all runtime source compiles with zero warnings/errors. This is source-only: guards currently stand idle at the bedside, one guard reserves the entire wall building, and daytime rest for night patrols is not connected. Sleeping visuals, per-bed sharing of quarters, full shift assignment and native acceptance remain outstanding. No new furniture is injected by this production survey.

Inventory review follow-up: witnessed indoor key theft now calls the finite building-response dispatcher instead of only setting the crime flag. Inventory cleanup also runs on native window changes, so deposits are preserved immediately after closing rather than waiting for a gameplay update. The live regression now returns a taken key and verifies it becomes native dropped loot without replenishing the corpse claim. Runtime compilation remains clean and core 580/580 passes; these runtime assertions still await Unity license renewal and a packaged native run.

Keeper business keys are now source-implemented. Runtime requires a registered native identity, matching workplace building, shop/tavern type and merchant faction classification before requesting the workplace key. Core rejects a mismatched workplace and deduplicates a shop that is also home. An off-site keeper home/business/save-load regression raises the core suite to 580/580. The outdoor scene probe now exercises the native corpse inventory, take-all, persistent key depletion and retained body visual. Runtime source compiles without warnings/errors, but the new native probe is not yet run: the latest Unity build still fails license activation.

Corpse-key inventory adapter is now source-implemented for indoor and outdoor managed bodies. Opening the native inventory is harmless; actual key transfers commit taken-state and invoke witnessed-theft handling. Keys cannot stack. Deposited items are moved to native serializable ground loot when the window closes; unclaimed generated keys remain in the corpse ledger. This currently supplies home keys only, not general NPC equipment or explicit off-site business keys. All runtime source compiles against the installed player's assemblies with `dotnet build tools/RuntimeCompile/RuntimeCompile.csproj` (zero warnings/errors), and core 579/579 passes. New native transfer assertions are added to the Unity build verifier but remain unrun. Unity's latest build still exits on licensing activation failure, so these changes are not packaged or live-tested.

Corpse-key ownership foundation now has `InitializeCorpseHomeKey`, resolving registered household/native homes and retired household generations rather than the death location. Repeated initialization preserves taken keys; native household heads receive no duplicate key from their two identity representations. A bedless native workplace is not assumed to be a home. Core suite: 579/579. This API is not yet connected to corpse inventory UI; business access for keepers living elsewhere still needs an explicit keeper ownership binding. These source changes await a Unity build after license renewal.

The controller failure is now traced and corrected in the installed build. On a first visit, exterior routine preparation pauses while indoors, but `Ready` previously stayed false until it finished. Household fallback plans now become ready before exterior surveying; newly calculated visits are published only after their routine ledger is stored. The retest binds inn 131078, finds 80 anchors and resolves keeper 308 with five common-room stops. Core tests remain 576/576 and Unity build passed. Full movement acceptance is still open: the synthetic movement fixture was not registering its forced destination in the authoritative routine ledger. Its source now registers/restores that fixture, but the next Unity build was refused because the editor license expired. This last probe change is not installed or runtime-verified. Evidence: `DFU/work/external-mods/inn-controller-bound-observed-visit-failure.log` and `license-expired.log`.

The subsequent readiness retest waits up to 30 real seconds after closing the entrance UI. It fails with `Live inn controller did not bind after entrance UI closed.` This narrows investigation to controller binding/readiness; a single-frame probe race does not explain the failure. No pathfinding fix is verified. Evidence: `DFU/work/external-mods/inn-binding-readiness-failure.log`. The owned test process exited.

Current display name: **People of the Iliac Bay**. Current installed order is recorded in `EXTERNAL-MOD-LOAD-ORDER.txt`; the initial list below is historical. The filename `livingcities.dfmod`, GUID and `mod_livingcities.txt` save names remain unchanged. Rename build/integration checks pass, as do 576 core tests and native custom-key inventory round-trip checks.

Final-selection retest still fails at inn building 131078: no surveyed doorway ground point in the live patron controller, after six rooms were successfully surveyed. This also occurs with Rosy's Residences disabled and both removed NPC mods absent, so it cannot be dismissed as their conflict. Diagnostic evidence: `DFU/work/external-mods/final-selection-tavern-failure.log`. No speculative doorway-search change was retained.

Latest selection supersedes the initial order/findings below: Lootable Villagers and Families of Daggerfall have been removed from player/editor Mods directories and Mods.json at the user's request. Their archives remain unchanged. Rosy's Residences is disabled; Rosy's Resources stays enabled as a dependency of Beautiful Cities/Villages. The active selection prioritizes Taverns Redone, Beautiful Cities/Villages, Lively Cities and Detailed City Walls. Corpse inventories and property keys belong to Living Cities, not a dependency.

The follow-up native tavern test (Families disabled) failed with `Inn movement probe lacks a surveyed doorway ground point`. This is a confirmed unresolved compatibility issue, not an accepted inn pass. Evidence: LivingCities/work/town-test.log. Doorway geometry investigation is underway.

16 October 2026. Mods installed separately; source archives retained under DFU/downloads.

## Enabled order

- 0: daggerfall expanded textures 1.3.0
- 1: RMB Resource Pack 0.3.0
- 2: DIEP - Rosy's Resources 1.1
- 3: Elves & Beastfolk - Resources 1.1
- 4: New Paintings 0.5.0
- 5: Flat Replacer 0.2.19
- 6: Harvestable Crops 1.2.2
- 7: DET Harvestable Crops 1.0.2
- 8: Windmills of Daggerfall 2.2
- 9: Finding My Religion 1.23
- 10: Fixed Desert Architecture 1.37
- 11: Taverns Redone 0.17
- 12: Detailed City Walls 1.23
- 13: Beautiful Villages of Daggerfall 1.4.2
- 14: Beautiful Cities of Daggerfall 0.5.0
- 15: Lively Cities 1.3.4
- 16: DIEP - Rosy's Residences 1.1
- 17: Elves & Beastfolk - Replaces 1.0
- 18: DIEP - Families of Daggerfall 1.1 — DISABLED (confirmed incompatibility)
- 19: Lootable Villagers 2.0
- 20: BasicRoads
- 21: Living Cities

Names of Tamriel Extended 1.1 is a loose StreamingAssets/Text override, not an entry in the mod list.

## Verified and unresolved findings

- Unity loaded all 20 downloaded bundles and extracted metadata. Required manifest dependencies were present. Load ordering respects non-peer dependency edges; resources precede consumers. Living Cities follows the installed gameplay and layout mods.
- Fresh Gothway Garden checks passed: 121 households, fresh bed capacity limits, movement, interior continuity, death persistence and save/load. This first run also logged an external Families exception; these specific passes do not certify the full stack.
- Families of Daggerfall is installed but disabled. Its FactionNPCInitializer unconditionally reads Player/Assets/StreamingAssets/Factions/Faction.Txt and throws DirectoryNotFoundException. Its TalkWindowScript also destroys native StaticNPC objects and substitutes CustomStaticNPC objects, conflicting with Living Cities binding. Disabling only dialogue would not fix the unconditional initializer.
- Rosy resources and residences remain enabled. Rosy residences load after Lively Cities: where both replace an entire building, Rosy wins. This preserves the selected residence layouts but does not merge conflicting decorations/NPCs. Beautiful Cities/Villages similarly override overlapping base architecture by their declared order.
- Lootable Villagers 2.0 remains enabled. Its source handles vanilla PopulationManager mobile NPC deaths, can spawn additional warriors and creates its own corpse. Managed Living Cities corpse loot/key integration is unfinished; do not claim that installing it adds keys to managed corpses.
- Fresh housing surveys traverse native WorldDataReplacement and therefore see modified RMB/interior beds. Existing saved households and routes lack layout fingerprints and migration. Their old BedCapacity and routes can remain stale. Do not regenerate identities or delete player saves as a workaround.
- DET 1.3.0 logs missing material JSON for its own bootstrap object named daggerfall expanded textures. Decompiled code compares that name against differently capitalized Daggerfall Expanded Textures. No claim that all material errors or all graphics are harmless; no upstream bundle modified.
- Flat Replacer and added racial NPC art need further appearance/binding verification. Existing generated sleep/combat art does not automatically cover new flats.
- Guard bed sleeping and persistent corpse property keys are still integration work, not features supplied automatically by installing these mods.

## Evidence

- DFU/work/external-mods/full-inspection.log and source-inspection.log
- DFU/work/external-mods/full-stack-first-smoke.log
- DFU/work/external-mods/dependency-inventory.json and layout-overlaps.json

Later verification: native DFU `NameHelper` generated 400 nonempty names across all eight humanoid banks and both genders using the installed editor override. `DFU/work/external-mods/names-validation.log` records `EXTERNAL_NAMES_VERIFIED` and a clean Unity exit. This verifies generation, not saved-identity migration or visual gameplay.

Property-key integration is in development: custom item and exact-property access, per-corpse persisted claims, and post-transfer inventory observation. `NativeItemTransferHooks` compiles against the player assemblies and observes single/bulk non-stacking transfers only after the item leaves the source and enters the destination. It is not yet attached to corpse inventories; do not claim loot is functional.

The user is downloading archives into `DFU/downloads`. Names of Tamriel Extended 1.1 is installed separately into both player and editor `StreamingAssets/Text/NameGen.txt`. The original RAR remains unchanged. Its JSON omitted a comma between Monster3 sets 2 and 3; the staged copy adds that comma and passes JSON parsing. Both deployed files match SHA-256 `921B560087396021AE55030D5961C2D6010D4AF0B4721CEEEA1D11219857693D`. Runtime name-generation acceptance is pending. Existing saved identities must not be regenerated. This supersedes the initial login request below.

Daggerfall Expanded Textures 1.3.0 and RMB Resource Pack 0.3.0 are installed separately in player/editor Mods folders. Unity successfully loaded both bundles and extracted their actual metadata (`DFU/work/external-mods/inspection.log`, `EXTERNAL_MOD_INSPECTION_SUCCESS`). Player Mods.json enables both before BasicRoads and Living Cities, preserving the existing pair's relative order; its original copy is `DFU/work/external-mods/Mods.before-dependencies.json`. <a id="source-future-dialogue-and-voice-md"></a>

## Original: FUTURE-DIALOGUE-AND-VOICE.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Future dialogue and English voices

## Current planning authority — 17 October 2026

See the [roadmap](ROADMAP.md) and [consolidated design](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design) for latest future requirements. Preserve ordinary free-form roleplay while adding authored main-story personas, speaker/stage-filtered quest facts, dated shared-experience memory and consent-based invitations. Hidden motives and future plot must not enter the social prompt or retrieval results; sensitive quest answers need native/approved stage content, since models can also know story spoilers independently. Mood may vary without changing story intent. Native dialogue actions/rewards remain authoritative. Companions and chat-command action adapters remain shelved. Older dated sections below record their respective implementation stage and may contain superseded approaches.

## Daily plans, personality and mood — 17 October 2026

Implemented for local free-text dialogue: each addressed NPC gets independently mixed personality traits (warmth, patience, curiosity, privacy and manner), stable where the NPC has a persistent identity. Mood is recalculated from a stable daily baseline, current personal disposition, bereavement, danger response, outdoor weather, daylight, activity and whether a previous text conversation with this persistent identity is recorded. The encounter count survives saves. Rain, storms, snow, fog and sunshine supply contextual signals; darkness can make a stranger seem riskier without forcing every NPC into one response. These are guidance for free-form replies, not canned response templates.

Managed residents receive their actual ordered daily itinerary and named local stops where DFU can verify the building name. Scheduled inter-settlement trips take precedence over the home routine. Stationary native NPCs receive verified work and home obligations when available. An NPC may mention a real next destination when inclined, or keep private plans to themselves. Unmanaged vanilla wanderers and stationary NPCs without a modeled itinerary have personality and mood but no invented detailed schedule. Future work: give every persistent NPC a physical itinerary first, then expose it to dialogue; add persistent event-driven mood beyond the current day and richer acquaintance memory. Never let dialogue imply a visit that the simulation cannot perform.

## Future NPC memory and shared-room context — recorded 17 October 2026

Requested for the future-feature backlog only; not implemented by this documentation change.

- Combine a sliding window of exact recent conversation exchanges with persistent memories belonging to each stable NPC identity within the current save. Keep authoritative NPC/world context fully present; only conversation history slides out.
- Store compact summaries and structured memories of meetings, personal details, promises, disputes and relationship changes. Retrieve relevant older memories for the current topic rather than loading every conversation or the entire NPC memory file. Start with summaries and structured lookup; consider semantic RAG when history volume warrants it. File layout and retrieval implementation remain undecided.
- Record in-game date, time and location of meetings. Supply the current game date and calculated elapsed time so an NPC can naturally recognize a previous meeting, such as a conversation last winter or a year ago. Use game time rather than real-world time.
- Preserve provenance: distinguish player claims from verified world facts, and keep memories isolated across NPC identities and saves. Summarization should run asynchronously without blocking gameplay. Retrieval and summarization must be measured for token cost and latency.
- Refresh scene context each dialogue turn with NPCs in the same indoor room, their known relationships to the speaker, and whether they can hear the conversation. Do not treat everyone in the building as present: separate rooms, floors and closed doors matter.
- Use this context for natural references and social awareness, such as asking a nearby person for help or declining to discuss something in front of a spouse. Do not invent relationships or awareness. Current room occupancy is transient context; only significant exchanges actually witnessed or heard should become persistent memories.

Future acceptance: recognize a prior meeting after a long in-game interval, retrieve a relevant old detail without sending the full transcript, avoid cross-NPC/save memory leakage, and update room awareness when listeners enter or leave without giving absent NPCs knowledge.

## Current structured social replies — 17 October 2026

Server lifecycle follow-up: after the PC shutdown/restart, the user saw repeated canned unknown replies. No llama-server process was running and the loopback health request timed out. `Prewarm` only sends a request; it does not launch the server. Classifier network failure currently becomes empty output and `ExactFallback`, which emits unknown replies for ordinary chat while exact native topics still work. Restarting the existing local server restored health and generated replies without changing the mod. TODO: explicit once-per-session offline/loading status and a controlled opt-in server launch/readiness/retry lifecycle; do not disguise backend failure as NPC confusion or spawn duplicate servers. Maintain the planned full LLM opt-out and loopback-only boundary.

The user authorized deploying separate conversation roles after isolated comparisons. Social replies now use authoritative system context, separate historical user/assistant turns, and the latest user input. Whole-exchange sliding history preserves fixed context. Temperature remains 0.75; unsuccessful experimental mood guidance is excluded. Classification and native factual/map paths are unchanged. 636/636 core checks, Unity build and strict live checks pass, including earlier-turn recall after trimming. This improves the tested behavior without guaranteeing all conversations; isolated testing still retained one failed neutral-follow-up seed. Evidence: `work/structured-dialogue-native-pass.log`.

## Current HUD verification, 17 October

The latest interface uses the classic HUD and edits the upper native question panel. The older bottom-field artwork and horizontal-scroll descriptions below are historical. The upper field wraps and scrolls vertically with the caret. Live screenshots now confirm visible, bounded text: `People-dialogue-scroll.png` shows four wrapped lines within the upper panel. The invisible-text bug came from the transcript leaving its scissor rectangle on a shared font material; the editor now sets its own clip rectangle while drawing and restores it afterward. Editing bounds use the native maximum width, not the label's text-dependent size. The native dialogue probe also checks focus shortcut suppression, selected-question copying, draft preservation and the actual model reply path. Physical mouse/keyboard acceptance remains a user check.

## Pending follow-up requested 17 October

Conversation continuity implementation, 17 October: the active native transcript now supplies speaker-labelled history to both intent classification and social replies. A token-aware sliding window removes only the oldest complete exchanges. Background requests query the local server's context size and tokenizer, reserve output tokens plus a safety margin, and preserve the current question, authoritative NPC/scene data, retrieved topics and instructions. If fixed context alone cannot fit, report the context limitation instead of silently truncating it. The full transcript stays visible, and history participates in reply cache identity. This is active-conversation continuity, not persistent memory after closing the conversation. The former 1600-character completion-prompt limit and 240-character assembled scene-context limit are removed. A uniquely named map destination also overrides an erroneous model category choice while retaining native knowledge/permission checks. Final build acceptance is recorded in MILESTONES.md.

- After one to three serious insults, an NPC warns that they will report harassment if the settlement has guards. Give the player an opportunity to apologize. Continued harassment ends dialogue; the NPC flees and physically reports to an available guard through the existing witness system. Use the agreed minor-crime fallback (Trespassing), not Murder. Guardless settlements must not invent guards.
- Vary grounded refusal wording without inventing facts the NPC cannot know.
- Diagnose and eliminate unsupported characters rendered as repeated question marks.
- Keep long player input inside its field with caret-following horizontal scrolling.
- Replace the dialogue background with a designed input-field frame rather than drawing a field over the original artwork. Inspect the original HUD before generating its replacement.
- Add optional LLM quest-giver tone flavor only at verified active NPC quest-message boundaries. Preserve native quest objectives, names, choices, rewards, places and deadlines exactly; if the source text cannot be preserved and validated, show the native text unchanged. Use current actual personal disposition and asynchronous bounded generation, with an untouched native fallback. Do not use stale `LastNPCClicked` alone or rewrite unrelated quest narrative and popups.

These items are recorded requirements, not a claim of implementation.

User addition, recorded 14 October 2026. Speech is a future optional extension after the vanilla NPC simulation is stable. It does not replace the planned local dialogue milestone.

## Local dialogue model

The selected first local model is Qwen2.5 7B Instruct, official GGUF Q4_K_M shards, served by CUDA llama.cpp. The target machine has a RTX 4070 SUPER with 12 GiB VRAM and 64 GiB RAM; this 4.68 GB quantization leaves capacity for DFU. `tools/StartLocalDialogue.ps1` serves only `127.0.0.1:8080`. Validate grounded paraphrasing, social-chat refusal of factual questions, latency and memory alongside DFU before accepting it as the shipped recommendation. Record actual measured output and model/runtime revisions.

17 October foundation: `src/LivingCities.Core/LocalDialogue.cs` now defines a pure bounded request/plan boundary. It embeds the vanilla answer as the authoritative fallback, carries limited NPC expression context, creates stable cache keys, normalizes untrusted text, and rejects empty, markup-like, role-labelled or overlong model output. `LocalDialogueCompletion.cs` only permits unauthenticated loopback HTTP endpoints (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `::1`), formats Qwen2.5-Instruct requests with ChatML turn markers, and creates a bounded llama.cpp `/completion` payload. `LocalDialogueDispatch` invalidates an in-flight result when a dialogue closes, so later UI cannot receive stale text. `mod/Scripts/LocalDialogueClient.cs` executes HTTP work on a worker, parses only the `content` response field, bounds its cache, and dispatches completion back on Unity's main thread. `LivingCitiesMod.Dialogue.cs` adds a small text field to the bottom of DFU's existing Talk window; submitted text and reply are appended to its existing conversation transcript, while left-side vanilla topics remain untouched. Default `Dialogue > EnableFreeText` and `Dialogue > PrewarmModel` are enabled but each remains configurable. Prewarm sends one context-free one-token loopback request after gameplay begins. It never occupies the chat request slot or stalls gameplay. It uses `127.0.0.1:8080/completion`, preserves vanilla topics, and falls back to a non-factual answer if no model responds. Local llama.cpp on RTX 4070 SUPER used 6.3 GiB VRAM and returned an eight-token ChatML reply in 457 ms (84.2 tokens/sec). Model text is display-only by contract; it cannot call quest, inventory, disposition or simulation APIs.

17 October Talk HUD verification: wait until `DaggerfallTalkWindow.IsSetup` before adding mod components. Earlier injection occurred before vanilla added its full-size background, so the field existed in the component list but was covered. A mod-owned 117 × 17 pixel stone-framed texture now marks the bottom-right input slot. A separate `Say:` label sits left of the native textbox to avoid duplicate placeholder rendering. The live dialogue probe captured the framed slot with `Say: Hello` visible after entering probe text. The vanilla Talk IMG, topic list and saves were not modified.

The extended `TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -DialogueProbe` calls the production submission method with the textbox value, performs the real loopback request, rejects fallback/busy replies, checks the question/answer pair in the native transcript, and captures the rendered result. The first passing run returned a model reply in 0.69 seconds; both messages were visible in `LivingCities-dialogue-reply.png` under the game's persistent-data directory. This does not simulate physical mouse clicks or the Enter key; those inputs still need manual acceptance. It also does not establish rich relationship memory or multi-turn conversation history.

17 October source follow-up: free-text requests now ask for a short in-character social reply from known NPC context. They no longer ask the model to paraphrase the non-factual failure message. Fact-dependent questions still direct the model to the native topic choices. A new outbound-payload regression failed before this change; the full core suite passed 597/597 afterward. This change is now in the installed package and the live model-to-transcript probe passed as described above.

### Native knowledge retrieval and map requests — implemented 17 October

Player testing exposed hallucinated temples and places in the original social-only path. That path carried identity, race, gender, role and disposition but no native answers. Prompt instructions alone did not enforce factual grounding. The installed replacement first classifies the player's intent against a bounded list of real native topics; factual replies use native answer text verbatim. Only social replies use unconstrained prose generation.

`LivingCitiesMod.ConversationKnowledge.cs` reads the active `TalkManager` trees (`ListTopicLocation`, `ListTopicPerson`, `ListTopicThings`, `ListTopicTellMeAbout`) and includes Work through its native answer path. It ranks candidates from the full trees for each question, sends up to 100 topics/8000 caption characters, and asks the local model for an intent, supplied topic ID and tone. A llama.cpp grammar constrains the format; host validation rejects invented IDs and unintended map actions. General category questions return actual child names for clarification. Unknown facts return a deterministic clarification. Only the chosen leaf calls native question/answer methods, preserving knowledge, tone, quest visibility and answer limits. Never enumerate all answer getters to construct context: they change dialogue state and can reveal locations or choose a work questor.

Explicit map requests for local buildings now check native knowledge, reaction and indoor restrictions, establish the real native subject and use the normal `%loc` discovery path. Discovery is checked before reporting success. Quest/person/regional answers retain their native reveal behavior rather than being forcibly marked. Pronoun requests such as “mark it” resolve to the previous answered subject, including an intervening native topic click; a default/previewed topic is not an answered subject. The model cannot provide arbitrary coordinates or executable commands.

### Disposition and input focus

Map intent validation also requires an explicit request rather than a mere mention of a map. A named target must match the selected real topic; a pronoun resolves to the previous answered subject. Invalid or stale follow-up subjects return clarification, including when the classifier is unavailable. Regression checks cover these cases.

The focused input sets `OverridesHotkeySequences=true`; DFU suppresses button shortcuts while it has focus and resumes them after focus leaves. A live regression failed before the change and passed afterward. The framed slot and native topic mouse controls remain in the original Talk window.

Free-text classification yields neutral, polite, rude or insult. The host applies fixed deltas of 0, +1, -2 or -8; the model cannot supply numbers. Managed resident and stationary NPC identities retain personal offsets in the additive `WorldState.ConversationAttitudes` field. Old saves without it load an empty ledger. Unmanaged vanilla wanderers have no durable identity, so their penalties last only for the current conversation. Faction reputation is untouched. Per-NPC offsets are bounded to -60..+20; daily positive gain is at most +3 and loss at most -30. The same normalized line applies once per game day, with at most 32 recorded lines per NPC/day. Direct insults are distinguished from reporting someone else's insult. Classification can still misread subtle sarcasm; these bounds limit the consequence.

`NativeConversationHooks` adjusts the native topic reaction tier after vanilla skill/personality checks: -8 personal offset lowers one tier, -24 lowers two, and +12 raises one. Free-text factual help is refused at -24 or worse. Mood also enters social-response context. `Dialogue > ConversationDisposition` and `MapRequests` allow these effects to be disabled independently.

Verification: 605/605 core checks; runtime and hook compilation with zero warnings/errors; Unity build; targeted native test covering focused hotkeys, real LLM reply (0.80 s for the first greeting including classification), category choices, invented-place refusal, unknown/known map targets, typed/native-topic follow-up, insult/rude/quoted/repeated speech, snapshot restore and native reaction hook. Evidence: `work/GroundedConversation-native-pass.log`, `work/GroundedConversation-build-pass.log`, `work/conversation-tests.log`. Rich multi-turn social memory, every quest-mod topic combination and physical keyboard acceptance remain separate from these automated checks.

Game state remains authoritative. The user's 17 October authorization supersedes the old strictly display-only rule only for validated native topic actions/map discovery and bounded personal disposition. Generated prose still cannot award items, invent relatives, grant services or execute arbitrary state changes. Rich relationship and memory context remains future work.

### Flirting and child reports (17 October follow-up)

Flirting is classified separately from politeness; ordinary non-romantic compliments remain polite. Adult same-sex approaches are unwelcome under the user's requested game rule. Opposite-sex reception is an evenly distributed deterministic choice for the NPC/player identity pair, so changing the wording or reloading does not reroll it. Welcome reception uses +1, unwelcome reception -2 through the existing daily/repetition/overall limits. Personal offsets of -24 or worse reject advances regardless of the initial choice. The model receives the resulting reception as authoritative context; it must not invent a spouse or promise a relationship. Actual marriage-specific weighting remains unimplemented.

Children do not participate in romantic dialogue. A detected flirt directed at a known child closes dialogue, displays "Leave me alone! Guards!", and reports Trespassing through the existing finite physical guard dispatcher. Existing charges, including Murder, are preserved. This reporting rule is independent of the disposition toggle. Ordinary compliments do not trigger it. The classifier can still misread ambiguous wording; no accusation is based on generated reply prose.

Verification: 608/608 core tests, clean runtime compilation and Unity bundle build pass. The native dialogue probe passed actual adult flirt classification, same-sex rejection, stable opposite-sex reception, ordinary compliment +1 and response context. A controlled child-age fixture then closed dialogue, charged Trespassing and dispatched four existing guards without increasing the roster; an existing Murder charge remained unchanged. This checks dispatch into the established pursuit/arrest flow, not a new complete prison encounter. Evidence: `work/Flirting-core.log`, `work/Flirting-build-pass.log`, `work/Flirting-native-pass.log`. The test now waits for garrison setup before opening Talk; pausing it earlier prevented setup from finishing. Live testing also caught the factual keyword gate rejecting "excellent job" and a classifier choosing a leaf for an explicitly named category; regressions now cover both corrections.

## Optional English TTS

Investigate reusing the fast English speech engine from the user's Telegram-Codex-Bot project. The current local worker source uses MiraTTS and caches `encode_audio(reference_audio)` contexts. Its inspected reference-context path does not take a reference transcript. Recheck the installed model's requirements during integration; a 5–7 second sample plus transcript is a proposed production format, not a verified universal requirement.

Target at least 52 female and 52 male distinct base voices, with additional suitable fantasy voices such as low, rough orc voices. Pitch and speed variants supplement this base diversity rather than replacing the requested 104 base voices. Prefer generated voices or samples licensed for the intended use and distribution, with source/license metadata. Evaluate voice-design models and generation services, including the user's suggested ElevenLabs/11.ai option. Free access without an account, download rights and sample suitability remain unverified; do not make them a dependency.

Create a catalog with stable voice IDs, reference audio, optional transcript, language, descriptors, provenance, processing version and quality checks. Assign each NPC a compatible voice deterministically and persist the catalog voice ID plus bounded pitch/speed variation in the save. Do not reroll voices on travel, reload, death replacement or catalog reordering. Newly recruited NPCs receive new identities and assignments. Define missing-voice fallback and migration explicitly.

Separate synthesis from Unity's main thread. Support bounded queues, interruption when dialogue closes, cached repeated lines, subtitles, volume/speech toggles and graceful text-only fallback. Limit ambient overlap and maximum audible speakers. Benchmark first-audio latency, warm/cold performance and combined LLM/TTS/DFU VRAM use; the bot's historical latency is not a DFU performance guarantee. Avoid changing or interrupting the bot's running worker during mod development.

Acceptance requires at least 104 audibly distinct, intelligible base voices; stable NPC voice across reload; suitable orc/fantasy options; adjustable playback; bounded memory; no UI freeze; and immediate text fallback when synthesis fails. Generation, downloading and model integration remain future work.
# Latest accepted dialogue rules — 17 October 2026

These thresholds supersede the earlier arbitrary one-to-three-insult ejection rule and the old personal penalty/daily penalty caps.

- First direct insult to a particular NPC costs 10 disposition; further insults cost 25 each, including repeated wording. Rudeness costs 2.
- Personal penalties can reach the engine minimum of -100 actual disposition. Positive personal gains stop at five points above the native faction/social reaction baseline. Faction reputation itself is unchanged.
- At -50 in the NPC's own home, shop or inn, the NPC demands the player leave and refuses information and map help. The context must distinguish ownership from simply being a visitor or tenant.
- At -100 the conversation ends. Outdoors, the NPC physically runs to an available guard/patrol before reporting. Indoors, the NPC immediately calls the finite garrison; guards still travel to the building and enter. The minor charge is Trespassing and must not replace an existing stronger charge.
- A valid apology can improve the personal reaction within the positive limits. The LLM receives authoritative location/ownership context and cannot invent that it owns the current building.
- At +75 actual disposition, a living resident can permit taking items from containers in their own private home. This does not authorize taking shop stock, inn rental property or public/faction property.
- Accepted pricing rule (supersedes the earlier 10%-discount decision): after vanilla pricing, actual disposition -100 raises shop purchases, repair fees and paid inn-room prices by up to 50%; 0 leaves native prices unchanged; +100 lowers them by up to 15%. Sale proceeds move in the opposite direction: -100 pays up to 50% less and +100 up to 15% more. Signed adjustments scale linearly and round down in absolute gold. Free rooms stay free; positive prices and proceeds stay at least one gold.
- The input keeps the entire draft but scrolls its visible text horizontally with the caret; neither edge may render outside the field. The generated HUD contains the recessed input area, and the text control is transparent.

Implementation and native acceptance status must be checked against the current code and test logs; this section records requirements, not a claim that every adapter is finished.

# Latest HUD decision — 17 October

Use the classic HUD background. The upper player-question panel is the editable chat area: clicking focuses it, native topic selection supplies editable question text, and Enter submits. Text wraps across lines and the viewport follows the caret vertically when full. Restore the full native transcript below; the bottom input field and replacement HUD are superseded. Keep native topic buttons and tone controls, with their letter shortcuts disabled only while typing.

## Open social roleplay decision — 17 October

The earlier rule that every question without a recognized native topic receives a fixed clarification is superseded. Unmatched questions, opinions, stories, and reactions now reach the local model. NPCs may invent small personal anecdotes and harmless details. Current game time, date, settlement, region, and actual outdoor weather enter each social turn. Native topics, map discovery, quests, inventory, services, and prices still require validated game actions; generated prose cannot perform them. If the local model fails, the interface reports that the model is unavailable instead of attributing a canned reply to the NPC. A resident already fleeing to report a crime or complaint must reject Talk before its window opens.
