<a id="source-guard-building-response-md"></a>

## Original: GUARD-BUILDING-RESPONSE.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Building guard response implementation

## 16 October: reacquisition and live recruitment

The user reported guards failing to resume pursuit after losing sight. Returning and posted guards were omitted from the controller's visibility-triggered engagement path. Guards now recognize a currently wanted player from their own prior player-crime response records, including after returning and save/load. Actual visibility and no invisibility are required. Uninformed guards and records of creature defense or neutral investigation do not grant this recognition. A posted guard gets a surveyed response route; a returning guard resumes from its current position. Cleared crimes do not trigger pursuit.

The full native controller regression passes with actual nearby recruitment, retained refusal during shared pursuit, wounded disk save/reload, search expiry, reacquisition after expiry and completed return, and posted self-defense (`work/GuardReacquire-runtime-pass.log`). The probe now preserves the current native surrender state on reacquisition rather than incorrectly assuming vanilla retains refusal after a complete disengagement/reload. Core 381/381 also passes (`work/GateReacquire-core.log`). This supersedes the pending live-recruitment status below. Dedicated gate validation remains separate.

## 16 October: nearby pursuit recruitment

User requirement: a guard chasing the player recruits other guards passed within roughly 10 metres, with slight variation. Loaded outdoor pursuit now checks contacts every 0.5 real seconds. Each candidate has a stable 8–12 metre radius derived from its saved appearance seed. Both guards must be living and outside; candidates must be available posted/patrolling guards, with no refuge or interior assignment. Creature-defense callers are excluded. Actual actor distance and visibility between guards prevent recruiting through walls. A surveyed path to the caller's last known suspect position is required.

Recruitment uses existing finite identities and native pursuit, preserves the caller's knowledge expiry, and does not reset the shared surrender flag. Callers are snapshotted to avoid instantaneous same-update cascades. Further pursuit can recruit more guards on later checks. The recruit receives the last known location rather than the player's hidden current position. Interior recruitment and a live passing-guard regression remain pending; the user's running game was not interrupted.

Verification: 379/379 core tests cover range boundaries, restore-stable radius, unavailable/dead candidates, expired pursuit, creature defense, finite identity count and saved pursuit knowledge/deadline. Unity build evidence: `work/PursuitRecruit-build.log`. This does not yet establish visual behavior in a live chase.

The native controller probe now exercises actual nearby recruitment after refused surrender and requires `LIVING_CITIES_PURSUIT_RECRUIT_PASS`. It checks inherited knowledge/deadline, finite identity count, native hostility and retained refusal. Initial dispatch assertions now count native reports separately from legitimate proximity recruitment. The repeated-attack loop excludes unassigned guards, because `BeginCombat` correctly rejects posted guards with no incident. Focused review identified that same fixture risk; the filter is included in the passing Unity build (`work/PursuitRecruitProbe-build.log`). The extended native probe is compiled but has not run while the user's game remains open.

Witness junction correction: surveyed polylines are split at existing route vertices and query points before shortest-path search. A branch touching the middle of a patrol segment now connects, and distances between interior points no longer detour through original endpoints. The regression failed before the fix and passes afterward (`work/WitnessJunction-red.log`, `work/WitnessJunction-core.log`). No arbitrary crossing point or unsurveyed shortcut is invented. Large-city query performance after this change still needs measurement.

## 16 October: surveyed witness routing

This supersedes the stationary-post-only routing limitation below. WitnessNavigation selects the shortest reachable route to an available post or current moving patrol position on cached approach/patrol paths. Escaped witnesses replan from their evaluated position every 24 game seconds until reporting, including when a target becomes unavailable. Dispatch still requires actual contact. Indoor escape preparation calculates this route only when an eligible witness needs it.

Verification: 377/377 core tests; native patrol selection/restore probe and full shop visitor/contact/inspection regression pass (`work/WitnessRouting-patrol-pass.log`, `work/WitnessRouting-shop-pass.log`). Final Unity build and integration checks pass and bundle is installed. One native route query measured approximately 6.6 ms. Full rendered moving-patrol interception, predictive meeting times, wall-separated contact and large-city performance remain unverified.

15 October witness retargeting foundation: CivilianEscape.RedirectExterior creates an isolated replacement street route from the witness's evaluated current position, preserves the original indoor exit and individual fleeing speed, and saves the redirect timestamp. It rejects pre-exit redirects and routes starting elsewhere. Historical saves default RedirectedAt to zero and retain original timing.362 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/WitnessRedirect-core.log`, `work/WitnessRedirect-build-editor.log`, marker LIVING_CITIES_WITNESS_REDIRECT_SAVE_PASS). This API is not yet called by patrol targeting: predicted moving-guard interception, selection by reachable contact time, unavailable-target replanning and live contact validation remain pending. No live game test was run, following the user's deferral.

## Required patrol interception, 15 October 2026

Witnesses must compare reachable stationary guards with moving patrols and choose the earliest reachable contact. Use route travel time and the patrol's predicted motion rather than straight-line distance or its obsolete departure position. Re-evaluate when the target dies, enters another scene, becomes unavailable or cannot be reached. A report requires physical contact with a living guard; do not dispatch on an expired route timestamp alone. Persist witness identity, progress and target across scene transitions and saves. Required acceptance includes a closer patrol, a closer stationary post, an unreachable nearby guard, a moving-away patrol, target loss and indoor-player/offscreen contact. The current `WitnessRoute` explicitly excludes patrols and does not implement this yet.

## Witness timing clarification, 15 October 2026

`CivilianEscape` saves the indoor route to the exit, exterior route to a guard post, start time and individual fleeing speed. `ExitAt = start + indoorLength / speed * 12`; `ReportAt = ExitAt + outdoorLength / speed * 12`. The factor12 is the current simulation time conversion. New fleeing speeds are3.25–4.55m/s. Arrival must still find a living posted guard at the endpoint before a direct witness dispatches guards. Guards then traverse their own surveyed approach routes at2.8m/s with the same time conversion; interior response starts at physical entrance arrival. There is no separate conversation-duration delay.

Known limitation: `WitnessRoute` chooses routes to available stationary posts and excludes active patrols; it does not intercept moving patrols. Missing indoor or exterior routes prevent planning, and an absent guard at the endpoint prevents successful reporting. General alternative-guard replanning remains unfinished. Do not describe this as instantaneous reporting or completed dynamic guard discovery. Report flavor text is emitted only when dispatch succeeds, not when the attack occurs.

## 0.3.22 durable native building alarms

Native reported crimes now retain their original building when its approach is unavailable. `QueueBuildingReport` saves an explicit `AwaitingApproach` report with no entrance, route or responders. Save validation permits only an unassigned non-investigation building report in this state and rejects fabricated responders or partial destinations. Existing saves default the additive marker to false.

The active controller retries unassigned native building reports once per real second while the player's crime remains active. Once a surveyed approach and available guards exist, normal measured travel starts from the current game time. Reload or entering another building cannot retarget that saved report. Repeated callbacks reuse the saved pending or active report instead of issuing extra responder batches. Clearing the player's crime removes unassigned native reports; assigned guards retain their existing return behavior. Discovery scenes and civilian messenger knowledge retain their separate mechanisms.

This supersedes the earlier native missing-route report limitation. It does not add unsurveyed navigation, patrol pairs, torches, universal crime attribution or missing civilian escape routes.

Verification:198/198 core tests and Unity/native build pass. Actual-player GuardBuildingProbe tests repeated alarms without an approach, no immediate responders, cancellation after crime clears, real pending save/reload, another repeated alarm after reload, automatic route retry, timed doorway arrival, wounded guard persistence and return. `work/PendingGuard-reload-red.log` reproduces duplicate reports after reload before the reuse fix; `work/PendingGuard-0322-pass.log` records the final passing sequence. Read-only Luna/high review found that reload issue and found no remaining confirmed important issue after the fix.

## 0.3.21 ownership without a cached approach

An active finite roster retains ownership of its supported settlement's loaded building interiors before their exterior approaches are surveyed. Dungeons, other settlements and disabled controllers remain outside that ownership. Navigation readiness must not enable transient vanilla reinforcements or reveal an unwitnessed murder. Resident home deaths and registered native keeper workplace deaths use witness evidence and settlement ownership for concealment, preserving an existing unrelated crime.

Physical dispatch still needs an actual approach; no missing-route teleport or invented path was added. Departure-triggered concealed scenes can retry discovery as surveyed routes become available. Durable queuing/retrying of already reported non-murder crimes without a cached approach remains unfinished. This checkpoint does not claim that every known crime survives unavailable navigation.

The regression removes a real residential approach while indoors, tests concealment and ownership, restores the approach, then verifies timed arrival, cleanup, ten-second warning, native surrender/court, real save/load and one-hour expiry. A separate native sleeper regression removes its workplace approach during a lethal hit. Both probes restore temporary cache changes in `finally` even when assertions fail. The older missing-survey vanilla fallback statement below is superseded.

Verification: 196/196 core tests and the Unity build pass. `work/UncachedGuard-owner-red.log` reproduces lost ownership; `work/UncachedGuard-conceal-red.log` reproduces lost concealment after ownership alone was fixed. Final passing residential and native keeper evidence is in `work/UncachedGuard-0321-pass.log` and `work/Housing-0321-pass.log`. The latter also verifies the two-person/two-bed Kinghart household, preserved native head, native services, sleep/wake and real reload. Base DFU tracked `Assets/Scripts` remains unchanged.

## 0.3.7 gameplay integration

Verified checkpoint: 150 core tests, native build, GuardBuildingProbe, GuardControllerProbe and GuardActorProbe pass. Evidence is retained in work/guard-building-runtime-pass.log, work/guard-controller-runtime-pass.log and work/guard-actor-runtime-pass.log. Clearing the player's crime now explicitly stops indoor combat and starts the doorway return, preventing visible guards from extending hostility indefinitely. The building probe checks this without forcing the deadline.

Surveyed Gothway Garden buildings now use the finite roster for native indoor alarms. Guards retain their street travel deadline and materialize at the interior entry landing only after arrival. Native combat starts on sight. Building-local 3D positions, last-seen positions, health and the path travelled indoors persist through direct interior reloads. On search expiry, guards retrace that path to the entry and then reverse their exterior approach to return to the post. Missing surveys still leave the interior on vanilla guards; visit the exterior with the preview enabled first.

The guard clock now explicitly promotes whole seconds and fractional seconds to double before addition. The former ulong-plus-float expression rounded DFU's approximately 12-billion-second absolute timestamp to 1,024-second increments. StreetPopulation's parenthesized fractional expression was already double and is unchanged.

The new focused command is tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -GuardBuildingProbe. It drives the native SpawnCityGuards entry point inside shop 131342, checks no premature guard body, advances game time to arrival, checks the entry landing and native combat activation, saves a wounded responder to SAVE31 (Living Cities - shop guards), reloads directly inside, and checks eventual exterior return. The time jump accelerates the test; it is not a real-time travel benchmark or an inventory theft interaction test.

Remaining acceptance: more interior layouts, stairways and blocked door landings; whole-town survey/frame-time budgets; full arrest dialogue; player escape before arrival and repeated scene changes during pursuit; active room-search movement when no suspect is visible. Patrol pairs, wall posts, torches and defense against other enemies remain required.

## Earlier implementation notes

The approved behavior is physical reinforcement: shop alarms dispatch existing municipal guards, each travels to the shop door and enters on arrival. Distance produces the escape window. Saving, loading or entering another building must not reset travel, create guards, or move responders to a different incident.

## Persistence contract

The deterministic core now stores collision-checked exterior approaches, keyed by building, with an entrance and routes from guard posts. The scene adapter is responsible for surveying actual collision geometry. The core rejects unknown guard identities, mismatched origins or door endpoints, duplicate keys, invalid points and inconsistent active report destinations.

Building reports retain the destination and entrance independently of the cache. An incident cannot be redirected to another building. Repeated reports preserve responders and arrival times. Missing approaches dispatch nobody. Recruits can use a deceased guard's cached post route without inheriting its identity. Earlier schema-6 saves have an empty approach cache.

Core tests cover reload timing, defensive copying, missing routes, immutable destinations, malformed restoration and replacement identities. DFU's native FullSerializer round trip also retains the cache and nullable building key.

Version 0.3.6 connects the exterior survey to GuardPopulation. It maps reachable entrances and calculates one guard-post approach per frame, using TownGeometry's existing collision checks. Only completed reachable building entries are persisted. The coroutine pauses outside its active exterior scene or while the game is paused, and Clear cancels it. Existing cached entries are retained after reload; newly recruited guards can reuse their post's route. This does not yet activate indoor dispatch or indoor guard actors.

The actual-player GuardControllerProbe checks that a reachable approach is produced and survives the wounded-guard save/load. It also places a temporary blocker overlapping a route start and requires route rejection. TownGeometry now checks waypoint overlaps as well as segment sphere casts. The probe does not yet certify a completed whole-town survey, frame-time budget, or interior doorway arrival. Initial door discovery is still synchronous and needs profiling.

## Remaining adapter work

1. Finish whole-town survey and scene-transition acceptance checks, including frame-time measurements. Initial incremental survey and save/load are connected.
2. Bind an indoor native crime report to the current building key. Dispatch against saved exterior approaches and suppress instant native reinforcements only when the replacement owns that building.
3. Maintain separate exterior and interior coordinates for each responder. Only materialize inside after the exterior arrival deadline, at a collision-checked doorway landing. Preserve health and identity through the transition.
4. Search the reported interior, pursue only observed suspects, return through the doorway and resume an exterior route. Handle player escape before arrival, repeated entry, death, paused time and loading directly inside.
5. Actual-player acceptance: native shop alarm, nearby and distant arrival times, doorway entry rather than player-centered appearance, no duplicated guards, save/load while en route and indoors, escape and return. Keep the exterior and civilian regressions green.

Patrol pairs, torches, wall posts, attacks against other enemies and broader settlement coverage remain required afterward.
# Repeated surrender fix, 16 October 2026

## Witness contact foundation, 16 October 2026

Exited witnesses are now eligible for contact checks along their exterior route, not only after its endpoint timestamp. `GuardRoster.GuardsInReportingRange` evaluates actual current guard positions (including patrols), within 0.75 metres, and excludes dead, indoor, sheltered or non-posted guards. Direct witnesses and neutral corpse observers use this check before dispatch. Hearing-only witnesses also use current positions rather than empty guard posts. New hearing reports persist the guard's contact position and validate it against the witness's saved route/time; legacy endpoint/post reports remain readable. Repeated neutral dispatch logging is suppressed after a populated report already exists.

Core 375/375 covers moving patrol versus empty post, guard death, roster restore, pre-endpoint hearing contact, report restore and corrupt contact rejection. Unity build, full inn alarm/inspection/replacement regression and full native keeper suite pass (`work/PatrolContact-core.log`, `work/PatrolContact-build-editor.log`, `work/PatrolContact-inn-regression-pass.log`, `work/PatrolContact-shop-regression-pass.log`). Those live tests verify existing reporting and cleanup paths; they do not demonstrate a rendered moving-patrol interception. Current `WitnessRoute` still selects a stationary posted destination. Closest-patrol route selection, prediction, retargeting after movement/death, robust wall-separated contact checks and complete offscreen encounter timing remain unfinished. This is contact handling, not completion of the requested moving-patrol routing system.

Opening the native surrender dialogue used to make `GuardPopulation.OwnsTown()` false because `IsPlayingGame()` excludes paused dialogue. The controller consequently hid its actors and called `EndCombat`. Vanilla then saw no hostile guards and reset `HaveShownSurrenderToGuardsDialogue`, reopening surrender after refusal. Ownership now depends on the loaded settlement, not dialogue pause; the existing update pause check freezes simulation without discarding actors or hostility. The shared vanilla surrender flag continues to govern all guards, without a per-guard replacement dialogue or base-game edit.

The real controller regression reproduces the old failure while the offer remains open, then passes with the fix: the actor stays active/hostile, No remains remembered across rendered frames, and repeated native attack calls from additional initialized guards do not reopen the offer. Health is replenished between test hits to exclude vanilla's separate lethal-hit forced-surrender policy. The complete controller suite also passes wounded save/reload, search expiry and posted self-defense. Evidence: `work/SurrenderRepeat-runtime-red.log`, `work/SurrenderRepeat-runtime-pass.log`, `work/SurrenderRepeat-build-editor.log`; core 372/372. The runner requires `LIVING_CITIES_SURRENDER_REFUSAL_PASS`.

# Guard effect persistence

Verified development checkpoint, 15 October 2026. Installed development0.3.34 SHA-256: `ED3A6FB3B446EC51F8245ADA067F8E7790755F86A41FD0BD3F784FAA2FF7CC6B`.

Guard records now retain DFU's serialized effect bundles and the last processed native magic minute. Actor restoration uses the native effect manager, including effect-specific state and caster identity. Elapsed magic rounds are replayed using the same two-day cap as native DFU. The processed broker minute avoids counting a pending global tick twice. Capture occurs on save, scene replacement and before native scene transitions; no engine source changes are required.

The pre-transition capture is essential: native `EnableExteriorParent()` schedules destruction of the interior before the next controller update. Capturing only when clearing views lost effects on actual shop exit, despite passing isolated recreation and save/load tests. `OnPreTransition` captures while the source actors still exist.

## Evidence

- 289/289 core tests: `work/GuardEffects-core-green.log`. Optional snapshots deep-copy, reject blank/oversized payloads and leave old guards without invented effects.
- Native player-origin continuous health damage survives native JSON and body recreation with exact remaining rounds. Three unloaded minutes reduce duration appropriately; expired effects remain absent. `work/GuardEffects-elapsed-pass.log`.
- Actual game-slot reload restores continuous fatigue damage on the wounded indoor guard. Actual shop exit preserves that effect on the same guard, which physically pursues outside. `work/GuardEffects-door-pass.log`.
- Doorway failure reproduced before pre-transition capture: `work/GuardEffects-door-red.log`.
- Unity build succeeds: `work/GuardEffects-final-build.log`.

The actual slot reload checks the bundle/key; exact remaining-round assertions are in the isolated native serialization/recreation test. The doorway check rejects absent, expired or restarted duration. Test saving is confined to the dedicated LivingCitiesTest character.

## Related save correction

The elapsed-time test exposed historical entry-messenger routes being rejected after guard post relocation. Historical routes now validate guard identity rather than requiring the guard to retain its former post forever. Planning and delivery still check the current post; an undelivered route to a relocated guard is cancelled without informing it. Delivered history survives. Regression: `work/EntryPostMigration-red.log` and the passing core suite.

## Limits

The native guard fixture is immune to paralysis; that immunity is unchanged. Tests use continuous damage instead. Poison, disease, custom effects, caster relinking from non-player enemies, and every transition layout are not exhaustively verified. The adapter uses pinned DFU private timing methods and needs revalidation when upgrading DFU. Timed roster travel does not yet explicitly model movement restrictions from every effect. Automatic indoor witness-to-enemy defense remains unfinished; its actor adapter is tested separately.

# Public guard posts and doorway pursuit

## 16 October gate and pursuit correction

Latest user requirement supersedes the standing destination below: exterior sentries end their shift in city-wall quarters and sleep in assigned beds. The inner on-duty pair remains posted. Current inner return points are only the intermediate gate-crossing destination; wall-quarter assignment, interior travel, visible sleeping and morning departure are not implemented by this checkpoint.

Scheduled recall now lives in the persistent core roster (`GuardPostRecall`). The open-gate survey supplies the return path and a departure/arrival deadline before closure. Core advancement evaluates that journey without exterior geometry, including time jumps and save restoration. Emergency reassignment, death or a changed post invalidates the routine recall. Completed routine movement reports are pruned; they never establish knowledge of player crimes. Core coverage includes JSON round-trip, intermediate position, delayed advancement, emergency/death, invalid deadlines and thirty repeated shifts with bounded report storage.

Daggerfall City's four native gates now receive two inner sentries and two outer sentries from the existing finite garrison. Posts flank the actual doorway and face away from the wall. Gate assignments are excluded from ordinary patrol/post migration. Outer sentries follow surveyed routes to separate inner posts before native 18:00 closure; deployment requires time for both outbound and return travel. A player crime no longer suspends recall of otherwise idle sentries.

Three placement/navigation causes were identified: valid gate-side ground has navigation weight 6, the 25.6-metre gate mesh includes the wall frame rather than only the doorway, and native navigation can disconnect the open crossing. Native models 446/447 have an 8.525-metre aperture centered at local X=-3.1875 metres. The survey uses these measured dimensions, a bounded nearby-cell search, collision clearance, and an explicitly swept aperture bridge. Closed gate geometry still blocks that bridge. This is native-model support; replacement gate meshes need separate compatibility validation.

Verification: 381/381 core tests, Unity build and the full live Daggerfall gate probe pass. All four gates have four distinct assignments; actual actors at the observed gate match positions/facing, and all eight outer sentries finish their simulated return paths before closure. Evidence: `work/GateGuard-runtime-pass.log`, `work/GateGuard-core.log`, `work/GateGuard-posts.png`. The image visually confirms the inner pair beside the doorway. The return check samples roster movement for all gates, not rendered continuous movement of all eight actors simultaneously.

Further verified checkpoint: 385/385 core tests and the final Unity build pass. The extended native test re-deploys eight exterior guards on the next day, enters a real inn, advances across dusk while remaining inside, and confirms all eight saved recalls completed (`work/GateRecall-runtime-pass.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_GATE_INDOOR_RECALL_PASS`). JSON recall persistence and bounded thirty-shift storage pass core tests. Full native gate-duty save/load coverage, other settlements/replacement meshes, elevated wall posts, and wider performance profiling remain open. The user's subsequent [wall-quarter sleeping requirement](HISTORY.md#source-guard-quarters-md) remains unimplemented beyond these recall foundations. Castle coverage remains a separate requirement in [activity and gate follow-up](HISTORY.md#source-activity-and-gate-followup-md).

The separate chase-reacquisition regression also passes: known wanted players reacquire pursuit from returning and posted guards when actually visible. See [guard response](HISTORY.md#source-guard-building-response-md) and `work/GuardReacquire-runtime-pass.log`.

## Earlier checkpoint

Installed development version:0.3.34. Bundle SHA-256: `89A2F556FA8848B7C1F68C38EC6FDE6833175B383357CA6239CE2DD06D0FEF82`.

The player's Daggerfall report exposed doorway post selection: guards were assigned resident commute starts at house entrances. A stationary post must instead oversee a road junction, public square, gate approach or reachable wall position, with a clear entrance and street-facing stance.

## Current source changes

16 October gate-shift update: outer sentries now continue from the pre-close crossing into reserved wall interiors and patrol until dawn, then return to staging for daytime deployment. Inner posts remain staffed. Beds/sleeping are deferred by the user's latest decision. See [GUARD-QUARTERS.md](HISTORY.md#source-guard-quarters-md) for verified scope and limitations.

The exterior survey samples paved junctions/open paved areas and road cells near native city gates. Static capsule clearance and route validation reject obstructed positions; posts remain at least five metres from building doors and six metres apart. Gate approaches receive priority. Paved-space edges are preferred over their centers. This is a native tile/geometry heuristic, not semantic identification of every market square. Elevated wall posts are not surveyed yet.

Existing idle saved guards keep identity, appearance, health and mortality while walking surveyed relocation routes from old posts to available public posts. Guards on active patrols, investigations, pursuits or refuge journeys retain that journey and relocate when safely posted again. Recruitment at an obsolete inherited post is handled by the same deferred migration. Post changes invalidate approach caches and resurvey routes; active response paths remain intact. Exterior surveying waits until the exterior is active after an indoor reload and completes without recreating active guard actors.

Stationary guards face the longest clear street direction instead of their last approach to a door. The static direction is cached per post, avoiding four physics casts per guard per frame.

## Shop exit pursuit

The previous exterior controller skipped every guard with an interior record. Thus a guard pursuing the player inside could remain trapped in that simulation state after the player left.

On a building-exit event, an actively hostile indoor pursuer retraces its recorded interior trail to the doorway. The same guard then emerges at the reported exterior entrance and resumes native pursuit/search. Health, identity, incident and last known exit are preserved. A distant guard does not instantly teleport outside. A cleared crime uses existing stand-down behavior. The optional saved `ExteriorPursuit` field is validated against the report's building entrance; ordinary older saves keep a null field. A guard killed during doorway travel remains dead and loses the pursuit marker.

## Verification status

286/286 core tests pass (`work/GuardPostMove-green.log`), covering public junction classification, identity/health-preserving post reassignment, relocation travel/reload, active patrol preservation, delayed doorway exit, pursuit save validation and ordinary return. Unity build passes. Native Gothway shop checks verify15 public posts, indoor alarm/save/reload/stand-down and the same wounded guard physically pursuing outside (`work/GuardPosts-door-motion-pass.log`). After the final migration correction, the combined Daggerfall haunting/patrol/refuge/city/save/movement and regional suite passes (`work/GuardPosts-city-regression-pass.log`):611 Daggerfall residents and49 guards; initial city setup11.62 seconds, not isolated post-survey or frame-time profiling. Elevated wall posts, full legacy-save migration coverage, other shop/storey layouts and broader city profiling remain open.

# Gate sentry shifts and wall quarters

## Current requirement — supersedes sleeping below

16 October: user deferred guard beds. Outer sentries should cross before closure, enter the city-wall interior, patrol there until the next shift, and return in the morning. Inner sentries remain on duty. No automatic bed furniture is enabled. The experimental bed survey/probe is retained only as unfinished future work; isolated/loaded bed placement did not match and must not be used for assignments.

Implementation uses the persisted recall plus a wall-duty purpose on the existing doorway journey. The planned route follows the checked gate crossing, continues to a reachable wall door, and patrols back and forth along the final portion of a surveyed indoor route. Each living assigned guard reserves a separate wall interior for now. At dawn it finishes that circuit and reverses the entrance route to the inner staging point before daytime gate deployment. Fear-refuge release does not release this shift. Saved timing works without loaded exterior geometry.

Verification: 388/388 core tests pass, including JSON restoration, continuous indoor movement, dawn return, copied route isolation, invalid crossing speed and emergency priority without health reset. Native Daggerfall probe passes all eight finite outer shifts, pre-close crossing, next-day deployment, time advancement through the night while the player remains in an inn, a loaded wall guard moving along its patrol, and morning return. Final Unity build is installed. Evidence: `work/WallDuty-core.log`, `work/WallDuty-final-runtime-pass.log`, `work/WallDuty-build-editor.log`. Visually inspected `work/WallDuty-patrol.png` confirms the guard rendered in the wall interior. Earlier blank captures were test timing: native billboards need an Update after a sudden camera turn, and capture must finish before jumping to dawn. Native disk reload during a wall shift remains unverified; core JSON round-trip is covered.

Limitations: indoor patrol is a bounded back-and-forth route rather than a varied multi-room itinerary. Bed placement, sleeping and a broader shift rota remain deferred. If no reachable unreserved wall interior is found, the safe inner recall remains the fallback.

## Historical sleeping proposal (deferred)

User requirement, 16 October: outer gate sentries finish their shift before gate closure, enter city-wall quarters, and sleep in beds. The two inner sentries remain on duty. The current additional inner return points are staging destinations, not the final nighttime behavior.

## Verified constraints

`docs/data/bed-census.csv` contains 38 Town23 block/record layouts. Every one has zero bed models and zero rest markers, including WALLAA00–WALLAA11. Vanilla wall interiors therefore cannot provide the requested beds without mod-added furniture. Existing `RefugeGeometry` and Daggerfall refuge probes establish reachable floor routes, not sleeping accommodation.

Use existing classic bed models 41000–41002 as mod-added props. Do not edit game data or silently assign guards to invisible beds. Bed placement must preserve doors, stairs and walkable access. One physical bed holds one guard.

## Implementation sequence

1. Survey eligible Town23 interiors in isolation. Find deterministic floor footprints for beds, reject slopes, stairs, wall overlap and obstructed entrances, then validate a path from the entrance to each bedside after adding the prop collision geometry. Prefer quarters near the assigned gate. Cache the result by settlement/building and layout identity.
2. Persist each guard's quarter building, bed anchor, exterior route, interior route and shift times. Reserve beds across the finite roster; do not create extra guards to fill beds. Retain identity, health, effects and death state.
3. Extend the existing saved gate recall beyond its inner staging point into the selected quarters. Exterior and interior routes use separate coordinate origins with an explicit doorway transition, as in `GuardRefugeJourney`. Time advancement must work while the player is indoors or absent.
4. Add a rest purpose distinct from fear refuge. `ReleaseSafeRefuges` currently releases shelter when haunting becomes safe; it must not immediately wake ordinary off-duty guards. Sleep starts only after the guard reaches the assigned bed. Morning wake and the reverse journey precede the next gate shift.
5. Render the same guard asleep in the physical bed. Existing `SleepingPose.BeginNative` assumes a StaticNPC-style renderer/collider and cannot be applied blindly to GuardActor. Adapt presentation while retaining the guard entity, effects, damage and wake behavior.

## Acceptance

- Observe outer sentries crossing before closure, entering wall doors, reaching their own beds and sleeping when followed indoors.
- Inner sentries remain on duty; no duplicate guards or shared beds appear.
- Repeat with player waiting inside another building, saved mid-journey and saved asleep.
- Verify health/effects, death, emergency interruption, haunting and dawn return without teleportation.
- Test all distinct native wall layouts and reject unsafe bed placement rather than clipping furniture through walls or stairs.

Historical status at proposal time: saved pre-close recall and existing wall-refuge navigation were foundations. The sleeping sequence above is deferred by the current requirement.

# Finite guards

0.3.34 replaces entrance posts with surveyed public junction/square/gate sites and gives stationary guards a cached open-street facing. Saved idle guards walk to reassigned posts; active journeys finish first. A hostile indoor pursuer retraces its trail through the door and resumes native exterior pursuit with the same identity and health. Core286/286, native physical shop chase/save/load and Daggerfall/city/refuge/regional checks pass. Elevated wall posts remain open. See [evidence and limits](HISTORY.md#source-guard-posts-and-doorways-md).

Current development correction: hostile quest ownership no longer blocks exterior guard defense. Awake generated citizens and registered merchants also flee existing hostile enemies. The quest's native AI flag is preserved, and threat reports do not accuse an innocent player. See [verification and limits](HISTORY.md#source-threat-response-md); historical quest exclusions below describe earlier versions.

## 0.3.26 quiet patrols and increased garrisons

Native EnemySounds plays City Watch's Halt as a periodic proximity bark even without a crime. Managed guards disable that automatic callback while preserving explicit hit/parry/attack sound methods. A guard may give one visible, in-range warning per saved report to a wanted player or hostile human NPC; creatures receive none, and combat does not repeat the warning. Saved speaker IDs prevent duplicate warnings after reload.

Quota is ceil(residents × 8%), with one guard in tiny hamlets under40 residents, minimum8 in larger hamlets/towns, minimum16 in cities and maximum350. Existing rosters can grow using distinct surveyed posts without renaming guards or reviving deaths. Insufficient survey capacity leaves expansion pending; actual quota/coverage must be checked per settlement. Gothway Garden's current census181 requests15 guards. Default night patrol assignment remains two pairs; a larger roster alone does not expand patrol circuits.

Verification:227/227 core tests, official Unity/native build, ten-second peaceful actor/audio check, wanted-player once-only warning and final creature/human defense probe pass. Evidence: `work/GuardQuiet-0326-pass.log`, `work/GuardController-0326-initial-pass.log`, `work/GuardDefense-0326-pass.log`. Daggerfall city support and quest-aware haunting remain pending in `DAGGERFALL-HAUNTING.md`.

## 0.3.25 exterior enemy defense and actual torch

Finite guards now detect visible active hostile enemy bodies within20 metres in the supported exterior. Dead, inactive, pacified, player-allied and City Watch bodies are excluded. Native quest foes retain their AI attackability restriction. All eligible reachable routes start at actual guard positions; core route-length sorting selects up to two closest available responders. Guards already responding to another incident are not reassigned.

The report's optional `ThreatId` distinguishes enemy defense from player crimes. Native LoadID targets resolve after reload; transient targets use a controller-session key and cannot bind accidentally to an unrelated enemy. Missing targets stand down through physical return. Native motor, senses and attacks supply movement, animation and damage; the adapter pins the reported enemy because native target selection omits enemy combat when infighting is disabled. Player hostility remains false during defense. A native player-assault reaction takes priority even when native AI keeps its adjacent enemy target; the adapter restores ordinary combat callbacks and native criminal response. Existing player crimes remain intact.

`EnemyDefense` defaults on. Target death, pacification, loss/search expiry or disabling the option produces a surveyed return from actual engagement position. Threat scans run once per second; large-city frame-time profiling remains required. Interior enemy detection, guard travel into an interior for enemy defense, civilian attacks by enemy actors, Daggerfall haunting exceptions and legitimate-defense welfare/loot rights are not implemented by this exterior pilot.

Live GuardDefenseProbe passes pacified/inactive/wall-obscured rejection, actual native enemy damage with infighting off, real native enemy and wounded guard reload, no innocent-player crime, target death and physical return to post, existing Theft preservation, and close-range player-assault takeover. SAVE46, `Living Cities - guard defense`, belongs to `LivingCitiesTest-f238d317`; the controlled rat has500health to keep the encounter available for testing. Evidence: `work/GuardDefense-0325-pass.log`; core222/222 and native WorldState target/health/position serialization pass. `work/GuardDefense-assault-red.log` reproduces the adjacent-target assault bug before the fix.

Torch presentation now uses native archive210 record17, an upright wooden torch with animated flame, replacing the brazier-like record0. Native source inspection exports eight references through `GuardTorchSurvey.Run` into `work/torch-source`; no generated artwork or base assets are changed. Existing attachment height, warm light, budgets and toggles remain unchanged. `work/GuardTorch-upright-red.log` reproduces the wrong-record check before replacement. Full hand grips and directional arm artwork remain future presentation work.

Final upright-torch actor and paired-patrol regressions pass: exact archive/record, multiple flame/walking frames, light budget/toggle/death lifecycle, pair spacing, real reload and actual-position crime dispatch. Evidence: `work/GuardTorch-0325-upright-pass.log` and `work/GuardPatrol-0325-pass.log`. The native patrol screenshot at Application.persistentDataPath/LivingCities-night-patrol.png was inspected and shows both upright torches. Final crime-scene regression also retains native surrender before damage, court, warning/reload and occupation expiry: `work/CrimeScene-0325-pass.log`.

## 0.3.24 paired street patrols

Gothway Garden now starts finite pairs at night on collision-checked native navigation circuits. Both guards physically join separate circuit entries, then share a saved time phase with two metres of path spacing. Formation validation rejects routes that bring them within one metre. Initial survey found two usable circuits of 57.6 and 102.4 metres. Failed surveys leave guards posted; there is no straight-line fallback. Route work yields between searches, with bounded attempts and circuit length.

`NightPatrols` defaults on. `MaximumPatrolPairs` ranges from 0 to 4, default 2; zero disables patrols. Starting a pair preserves at least four available posted guards. Reducing the cap, disabling patrols or reaching dawn sends excess pairs physically back to their posts. Crime responses use actual patrol positions; cached building responses join their existing approaches through physical travel. A fleeing witness targets a stationary posted guard. Existing torch controls remain independent.

Circuit survey is bounded by the pair cap when the town loads. Increasing the cap beyond already surveyed pairs requires reloading the town; a failed survey does not retry every frame. Reducing the cap takes effect during the current session.

Actual-player GuardPatrolProbe checks surveyed pairs, both walking animations, spacing, point light, real save/reload phase, disabling patrols and native crime dispatch from the current returning position without adding guard identities. Evidence: `work/GuardPatrol-0324-pass.log`. SAVE45, `Living Cities - night patrol`, belongs to `LivingCitiesTest-f238d317`. Native JSON timestamps round at this magnitude; reload comparison allows 0.001 game-second while verifying IDs, interruption state and actor positions separately.

Final housing, physical building response and crime-scene regressions also pass with the compiled package. They preserve named keeper families, reserved-bed sleep/services and real reload; delayed guard entry, wounded persistence and return; and native surrender before damage, court, warning and occupation expiry. Evidence: `work/Housing-0324-pass.log`, `work/GuardBuilding-0324-pass.log`, `work/CrimeScene-0324-pass.log`.

The package uses DFU's official precompiled mod assembly support after source-text compilation failed during native navigation. The exact compiler failure is not established. Guards remain classic sprites with attached flame artwork; wall patrols, simultaneous join/response crowd avoidance, additional settlements and large-city frame-time profiling remain unfinished. M4 and the overall goal remain open. Earlier checkpoints below are historical where superseded.

## 0.3.23 night torches

Visible exterior roster guards now carry Daggerfall's animated210/0 torch flat at night, using the game's dawn/dusk boundary. Each carried sprite follows its guard transform. Daytime, interiors, the setting toggle and guard death extinguish the torch. Torch flats have no active colliders and do not change guard identities, paths or save ownership.

The mod menu exposes `NightTorches`, `TorchLightLimit` (0–16, default8) and `TorchRange` (3–12 metres, default8). Only the nearest selected guards within48 metres emit actual warm point light. A zero light budget retains visible flames. All guard lights have shadows disabled; light selection refreshes four times per second using a reused candidate list. The carried flat is a presentation attachment, not new guard hand/weapon animation artwork. Paired patrol routes and wall patrols remain unfinished; existing posted/responding guards benefit immediately.

Live GuardActorProbe verifies point-light settings, flame visibility independent of the lighting budget, toggle/death extinction and classic animation. GuardControllerProbe verifies actual game night/day, a one-light cap, zero budget and the toggle, followed by native response/combat, wounded save/load and return. The controller takeover fixture now clears ownership before creating its transient native guard; because finite guards default on, the previous fixture incorrectly received an owned roster guard and expected its destruction. That correction changes only the test setup.

The following version statements are historical. See GUARD-BUILDING-RESPONSE.md for current ownership, persistent missing-route reports and discovery behavior.

Evidence:198/198 core tests, Unity/native build, `work/GuardTorch-red.log` (missing torch presentation), `work/GuardTorch-0323-actor-pass.log` and `work/GuardTorch-0323-controller-pass.log`. The final actor probe also observes multiple rendered flame frames. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed important defect. Base DFU tracked Assets/Scripts remains unchanged. Large-city lighting/frame-time profiling remains open.

Current 0.3.9 adds delayed indoor witness reports when a physical exit-and-post route exists. Guard dispatch retains the crime building while the witness crosses scenes; ordinary native indoor alarms still use the 0.3.7 adapter. Unwitnessed discovery is not implemented. See INDOOR-WITNESSES.md for the route, persistence and fallback boundaries.

Current 0.3.8 adds public witness reporting. Outdoor generated-resident kills require a local guard witness or a fleeing direct witness reaching a posted guard; reports retain the original crime position and cannot replenish their finite responder group. Both visibility checks use 20 metres. See CIVILIAN-RESPONSES.md for tested scope and limitations. Indoor alarms retain the 0.3.7 behavior below.

Current 0.3.7 adds finite indoor responses for surveyed Gothway Garden buildings: delayed doorway entry, native combat, wounded direct-interior reload and path-based exit/return. The same existing ExperimentalExteriorGuards setting now enables both supported contexts. Unsurveyed interiors remain vanilla. See GUARD-BUILDING-RESPONSE.md; historical limitations below are superseded only where that checkpoint provides evidence.

Version 0.3.6 now incrementally surveys real exterior post-to-door approaches and preserves completed building routes through actual save/load. Indoor dispatch and actors remain pending; see GUARD-BUILDING-RESPONSE.md. Older installed-version statements below describe their historical checkpoints.

The next building-response core now persists validated exterior approaches and immutable building-bound reports. Core tests: 146/146; DFU native serializer verification passes. The installed player remains 0.3.5 while the indoor adapter is unfinished. See [building response implementation](HISTORY.md#source-guard-building-response-md).

## Live controller checkpoint, 14 October 2026 — version 0.3.5

Enable General / ExperimentalExteriorGuards to try the exterior Gothway Garden controller. Indoor guards remain vanilla. This supersedes older statements below that guard bodies were test-only.

A fixed saved roster supplies bounded reinforcements along validated routes. The controller removes old non-quest transient guards and hides pooled guards while it owns the exterior. Native combat activates on sight or direct hostility. Wounded health, actual position and last-seen suspect position persist; reload does not grant fresh player-position knowledge. Search expiry routes guards back from their actual positions.

Guards / MaximumResponders permits 1–8 (default 4); ActorRadius permits 64–256 metres (default 160). Engaged guards remain active beyond that radius. Settings never reroll existing rosters.

Verified: 142/142 core tests, native Unity build/settings/serialization checks, and actual-player GuardControllerProbe plus GuardActorProbe. Tests cover bounded repeated reports, takeover, combat readiness, wounded SAVE30 reload, search expiry, posted self-defense, walking animation and persistent death. Full arrest dialogue and an actual weapon hit on a guard are not covered by these probes.

Next: physical shop reinforcements and door transitions, paired patrols/torches/walls, citizen reports and defense against other enemies. Guards outside the reachable navigation grid can stop and retry their return route indefinitely; there is no teleport fallback. M4 remains incomplete. Evidence: work/guard-controller-runtime-pass.log and work/guard-actor-runtime-pass.log.

This is the foundation for M4 under the existing approved gameplay requirements. The installed 0.3.4 development preview still uses native spawning in ordinary play until the replacement controller is validated.

## Physical actor checkpoint

`GuardActor` now materializes a saved identity as a native City Watch body. It follows saved route positions and native directional walking animation, retains native colliders and death handling, and records deaths in the roster. `LoadID = 0` excludes the view from native enemy persistence, preventing a second native save owner. Creation preserves shared random state. A dead saved guard cannot materialize again.

Native `EnemyEntity.Update` normally deletes City Watch bodies when the player has no active crime. A narrowly scoped `NativeGuardLifetimeHooks` patch skips that destruction only for bodies carrying `GuardActor`, while preserving the rest of the native entity update. Ordinary vanilla guard cleanup remains active.

The actual-player command `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -GuardActorProbe` passes repeated fixture loads, timed route placement, multiple rendered walking frames, crime-free survival, unchanged RNG, unowned vanilla cleanup, native death and serialized no-resurrection checks. Evidence: `work/guard-actor-runtime-pass.log`. Build and native hook/serializer checks pass. No old save slot is rewritten by this focused probe.

This actor view is currently exercised by the explicit test mode only. Native motor, senses and attack are disabled while the view owns its position. The production controller still must enforce one view per ID, handle scene/door transitions and distance visibility, preserve wounded health, dispatch crime reports and connect pursuit/combat/arrest. Do not claim that normal play already has finite guards.

## State and behavior

A settlement owns a saved roster. Each guard has a permanent identity, a post, current duty, location/route and life state. Creating a settlement twice returns the same roster. Population changes and menu changes never silently reroll existing guards.

The initial policy uses five percent of residents, at least four guards in towns and eight in cities. Very small hamlets (fewer than forty residents) have no garrison. Larger hamlets have a small contingent. These defaults are a starting policy, not a claim that settlement-size tuning is final. Later settings must expose supported controls.

Reports dispatch available living guards by actual route length, with up to four responders. A guard cannot answer two incidents simultaneously. Repeated reports reuse the same dispatch rather than replenishing it. Travel uses game time and measured route length; actor visibility and player door transitions do not control elapsed travel. No reachable route or no available guard means no responder.

A report with no assigned responders remains pending and can retry when a route or guard becomes available. Once any responders are assigned, the same report never recruits additional responders. Invalid route requests leave the entire roster unchanged, including other guards whose travel would otherwise advance.

Arrival starts a bounded search at the reported location. Fresh sightings may update pursuit; loss of sight leaves only a last-seen position. Search expiry returns guards to their posts. Recruitment preserves dead identities and introduces a new identity only after a delay, with at most one replacement per day. It never happens while loading a save simply because an actor is absent.

## Implementation sequence

The production `NativeGuardHooks` bridge now intercepts both native spawn entry points and suppresses automatic pooled-guard conversion while the roster owns a town. Its owner supplies a supported-town predicate, a report callback and a real roster-actor resolver. Native Unity tests exercise immediate/deferred calls and actor returns, unsupported-town fallthrough, conversion gating, disposal and rejection of null actors. The hook is deliberately not installed by `LivingCitiesMod` yet: there is no physical roster controller to satisfy its actor contract.

The controller must also take ownership of native mobile guards. `WeaponManager` immediately dereferences a guard returned while attacking a mobile guard, and `PlayerEntity` can convert additional pooled guards after a response. Leaving native pooled guards active alongside the roster would duplicate the population. Entry from an interior must retain exterior route/door information; current saved citizen routes do not provide a complete navigation cache. These are integration requirements before activation, not reasons to keep instant native reinforcements in the final mod.

1. Test and implement roster identities, settlement counts, finite dispatch, distance-based arrival, search/return, death and bounded recruitment in the deterministic core.
2. Persist and validate the roster in schema 6, including native DFU serialization and migration from schemas 1–5.
3. Replace supported-town native batch/single spawn paths with roster ownership, preserving the return contract when vanilla hits an already visible guard.
4. Materialize patrol/post/response actors at their real locations. Preserve native combat/arrest integration while controlling sight/search and door arrival.
5. Test repeated reports, exhausted/dead roster, nearby/far reinforcements, escape, shop entry, save/load mid-response and old-save compatibility in the actual player.

Paired torch patrols, walls/headquarters, creature defense and wider settlements remain part of M4 after the first dispatch adapter. They are not optional replacements for the above requirements.

## Night patrol lighting requirement

Night street patrol guards carry visible torches that emit moving local light. This is part of the planned paired-patrol behavior, not yet implemented. Limit active lights by distance and expose sensible lighting controls in mod settings so large cities remain practical.

## Surrender and arrest contract

Guards must preserve vanilla surrender before damaging the player for a crime. Native EnemyAttack.ApplyDamageToPlayer opens the Yes/No surrender message on the first successful City Watch hit when a crime is active and the dialogue has not already been shown. That branch does not call SendDamageToPlayer. Yes calls SurrenderToCityGuards(true) and opens court; No applies the attack damage. The adapter must not bypass this flow. Actual-player verification now passes: a real roster guard runs the native attack handler with god mode disabled, opens surrender before damage, and accepting Yes enters the native court window. The probe calls the native button handler directly rather than clicking the UI. Evidence: work/SurrenderProbe-pass.log. Full sentencing and imprisonment outcomes remain unverified.

## Original: HOUSEHOLD-LOCKS.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Household absence locks

17 October empty-house entry requirement: when no living person is present, the house greeting must read "You are greeted with an eerie silence..." instead of a random voice answering from inside. Dead inhabitants/corpses do not count; visitors and sleeping living occupants do. The greeting query uses current effective resident locations, native occupants and guard scene occupation; unknown placement leaves vanilla behavior unchanged. The native text override is scoped to text ID 256 during exterior door activation and must not change other text or door permissions. Core occupancy tests pass 631/631; a live scoped text regression is included in the final dialogue test.

Messenger runtime checkpoint: successful civilian-witnessed entry can now start a saved collision-surveyed street route to the nearest reachable posted guard. The route is selected before exterior unload. Its saved position overrides the ordinary routine while active, including home occupancy queries, and delivery advances while the player stays inside. Arrival notifies only the receiving guard, assigns Trespassing without replacing a prior crime, and triggers finite building response. Direct guard observations bypass messenger travel. Completed guard reports are not redispatched. HousingProbe/EntryProbe passes delayed indoor notification, the route-position override, direct/unseen/cancelled entry and prior Murder preservation; work/EntryMessenger-pass.log. Runtime render-path wiring is present, but a separately observed outdoor messenger, smooth routine rejoining, retargeting/retries and broad permission/quest acceptance remain pending. The disabled finite-guard setting has no roster to receive a civilian messenger.

Messenger core checkpoint: EntryReport can now carry one saved EntryMessenger route, citizen/guard identities, start time and delivered/cancelled status. Travel evaluates continuously at 2.8 metres per real second with the normal 12x game clock. Delivery occurs only after route arrival while the original witness lives and the destination guard remains alive and posted outdoors. Death or a guard leaving the post cancels the attempt rather than inventing a report. Caller routes are copied; invalid endpoints and concurrent errands for one citizen are rejected, including on restore. 181 core tests and native Unity FullSerializer checks pass, including a large game epoch. Runtime route survey, actor placement, retries and delivery-triggered accusation/dispatch remain unwired. This core-only validation does not replace the installed bundle.

Live entry checkpoint: EntryProbe now passes within the actual-player HousingProbe. Controlled civilian and guard observers exercise native successful building transitions: a civilian observation creates saved evidence without an immediate charge; no observer creates no evidence; a direct guard observation assigns Trespassing and dispatches only that informed guard; an existing Murder charge is preserved. Explicit callback checks also verify pre-transition capture does not commit and cancellation discards pending evidence. Evidence: work/EntryProbe-pass.log. Native build and integration pass; installed development bundle still reports 0.3.13, SHA-256 A6747A50043B1A87512E0FA855602F2A901CC618CFC861C56046622E55601703. The observer positions are controlled fixtures, not an end-to-end lockpicking or natural patrol test. Citizen messenger travel/delivery, direct-guard arrival acceptance, permission exceptions and save/load transition acceptance remain pending. This supersedes the uninstalled-runtime statement below.

Development update: EntryAwareness now captures visible citizen/finite-guard witnesses before transition, discards failed transitions, and records the observation after successful entry. Direct guard witnesses can request finite dispatch. Unity compilation/native persistence checks and 178 core tests pass. This runtime path is not yet player-tested or installed; civilian report delivery and transition acceptance tests remain unfinished. The installed bundle remains 0.3.13.

Burglary ledger checkpoint: WorldState.EntryReports now saves a successful entry's household, time, citizen witnesses and informed guards. RecordEntry creates no incident for empty witness sets and rejects unknown/dead witnesses before mutation. Existing historical witnesses may later die without erasing evidence; only a living original citizen witness can inform a living guard. Snapshot/restore deep-copies and validates references transactionally. Entry identity uses whole game seconds to survive native JSON epoch rounding. 178 core tests and native FullSerializer entry round-trip pass. Runtime pre-entry capture, successful-transition commit, citizen travel/report delivery and accusation are still pending; playable 0.3.13 is unchanged by this core validation.

Menu control: Awareness / HouseholdLocks (default true) enables the household door override. Disabling returns native door access behavior without deleting population or saved routines. Build/native settings validation and existing-save housing/theft regression pass; work/AwarenessSettings-pass.log.

Review clarification: native DFU automatically unlocks any active residential quest site before evaluating routine hours. The new household override intentionally does not treat destination status alone as permission. A mapped, scheduled household can therefore remain locked while it is an active quest site; player-owned/faction exemptions and unknown quest-actor placement fallback still apply. Explicit access-grant semantics require further quest integration and acceptance testing. This is a visible compatibility limitation, not proof that all quest entry behavior is finished.

Witnessed-entry prerequisite: GuardRoster.DispatchBuilding now accepts an optional informed-guard set. A new core test proves that one informed guard responds while three uninformed guards remain posted, an empty set dispatches nobody, and the selected report survives validated copying. Core suite: 176/176. This is not yet connected to exterior entry witnesses. Source event order requires capturing sight before OnPreTransition unloads the exterior, then committing only on successful OnTransitionInterior; OnFailedTransition must discard the pending observation. Citizen reports also need persistence when no nearby guard can receive them.

Additional live acceptance: HousingProbe finds a living household whose deterministic schedules put every owner away during 09:00–18:00, advances to that time, and verifies both the occupancy query and actual native door are locked. Build and the extended actual-player probe pass; evidence work/HouseholdLocks-daytime-pass.log. Source inspection confirms successful native lockpicking proceeds to transition in the same activation, without a second occupancy query. Actual lockpicking and witnessed/unseen entry acceptance still require dedicated runtime tests.

Latest verified checkpoint: the occupancy query is now connected through NativeHouseholdDoorHooks to PlayerActivate.BuildingIsUnlocked. The actual-player HousingProbe verifies a residential family door unlocked while awake, locked at 02:00 while asleep, and unlocked again at 07:00. Native hook integration and 175 core tests pass; the development bundle is installed (still version 0.3.12). Log: work/HouseholdLocks-pass.log. This supersedes earlier statements that the query is unconnected. Ordinary generated homes are covered; owned homes and faction buildings retain native access, and unknown/quest-owned placement falls back to native behavior. Witnessed entry reporting, explicit access permissions and full quest-security arbitration remain unfinished. Native lockpicking/bashing behavior still applies; do not claim the complete burglary policy is implemented.

Latest user requirement, 14 October 2026: a house locks whenever no living, awake member of the owning household is inside. This includes an empty home and a home containing only sleeping residents, at any time of day. The routine lock releases when a resident inside wakes or an awake resident returns. For example, a father waking early releases the routine lock; when he leaves while everyone else sleeps, it locks again. This supersedes fixed night-to-morning routine locking and the earlier rule that any resident presence unlocks the house. Use authoritative scheduled/persistent location and sleep state, not whether an actor happens to be loaded or visible. Residents travelling home have not returned until they cross the home threshold. A family with no living members follows the separate vacancy/repopulation rules rather than unlocking because the household is empty.

Entering the locked, absent household is trespassing when a surrounding NPC witnesses the entry. Use nearby visibility and the bounded citizen report system, followed by finite guards travelling from actual positions. Do not grant guards omniscient knowledge or invent immediate responders. An unwitnessed entry remains unreported until independently discovered. Legitimate ownership or explicit access permission must be respected; merely having a quest in the building is not automatic permission.

Persist lock changes through transitions and save/load. Departure, arrival, sleeping and waking can change the routine lock independently of clock time. Preserve explicit security and quest locks, but replace fixed routine night locking with this occupancy rule. An awake resident does not automatically grant permission to enter. Distinguish resident access, player access, native quest-required locks and the mod's routine lock so native door state is not permanently corrupted.

Acceptance: all residents away at noon; only sleepers remain; first resident wakes; that resident leaves while others sleep; another wakes; awake resident returns; temporarily unloaded actors; player crosses town boundaries; reload while absent/asleep; witnessed versus unseen entry; nearby guard versus distant/no guard; pending alarm survives transition; player-owned home; explicit permission; quest destination; deceased family/vacancy.

Implementation checkpoint, 14 October 2026: HouseholdAccess.RoutineLocked now evaluates living owners against supplied authoritative activity/location snapshots. Three new tests cover sleep/wake, departure/return thresholds across distant days, and dead/vacant households; the full core suite passes 174/174. This is core policy only, not yet connected to native doors. Runtime integration must supply saved emergency/flight locations rather than blindly using the ordinary schedule. Native PlayerActivate.BuildingIsUnlocked also grants blanket access to active quest residences; distinguish actual permission before replacing routine locking, and preserve faction/owned-home rules. Witnessed entry reporting remains pending.

Further checkpoint: EffectiveState now uses active alarm locations and evaluates saved escape routes without mutating the saved actor. Tests cover the exact exit boundary and fighting residents being awake; core suite passes 175/175. StreetPopulation.HouseholdRoutineLocked supplies this policy from the current town's saved residents and reactions, returning unknown while schedules or quest-owned placements are unavailable. Unity/native validation passes. The query is not yet hooked into native door activation, so this does not claim playable routine locks or witnessed-entry reporting.

## Original: HOUSEHOLD-RECONCILIATION.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Household reconciliation and entrance navigation

## Verified 0.3.15 checkpoint, 14 October 2026

The three-bed residential fixture at Gothway Garden building 196864 contained two untouched generic native StaticNPCs as well as its three generated residents. These were extra occupants, not intentionally scheduled house guests. HousingProbe first failed the total occupancy check. HouseholdOccupants now suppresses unclaimed faction-zero, building-context static flats in ordinary mapped family residences while the authoritative saved family is represented. Active quest resources, actions, faction listeners and nonzero factions are excluded; shops, taverns, castles and guilds are outside this rule. Original objects and active states are restored on room change, Clear and destruction. This is a population reconciliation checkpoint, not completed original stationary appearance/identity conversion. That conversion must reuse family/service ownership without reintroducing duplicates.

Enter markers in this layout led the interior graph to the attic, placing awake residents at local y=4.2 while native downstairs occupants stood at y=1.0. Using a lower ray origin alone did not resolve it: the marker itself was upstairs. InteriorGeometry now tries the actual interior exit door and inward normal before enter-marker fallbacks, following the native player's landing method. The live fixture has three residents at local y=1.0 and a validated route from each to the exit. Full navigation across stacked storeys/stairs remains unfinished; this check establishes correct entrance-floor placement, not a complete multilevel solver.

The female family member Elyzyssa Hawkcroft opens native dialogue with female portrait record 33; a rendered screenshot was inspected. The exact target in the user's original female-flat/male-portrait pair was not conclusively identified. Do not treat this one-person check as exhaustive portrait/activation coverage. Artifact: the local test player's `LivingCities-female-dialogue.png` under the standard Unity persistent-data directory.

## Reporting and controls

Managed generated civilian weapon hits no longer fall back to an immediate guard report because an escape path is unavailable. Active indoor direct witnesses retry route preparation every half real second. InteriorEscapeProbe removes the graph for the native hit, proves no guard report, restores it, then verifies autonomous preparation, physical escape, delayed finite response and real save/load. Finite guards now default on in the supported town; explicit user settings can still disable them. Disabled or unsupported guard behavior remains native.

An active guarded crime scene uses the agreed ten-second warning instead of the family lock/burglary rule. It releases the routine lock during the one-hour investigation window. CrimeSceneProbe verifies re-entry warning, subsequent Trespassing, native surrender, save/reload and expiry. Existing crimes retain their normal arrest behavior.

The local portable player used `WeaponSwingMode = 0`, the native hold-and-drag mode. It was changed to mode 1, native click-to-attack, for the user's testing. This is a local preference change and is not shipped as an engine or mod input patch. Hardware click responsiveness remains a manual acceptance check.

Evidence: 182 core tests; native build/integration; `work/HouseholdOccupants-0315-pass.log`, `work/EscapeRetry-pass.log`, and `work/CrimeScene-0315-pass.log`. Focused read-only review found no confirmed important defect in reconciliation, restoration and retry behavior. Broader quest/access and stationary-service acceptance remain open.

<a id="source-indoor-witnesses-md"></a>

## Original: INDOOR-WITNESSES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Indoor witness escape and reporting

This implements the already approved physical civilian flight/reporting behavior. A direct fleeing generated witness uses a collision-checked interior route to the entry landing, followed by a previously surveyed exterior route to a living posted guard. Preserve identity, game-time progress and the original crime building across save/load. Dispatch the existing finite roster only after a living witness reaches a guard; never infer the player's new location from the report.

The current entry-connected interior survey becomes a navigation tree. It provides routes back to the doorway, rejecting unsupported floors, blocked segments and disconnected rooms. Missing routes retain existing conservative room retreat and indoor native alarm behavior. Unwitnessed murders retain that existing behavior until the separate discovery/occupation system replaces it.

Implementation sequence:

1. Add and test a serializable civilian escape plan with absolute timing, interior and exterior coordinates, and dead-witness suppression.
2. Retain the interior survey's parent links and expose validated exit paths.
3. Connect direct witnesses, delayed guard reporting and scene-specific actor placement. Use cached exterior paths while inside.
4. Add an actual-player probe: native kill, no premature response, physical interior escape, reload continuity, delayed guard report and original building destination. Re-run existing civilian and building guard regressions.

The first implementation uses entry-connected floors and existing sprites. Multi-level disconnected spaces, movable obstructions, original stationary NPCs, and corpse discovery remain explicit follow-up work. Do not claim all-interior coverage from one test house.

## Implemented behavior (0.3.9)

Direct fleeing witnesses receive a persistent `CivilianEscape` containing interior XYZ waypoints, an exterior route to a living posted guard, and absolute departure time. The scene survey retains its navigation tree; exit paths follow its checked segments. Outdoor paths reverse the existing guard-post-to-building survey. Actors retain their identity across the door transition. The same timeline continues while that scene is unloaded and after a native save/load. Dead witnesses do not move or report. Witnesses wait at the post once they arrive; automatic return home after the incident expires remains a later refinement.

For generated indoor victims with a valid fleeing witness plan, the scoped weapon hook suppresses the immediate native alarm. Reports use the original building key and existing finite roster. All other indoor alarm cases keep the previous behavior, including unwitnessed kills and missing routes. This is deliberately not the finished corpse discovery policy. A busy or dead destination guard cannot receive the report; the witness waits for an available guard at that post during the remaining reporting window. Retargeting another post is not implemented.

The base alarm window stays 900 game seconds. An escape plan extends effective activity through its arrival plus 300 seconds, preventing a distant guard route from expiring halfway there. The actual test house required about 916 game seconds. Validated copies cache route lengths; ordinary alarm queries do not repeatedly allocate street-path arrays.

Core coverage: interior/exterior interpolation and timing, defensive copies, native-data validation, persisted progress, dead witness suppression, long journeys and expiry. The actual-player probe rejects a temporary overlapping obstacle, uses a native weapon kill in house 1, checks no immediate response, observes interior movement, saves/reloads SAVE32, follows the same named NPC outside, observes exterior movement, and verifies a bounded report to the original building. Travel deadlines are accelerated in the test; this is not a full real-time benchmark or a multi-layout certification.

Outstanding: stair/door coverage, dynamic obstructions, alternative guard targets, catch-up of newly dispatched guard reports after a whole-town absence, return-to-schedule transitions, unwitnessed discovery, one-hour crime-scene occupation, original stationary actors and remaining guard behaviors. No new art was generated.

## Verified release evidence

The final 0.3.9 bundle passes 154/154 core tests, native build/settings/serializer checks, and separate actual-player InteriorEscapeProbe, ResponseProbe, GuardBuildingProbe and GuardWitnessProbe runs. Logs are `work/<ProbeName>-039-pass.log`. The indoor escape probe writes SAVE32; existing regression probes update only their dedicated SAVE29/SAVE31 fixtures. A test harness race was fixed by waiting for the owned player process to exit before starting the next probe. Read-only Luna/high review identified a malformed-save gap for a fighting escape witness; restore now rejects that state, with a regression assertion. Upstream tracked source remains unchanged.

Installed bundle SHA-256: `C6A5D3105F03CAA727D0C205A99A6E25C6F81CE4BFACCFEE0C23748CCEB01283`. The full mod goal remains active.
