# Installation and local dialogue

Status checked 17 October 2026. This repository is a development source snapshot. There is currently **no published release bundle or complete end-user installer**. `StartLocalDialogue.ps1` starts an already-installed server; it does not download the model, install CUDA libraries or install the mod.

## Daggerfall Unity mod

The tested project targets **Daggerfall Unity 1.1.1**. For source builds, see [repository setup](DEVELOPMENT.md#repository) and [integration notes](DEVELOPMENT.md#integration); those need a separate DFU source tree, Unity editor and build dependencies. Downloading this repository does not produce a playable installation by itself.

When a built `livingcities.dfmod` is available, close DFU, place it in the player's `DaggerfallUnity_Data/StreamingAssets/Mods` directory and enable **People of the Iliac Bay** in the mod list. The internal filename and GUID remain stable despite the public rename. Back up your saves and existing bundle before an update; use dedicated test saves during development.

Install third-party mods separately, following their authors' requirements. The local integration set and explicit limitations are recorded in [external requirements](COMPATIBILITY.md#requirements), [external audit](COMPATIBILITY.md#external-mods) and [tested local order](EXTERNAL-MOD-LOAD-ORDER.txt). These are development evidence, not a promise that arbitrary versions work together. Changed interior layouts require a restart; save outdoors before changing those mods.

## Optional local LLM: what is actually used

The tested local setup uses:

| Component | Recorded configuration |
| --- | --- |
| Runtime | Prebuilt Windows x64 llama.cpp, build `b11680` |
| GPU backend | NVIDIA CUDA 12.4 runtime package and matching DLL package |
| Model | Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, GGUF `Q4_K_M`, two shards |
| Endpoint | `http://127.0.0.1:8080` |
| Launcher defaults | Context 4096, GPU layers 99, one parallel request |
| Reference machine | Windows, RTX 4070 SUPER with 12 GiB VRAM, 64 GiB system RAM |

Official downloads: [llama.cpp b11680 release](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases/tag/b11680) and [Qwen's Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct GGUF repository](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-GGUF). These are the recorded runtime/model choices, not a claim that the newest available versions were tested.

The local runtime archives are `llama-b11680-bin-win-cuda-12.4-x64.zip` and `cudart-llama-bin-win-cuda-12.4-x64.zip`. Extract the server and matching dependencies into the same `bin` directory. Retain the runtime's DLLs, including its CUDA/cuBLAS and GGML dependencies. This prebuilt arrangement does not require Python or a Python inference framework.

## Manual folder layout and startup

The current launcher expects `local-llm` alongside the `LivingCities` source folder. If you clone using another directory name, the launcher still resolves `local-llm` from the repository's parent directory.

```text
DFU/
  LivingCities/
    tools/StartLocalDialogue.ps1
  Player/
    DaggerfallUnity.exe
  local-llm/
    bin/
      llama-server.exe
      ...matching runtime DLLs...
    models/
      qwen2.5-7b-instruct-q4_k_m-00001-of-00002.gguf
      qwen2.5-7b-instruct-q4_k_m-00002-of-00002.gguf
```

Download **both** matching model shards into `models`. Point the server at the first shard as the launcher does; keep the second alongside it. Do not rename unrelated quantizations to match these filenames. The recorded shards total approximately 4.68 GB on disk; disk size is not the GPU memory requirement.

From the repository directory in PowerShell:

```powershell
.\tools\StartLocalDialogue.ps1
```

The server occupies that console while running. From a second console, check readiness:

```powershell
Invoke-RestMethod -Uri 'http://127.0.0.1:8080/health'
```

Enable the mod's free-text dialogue option and use the matching local endpoint. `EnableFreeText` and `PrewarmModel` are the current dialogue controls. Prewarming sends a background request to an available server; **it does not launch or install that server**. Leave the server running while using generated dialogue; stop the console process when finished.

To use native conversation without generated chat, turn off free text and prewarming. The requested complete optional installer and broader chapter-by-chapter module isolation are future work, not provided by these two settings alone. A model server is not a game-content authority: game state, allowed facts, prices and actions remain controlled by the mod/native systems.

## Hardware and prerequisites: measured versus unknown

The reference GPU has 12 GiB VRAM. Earlier local model testing recorded roughly 6.3 GiB GPU use at a 4096-token context, but graphics/mods/context length add their own demand. **A minimum supported VRAM or RAM value has not been established.** Do not interpret the reference machine's capacity as a verified minimum, or model size as enough free VRAM for the game and inference together.

Only the Windows/NVIDIA/CUDA setup above has been exercised here. CPU inference, AMD/Intel GPUs and other operating systems are not certified by this project. Other llama.cpp backends may exist, but their availability does not establish mod integration or acceptable performance.

Player-side use of this prebuilt server needs **no Python version**. The .NET 8 SDK is for source/core-test development; Unity 2019.4.40f1 is for runtime builds. Neither is an LLM Python environment. If a future bootstrap uses Python, its exact supported interpreter and dependency versions must be specified and clean-machine tested before release.

## Installation work still in the backlog

- Publish a versioned mod bundle and tested dependency list with hashes.
- Supply an optional runtime/model bootstrap or packaged launcher with consent, checksums, resumable downloads, prerequisite checks, health checks and update/removal behavior.
- Keep the no-LLM path free of model/CUDA downloads and background processes.
- Measure minimum/recommended combined game/inference hardware and test failures such as missing DLLs, insufficient VRAM, occupied port and missing model shard.
- Document clean-machine validation and module-toggle/save migration behavior.

See the [roadmap](ROADMAP.md) for priorities. None of these installer features is claimed as implemented by this guide.

<a id="source-integration-md"></a>

## Original: INTEGRATION.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# DFU integration evidence

Target inspected: upstream `v1.1.1-cve-2025`, commit `fb2627082fabe473bf108c816263b71c6e26ace6`, Unity 2019.4.40f1. Paths below are relative to the upstream checkout.

## Available interfaces

| Concern | Evidence | Implication |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mod lifecycle and saves | `Assets/Game/Addons/ModSupport/ModTypes.cs`, `IHasModSaveData`; `SaveLoadManager.cs` save/load loops | Standard mod can own versioned population data without adding fields to vanilla saves. DFU catches individual mod failures. |
| Failed loads | `SaveLoadManager.cs` raises `OnStartLoad` before deserializing mod data | Suspend before loading, resume only after a valid restore; otherwise data from the previous character could remain active. |
| New characters | `StartGameBehaviour.OnNewGame` | Clear population and select a new persisted world seed. |
| Buildings | `BuildingDirectory.GetBuildingsOfType`, `GetBuildingSummary`; `StreamingWorld.GetCurrentBuildingDirectory` | Stable map/building keys available. StreamingWorld rejects mismatched map IDs while loading. |
| Streets | `CityNavigation` nav grid, conversion methods, `GetNavGridWeightLocal` | Feed walkability and road costs into a bounded CPU pathfinder. Conversion includes origin and coordinate conventions requiring in-world tests. |
| Mobile people | `PopulationManager.MobileNPCGenerator`, `OnMobileNPCCreate/Enable/Disable`, `PopulationPool` | Actor integration possible, but vanilla pooling randomizes/recycles citizens; lifecycle events alone do not establish persistent identities. |
| Quest people | `StaticNPC.AssignQuestResourceBehaviour`, `Data` fields and quest resource attachment | Original-object movement, native quest clicks, hide/show and reconstructed save/load passed the runtime probe. Cross-building proxies and broad compatibility remain unverified; see `QUEST-IDENTITY.md`. |
| Conversation UI | `UIWindowFactory.RegisterCustomUIWindow(UIWindowType.Talk, Type)` | Talk window extension is available. Other mods may replace the same window, so coexistence needs explicit checks. |
| Awareness | `EnemySenses.StealthCheck`, `FormulaHelper.CalculateStealthChance`, existing player crouch | Extend existing stealth semantics; avoid an unrelated second player skill. |
| Basic Roads | Built 1.3.1 source exposes `getPathData`, `getRoadPoint`, `getTrackPoint`, `getPathsPoint` messages | Wilderness path data available. Installed coexistence and actual NPC road following are separate acceptance checks. |

## Finite guard interception: feasibility passed, behavior pending

`PlayerEntity.SpawnCityGuards(bool)` (line 621) and `SpawnCityGuard(Vector3, Vector3)` (line 747) are public, non-virtual methods. No dedicated cancellation event was found in those methods. Subclass overrides cannot replace these calls.

The immediate interior branch creates 2–5 guards at a door. Exterior branches convert pooled people, and a fallback directly creates a foe spawner. Intercepting only `SpawnCityGuard` would miss that fallback.

Callers include `PlayerActivate`, `WeaponManager`, `DaggerfallEntityBehaviour`, inventory/trade/rest windows, player crime checks, and the quest `SpawnCityGuards` action. Some callers use the returned guard object. A replacement must honor return expectations and quest behavior while dispatching a finite roster. Deleting guards after creation does not satisfy the requirement.

A temporary precompiled standard mod using Harmony 2.3.6 intercepted both entry points in the shipped Mono player. The probe verified immediate/deferred batch calls, a supplied return object for the single-guard call, and removal of its own patches. It then stopped its owned process, and its temporary package and Unity staging were removed. Evidence: `work/guard-probe-player.log`; build log: sibling `../work/guard-probe-build.log`. Source and constraints: `probes/guard`.

This resolves basic runtime interception feasibility on this pinned Windows player. It does not prove the roster implementation, quest callers, arrest compatibility, other patching mods, or other platforms. No guard patches are installed by the main mod yet. Preserve vanilla fallback outside supported settlements in the eventual integration.

DFU's runtime source compiler excludes references to precompiled assemblies loaded from byte arrays because they have no file location. The Harmony-referencing probe therefore ships as a precompiled DLL alongside Harmony, both in the mod bundle as `.dll.bytes`. This avoids adding DLLs to the player installation's Managed directory or modifying the engine.

## Foundation limitations

The quest identity baseline probe now passes in the shipped player. A synthetic quest bound to an original tavern NPC retains UID/resource/data/name, receives native click tasks after movement and reload, and honors hide/show. Runtime reconstruction is checked with a new instance ID and exactly one matching NPC. `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -QuestProbe` reproduces it. The helper now follows native building discovery/entry setup, fixing previously hidden vanilla occupants in scripted test entries. This is not full service, main-quest or cross-location compatibility. Detailed evidence and constraints: `QUEST-IDENTITY.md`.

Version 0.3.0 supersedes the demographic parts of the historical 0.2.0 checkpoint below. It adds housing-aware families, schema-3 migration and home-only child flats in the same pilot town. Children receive a brief greeting; this is the only shipped dialogue customization. Housing profiles count actual bed models 41000–41002 and derive an exterior footprint preference without opening interiors. These hints do not establish ownership or sleeping anchors.

Two runtime findings have regression coverage. The shipped mod compiler treats the readonly-reference parameter of `MapsFile.GetRmbBlockName` differently from the editor compiler; `HousingSurvey` resolves the method once and invokes it once per cached block, avoiding source compilation failure. `TalkManager` retains its last mobile target during later static conversations, so the child talk hook checks `CurrentNPCType.Mobile` before intercepting. The actual player passed family save/load, child greeting and stale-target checks. See `TEST-FIXTURES.md` for fixtures and boundaries.

Version 0.2.0 connects scheduled adult street visits and interior presence in Gothway Garden. Other towns retain vanilla physical populations. F8 reports household/schedule/actor counts. Saved routes avoid relying on `CityNavigation.Start` or exterior physics during indoor save loads; schema 1 migrates to schema 2 without changing residents. Exact door, animation, identity and death-save evidence is recorded in `TEST-FIXTURES.md`.

House types 1–6 are candidates; explicit `HouseForSale` types are excluded. Dynamically listed properties, player purchases, beds, existing permanent occupants, and quest reservations still require allocation rules before broad physical population replacement. The pilot allocation must not be mistaken for a complete housing census. Existing stationary quest/service NPCs are retained; replacing them without duplicate identities remains work.

The adult preview uses vanilla mobile sprite assets with stable names and identities; home-only children use original child flats. No changes to vanilla guard spawning, enemy behavior, crime or quest actors are shipped yet. Current indoor residents stand rather than use furniture. Full interior navigation, corner smoothing, actor avoidance and radius behavior across loaded map boundaries remain unfinished.

## Original: LEGITIMATE-DEFENSE.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Legitimate defense and loot rights

## Current welfare checkpoint, 15 October 2026

Outdoor welfare now passes through the running guard controller (`work/OutdoorWelfare-pass.log`, build `work/OutdoorWelfare-build.log`). After native enemy/guard save-load and actual guard damage, the player kills the hostile creature using native sourced damage. The responder witnesses the kill, walks from combat position toward the injured player, opens courtesy and consumes a decline without charging the player or giving a potion. The existing probe then verifies return to post, preserved prior crime, silent creature defense, quest-tagged human defense and player-assault takeover. This is a controlled Gothway street encounter with surveyed player placement; it does not establish dense-crowd, blocked-route or every-settlement acceptance. It supersedes the entirely-unverified outdoor approach gap below.

Civilian witness contact is now connected to welfare evaluation. A guard already assigned to the exact threat can learn the player's participation from a recorded, recent, living and awake civilian witness within four units and clear sight in the same scene. Generated residents and registered native stationary people are supported; observing a corpse does not create fight evidence. Contact attempts are throttled per guard to four per real second and stop for expired/consumed opportunities. No extra guards are dispatched. The native controlled contact fixture verifies nearby success and rejection of distant, dead and other-room witnesses, followed by approach/recreation/dialogue/potion/save regressions (`work/DefenseRelay-pass.log`, build `work/DefenseRelay-build.log`; missing adapter reproduced in `work/DefenseRelay-red.log`). This covers contact with an existing responder after death, not a complete fleeing civilian journey or cross-interior messenger relay. Sleeping/native-stationary contact branches and obstructed contact still need dedicated live acceptance.

Actual save-slot loading during the integrated indoor approach now passes (`work/WelfareSlot-pass.log`, build `work/WelfareSlot-build.log`). After the real responder starts moving toward the injured player, `WelfareProbe` writes and loads the dedicated character's `Living Cities - Welfare approach` slot, reacquires the restored store and same guard identity, verifies the unconsumed/unexpired opportunity and prior Theft, then lets normal controller updates finish welfare and open native surrender. This closes the specific real-slot gap below. Outdoor approaches, broader layouts, civilian witness relay and dynamically blocked routes remain open.

Native serialization during a moving welfare approach now passes (`work/WelfareRecreate-pass.log`, build `work/WelfareRecreate-build.log`). The probe captures the real actor after it moves, serializes the entire isolated WorldState through DFU's serializer, destroys the actor, restores a new store and recreates that same guard. It checks position and eligibility before allowing the actual controller approach to finish. A second round trip after accepting a potion preserves consumption/reward state and refuses duplicate completion. This verifies serialized reconstruction, not a real save-slot load or transition mid-approach. The test runner requires the recreation marker so this check cannot silently disappear.

The two-responder regression now passes (`work/WelfareCompanion-pass.log`). Previously, an uninformed companion could clear the shared threat report for prior-crime handling before the informed guard offered welfare. Companions now preserve that report while an eligible responder can approach. Failed routes are remembered per guard and player destination, allowing normal handling when nobody can reach the player and a retry after the player moves. The native probe verifies companion coordination, release after health recovers above the threshold, physical approach, dialogue, potion inventory and preserved prior crime. This supersedes the unchecked simultaneous-responder case below; broader multi-guard layouts remain unverified.

The integrated shop encounter now also passes (`work/WelfareChain-pass.log`, build `work/WelfareChain-build.log`, command `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -WelfareProbe`). A real registered keeper flees and reports the spawned hostile, the finite roster dispatches a guard who enters and damages it, the native sourced player-damage path kills it while the guard witnesses the fight, and the actual running controller offers welfare. Declining preserves Theft, then native EnemyAttack opens its surrender prompt. No separate ledger/controller or forced welfare call is used in this probe. The fixture still controls enemy placement/hostility, advances travel time and positions the player on a surveyed visible spot; this proves the integrated path, not arbitrary quests or layouts. Multiple simultaneous responders, dynamic blocked approaches, outdoor welfare and mid-approach reload remain to be checked.

The native guard controller now attempts courtesy after its reported enemy dies. Core eligibility is shared by approach and completion: living player below66%, same incident scene, unexpired witnessed lawful defense and a living guard still assigned to that exact threat report. No new courtesy guards are dispatched. The guard disables combat while following surveyed, collision-aware indoor or outdoor routes, stops within2.5 units with sight, and opens a native message box. Unreachable routes fall back to normal return handling; exhausted routes can be recalculated after the player moves. Routes are scoped to the incident and walking animation is not restarted every frame.

Closing the exchange consumes its saved opportunity once. Accepting a preselected offer adds one native Healing potion (DFU recipe4975678); declining does not. The player's crime is never cleared. A remaining crime hands the report back to existing native surrender handling. An actual player assault on the approaching guard immediately cancels courtesy for Assault handling.

Evidence: core310/310 (`work/WelfareApproach-core-green.log`), Unity native serialization/build (`work/WelfareApproach-final-build.log`), controlled indoor native approach/dialogue/potion/assault fixture plus combat, loot and real slot reload (`work/WelfareApproach-final-pass.log`). The fixture uses a separate ledger and controller with a surveyed starting point, calls the controller's actual welfare routine, operates the native message-box callback and verifies real inventory including existing potion stacks. It verifies preserved Theft and the report's crime handoff, not the subsequent surrender popup itself. Civilian flight, actual keeper-to-guard reporting, guard arrival/combat, death stand-down and effect persistence also pass (`work/WelfareApproach-citizen-pass.log`).

Remaining acceptance: a natural player-fought encounter through the complete witness/dispatch/death chain, civilian witness relay into the defense ledger, outdoor welfare movement, unavailable-route layouts, mid-approach reload and the complete post-dialogue surrender interaction. Broader corpse/provenance and scene-security work remains open. This checkpoint supersedes older statements below that welfare was entirely unconnected.

User requirement, 14 October 2026: killing a genuine hostile attacker, such as an attacking burglar, assassin, thief or creature, is not Murder, indoors or outdoors in a settlement. Guards still attend to secure the scene. If the player was seen fighting or killing the attacker, guards approach to ask whether the player is all right, sometimes offer a healing potion, and explain that the defeated attacker's belongings may be taken.

Loot rights apply immediately to a legitimate-defense kill. The player does not have to wait for a guard to arrive or grant verbal permission. Taking those belongings is not Theft, whether observed or not. Save the entitlement with the victim/corpse identity so transitions and reloads preserve it. Do not clear an already-earned entitlement merely because witnesses leave or die.

Existing bounties and other known crimes survive lawful self-defense. When the situational welfare check is available, guards complete that brief exchange, recognize the wanted player and then pursue the applicable outstanding charges through normal surrender/arrest. The courtesy dialogue does not pardon crimes, clear regional reputation/bounty state, grant temporary immunity beyond the exchange, or convert the lawful defense into Murder. If the courtesy window has expired, existing crime enforcement proceeds normally without a delayed welfare exchange. Corpse loot entitlement remains valid even if the player is wanted for an unrelated offense.

Classify the incident from combat provenance: a genuine attacker is distinct from a civilian who turned hostile because the player attacked them, killed a relative or committed another crime. Provoking a civilian into defense must not make killing them lawful or grant loot rights. Background enemy hostility and the initiator of combat must survive the handoff from stationary to mobile actors and save/load. Do not infer a criminal profession from appearance or faction alone.

The welfare dialogue requires a witness who actually saw the fight, including guards with line of sight. Body discovery alone can authorize scene security but cannot invent a witnessed heroic act. Make healing-potion offers occasional, bounded and persistent per incident so repeated activation/reloads cannot farm gifts. Legitimate-defense scenes should not use unidentified-visitor trespass warnings against the player while the player is cooperating with scene security or taking entitled loot.

The user explicitly requires a situational approach within the next few minutes, never hours or days later. Use a five-in-game-minute default expiry from the end of the fight, configurable when the feature is implemented. Leaving the local incident area expires the welfare opportunity sooner. Store the absolute expiry and consumed/expired state so unloading, reloads and returning cannot queue a delayed greeting or potion offer. Normal scene security may continue after this window; earned loot rights do not expire with the courtesy dialogue.

Native combat/corpse adapters are partially connected as described below; welfare remains pending. There is no separate random-incursion spawner. The generated-resident corpse pipeline covers civilian violence and has no loot inventory. Native enemy combat remains outside that generated-resident interception. Full acceptance must cover unprovoked attackers, player-provoked civilians, indoor/outdoor scenes, witnessed/unwitnessed defense, immediate looting before guard arrival, innocent passersby, potion limits and save/load.

User clarification, 15 October 2026: the welfare exchange is performed by guards who came to fight this particular threat. Do not dispatch separate courtesy responders or select an unrelated nearby guard. Existing threat-response membership must be checked in addition to the saved witness, timing and availability requirements. If no responding guard can complete the approach within the window, courtesy expires without a gift.

The player's current health must be strictly below 66% and above zero for welfare. The user's correction supersedes 75%. This gates the entire welfare exchange, including any potion, without changing loot rights or outstanding crimes. The core completion API now requires an explicit health fraction and checks the guard's current threat report and membership; unrelated, returned or reassigned guards cannot complete it. Physical approach and UI must use these same conditions before greeting, then recheck at completion.

Presence expiry is now connected to the runtime: every quarter-second, pending courtesy checks the player's settlement, interior identity and distance from the incident (40 world units). Native pre-transition events expire the opportunity before leaving a scene; save loading is excluded from observation. Departure never revokes loot entitlement, and returning cannot renew courtesy. Core tests cover scene, distance, exact deadline and reload (307/307, `work/WelfarePresence-core-green.log`). The native probe verifies same-room retention, departure, return and retained loot rights (`work/WelfarePresence-pass.log`), alongside the existing combat/loot/save probes. The pre-transition subscription is implemented but not separately exercised by this new probe. Physical approaches, responder membership enforcement and welfare dialogue are still pending.

## Native adapter checkpoint, 15 October 2026

The development adapter now observes native weapon attempts and sourced health damage before hostility changes, and binds confirmed deaths to the actual native corpse LoadID. A live shop probe covers hostile, passive and player-allied targets, lethal native longsword attacks, existing Theft retention and native JSON roundtrip. Visible observers require sight of both the player and target; invisible players cannot gain identified fight witnesses. Observer failures are logged without replacing native combat exceptions; an injected death-observer failure and a visible/invisible witness control both passed after reproducing their failures. Evidence: `work/NativeDefense-safety-pass.log`, `work/NativeDefense-safety-build.log`; core remains305/305 (`work/NativeDefense-core.log`).

Disintegrate now has an explicit source-aware hook because its native damage round calls SetHealth directly. Native tests distinguish player casts against hostile/passive targets from unsourced deaths. Native melee and sourced attacks on the player supply unprovoked-attack evidence for previously passive enemies, preserving their original origin. Player-provoked retaliation remains unlawful. A scripted former ally retaining its team flag qualifies only after recorded actual aggression. Tests exercise these native paths rather than treating a hostility toggle alone as attack evidence.

The live probe also transfers an item from the victim's inventory through its actual native corpse and the inventory-window pickup handler before guards arrive. A dedicated `Living Cities - lawful defense loot` slot under the test character verifies the same corpse LoadID, remaining inventory, entitlement and prior Theft survive real save/load; pickup still succeeds after reload. Existing native enemy loot remains native inventory, with no duplicate inventory or required guard permission. Evidence: `work/NativeDefense-spells-final-pass.log`, `work/NativeDefense-spells-final-build.log`;305/305 core pass (`work/NativeDefense-spells-core.log`).

This is partial Stage2 integration, not completed lawful-defense gameplay. Target-owned scene/building context and cross-settlement tracking remain incomplete; changed settlements are skipped instead of rewriting provenance. Broader spell/mod coverage, outdoor acceptance, indirect group provocation, physical scene security, welfare dialogue, potion delivery and post-courtesy bounty enforcement remain pending. The native attack probe validates the event path used by quest-activated enemies, not every quest package.

### Target scene ownership follow-up

Death coordinates now use the victim's owning interior or exterior building directory, including inactive parents. Restored interiors can have no exterior DoorOwner; their exact canonical DFU scene key supplies the saved map/building identity instead. An unowned or unrecognized scene is not assigned the player's current location. A controlled relocation probe keeps the player in the original room while moving the victim to another interior: death coordinates and the native corpse parent follow the victim. A separate inactive exterior owner produces an exterior death with no indoor-player witnesses. These checks, native combat, inventory pickup and actual slot reload pass together in `work/NativeDefense-flight-origin-pass.log` after `work/NativeDefense-flight-origin-build.log`.

This closes the basic target-coordinate gap, not all transitions. Interior-to-interior corpse parenting is corrected; cross-world-context corpse creation/tracking, cross-settlement deaths and explicit ownership for unparented mod actors still need work.

Observers now also require their own scene to match the player's scene and settlement before line-of-sight checks. A native fixture reproduced a false witness by moving an identified NPC into another active interior at the same world coordinates (`work/NativeDefense-witness-scene-red.log`). The corrected build rejects that observer while retaining the visible same-room control and invisible-player exclusion. Combined combat attribution, corpse pickup and actual save-slot reload pass in `work/NativeDefense-witness-scene-pass.log`; build evidence is `work/NativeDefense-witness-scene-build.log`.

The extended run exposed `Escape must start at witness position` from resident escape planning. The route used the rendered actor position while `CivilianEscape` validates against authoritative saved alarm coordinates. Resident planning now uses the same saved coordinates, matching native-keeper planning. The failing run is `work/NativeDefense-flight-origin-red.log`; the corrected combined run is `work/NativeDefense-flight-origin-pass.log`.

Keeper escape/reporting, finite guard arrival and combat, assault/death handoff, effect recreation/expiry and outdoor citizen flight also pass on this build (`work/NativeDefense-scene-citizen-pass.log`). Installed0.3.34 SHA-256: `E970AC27BAAE5A6D74E4DC6316436A51E0E7DA0A48AD9385CFEBE41A799BB066`.

With the adapter active, the native civilian-flight regression also passes: keeper escape and physical reporting, finite indoor arrival/combat, player-assault handoff, attacker-death return, guard effect recreation and elapsed-time expiry (`work/NativeDefense-citizen-pass.log`).

Final native doorway regression passes with the expanded hooks: the same wounded guard preserves its player-cast effect through save/load and shop exit, then physically pursues outside (`work/NativeDefense-door-regression-pass.log`). Installed development0.3.34 SHA-256: `77087CDDBB2BA33181FA176DEB3F7AFDCF5113D2D65D90C439ABA252611CE163`.

## Earlier persistent core checkpoint, 15 October 2026

Schema7 adds a saved defense ledger. Schemas1–6 migrate with an empty ledger, preserving existing populations and guards. Core operations retain original origin, player participation and provocation, actual fight-witness observations, death location, one corpse binding, informed guards, loot entitlement, a fixed courtesy deadline and consumed gift state. Duplicate death callbacks cannot move the deadline or reroll the gift; departure or exact deadline expires courtesy without revoking loot. A guard may finish a witnessed player fight without inventing a player kill.

A separate authoritative unprovoked-attack observation supports quest/script activation of a previously passive enemy without rewriting its original origin. It cannot pardon provocation or turn civilian retaliation into lawful defense. Witness calls must mean that the native adapter actually observed the player's participation; body discovery or seeing guards fight is insufficient.

305/305 core tests pass (`work/LawfulDefense-final-core.log`), following12 initially failing contracts and a separate failing transition case. Native DFU JSON verifies large game timestamps, original passive identity and subsequent attack, corpse entitlement, absolute expiry, and consumed gifts. Existing dedicated shop save migration, native effect reload and physical doorway pursuit pass (`work/LawfulDefense-migration-pass.log`). Build evidence: `work/LawfulDefense-native-final-build.log` and the `LIVING_CITIES_DEFENSE_LEDGER_SERIALIZATION_PASS` marker.

At this earlier checkpoint the core was not connected to gameplay hit/death/loot events. The native adapter section above supersedes that limitation for its verified paths; welfare remains unconnected. The core itself does not award inventory items or alter player crimes.

Installed development0.3.34 bundle SHA-256: `0B32E17C6F5907A99C49FFED9BE74A72392BEF3567B47618B398947FDE470276`.

Like the existing world/guard records, ledger records are mutable objects for trusted mod code; adapters must use the mutation methods to preserve provenance. Restore checks structural and derived-state consistency, not authenticity against deliberate save editing. Observer sight and physical contact are authoritative adapter inputs, not inferred from timestamps by the core.
# Welfare recognition update — 15 October 2026

The `Living Cities - Welfare approach` test save deliberately includes outstanding Theft to verify courtesy followed by native surrender. Lawful self-defense does not create that charge. Guards now interrupt the welfare greeting with "Wait, don't I know your face?" when an existing crime is present, then resume native surrender when the message closes. This recognition branch does not offer or grant a healing potion. Innocent-player dialogue retains the explicit optional potion question; eligibility requires living player health strictly below 66%, with a 20% offer chance.

Verification: 332/332 core tests, Unity build/install, native welfare encounter with actual save/reload and surrender (`work/WelfareRecognition-pass.log`), and native defense ledger/recognition inventory regression (`work/WelfareRecognition-ledger-pass.log`). Earlier potion-grant tests remain historical evidence; this latest recognition test asserts no reward and no duplicate exchange after serialization.

<a id="source-milestones-md"></a>

## Original: MILESTONES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Living Cities milestones

## Consolidated future delivery order — 17 October 2026

Use the [TL;DR roadmap](ROADMAP.md) and [phased plan](ROADMAP.md#delivery-plan) as current planning entry points. The [design/backlog](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design) records housing-first authored actors, market visits, shop tenants/keeper commutes, mobile inn visitors, limited shared beds, native quest capability gates and spoiler-safe story personalities. Invitations, human stats, recruitable nonquest companions, inventory/progression/action adapters and persistent shared-experience memory are recorded but shelved. Existing requirements below remain in scope; dated checkpoints are historical evidence. Installer and modular opt-out requirements are explicit, with current limitations in [installation](INSTALLATION.md).

This consolidation implements no gameplay. The subsequent user request separately authorizes the 2× clock fix and rebuild; other future slices await selection.

## End-of-session requirements checkpoint — 17 October

Final build installed: SHA-256 `09B32F751F2549743BCE6D664727900B9174442E84A36DC13CFC947B969ADCBB`. Core evidence: 630/630 (`work/final-disposition-core.log`); Unity build and integration passed (`work/final-disposition-build.log`). `work/final-disposition-native-check.log` contains passes for upper-panel editing, actual model replies, grounded map requests, household container consent, inn quote/payment/refusal, owned-place information refusal, physical outdoor complaint delivery and finite indoor dispatch preserving Theft. The strict runner still exits 1: after the terminal pass marker, `FlowerDeActivatorMod.FlowerDeActivator.TeleportPlayerToEnterDoorOrMarkerAfterDelay` throws `NullReferenceException` during the final exterior transition. Track this external-mod transition interaction for investigation; do not describe the entire run as clean. Native sale, repair and each individual guild purchase/service still need live transaction acceptance. No new deferred feature work starts tonight.

This checkpoint preserves the latest requested scope. An entry being recorded is not proof of implementation or live acceptance; use the dated evidence in the linked documents.

- **Commerce, current build:** personal disposition must affect everyone who buys or sells products or provides paid services, including shop buying/selling, repairs, inn food/rooms, guild potions, spells, training, cures, identification, enchanting, spellmaking and summoning. Purchase/service endpoints are +50% at -100 and -15% at +100; sale proceeds are -50% at -100 and +15% at +100. Refuse paid service at -50 or lower. Check displayed quotes, actual charges/proceeds, exact provider identity and service refusal separately. Unsupported third-party service windows remain a compatibility obligation, not an assumed success.
- **Guild expulsion, pending:** authorized guild masters/quest givers tolerate hostility according to player rank: beginner approximately -25, high rank approximately -80, highest rank -100. Implement native expulsion and membership consequences, not merely service refusal.
- **Castle nobles, pending:** faster disposition loss for insults in castles; existing castle guards attempt arrest first; punishment scales with offense/negative disposition. Verify native custody and penalty hooks.
- **Dialogue requirements:** upper classic HUD question panel is editable, visible, bounded, multiline and caret-scrolling; typing suppresses topic shortcuts. Keep native facts/map knowledge authoritative, resolve contextual map requests, avoid UI instructions and unsolicited race labels in NPC speech, use first-person ownership language, and reject unsupported glyphs. Preserve insult/repeated-insult rules, family-threat escalation, ownership context, household consent, compliments/flirting rules and physical guard reporting. See [FUTURE-DIALOGUE-AND-VOICE.md](HISTORY.md#source-future-dialogue-and-voice-md).
- **Quest tone, pending:** disposition-sensitive LLM wording must preserve quest facts, choices, objectives, rewards and deadlines, with native fallback. Quest identity/household compatibility remains covered by [QUEST-IDENTITY.md](HISTORY.md#source-quest-identity-md).
- **Modular installation/settings, pending:** optional whole LLM subsystem without mandatory server/CUDA/model downloads; independent population/guard/replacement/bank-sale/commerce chapters with safe save transitions.
- **Existing gameplay backlog remains open:** traveler identity and physical movement, inn reception/rooms, shop browsing/departure, navigation and disappearance bugs, civilian reactions, guard behavior/quarters, household replacement or bank sale, full sprite coverage, external-mod compatibility and performance stalls retain their detailed requirements in the existing topic documents. This final pricing build does not close those milestones.

User requested one final pricing build and verification, then a stop for the night. Do not start the deferred systems as part of this checkpoint.

## Future modular settings (requested 17 October; deferred)

The player should be able to use the population mod without the LLM subsystem. Add a clear master LLM switch that restores the ordinary non-editable dialogue HUD, stops model requests/prewarming, and makes server installation, CUDA tooling and model downloads fully optional. The non-LLM mod installation must not require those downloads or scripts.

Expose major gameplay chapters independently where their dependencies permit: finite guard limits, mobile guard patrols, stationary guard posts, household repopulation, vacant homes offered through banks, and disposition effects on prices/services. Define safe defaults, dependency handling, save migration and behavior when disabling each chapter in an existing save. Do not leave orphaned responders, travelers, leases or ownership state. These are future features, not claims that the current individual switches already provide complete modularity.

The user requested that today's work finish with the current disposition-based prices/services, a build and verification, then stop. Quest-dialogue tone rewriting and the modular-settings pass are deferred.

### Future guild expulsion through personal hostility

Repeated insults to an authorized guild master or guild quest giver can cause expulsion when that individual's disposition crosses a rank-dependent threshold: approximately -25 for a beginner, -80 for a high-ranking member, and -100 at the highest rank. Higher membership rank therefore tolerates more personal hostility. Resolve the intermediate rank mapping against each guild's actual rank scale during implementation. Scope the effect to membership in that NPC's guild and authorized leaders/quest givers; an unrelated ordinary member must not be able to expel the player. Preserve native expulsion, membership and rejoining behavior. This requirement was added after the user requested tonight's final build; it is recorded for future implementation and is not included in that build.

### Future castle etiquette and noble complaints

Insults directed at nobles inside a castle should reduce disposition faster than ordinary civilian insults and can alert the existing castle guards. For insults, guards must attempt arrest before combat, with punishment severity reflecting the degree of negative disposition/offense. Verify the original castle assault/theft and custody behavior before choosing hooks; do not assume that it matches street guards. Define exact insult multipliers and lawful penalty tiers during implementation. This is deferred alongside guild expulsion and must not silently alter main-quest noble interactions or replace stronger existing charges.

17 October M6 foundation: `LocalDialogue` supplies a bounded, pure local-model contract. Runtime source now has a single-request worker HTTP client with main-thread completion and a 64-entry cache. Core tests cover grounding, fallback, cache keys, endpoint rejection, JSON escaping and cancellation (592/592); Unity integration build passes.
