16 October scoped road-stop exits are now persisted and consumed by ResidentTrip. Outbound and return exits retain interior coordinates/height, finish their local exit before road travel and recompute onward timings. Prepared-counter refusal at a full intermediate inn creates the exit and retimes future destination/return receptions by stable IDs. Primary exits filter road exits and retime return receptions too. Invalid rewrites of completed future visits are rejected; provisional refusal records are rolled back. Core 488/488, Unity build and native isolated full-inn refusal/serialization plus cross-region regression pass (`work/RoadStopExitSave-red.log`, `work/RoadStopRefusal-red.log`, `work/RoadStopExitSave-core.log`, `work/RoadStopExitSave-build.log`, `work/RoadStopExitSave-native-pass.log`). Production intermediate selection remains inactive. Runtime crime/actor ownership, actual surveyed intermediate inn rendering, extended room holds and performance still require acceptance.

16 October road inn timing now accepts scoped service-exit routes. It keeps the traveler at the road join while exposing the actual interior exit position/height, resumes local walking only after the exit completes, then derives later road/inn times. Tests cover copied route isolation, equal building keys, mismatched/duplicate scopes and an early departure that avoids the next overnight gate wait. Core 484/484 and Unity build/integration checks pass (`work/RoadStopExitTimeline-core.log`, `work/RoadStopExitTimeline-build.log`; missing-overload red fixture in `work/RoadStopExitTimeline-red.log`). This is the timing prerequisite only: ResidentTrip copying, service-exit persistence, reception retiming and prepared-keeper refusal still need to pass scoped exits into it before production activation.

The shared admission changes also pass the complete ResidentTripProbe regression: 38 runtime pass markers, terminal exit 0 (`work/RoadStopAdmission-full-pass.log`). Coverage includes keeper rejection, overnight capacity, original identity, road actor reconstruction/transfers, destination save continuity and surveyed interior service exit. This is regression evidence for existing trips, not proof of physical intermediate stop refusal.

16 October intermediate inn admission now enumerates all scoped visits, uses each stop's room checkout and validates saved stays against the matching settlement/visit. Core 482/482 covers paid outbound/destination/return beds, save restoration, duplicate admission, mismatched settlement rejection and first-come capacity without borrowing another town's bed. Unity build/integration checks and cross-region regression pass; an isolated native fixture verifies three paid stays after keeper service without duplicate bookings (`work/RoadStopAdmission-red.log`, `work/RoadStopAdmission-core.log`, `work/RoadStopAdmission-build.log`, `work/RoadStopAdmission-native-pass.log`). The first native check queried exactly the pre-serialization attempted-payment time; the final check samples 0.25 seconds later to avoid native double rounding at that boundary (`work/RoadStopAdmission-native-red.log`). Prepared-reception refusal still needs generalized service exits and downstream retiming; production intermediate stop selection remains disabled. No rendered intermediate entry/payment/exit is claimed.

16 October intermediate keeper approaches now resolve against the full outbound/primary/return itinerary using stable visit IDs and settlement identity. Equal building keys in three towns remain distinct; mismatched IDs/settlements are rejected before duplicate writes or restore, and absent metadata is inferred from matching itinerary entries. Core 480/480, Unity build/integration checks, isolated native reception serialization and cross-region historical-discovery regression pass (`work/RoadStopReception-red.log`, `work/RoadStopReception-core.log`, `work/RoadStopReception-build.log`, `work/RoadStopReception-native-pass.log`). This verifies planned reception persistence, not physical intermediate admission. Payment enumeration/stay validation and scoped refusal/exit retiming remain pending; existing primary-only retiming must be generalized before production stop activation.

16 October road-stop persistence and timeline integration verified: schema 16 saves ordered outbound/return stops and stable direction/stop/visit IDs. Evaluation extends arrival/home-return timing, exposes the original resident in intermediate settlements and excludes a simultaneous road actor. Legacy no-stop trips retain timing; historical discovery rejects stops until past admission is supported. Core 479/479, Unity build/integration checks and CrossRegionTripProbe pass (`work/RoadStopSave-core.log`, `work/RoadStopSave-build.log`, `work/RoadStopSave-native-pass.log`). Native coverage includes an isolated stop fixture at year-scale timestamps plus existing 78-point cross-region travel and production historical discovery. Intermediate reception, payment, refusal/exit retiming, crime ownership, road-inn selection and physical actor acceptance remain pending; production planning does not yet create intermediate stops.

16 October stable visit IDs implemented: evaluated/scheduled primary-destination visits use destination/<itinerary index>, scoped to the resident trip. Inn stays, receptions and service exits retain VisitId; legacy null IDs are inferred from a validated matching itinerary entry. Explicit conflicting IDs are rejected on restore and before duplicate-write shortcuts. Admission/reception lookup uses the stable ID plus settlement/building, while paid booking identifiers remain unchanged. A core test verifies an earlier exit moves a later inn visit from 430 to 345 seconds without changing destination/2. Core 475/475, Unity build, six native paid/refused ID migration checks across three inn layouts and all 38 broad traveler runtime checkpoints pass (work/VisitIds-red.log, work/VisitIds-core.log, work/VisitIds-build.log, work/VisitIds-native-pass.log, work/VisitIds-full-pass.log). IDs assume committed primary itinerary order remains fixed apart from truncating future visits; intermediate stop scopes, persisted road-stop lists and production activation remain pending.

16 October visit settlement identity prerequisite implemented: ResidentInnStay, ResidentInnReception and ResidentServiceExit now carry optional SettlementId. Trip validation resolves absent legacy fields to the existing destination and rejects a conflicting explicit settlement before authoritative state changes, including duplicate writes. New admissions and runtime reception/exit records bind the evaluated settlement; keeper, paid-bed and booking validation use that identity. Core 472/472, Unity build and all three native inn layouts pass six paid/refused settlement serialization and legacy-inference checks, alongside room sleeping, physical checkout, refusal exits and return-home behavior. Booking IDs are preserved. Evidence: work/VisitSettlement-red.log, work/VisitSettlement-core.log, work/VisitSettlement-build.log, work/VisitSettlement-native-pass.log. This is additive single-destination migration support, not activation of intermediate stops. Stable stop identity, persisted stop lists, timeline integration and production planning remain pending.

16 October intermediate road inn timing foundation: ResidentRoadJourney composes a surveyed road with ordered intermediate ResidentDestination inn visits, deriving onward arrival from each visit's actual gate/walk/dwell/exit timing. Snapshots keep map coordinates separate from local visit coordinates and carry settlement plus stop index, so equal building keys in different towns remain distinct. Input copies, exact boundaries, two stops, closed gates and independent reversed return timing are tested. Missing-API red evidence precedes implementation; core 469/469 and Unity build pass (work/RoadInnTimeline-red.log, work/RoadInnTimeline-core.log, work/RoadInnTimeline-build.log). The component is not wired into persisted ResidentTrip, admissions, production planning or actors yet; it does not reserve/pay beds or prove road connectivity. Next implement stop-scoped persistence and booking/reception/exit identity before enabling intermediate inns. Plan: docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-road-inn-stops.md.

16 October guard approach preparation now spreads route work across frames and prepares the nearest guard for each building before filling farther routes. Core 464/464, native shop readiness/response/save/load/pursuit and an extended three-minute Daggerfall performance probe pass. Approach slices peak at 10.34 ms; initial overall frame maximum remains 101.26 ms. See docs/PERFORMANCE.md and work/ApproachCoverage-performance-pass.log. This improves setup stalls without completing world-scale performance validation.

16 October final incoming-discovery build passes the complete ResidentTripProbe/TownTest regression (38 runtime pass markers, exit 0; work/IncomingDiscovery-full-pass.log). Coverage includes original hometown reservation, incoming production planning, three-day keeper-bound lodging without prepayment, cancellation, native serialization, same-body road/street transfers, destination actors, crime identity, and scheduled interior entry/exit. This verifies the broad traveler suite after shared inn NonRentable normalization; it does not prove every settlement layout, historical destination occupants, or overnight stops along long roads. Existing unmatched inn occupant and missing sprite warnings remain separate limitations.

16 October cross-region journey verification: the dedicated CrossRegionTripProbe uses the production frame runner and real source/destination surveys with guaranteed fixture demand (1000/1000, maximum one). After one skipped origin, resident Vannyn Kingfield from settlement 430060351 in region 18 reaches Daggerfall 1291010263 in region 17 through a 78-point regional road route and five scheduled service visits. Native serialization preserves the original named resident, one partial reserved source household, outbound/returning phases, destination building presence, and home absence until return. Unity build and TownTest -CrossRegionTripProbe pass (work/CrossRegion-build.log and work/CrossRegion-native-pass.log). This verifies planning and evaluated lifecycle, not a rendered end-to-end journey or guaranteed feasibility for every foreign origin. The run also reports the existing bounded interior survey limit at building 132622; expanded-world performance and road rests remain unverified/unfinished.

16 October weighted origin selection: incoming discovery now uses deterministic 60/35/5 default weights for nearby (nearest sixteen supported settlements in the destination region), distant (other settlements in that region), and foreign (other regions). Travel menu sliders accept relative weights 0–100; all zero disables new incoming-origin discovery, while existing trips/local outbound planning remain. Settings changes cancel pending preparation. A sliced cached map-only catalog covers 3,444 supported settlements without creating their households. Each selected origin must have a regional road route before any source household is reserved; that route is reused by the traveler batch. Missing/excluded/unreachable origins are skipped without replacing the category with nearby traffic. At most sixteen origin attempts occur per destination/day; normal sparse source demand and trip ownership still apply. These weights govern origin attempts, not guaranteed arrival headcounts: population, demand, prior planning, road-search limits and journey duration affect observed arrivals. Two red selector cases preceded implementation; 459/459 core tests, Unity build and full native traveler suite pass (`work/VisitorOrigins-red.log`, `work/VisitorOrigins-core.log`, `work/VisitorOrigins-build.log`, `work/VisitorOrigins-native-pass.log`). Native coverage verifies the complete catalog, frame slicing, all three region/index references and hometown identities, plus existing near-origin travel lifecycle. A full distant/cross-region physical journey has not yet been independently exercised; global arrival history, long-road rests and expanded performance profiling remain open.

16 October sparse unvisited-origin integration: ReserveResidentTravelProposals previews deterministic household identities against saved partial households, honors deaths and overlapping trips, and persists only households of selected travelers. Selection matches a fully generated town for the same seed/day; zero demand creates no settlement. Housing metadata is read in batches of eight, and source navigation is reused by the sliced route planner. Normal planning now considers the loaded origin plus its nearest sixteen same-region settlement origins, each retaining its own nearest-sixteen destination pool and default 10/1000 adult demand capped at eight proposals. Planning remains sequential/frame-sliced and context-cancellable; this is not a full-world scan or the requested 60/35/5 weighted selector. The native production frame runner schedules a Gothway-origin visitor to loaded Daggerfall while saving one household, without loading the origin scene. Reservation/save/full-population completion and original absence/return pass. Review found missing names/race before departure; a native red check reproduced it, and RegionalIdentityAdapter now accepts explicit hometown metadata, used when reserving households/demand while preserving saved identity and DFRandom state. Core 457/457, Unity build and full traveler suite pass (`work/SparseOriginDemand-red.log`, `work/SparseOriginDemand-core.log`, `work/SparseOriginDemand-build.log`, `work/SparseOriginDemand-native-pass.log`, `work/ReservedIdentity-native-red.log`). The native incoming-demand fixture deliberately uses 1000/1000 with cap one to guarantee an exercised trip; normal play retains 10/1000. Expanded neighborhood performance has not yet been profiled. Remaining: 60/35/5 weighted distant/cross-region origins and settings, road rests, historical arrival discovery, global indexing and performance.

16 October reserved hometown households (user-requested direction): WorldStateStore.ReserveHousehold creates only a bed-surveyed household in an explicitly partial settlement. Stable building/slot identities and the household's names, relatives and death state survive snapshot/native JSON. GetOrCreateFamilies later builds the remaining town and retains the reserved household objects; missing homes or insufficient new capacity fail before mutation. Existing completed populations remain unchanged, and reservation never revives an existing person. UnloadedPopulation.ReserveHome reads only the selected house's beds/footprint without changing scenes. Two red core cases preceded implementation; 455/455 core tests, Unity build and full native traveler suite pass (`work/ReservedHouseholds-red.log`, `work/ReservedHouseholds-core.log`, `work/ReservedHouseholds-build.log`, `work/ReservedHouseholds-native-pass.log`). Native check reserves one Gothway household while Daggerfall remains loaded, serializes, then completes Gothway while retaining identity/name/family. This supersedes the attempted full-origin initialization approach (`work/RemoteOrigins-native-red.log`). The reservation mechanism is ready; automatic demand from unvisited origins is not yet wired. Next: derive sparse candidates from deterministic home/slot metadata, reserve only selected households, and route their original residents without filling entire hometowns in advance.

16 October late day-visit promotion: automatic planning now promotes a one-day request to a two-day lodging plan when its first service requires an overnight opening wait. Short opening waits and outside-the-gate waits remain unchanged; explicitly requested two-to-four-day stays retain their duration. The promoted plan uses the same capacity, keeper, reception and no-prepayment checks as other overnight trips. Core regression failed before the policy change; 453/453 core tests, Unity build and full native traveler suite pass (`work/DayVisitLodging-red.log`, `work/DayVisitLodging-core.log`, `work/DayVisitLodging-build.log`, `work/DayVisitLodging-native-pass.log`). Native fixture verifies one keeper-bound night followed by the original service, no paid stay before arrival, and home absence/return after serialization. The existing production three-day runner check also passes. This does not yet cover overnight rests along roads or all-day services that finish too late to return home.

16 October automatic multi-day activation: the daily controller no longer drops two-to-four-day proposals. It prepares the first service route, then requires capacity-aware lodging, saved keeper receptions and a complete return itinerary before committing the original resident's trip. No feasible lodging means no trip, not a shortened day visit. Stable resident/destination/day seeds choose overnight timing; existing sparse demand, frame slicing, cancellation and future-departure checks remain. A native red run committed zero trips for a three-day request. Core 452/452, Unity build and full traveler suite now pass (`work/AutomaticOvernight-native-red.log`, `work/AutomaticOvernight-core.log`, `work/AutomaticOvernight-build.log`, `work/AutomaticOvernight-native-pass.log`). The production Update runner schedules two nights without prepayment; native serialization preserves original identity, absence and return, and cancellation commits nothing. Generation still starts from loaded settlements and nearest same-region destinations; this is not all-world demand generation. One-day requests that arrive too late for a service still need lodging promotion; global indexing, road rest stops and legacy reception migration remain open.

16 October lodging-first routing: travelers reaching a closed service with an overnight wait now route to an inn first, then return to the original service after morning checkout. Short opening waits and waits outside closed city gates retain their existing behavior. Core 452/452, Unity build and the full native traveler suite pass (`work/LodgingFirst-core.log`, `work/LodgingFirst-build.log`, `work/LodgingFirst-native-pass.log`). The native fixture initially assumed a universal 20:00 closing time; it now uses the actual service closing time plus fifteen minutes. Native save/load preserves the arrival night and original service appointment. Automatic multi-day proposal activation remains pending; this checkpoint verifies planning, not worldwide generation.

16 October same-inn social stay: a social/inn destination that is itself a tavern can now host the two-to-four-day plan directly. StayAtInn preserves the original approach/exit geometry, converts the single stop to lodging, extends the room hold through the requested nights and keeps morning checkout. ExtendOvernight prefers that inn unless capacity selection has excluded it; other-inn fallback remains available. The native fixture excludes every other inn and verifies two nights at inn 65542 with original approach and serialized home absence. Core red reproduced the missing lodging conversion; 451/451 core tests, Unity build and full traveler suite pass (`work/SameInnStay-red.log`, `work/SameInnStay-core.log`, `work/SameInnStay-build.log`, `work/SameInnStay-native-pass.log`). This adds planning support, not automatic multi-day activation or new daytime tavern animations. Late-arrival inn-first routing, legacy keeper-binding migration and automatic multi-day generation remain pending.

16 October keeper-bound admission: new loaded and remote reception plans persist KeeperId, validated against the destination inn's native identity. Remote inventory registers that existing record without reviving it or assigning a new name; dead keepers are excluded from new plans. Arrival-time payment checks keeper death and active alarm participation; refusal saves the physical return route. Loaded reconciliation processes already prepared refusals even when no available keeper can prepare new routes. Core tests cover dead/alarmed keepers, zero booking, escape, identity re-registration, invalid keeper references and exit-reference rounding; 450/450 pass. Native cross-town fixture kills the bound keeper after planning, verifies rejection/exit and persistent death through JSON. Unity build, full traveler suite and all three inn lifecycle tests pass (`work/KeeperAdmission-red.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-core.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-build.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-native-pass.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-inn-pass.log`). Native restore exposed another exact timestamp reference in service exits; those now use the same bounded 1ms comparison, with larger mismatches rejected (`KeeperAdmission-native-red`, `-exit-red`). Older receptions with null KeeperId retain legacy behavior and still need migration; this does not claim keeper checks for every legacy generated-traveler path. Automatic multi-day activation, late-arrival inn-first routing and same-inn social plans remain open.

16 October interrupted-visit retiming: saving a non-refusal service exit now recalculates future planned reception times and itinerary room-hold bounds from the revised journey. Existing paid bookings retain their original expiry; already processed future admissions/exits cannot be rewritten. Restore first validates exit timing against a provisional schedule, then validates reservation bounds against the final interrupted schedule. The red regression reproduced invalid room-hold bounds before reception matching; cases with an unpaid upcoming inn and an already paid multi-night room now pass restore, revised admission timing and unchanged booking expiry. Core 449/449, Unity build and full three-inn suite pass (`work/ReceptionRetiming-red.log`, `work/ReceptionRetiming-core.log`, `work/ReceptionRetiming-build.log`, `work/ReceptionRetiming-native-pass.log`). Native suite covers existing paid-room excursions, serialization and refusal lifecycle; the new retiming scenarios are core-level tests. Loaded reception caching now uses the same 1ms reference tolerance, avoiding exact-comparison cache misses after native timestamp rounding. Keeper availability/death, late-arrival inn-first routing, same-inn social plans and automatic multi-day activation remain pending.

16 October planned remote reception: capacity-prepared overnight trips now carry copied keeper approach routes for every inn stop, beginning at each authoritative arrival. Forecast capacity starts after counter service; no payment occurs during planning. Refusal discards future reception plans while preserving completed admission/exit records. Native Daggerfall-to-Gothway capacity fallback verifies the planned route survives JSON, payment is rejected before arrival at the counter, payment succeeds after service, and the paid bed survives another native restore. Core 448/448, Unity build and full resident-trip suite pass (`work/PlannedReception-red.log`, `work/PlannedReception-core.log`, `work/PlannedReception-build.log`, `work/PlannedReception-native-pass.log`). Native testing exposed timestamp rounding: reception references now use a bounded 1ms comparison, and persisted booking IDs retain their original timestamp text while validating against the rounded visit reference and matching ledger. Regressions cover both rounding directions, refusal, retained current reception, no duplicate payment and rejection of a one-second mismatch (`PlannedReception-native-red`, `-precision-red`, `-rounding-red`, `-booking-red`). Still required before automatic multi-day activation: keeper availability/death, reception retiming after non-refusal early service exits, late-arrival inn-first routing and same-inn social visits. Review the runtime reception cache's exact timestamp comparison when connecting these plans to loaded actors.

16 October unloaded reception geometry: remote inn surveys now retain the native keeper hash and doorway-to-counter path from passive person records and a sliced InteriorGeometry flood. No NPC is spawned; results publish only after the complete remote operation. Regular inventory-only surveys do not add this flood. A native red check reproduced missing routes. Core 447/447, Unity build and full three-inn suite pass: routes of 65, 40 and 58 points match actual loaded entrance/keeper positions and clear loaded geometry; existing scene-preservation, cancellation, room ownership and refusal checks still pass (`work/RemoteReception-native-red.log`, `work/RemoteReception-core.log`, `work/RemoteReception-build.log`, `work/RemoteReception-native-pass.log`). TownTest requires all three reception markers. Routes are not yet attached to planned trips; keeper availability/death must be checked before payment, and the added survey cost has not been profiled. Automatic multi-day activation remains pending.

16 October saved-reception offscreen refusal: room refusal now reverses the persisted doorway-to-counter route at the authoritative admission time, saves the physical exit and cancels future appointments immediately, including during late admission catch-up. Queued admission candidates recheck the current itinerary, preventing a later night from booking after the first refusal replaced the trip. Red regression reproduced three retained stops; 447/447 core tests, Unity build and full three-inn native suite pass (`work/OffscreenRefusal-red.log`, `work/OffscreenRefusal-core.log`, `work/OffscreenRefusal-build.log`, `work/OffscreenRefusal-native-pass.log`). The core test covers late catch-up and restore without actors; native checks cover loaded physical exits and serialization. This requires a saved reception route. Route-less offscreen inns, existing refusal migration and automatic multi-day activation remain open.

16 October refused-inn return: saving a physical exit after room refusal now cancels remaining appointments and reverses the already surveyed street legs to the regional road, preserving gate timing and interior exit continuity. Core regression reproduced the retained-appointments bug (445/446), then passes 446/446. Unity build and all three native inns pass physical exit, cancelled appointments and native serialization/home-return checks (`work/InnRefusalReturn-red.log`, `work/InnRefusalReturn-core.log`, `work/InnRefusalReturn-build.log`, `work/InnRefusalReturn-native-pass.log`). This covers newly saved loaded-interior exits; offscreen refusal without an exit plan, migration of already saved exits, late-arrival inn-first routing and automatic multi-day activation remain pending.

16 October overnight capacity preparation: unseen destination households are initialized from real house/bed metadata in eight-building slices; inn inventory reconciles receipts/occupants before forecasting the full stay. Native fixture initializes 162 Gothway households and rejects full inn 65542 for reachable inn 131590. Cross-town testing exposed DFU automap's hidden PlayerGPS block lookup; the mod now supplies cached destination records to its isolated model builder without engine edits. Core 445/445, build, full traveler and three-inn suites pass (`OvernightCapacity`, `RemoteBlock` evidence). Automatic multi-day activation still awaits arrival-refusal handling; forecasts do not prepay or guarantee a future vacancy.

16 October multi-day geometry: overnight route extension now connects a reachable inn with subsequent services, preferring unvisited matching buildings, and joins the final service back to the regional exit. Native Daggerfall resident -> Gothway two-day fixture visits shops 196624/196622 and inn 65542; original identity absence/return survives serialization and cancellation publishes nothing. Build/full traveler suite pass (`work/OvernightRoutes-*`). Geometry/timing only: automatic selection, room admission and full-inn runtime fallback are not yet connected.

16 October overnight timing generator: routed service/inn itineraries now generate deterministic 09:00–11:00 morning checkout, bounded one-to-seven nights and a continuous first-room hold. Core 445/445, build and full native traveler suite pass; real Daggerfall service-to-inn geometry retains overnight/return timing through native serialization (`work/OvernightTiming-*`). Automatic multi-day selection remains disabled until destination population/inventory setup and refusal fallback are connected; this is timing generation, not full automatic overnight travel.

16 October multi-night room continuity: an explicit itinerary checkout keeps a paid room through daytime services; later visits reuse it without a second admission. Interior room walks use each visit's entry/exit while the booking spans the full stay. Core 444/444, build and all three native inns pass daytime absence, serialization, same-bed return, second-night sleep and final release (`work/ResidentRoomHold-*`). Native serialization exposed a 23-microsecond counter-time discrepancy; restore now permits 1 ms rounding while rejecting a one-second early admission. The service excursion is synthetic; automatic multi-day route generation is not yet connected.

16 October paid remote rooms: shared inventory registration now reconciles native player rentals before permanent occupants; exterior catch-up uses it too. All three inn fixtures preserve paid rooms across repeated registration, reject overlapping guest bookings and reject invalid rental markers before publishing inventory. Build/full inn suite pass (`work/RemoteReceipt-*`). Automatic overnight itineraries, unavailable-keeper/full-inn routing and continuous remote reconciliation remain pending.

16 October destination-aware inn claims: NativeInnOccupants accepts explicit settlement/location metadata; names and race fallback use the destination rather than PlayerGPS. Remote surveys serialize hidden geometry and release the slot on cancellation. Build and all three native inn checks pass, including local/remote claim identity, foreign-region race, random-state preservation and active/queued cancellation (`work/RemoteClaims-*`). Automatic overnight itinerary/receipt/service integration remains pending.

16 October remote inn inventory: unloaded settlement entrances retain original StaticDoor metadata. A destination-climate survey now produces room/bed, rental-marker and permanent-occupant mappings without requiring a loaded town owner. Build and all three native inn comparisons pass (`work/RemoteInn-*`); cancelled-before-start work publishes nothing. Automatic overnight claim/admission integration remains pending, as do concurrent-survey isolation and in-progress cancellation coverage. Model layout creation is still synchronous.

16 October mixed inn admissions: runtime now uses one chronological queue and one eight-attempt budget for original residents and generated travelers. An earlier resident can no longer lose the last room solely because generated guests were processed first. Loaded inns prepare resident counter service before admitting competing guests. Core 442/442, build and all three native inn checks pass (`work/MixedInn-*`). Automatic overnight trips, unloaded-world reconciliation and keeper-unavailable fallback remain unfinished.

16 October inn catch-up: original-resident admissions now process every due inn visit in chronological arrival/reception-completion order, including completed visits. Earlier occupancy therefore blocks overlapping later guests even when reconciliation happens after checkout. Budgeted processing and save/restore remain idempotent. Core 441/441, Unity build and all three native test inns pass (`work/InnCatchup-*`). Automatic overnight planning and mixed traveler/global admission ordering remain unfinished.

16 October overnight route preparation: direct building-to-inn paths now preserve doorway continuity and pass a 134-segment native Daggerfall collision check. Chained inn/return timing, build and full traveler regression pass (`ServiceInnRoute` evidence in the traveler plan). Automatic overnight selection and room admission are not yet connected; schedules do not grant beds.

16 October short regional roads: neighboring-settlement routes no longer require an interior road node between two trimmed endpoints. Validation accepts only the same unit cardinal/diagonal edge and rejects off-grid shortcuts. Core 439/439, build, native persistence and full traveler regression pass (`AdjacentTrips` evidence in the traveler plan). Actual adjacent-settlement gameplay coverage remains separate from the synthetic persistence fixture.

16 October automatic original-resident travel: normal play now considers next-day sparse travel proposals in the current settlement, choosing among the nearest 16 supported settlements in its region. It prepares supported one-day visits across frames and commits only complete, still-current plans. Native live Update batch schedules one Daggerfall resident to Gothway; cancelled/late/overlapping commits are rejected, save/load absence/return and the full traveler suite pass (`work/AutomaticTravel-native-pass.log`); build passes. Multi-day proposals remain deferred for lodging rather than shortened. This is not yet continuous planning for every unloaded settlement: other regions, adjacent-pixel trimmed routes, overnight/multi-stop trips, road rests and non-model/terrain clearance need work. See the traveler plan for precise limits.

15 October shop activity expansion: runtime requests3–5 distinct browsing anchors with varied dwell, a deterministic50% purchase choice, brief3–7 real-second keeper interaction for buyers, and direct departure for non-buyers. Reservations now conflict only for overlapping visits.364 core tests, Unity build and live buyer/non-buyer approach/browse/dialogue/departure checks pass; the fixture plans5/4 shelf stops respectively. Item/money transfers and uninterrupted traversal of every stop/layout remain unverified or unfinished. Details and evidence: [SHOP-VISITS.md](HISTORY.md#source-shop-visits-md). The running-art pilot is not connected: user review found repeated leading arms in some angles, and corrections remain under visual review.

15 October shelf-facing correction: managed resident actors preserve task-facing against small crowd corrections, keep directional standing art, face movement while still approaching, and immediately face the player during dialogue before restoring their activity. Resident interior clearance now matches the physical 0.4-metre crowd body; a live shelf overlap exposed the previous 0.24/0.4 mismatch. Actual shop movement/browsing/haggling/dialogue/exit checks and editor regressions pass;362 core tests pass. See [SHOP-VISITS.md](HISTORY.md#source-shop-visits-md) for evidence and limits. New escape-priority, one-hour shelter locks, pursuit shouts/reinforcements and randomized 10–20m bystander behavior are recorded as pending in [ACTIVITY-AND-GATE-FOLLOWUP.md](HISTORY.md#source-activity-and-gate-followup-md).

15 October activity/gate follow-up: the latest reported shelf flicker and requested task-facing, varied shelf browsing, 50% purchase interactions, timed street conversations, wider pedestrian spacing, castle coverage and paired inside/outside gate posts are tracked in [ACTIVITY-AND-GATE-FOLLOWUP.md](HISTORY.md#source-activity-and-gate-followup-md). These remain pending, not completed features. Exterior gate guards must move inside before closing. Live testing is permitted again; the older deferral entries below are historical.

15 October non-rental bedroom integration: schema11 adds explicit NonRentable room metadata. Runtime inn inventory includes all surveyed bedrooms; only rest-marker-backed rooms are offered for rent. Existing inventories can gain strictly non-rental rooms without removing/reassigning old beds, changing eligibility or losing bookings/claims. Ordinary native occupants in those rooms now enter the same claim/sleep path.359 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/NonRentalRooms-core.log`, `work/NonRentalRooms-build-editor.log`). Live tests remain deferred by user request. Pending live validation must cover occupied-room claims, night/midday poses, save/reload and updated rental fixtures; older fixtures assume fixed available markers, which may now belong to native occupants and require deliberate setup reconciliation. Excess occupants and corpse-cleanup claim release remain open.

15 October inn claim runtime integration: exterior inventory and loaded-inn rental refresh now register ordinary native room identities from original records and commit surveyed bed claims after native player receipts reconcile, before traveler admission. Name generation preserves DFRandom state. Existing paid-room conflicts defer the claim instead of displacing payment. Claimed native occupants use seeded sleep/wake times and appearance-matched bed poses through the existing native wake/crime path.358 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/InnClaimRuntime-core.log`, `work/InnClaimRuntime-build-editor.log`). Live automatic-claim and night/midday assertions were added to TavernSurveyProbe but have NOT run: the user asked to keep working without live tests while their game is open. Integration currently covers marker-backed managed rental rooms; non-rentable bedroom occupants, excess occupants, cleanup release, actual sleep/reload and broader layouts remain pending. Do not report native gameplay acceptance from this build.

15 October inn occupant survey checkpoint: loaded and model-only unloaded surveys now classify original people records using door-separated room regions, excluding the entrance-connected common room. Assignment is deterministic by native position hash and original bed record. The actual three-Gothway-inn test matches three assignments (65542:372/bed30 and -1016/bed45;131590:102/bed15), with all rental/payment/rest regressions passing (`work/InnOccupantSurvey-runtime-pass.log`).358 core tests and Unity build pass. One bedroom has more original NPCs than beds; excess occupants log `LIVING_CITIES_INN_OCCUPANT_NO_BED` without assigning a shared bed or breaking the rental survey. Initial whole-inn survey failure is preserved in `work/InnOccupantSurvey-runtime-red.log`. Claim creation, native sleep integration, excess-occupant behavior, corpse cleanup and wider layouts remain unfinished; room classification alone does not reserve beds.

15 October native inn claim foundation: schema10 persists named native occupants with a physical bed and claim start time. A claim excludes its entire private room from offers, bookings, extensions and rest checks; conflicting paid intervals prevent the claim rather than being cancelled. Schema9 migrates empty claims while preserving bookings.358 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/InnOccupants-core.log`, `work/InnOccupants-build-editor.log`). This is ledger infrastructure only: actual loaded/unloaded NPC room classification, assignment, sleeping, corpse-cleanup release and live inn acceptance remain pending. No runtime controller creates these claims yet.

15 October overland foundation: `OverlandRoads` finds bounded deterministic routes through reciprocal Basic Roads direction bits.355 core tests and Unity build pass (`work/OverlandRoads-core.log`, `work/OverlandRoads-build-editor.log`). Coverage is synthetic connectivity, not native world travel. The traveler plan now explicitly requires source households, shared identity/death, whole-trip home absence, road rendering and return before claiming real inter-settlement travel. Destination-generated traveler demand remains unconnected to source residents.

15 October refusal checkpoint: a fully booked inn now sends a refused traveler back along the surveyed exterior route without reserving a bed. Closed gates delay departure; admission changes invalidate cached journeys so the proposed multi-day stay cannot keep a refused actor alive.352 core tests and the native Daggerfall full-inn test pass, including visible return movement and actual save-slot reload (`work/TravelerRefusal-core.log`, `work/TravelerRefusal-runtime-pass.log`, `work/TravelerRefusal-build-editor.log`). The test runner now requires `LIVING_CITIES_TRAVELER_REFUSAL_PASS`. Automatic demand, keeper interaction, daytime visits, historical retention and broader layout acceptance remain open.

15 October inn walking checkpoint: deterministic surveyed 3D entrance/bed routes now drive arrival and pre-checkout movement, with normal door opening and awake awareness during walking.350 core tests and live Daggerfall checkout/sleep/reload/lifecycle regression pass (`work/TravelerWalk-runtime-pass.log`). Missing routes retain the prior room-placement fallback and log a limitation; broader layout/arrival/gate-wait acceptance and automatic demand remain open.

15 October physical inn guests: admitted travelers appear in their paid inn, sleep in a stable bed belonging to their booked room, retain identity/pose on reload and wake beside the bed.349 core tests and native Daggerfall inn/lifecycle regression pass; Unity build passes (`work/TravelerInn-runtime-pass.log`). Test save: `Living Cities - traveler sleeping`. Entrance/checkout walking, daytime activities, automatic demand and broad inn-layout acceptance remain open.

15 October registered traveler admission is live: each inn reconciles paid native receipts before allocating due travelers; player rental actions respect earlier arrivals with bounded catch-up. Actual Daggerfall automatic booking/death/reload and all three Gothway tavern rental/rest regressions pass;348 core tests and Unity build pass. Fixed fractional calendar timestamp round-trip rejection without changing save schema. Automatic demand and physical inn guests remain open. Evidence and limitations: `docs/TAVERN-LODGING.md`.

15 October traveler retention follow-up: current-day visit cache replaces half-second full-history scans; dead/completed plans leave per-frame processing while active civilian reactions are retained.346 core tests, Unity build and Daggerfall lifecycle regression pass (`work/TravelerWindow-runtime-pass.log`). History is still scanned on day/revision changes; automatic demand and full history indexing remain open.

15 October traveler actor checkpoint: registered exterior arrivals and admitted departures now use named resident actors. Sleeping-art catalogue, 15 October: source inspection and built-in image generation now cover 191 distinct appearances, mapped to 215 source references, including all 25 mobile archives. The bald, grey-bearded shopkeeper 182/0 is corrected; the separate book-holding scribe is 184/2. M4 outdoor welfare acceptance: a real street defender witnesses the player's lawful native kill, physically approaches, completes courtesy without charging the player, then returns to its post. M3/M4 witness handoff: existing threat responders can now receive recorded lawful-defense evidence from nearby awake civilians in the same scene with clear sight. This advances civilian-to-guard knowledge without spawning courtesy guards; full fleeing-messenger journeys and broader contact layouts remain open.

M4 actual reload acceptance: the integrated keeper/guard encounter now saves and loads a real dedicated test slot while the responder approaches for welfare. This closes the real-slot gap in earlier checkpoints; outdoor and broader layout acceptance remain unfinished.

M4 welfare validation: the integrated shop encounter reaches native surrender after courtesy while retaining Theft. Two responders coordinate without an uninformed companion preempting the eligible guard. Paired patrol core integration now persists assignments in GuardRoster/WorldState, derives actual available-guard positions and interrupts pairs for stop/death/crime. Theft awareness runtime checkpoint: supported-town building interiors now query living, awake nearby NPC/guard sight before the shared native theft roll. No witness gives zero detection; witnesses retain the skill calculation. Theft amendment: unobserved shop/private-property theft must succeed 100%; only living nearby potential witnesses enable skill-based detection. Dead people never witness. Both native theft flows share CalculateShopliftingChance, currently clamped to a minimum 5% detection risk without occupancy checks. See THEFT-WITNESSES.md; integration pending.

Household lock checkpoint: actual-player HousingProbe now verifies awake/unlocked, all owners away during daytime/locked, sleeping/locked and waking/unlocked through the native door query. Core suite is 175 tests; build/native checks pass. Ordinary generated households are integrated; faction/owned homes retain native handling and unknown quest placement falls back. Witnessed-entry reporting and explicit access/quest-security arbitration remain pending. See HOUSEHOLD-LOCKS.md.

Shopping and stationary identity amendment: customers must browse shelves, face goods, approach the keeper, haggle and leave for their next activity. Convert stationary non-main-quest NPCs into persistent named people with homes, beds and mobile behavior while preserving native services and side-quest bindings. Shopkeepers reserve their own beds; bedless keepers commute home. See SHOPPING-AND-STATIONARY-IDENTITIES.md. Current generic visit anchors and separate native keepers do not fulfill this requirement.

14 October runtime checkpoint: outdoor discovery/departure cleanup and finite investigation routes passed the actual-player OutdoorSceneProbe. The revised indoor ten-second warning, Trespassing surrender and real save/reload passed CrimeSceneProbe. Core suite passes 171/171; Unity build and native integration checks pass. A subsequent review fix keeps issued warnings active when a guard dies or leaves; it builds successfully, but its repeat player test deferred because the player was already running. The installed development bundle retains version 0.3.12. Earlier checkpoint paragraphs below are historical and superseded where they disagree with the approved policy. Actual corpse inventory transfers, lawful-defense classification and welfare dialogue remain pending. Existing bounties must survive any future welfare exchange.

Latest approved crime policy removes all guilt-by-presence: harmless corpse examination, witnessed actual looting as Theft, ten unpaused seconds for unidentified indoor visitors to leave before Trespassing, and existing Murder only for identified crimes. Genuine hostile-attacker kills grant immediate free loot rights; guards secure the scene and witnessed defense can receive welfare dialogue and an occasional bounded potion. See OUTDOOR-CRIME-SCENES.md and LEGITIMATE-DEFENSE.md; runtime integration is in progress and defense/loot systems remain pending.

Outdoor corpse amendment: first discovery plus player departure authorizes cleanup; unseen bodies persist. Later passersby are not murder suspects, searching guards refuse conversation, and witnessed corpse interaction triggers Theft. New core discovery evidence and one-hour finite patrol routes pass 170 tests and native serialization; runtime wiring is pending and installed 0.3.12 is unchanged. See OUTDOOR-CRIME-SCENES.md.

0.3.12 corpse checkpoint: generated residents killed by native weapon damage leave saved placeholder bodies at their death positions. Guard discovery clears them, including after reload. CrimeSceneProbe and the escaping-witness regression pass; core suite is 162/162. Resident-specific corpse art, bed corpse presentation, loot and other death paths remain pending. See CRIME-SCENE-DISCOVERY.md.

Physical-space requirement: moving NPCs must avoid and pass each other, with bounded local queries, doorway yielding and distinct waiting positions. Direct scheduled placement currently permits overlaps. See NPC-LOCAL-AVOIDANCE.md; implementation pending.

Latest installed 0.3.11: investigation re-entry charges Murder; actual native guard surrender-before-damage and accepting Yes into court are verified. Automated tests disable mouse-look and retain an unlocked visible cursor. Corpse lifecycle core/native serialization passes (161 tests), but runtime corpse presentation remains pending.

Current 0.3.10 work connects unwitnessed home murders to departure-triggered finite guard investigation, prior-crime preservation, arrival discovery and re-entry custody. Occupation is now **one game hour from first arrival**, per the latest user decision. SAVE33 is the investigation fixture. Corpse persistence/cleanup, stationary citizens, patrols and defense remain unfinished. M3/M4 and the overall goal remain active.


Historical 0.3.9 checkpoint implements saved indoor witness escape through entry-connected floors, exterior travel to a guard post, delayed finite response to the original house, and real SAVE32 reload/identity continuity. Core suite: 154 tests. Unwitnessed murder discovery, crime-scene occupation, broader interior navigation and stationary-NPC conversion remain open. See INDOOR-WITNESSES.md. M3/M4 are not complete.

0.3.8 connects public citizen witnesses to finite guards: local visibility, immutable crime destination, bounded physical dispatch and persistent report identity. The controlled actual-player witness probe and 151 core tests pass. Autonomous indoor escape/reporting, unwitnessed murder discovery and original stationary-citizen conversion remain next behavior work. Art stays deferred; M3/M4 remain open.

0.3.7 guard integration adds surveyed-building alarms, travel-before-entry, indoor combat, persistent 3D location/health, cleared-crime stand-down, and retraced indoor exit followed by exterior return. SAVE31 is the shop-response fixture. M4 remains open: unsurveyed interiors, broader layouts, patrol pairs/torches/walls, full arrest/escape validation and enemy defense remain. Core suite: 150 tests.

Current 0.3.5 checkpoint: 142 core tests and actual-player controller/actor probes pass. Opt-in exterior Gothway Garden guards now have a fixed roster, bounded physical responses, native combat, wounded save/load and last-seen search expiry/return. Indoor guards remain vanilla. Physical shop response and door transitions are next. See GUARDS.md for tested limits. Older checkpoint paragraphs below are historical; all unfinished milestones remain open.

> Current amendment, 14 October 2026: one NPC per bed; maximum household residents equals actual bed count, with no separate five-person cap. Preserve old saves and use a fresh compliant test population. Shared-bed rendering, kill-all-bed logic and separate head/blanket layers are superseded. Keep complete green-blanket/white-pillow single-sleeper sprites. Children may inherit either parent's race; keep existing child sprites now. Gameplay takes priority over art. See [current citizen behavior and next guard work](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md). Earlier checkpoint descriptions below remain historical evidence.

Approved on 13 October 2026. The active Codex goal covers all milestones; this file records concrete acceptance evidence.

Current 0.3.3 checkpoint: 127/127 core tests, native build and actual-player civilian response/bed-capacity checks pass. Generated residents react to witnessed weapon kills with flight or basic counterattack, retain identity and persist bounded one-hop warnings. Fresh fixture `LivingCitiesTest-f238d317` has 181 residents and eight children; SAVE28 is a four-bed/four-person home, SAVE29 is the disposable crime-response fixture. Further art is deferred. Next implementation priority is the finite guard roster and replacement of instant native guard spawning, followed by physical response and pursuit/search/escape. See `CIVILIAN-RESPONSES.md` for tested boundaries and remaining work.

- [x] M1 Baseline and integration probes. Official assets and patched player verified; Unity compilation, Basic Roads and Living Cities builds/startup passed. Temporary standard-mod guard interception and actual inside/outside Gothway Garden saves/reloads passed in the shipped player. The quest identity probe now passes native clicks, in-place movement, hide/show and actual save/load reconstruction of an original NPC bound to a synthetic quest. This establishes integration feasibility, not full quest/service compatibility; see `QUEST-IDENTITY.md`.
- [ ] M2 One-town persistent population. Stable households with shared surnames; maximum residents equals actual bed count, one resident per bed, no independent five-person cap. Explicit family roles, child-local play/home schedules, shopkeepers commuting home when their shop has no living quarters, deterministic adult work/travel/sleep, nearby physical actors, no duplicates, save/load and leave/return continuity.
- [ ] M3 Civilian awareness, relationships and activities. Sleep poses/anchors, closed shops, burglary/waking, friendships/rivalries, meetings, visiting and tavern groups; persistent bereavement with fading salience and witnessed-killing hostility; quest bindings preserved.
- [ ] M4 Finite guards. Settlement-specific rosters, one guard in tiny hamlets, small-town contingents, patrol pairs with torches, reachable wall posts, physical response, last-seen search/escape, arrest compatibility, defense, persistent deaths and bounded recruitment.
- [ ] M5 Existing threats and haunting. Guard intervention and citizen flight for vanilla/quest/mod attackers, night crowd changes, reachable refuges, and actual main-quest state controlling haunting behavior. No separate random-incursion spawner.
- [ ] M6 Optional local dialogue. A benchmarked local 7B model, asynchronous loopback service, free-text UI plus topics, factual grounding including relationships and bereavement, timeout/cancellation and vanilla fallback.
- [ ] M7 Broad validation and city-mod patches. Multiple settlement layouts, world-origin shifts, fast travel/rest/load, other-mod conflicts, versioned saves, performance budgets, packaging and gameplay checks. After vanilla stability, add explicit adapters for city overhauls, market stalls and currently decorative vendor NPCs.

Cross-cutting requirement: a mod settings menu with bounded controls, useful presets, subsystem toggles, clear save/reload semantics, and defaults. Expose working features as they are implemented. See `SETTINGS-AND-COMPATIBILITY.md`.

Quest-household amendment: authoritative quest names, destinations and relationships win over generated families for main and side quests. Ordinary-home generated relatives adopt a permanent named resident's surname through a stable binding/override; temporary guests and captives do not rename homes. Never redirect a quest to a matching generated surname. Castles/palaces retain vanilla arrangements. Test late-starting quests and save/load as well as first visits; see `QUEST-IDENTITY.md`. This remains pending M2/M3 integration.

Destination consistency extends to varying guild/other quest offers and all destination-based quest families. Resolve names before the offer is shown; preserve agreement through journal, rumors, map/building labels, actual target and completion. Test remote towns, concurrent quests, old active saves and changing quest stages. Source inspection confirms `OnQuestStarted` is too late for guild offer text and native recipient/residence names are generated separately. Full integration is still pending.

Tavern amendment: nonlocal travelers rent for one or several nights, visit town, then depart. Paid reservations retain rooms while guests are out; first come, first served, no guaranteed player vacancy. Reserved rooms reject player sleep; full taverns report the earliest room release. Preserve existing paid player bookings. See `TAVERN-LODGING.md` for persistence and acceptance requirements.

Death/recovery amendment: extinct households stay vacant; corpse cleanup waits for elapsed time and an unobserved interior. New families use new identities. Dense-city recovery or bank listing takes 3–7 days; other settlements 7–30 days, with emptied isolated hamlets toward 30. Preserve ownership, deceased identities and memories. See `VACANCY-AND-RECOVERY.md`.

Indoor-crime refinement: a silent one-blow kill of the lone occupant raises no alarm until player departure. Guards travel to the house, clear the body upon arrival, and occupy it for one game hour; player return during occupation triggers attempted custody without magically identifying the unwitnessed killer. Other present occupants, including stationary NPCs, can mobilize to fight or flee and alert guards. Use actual finite responders and preserve quest identities. See `CRIME-SCENES.md`.

Public-crime refinement: direct witnesses flee to reachable safe interiors, alert nearby guards and warn at most two encountered NPCs. Recipients flee or fight but do not relay secondhand warnings. Incident IDs and persisted budgets prevent repeated or unbounded propagation. Player-caused deaths may include important quest NPCs and break their quests; preserve technical bindings and saves, with separate default protection from random background events. These amendments supersede the goal's original blanket quest-protection clause. See `CRIME-SCENES.md`.

Shop-theft refinement: responding guards physically travel from existing exterior posts, patrols or real guard quarters and enter the shop as they arrive. Distance and reachable routes determine the delay, allowing the player to leave and escape before arrival. Headquarters supply only their existing roster; no automatic nearby source or indoor spawning. The shopkeeper may attack or remain passive independently of reporting. Preserve travel across interior transitions and save/load; subsequent pursuit uses actual sight and last-known position. See `CRIME-SCENES.md` for acceptance cases. This belongs to M4 and is not yet implemented.

Essential animation amendments (14 October): every stationary and mobile adult human NPC appearance needs walking, running, weapon combat and spellcasting from all viewing angles, plus a sleeping sprite lying in bed. Children need directional walking/running and sleeping, but no battle sets. M2 requires genuinely mobile children; M3 requires identity-preserving movement/combat/sleep. Existing mobile walkers need distinct running animations too. Generic fighter substitutions and gliding static flats do not satisfy acceptance. Track every source appearance and action, beyond the first pilot sets. This is functional identity continuity, separate from deferred curved paths and cosmetic polish. See `DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md`.

Sleeping color amendment: all sleeping blankets must match the original bed cover's green; pillows stay white. This supersedes the previous white blanket choice while preserving full neck-down coverage and appearance-specific heads.

Sleeping combat amendment (latest 14 October decision): one person per bed supersedes the earlier kill-all-shared-bed rule. Slain-in-bed covered corpse poses and discovery/cleanup persistence remain required follow-up work; current death disappearance is not final behavior. Shared-bed rendering and modular head/blanket composites are cancelled. See `CRIME-SCENES.md`.

Future extension: NPC inventories/pickpocketing, house keys and corpse-only equipped clothing/armor loot; reusable outfit diversity metadata. Additional corpse-sprite coverage remains a separate expansion decision; spellcasting and sleeping are now required above. See `RELATIONSHIPS-AND-FUTURE-INVENTORIES.md`.

Child diversity amendment: at least 12 distinct child appearances: three variations for each gender in each peasant/noble class. Each requires a standard flat, directional idle animations, directional walking/running and a sleeping pose at matching classic pixel scale/style. Validate one complete set before scaling production; no generation-speed assumption follows from low resolution.

Future speech extension: optional English TTS, investigating the Telegram bot's MiraTTS engine; at least 52 female and 52 male base voices plus fantasy options, with persistent per-NPC voice/pitch/speed. The user delegates the 7B dialogue model choice; selection awaits benchmarks. See `FUTURE-DIALOGUE-AND-VOICE.md`.

## Current work

On-site keeper family checkpoint 0.3.18 (14 October): native keeper is a same-ID household head without a generated duplicate. Named relatives share the keeper surname, race metadata and persistent partner/parent links, using beds remaining after native staff reservations. Single-bed capacity, male/female heads, sparse staff slots, lifecycle reference rebuilding and legacy identities pass; 192 core tests and native Unity build pass. Actual-player SAVE35 preserves Peryrick and Vannara Kinghart, two people/two beds, with correct actor identity/gender and native head retained. Existing merchant flight/service-response checks pass with the family. Native keeper combat/death, sleep/work, bedless family linkage and new family daytime visits remain unfinished. Service households keep native quest placement until a service-aware binding rule is reviewed. See SHOPPING-AND-STATIONARY-IDENTITIES.md.

Native merchant response checkpoint 0.3.17 (14 October): saved keeper witnesses now block native activation/dialogue, physically follow collision-surveyed interior routes and report only after travelling to a finite guard. Real SAVE36 reload and recovery after incident expiry plus crime clearance pass. Native WeaponDamage on a controlled generated visitor captures keeper sight. Ordinary unlocked-door opening has wall/lock/script barriers; instant opening is a current limitation. 186 core tests, native build and actual-player housing/escape/crime-scene regressions pass. Keeper families/head linkage, native deaths/combat, exterior presentation and home/return commuting remain unfinished. See SHOPPING-AND-STATIONARY-IDENTITIES.md.

Shop-household amendment (14 October): native shopkeepers are ordinary persistent people and household heads. Multi-bed shops can contain their families, sharing the keeper's surname; the keeper consumes one bed and counts toward total capacity. Visitors are separate. Identity/bed reservation alone does not complete this integration. See SHOPPING-AND-STATIONARY-IDENTITIES.md.

Native keeper checkpoint 0.3.16 (14 October): native merchants now retain persistent identity metadata and an exclusive bed reservation without spawning a duplicate actor. Prefer on-site beds, otherwise a free known residential bed; preserve saved assignments. 185 core tests, Unity native persistence/build and actual-player HousingProbe pass. SAVE35 preserves Peryrick Kinghart's identity and bed in general store 131343, which has two actual beds. The reported one-bed shop remains unidentified. Keeper commute/sleep, crime movement/service blocking and shelf/haggling visits are next; M2–M7 remain incomplete. See SHOPPING-AND-STATIONARY-IDENTITIES.md.

Guard actor checkpoint 0.3.4 (14 October): native City Watch views now follow saved routes with verified walking-frame changes, retain native death handling, and cannot resurrect from a dead roster record. A scoped lifetime hook prevents the engine from deleting owned guards merely because there is no active crime, without changing vanilla guard cleanup. Actual-player focused checks and native build checks pass. Installed bundle includes this infrastructure, but ordinary gameplay still uses vanilla guards until the roster controller, physical dispatch and combat integration are connected. See `GUARDS.md`.

Finite guard core checkpoint (14 October): schema 6 now persists fixed settlement rosters, nearest-reachable finite dispatch, game-time travel/search/return, death positions and bounded replacement identities. All 140 core tests and native Unity serialization/migration checks pass. Invalid dispatch requests are transactional; empty reports can retry, while assigned reports cannot summon extra replacements. The runtime adapter is not connected yet, so installed 0.3.3 still uses vanilla guards. Next work is physical roster actors and interception of native reinforcement paths, then actual-player pursuit, shop arrival and escape tests. See `GUARDS.md`.

Sleeping expansion 0.3.2 (14 October): separate male-385 and female-453 five-view atlases now support unshared home beds. Both use green blankets and white pillows. Build, actual-player bedtime/waking/toggle/raycast/death/save-reload tests for both appearances, existing street/interior/family checks and ordinary startup pass. SAVE21 adds the female preview. Visual review exposed an oversized end-view billboard; a failing viewport regression now passes after sizing/centering is bounded by the bed. Full appearance coverage, shared beds and other sleep activities remain incomplete; see `SLEEPING-CHECKPOINT.md`.

Priority amendment (14 October): close the bounded automatic delivery checkpoint below, then resume M2/M3 gameplay and animation implementation. Broad main/side/mod-quest compatibility remains required but is deferred to its own expansion milestone; do not keep expanding quest research before delivering visible population behavior. Next work is wider appearance-specific sleeping coverage and shared-family beds, followed by child mobility/local play and identity-preserving adult walking/running/combat/casting. Continue routines, native home/shop occupants and tavern lodging alongside the relevant animation sets. Every required adult appearance and the twelve child variants remain tracked requirements, not optional polish. Test usable slices in the actual player instead of waiting for all art to be finished.

Automatic quest pilot checkpoint (14 October): normal native parsing now binds the reviewed, unchanged A0C00Y14 local recipient to an existing Gothway Garden household before offer text. The native social acceptance flow, package creation, real SAVE20 reload, journal/recipient agreement, hand-in and 40-gold collection pass. The new ninth menu control, `General/QuestHouseholds`, stops new or pending bindings when disabled while preserving accepted contracts. Modified quest sources and other quests retain native behavior. This closes the initial pilot, not broad quest compatibility. Latest combined runtime evidence: `work/quest-auto-runtime-pass.log`; standalone startup also passes. No engine files changed.

Shipped quest checkpoint (14 October): unchanged vanilla A0C00Y14 now passes native package generation, SAVE20 reload, journal/recipient name agreement, actual item hand-in and collection of its gold reward. This is an explicitly bound test of one randomized local delivery branch using native handlers, not automatic production eligibility or broad quest compatibility. No engine or quest source files changed. See `QUEST-IDENTITY.md` and `TEST-FIXTURES.md`.

Native offer checkpoint (14 October): explicitly staged bindings now pass native guild offer refusal, dead-recipient rejection before acceptance text, valid acceptance/commit, actual native target placement/name/activation, and SAVE19 reconstruction with repeated activation. Core tests pass 119/119. The standard `.dfmod` contains scoped runtime hooks and dependency notices; no engine files change. Editor manifest library discovery is verified through DFU's loader. Automatic quest-role classification, broad quest compatibility and item hand-in remain open. See `QUEST-IDENTITY.md` for evidence and exact limits.

Quest actor ownership now suppresses the generated representation while a bound native quest controls the resident. Actual nighttime placement and save/load show no household duplicate, and ending the native quest restores the normal scheduled actor. Native quest appearance, mobile schedules and sleeping remain pending; this is a tested placement priority, not completed radiant quest integration.

Persistent quest ledger checkpoint (14 October): schema 4 now stores immutable published person/home contracts with native quest UID/symbol, stable resident ID and rule provenance. Offer preparation has no persistent side effects; acceptance commits atomically and rejects conflicting contracts or newly deceased recipients. Schemas 1–3 migrate without population/route regeneration. Core tests pass 118/118; native serializer/build and actual SAVE19 quest-plus-mod save/load passed. The probe starts a synthetic quest directly; production guild acceptance, semantic eligibility, old accepted-quest reconciliation, household target actors and hand-in remain open. No production quest rewriting or engine change is enabled. See `QUEST-IDENTITY.md` for evidence and limitations.

Earlier quest binding checkpoint (14 October): a separate opt-in standard-mod probe intercepts native parsing before offer expansion, binds a synthetic procedural person/home name to an existing household, checks actual native prompt labels, fixed/partial/unrelated exclusions, a changed dialog destination, and native FullSerializer reconstruction. Persistent population remains unchanged during that pre-publication phase; patches and temporary package are removed. This proves an integration boundary, not production quest compatibility. A reproducible census covers 265 shipped quests and 125 NPC placement actions; see `QUEST-IDENTITY.md` and `QUEST-SOURCE-SURVEY.md`. Semantic eligibility and actual actor/hand-in integration remain open.

Sleeping prototype 0.3.1 (14 October): 97 core tests pass; native build checks cover all three classic bed meshes and a five-view blanket atlas. The runtime now places one supported adult male appearance in unshared home beds and restores the same standing actor on waking or disabling sleep poses. A new native setting controls these poses. Shared-bed rendering, remaining appearances, original NPC binding, bed approach and tavern booking remain incomplete. The generated art is a functional prototype, not native-resolution production coverage. See `SLEEPING-CHECKPOINT.md` for actual-player evidence and fixture limitations.

Population foundation implemented and reviewed. Full milestones remain incomplete.

M1 baseline probes are complete. The opt-in quest probe found and fixed a harness fidelity issue: direct interior entry skipped native discovery/open-state setup and hid vanilla occupants. All test entries now use the native handler; the actual-player family/street/quest run passes. Original questor data, name, binding and task behavior survive movement and reconstructed save/load. This supports retaining original components for upcoming sleep poses; cross-building proxies, actual beds/waking and broad quest/service behavior remain unverified.

The active goal's original text still mentions three residents; the user's later explicit five-person family limit above supersedes it. The goal tool permits status updates only, so this file and the amended design are the authoritative evolving scope. Installed version 0.3.0 now uses housing-aware families, a 1–5 creation cap and schema-3 saves. Legacy saved households remain unchanged.

- 87/87 behavior tests pass with `dotnet run --project tests/LivingCities.Tests`, including deterministic families, soft bed/size preferences, deceased parent links, transactional family validation, legacy adult migration, rejection of child adult-visit routes and the five-person settings bound.
- The Unity-independent core supports new families of up to five, saved surname/sex/age/partner/parent fields, deterministic bounded weighted routes, analytical work/travel/home/sleep schedules and persistent deaths. Existing v1/v2 residents remain adults with unchanged identities, appearances and deaths; saved v2 routes remain intact. Existing households are not rerolled by changed settings or housing profiles.
- `LivingCitiesBuild.VerifySaveState` passed actual Unity/DFU FullSerializer family round trips, raw legacy v1/v2 migration and adapter failure isolation (exit 0, `../work/family-save-validation.log`). Subsequent 0.3.0 build/install and actual-player family tests also passed. Read-only Luna/high reviews found no confirmed critical or important findings. Generated animation assets remain pending.
- Unity compiled the deployed sources. `LivingCitiesBuild.VerifyAndBuild` passed real DFU FullSerializer round-trip, adapter restore-failure isolation, API checks, and bundle reopen checks; exit code 0.
- The standard mod is installed in the isolated portable player. `tools/SmokeTest.ps1` verified its runtime source compilation and initialization alongside Basic Roads, then GameManager readiness. The owned process was stopped after the check.
- Read-only Luna/high reviews covered the foundation, street actors and routine/interior lifecycle. A House1 destination bug was found, reproduced by a failing Unity check, and fixed by validating actual opening windows. No further confirmed critical or important blocker was found in the reviewed changes; review does not replace runtime acceptance.
- Temporary Harmony probe intercepted both guard spawn entry points, including immediate/deferred batch calls and single-call return value, then removed its patches. Probe package removed after testing. This proves feasibility, not finite-guard gameplay.
- Seven native settings controls include the physical-population preview toggle. Unity verified the actual DFU settings deserializer; the player logged settings loaded. Existing residents/deaths survive capacity changes. Visual menu and live in-game adjustment checks remain pending.
- Real-town geometry adapter maps 156 building entrance anchors in Gothway Garden, conservatively rejecting 17 door candidates. Eleven sampled entrance-to-entrance A* routes pass in the actual player and after fixture reload. Door access checks include static collision sweeps, initial overlap rejection and a ground-floor height limit. Whole-town route connectivity, navigation around all props and performance profiling remain pending; this is not a guarantee that every route is traversable by an NPC.

Version 0.2.0 now connects physical adult actors to real doors in Gothway Garden only. The fixture has 324 resident schedules; 285 received visit routes in the initial survey and the rest remain home. Nearby exterior actors use 140/165-metre activation/removal radii and an 80-actor cap. Household surnames and individual appearances are deterministic. The runtime test observed 13 nearby actors and four distinct walking animation frames. Changing animation state only when needed fixes the reported gliding bug.

Following a sampled resident into building 13 finds the same name, appearance and ID. Exit/re-entry produces one active actor, a living-resident interior save reload preserves presence, and the actual entity death callback plus another save/reload keeps that resident dead. Schema 2 persists routes to avoid rebuilding against inactive exterior geometry; schema 1 migrates without regenerating residents. Legacy indoor saves without routes use home presence until their first exterior survey. Indoor positions currently use checked reachable standing anchors, not beds or activities. The preview leaves vanilla stationary quest/service actors untouched; their proper household/workplace mapping is still pending.

Version 0.3.0 extends this pilot with actual bed-model counts and exterior footprint preferences, family fields and home-only child actors. The new fixture has 402 residents including 118 children across 162 households. A five-person family, child name/appearance and relationships survive an interior reload. Children receive a brief greeting; a regression check ensures a later static NPC conversation is not intercepted. The legacy fixture still has 324 adults. Children currently stand at home, with no outdoor play or generated animations. Sleeping sprites for all NPC flats come first when actual home/tavern sleeping is implemented; bed alignment and waking transitions are acceptance gates.

Survey: 175 buildings, 162 candidate homes, 215 doors, and 33,143 walkable cells in a 256 by 192 grid. Candidates still need filtering for ownership, quest occupancy and residence suitability. Bed placement, complete offscreen/adjacent-chunk continuity, origin shifts, melee/death/crime integration, guard gameplay and LLM dialogue remain open. The old fixture's route cache is about 9.6 MB before compression; optimize it and profile large-city loading before broader release. The user prioritized functional milestones over curved turns and appearance polish.

Evidence: `work/unity-build-result.json`, `work/town-test.log`, historical `work/player-smoke.log`, sibling `../work/livingcities-build.log`. `tools/Build.ps1` checks build completion before reporting success. See `TEST-FIXTURES.md`, `INTEGRATION.md` and the separate `probes/guard` experiment.


Historical native damage completion prerequisite: per-hit finalizers preserve nested target scope and original exceptions. Actual patched-method checks pass in Unity. Runtime crime concealment, corpse cleanup and custody remain pending; installed 0.3.9 is unchanged.

Household routine locks: lock whenever no awake living owner is inside, including empty homes and homes containing only sleepers. Release this routine lock when someone inside wakes or an awake owner returns; relock if that owner leaves while others sleep. This supersedes fixed night locking. Witnessed unauthorized entry is reported as trespassing through bounded citizen alarms and physical guard response. Use authoritative location/sleep state, not actor visibility. See HOUSEHOLD-LOCKS.md; implementation pending.

Taverns require separate keepers, employees, local patrons and visiting travelers, without a common surname imposed on all. Paid bookings may sell out; no player bed is guaranteed. Local evening/night patrons may socialize inside and outside, leaving as late as about 03:00. Travelers respect gate closures in walled cities; unwalled settlements allow any-hour travel. All routines need constrained deterministic time variation and spaced waiting positions at closed destinations. See TAVERN-LODGING.md; implementation pending.

Housing report verified: SAVE28 is native House2 despite sharing model 249 with a tavern. All three actual town taverns are excluded from profiles and saved households. SAVE34 provides a clearer residential three-person family fixture. See TEST-FIXTURES.md.
