<a id="source-npc-integration-audit-md"></a>

## Original: NPC-INTEGRATION-AUDIT.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# NPC integration inventory and compatibility plan

Audited 17 October 2026 against the local source, installed mod configuration, extracted bundle metadata, and DFU 1.1.1 source. This is a source audit, not proof that every actor in every settlement has been exercised in the player. Historical checkpoint documents describe successive states; this report distinguishes current code from proposed integration.

## Main findings

**Future-policy follow-up, 17 October 2026:** see the [TL;DR roadmap](ROADMAP.md), [consolidated requirements](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design) and [delivery plan](ROADMAP.md#delivery-plan). Those describe requested housing priorities, inn visitors/shared beds, tenant keys, market commutes, quest-safe story context and shelved companions. They do not change the implemented coverage recorded below.

There is no universal NPC adoption system yet. Generated household residents, generated visitors, ordinary native interior people, and quest-owned actors follow different paths. Outdoor authored NPCs from external mods are not automatically integrated. Registering an identity does not establish a home, commute, sleep schedule, or complete movement art.

| Population category | Current behavior | Home and beds | Missing coverage |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Generated local residents | Persistent household identity, schedules, visits, reactions and physical actor paths | Household beds; bounded family capacity | Known interior movement and observed-arrival problems; not every routine completes reliably |
| Generated travelers | Separate trip/admission state and local representations; home absence is tracked | Reserved origin household and destination lodging | Full long-distance journey and all interior layouts still need gameplay acceptance |
| Native shopkeepers | Ordinary StaticNPC registered; original service identity retained; death/reaction handling | On-site free bed first, then an unoccupied bed in an existing residential household; on-site keeper family can fill remaining capacity | Off-site housing does not provide an implemented daily commute |
| Authored inn room occupants | Native room occupants are identified from room survey and block people records | Occupied room beds are reserved and unavailable to rent; supported sleep art is used at night | Not classified as local or foreign; no origin household/travel biography follows from the room reservation |
| Other inn common-room flats | Eligible ordinary interior NPCs register | No blanket home or rented-room assignment | Not all are inn residents, generated customers, or travelers; role classification needed |
| Ordinary guild/temple/castle staff | Eligible ordinary interior StaticNPC identities register | No general staff-home or institutional-bed allocator | General sleep, commute and staff duties absent |
| Individual-faction, quest-resource or action NPCs | Excluded from generic interior registration/reconciliation | Native placement remains authoritative | General mobile/sleeping quest integration absent |
| Outdoor authored/mod market NPCs | Not adopted by the indoor registration paths | No general home reservation before generated families | Full identity, housing, reactions, routines and lifecycle integration required |
| Guards managed by this mod | Separate finite roster and duty/response system | Wall-bed reservation/rest paths exist | Bedside idle is not a completed lying-down guard animation; broader wall-layout acceptance remains open |

Sources: `mod/Scripts/KeeperIdentities.cs` (`SurveyKeeperIdentities`, `KeeperSleeping`, `UpdateKeeperSleep`), `NativeInnOccupants.cs` (`Claim`), `NativeTavernRentals.cs`, `ResidentInnActors.cs`, `StreetPopulation.cs`; `src/LivingCities.Core/NativePeople.cs` (`AssignNativeBed`, `ReserveNativeBed`, `CreateKeeperHousehold`) and `WorldState.cs` (`GetOrCreateFamilies`).

## Housing order is not yet sufficient

`GetOrCreateFamilies` populates surveyed residential homes. Later, `SurveyKeeperIdentities` registers eligible indoor people. `ReserveNativeBed` refuses occupied family slots; it does not displace a generated resident. The current system therefore does **not** first reserve housing for all authored citizens, stall owners, guild staff and temple staff before filling the town.

The separate traveler-origin reservation mechanism preserves previously reserved households during partial-town completion. It is not an outdoor native-NPC census or a solution for market workers.

Proposed allocation order for a new town:

1. Read effective world-data replacements and enumerate authored people, including initially inactive people.
2. Classify identity, quest ownership, service role, apparent workplace and known residence. Do not assume workplace equals home.
3. Preserve explicit authored room occupants, existing saved homes, player-owned properties and active quest placements.
4. Allocate reachable institutional staff beds where appropriate; otherwise reserve real residential capacity for local workers.
5. Classify remaining inn occupants as staff, local patrons, lodgers or visitors using explicit policy. A static inn placement alone proves none of those origins.
6. Generate additional households only in remaining capacity. Preserve existing saves through explicit migration; never silently replace families or identities.

Institutions do not universally contain usable beds. Count actual loaded/replaced geometry, accessible floors and routes. If capacity is absent, record the person as needing housing; do not invent beds or move them into arbitrary occupied homes. Visitor status needs a persistent origin identity, not a convenient excuse for missing housing.

## Quest compatibility

### What works now

The automatic household binding is one deliberately narrow rule: unchanged vanilla `A0C00Y14` (The Delivery Boy), `_pickuplocal_`, Gothway Garden. `KnownQuestBindings.cs` checks exact source hash, settlement and role. Individual NPCs, questors and incompatible households are excluded. The offer adapter preserves quest UID, source name, person symbol, home map/building and published identity. Active contracts suppress duplicate generated actors.

The recorded native identity probe demonstrates that moving the **original** StaticNPC within an interior can preserve click-task activation and save/load identity. It does not prove arbitrary cross-building movement, sleeping, streaming replacement, all quests, or main-quest support.

### Why moving a quest NPC can break more than convenience

DFU matches questors using `hash`, `mapID`, `nameSeed` and `buildingKey` (`QuestMachine.IsNPCDataEqual`). A matching name or sprite is insufficient. `StaticNPC` derives some runtime data from the current player location. Recreating that person in another building can therefore change identity unless an adapter preserves the original binding.

`Person.GetDialogPlace` prefers the quest-assigned place. Quests can place, hide, mute, restrain, restore or replace resources. `QuestResourceBehaviour` controls activation and foe death/injury events. A routine controller that unhides a quest-hidden person, deletes a foe, or creates a new generic actor can invalidate the plot.

Fresh local script census: 265 vanilla quest `.txt` files, of which 34 have the main-quest `S000` prefix and 231 have other names. These are script counts, not distinct NPC counts or completed compatibility tests.

| Quest mechanic | Concrete local examples | Required preservation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Delivery and reward | `A0C00Y14` | Published person/place, carried package, click handler, reward and completion |
| Fixed main-story people | `S0000003` Medora/Nulfaga; `S0000004` Morgiah; `S0000005` Nulfaga/Aubk-i | Individual identity, authored relationships, questor/mute transitions and required clicks |
| Human/monster substitution | `S0000007` Cyndassa/werewolf; `$CUREWER`; `A0C0XY04` The Impostor | Separate Person/Foe resources, hide/place sequence and native kill conditions; not a generic citizen transformation |
| Escort and relocation | `A0C00Y17` | Quest-directed destination and placement over normal home routine |
| Named relatives and service destinations | `A0C01Y09` | Explicit relationship and merchant-at-store assignment, not inferred household surname |
| Hunt/assassination | `M0B00Y00` and other kill tasks | Correct target, intended hostility and actual native death notification |
| Timed/restricted encounters | Quest clocks, assigned places, location conditions | Player can still reach the required target; schedule does not override scripted timing |

Native sources: `Assets/Scripts/Game/StaticNPC.cs`, `Questing/QuestMachine.cs`, `Questing/Person.cs`, `Questing/Components/QuestResourceBehaviour.cs`; quest assets under `Assets/StreamingAssets/Quests`. External bundle sources remain local and are not redistributed here.

### Proposed ownership policy

Use quest roles and capabilities, not a blanket main/side classification. A side-quest giver may be as fragile as a royal main-quest actor. An ordinary NPC may become quest-owned later.

- Quest identity and scripted placement/visibility/hostility take priority over routines.
- One persistent person has one active representation. Preserve original native activation and service bindings.
- Permit same-room sleep/movement only after that role's adapter passes native activation, save/load and quest transition checks.
- Cross-building movement requires a tested identity/placement adapter and consistent directions. Otherwise retain native placement until supported.
- Re-evaluate ownership when quests start, advance, relocate, finish or abort; do not classify once at initial spawn.
- Respect the user's policy allowing deliberate player kills even when a quest becomes impossible. Background simulation must not silently kill or replace important actors. Current generic exclusions do not implement universal quest-NPC mortality.
- Do not fabricate quest success, revive a dead target, or transfer a dead person's quest to an unrelated replacement citizen. Native quest failure/death semantics remain authoritative.

## Installed mods and art

| Mod | Verified contribution | Integration consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lively Cities 1.3.4 | Static exterior NPCs; registers archives 1200 and 10015; visibility callbacks for day/night/rain | No walking-home integration. Coordinate visibility ownership before adoption; its broad exterior callback can toggle adopted actors too |
| Beautiful Cities 0.5.0 / Beautiful Villages 1.4.2 | Settlement/world-data replacements | Census must use the effective loaded override. Do not attribute every market actor to Lively Cities |
| Taverns Redone 0.17 | Replaced inn layouts | Existing room survey can consume native block records, but full patron navigation is not accepted for every layout |
| Elves & Beastfolk Resources 1.1 / Replaces 1.0 | NPC appearance replacements, including 182/33 and mobile portrait asset `TFAC00I0.RCI_451-0` | Archive/record alone may select a vanilla sleeper for a replaced appearance. Portrait replacement is not proof of a new mobile animation set |
| Flat Replacer 0.2.19 | Replacement framework | Resolve actual appearance/provider before assigning animation art |
| Detailed City Walls 1.23 | Wall geometry/interiors | Guard quarters need real-bed and route survey |
| Rosy's Residences | Installed but disabled | Not an active source of present NPC placements; Rosy's Resources remains enabled |
| Families of Daggerfall / Lootable Villagers | Removed | Not sources of current actors; retained download archives are not active mods |

Lively texture candidates include 1200 records 14,15,17,19,53,54; 1210 records 8–20; 1230/21. These are bundle texture records, **not** a verified list of placed humans. Archive 10015 is registered but needs effective-source resolution. Decorative statues, crops, props and editor markers must not become citizens just because a texture looks humanoid.

Current art inventory:

- Native mobile citizens: 24 archives, 381–398 and 451–456. Five unique walking directions mirror to eight bearings. Record 5 provides two frontal idle frames; complete directional standing art is not accepted.
- Sleeping: current `SleepArtCatalog` contains 215 source mappings backed by 191 distinct generated appearances, including all 24 mobile appearances and many stationary flats. Eight requested source records remain absent: 184/5–184/12. Art availability does not supply a routine or bed.
- Sleeping runtime uses the full catalog, not only the historical 385/453 pilot. Unknown source identities receive no substitute face.
- Lively archives 1200 and 10015 have no sleep mappings. New/replaced appearances need inspected references and explicit provider-aware mapping.
- Running: archive 385 has a rejected pilot available for a bounded preview. It is not accepted all-NPC coverage. Stationary NPCs generally lack complete walking/running cycles.
- A stationary flat cannot become a proper walking animation merely by translating the billboard. Retain its native interactive identity while adding a separate directional renderer, once approved art exists.

Sources: `SleepArtCatalog.cs`, `SleepingArt.cs`, `art/sleep/coverage.json`, `art/standing/README.md`, `art/run/pilot-385/README.md`, installed bundle manifests and local asset lists.

## Required census and acceptance work

The category/source inventory above is complete for this audit. An exhaustive live census of every settlement and every active/inactive actor is **not** complete. No claim of universal compatibility follows from source inspection.

Record these fields for each distinct placed actor: stable map/block/building/record identity; effective provider/override; native hash and name seed; faction/service role; active quest UID/symbol and individual faction; appearance provider/archive/record; authored home/workplace; assigned bed; enabled state and visibility owner; available walk/run/idle/sleep assets; adoption status and exclusion reason. Never persist Unity instance IDs as identity.

Prioritized next work:

- [ ] Add incremental live census/export and compare source/replacement provenance, including disabled actors.
- [ ] Introduce a unified identity/ownership registry and migration design before generic adoption.
- [ ] Allocate authored-local housing before generated population, including staff and stall owners.
- [ ] Resolve inn occupant roles and institutional beds without assuming every interior has housing.
- [ ] Adapt external visibility callbacks for adopted actors only.
- [ ] Preserve native quest/service identity through same-interior movement/sleep; then test cross-building adapters.
- [ ] Build appearance coverage from actual rendered/replaced assets; prioritize common stall/staff flats before unique story actors.
- [ ] Test offer, rejection, acceptance, directions, journal, arrival, sleep/wake, hand-in, reward, hostility, injury/death, relocation, abort, completion, save/reload and concurrent quests.
- [ ] Test fresh and existing saves, mod toggle/load-order changes, dawn/dusk/rain, murder/creature reactions, housing capacity and large-city frame times.

Quest Pack 1 and Quest Actions Extension are included in the separate [additional quest audit](COMPATIBILITY.md#quests). Implementation should proceed by tested role adapters; generic movement of all authored NPCs is not yet safe.

<a id="source-npc-local-avoidance-md"></a>

## Original: NPC-LOCAL-AVOIDANCE.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Physical space and local avoidance

User requirement, 14 October 2026: NPCs occupy physical space. Walking residents must pass around each other rather than overlap or walk through one another. Apply this to citizens, children, travelers and guards, including normal routines, flight and pursuit.

Use local avoidance over collision-safe navigation routes. Preserve walls and other static obstacles while providing enough lateral clearance for the actors' footprints. In narrow passages or doorways where passing is impossible, agents must slow, wait and yield with a stable priority rule. Do not solve a conflict by pushing another actor through a wall or teleporting it past the obstruction. Queues and social gatherings need separate reachable standing positions and must leave door access clear. Waiting for shops to open uses the same spacing policy.

Keep avoidance local and bounded for large populations, using nearby spatial queries rather than every NPC comparing with every other NPC. The persistent schedule remains authoritative for intended activities, but actual progress must account for short delays and avoid snapping a visible actor back to its ideal timetable position. Save/load and distance-based materialization must not introduce overlapping actors at doors or route origins. Player interaction and collision should be checked alongside NPC-to-NPC behavior so crowds do not trap the player indefinitely.

Acceptance: two opposing walkers in an open street; overtaking a slower walker; opposing traffic at a narrow door; groups entering and leaving a busy tavern; shoppers waiting for opening; stationary conversation groups; fleeing citizens crossing guard reinforcements; spawn/reload into a busy area; profiling with hundreds of scheduled NPCs. Current ResidentActor.Place directly assigns scheduled positions and does not implement this requirement. This document records pending work, not a completed feature.

<a id="source-outdoor-crime-scenes-md"></a>

## Original: OUTDOOR-CRIME-SCENES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Outdoor corpse discovery and investigation

## Current runtime evidence

The development bundle now connects outdoor discovery and absence cleanup, finite responders and patrol views. OutdoorSceneProbe passes actual-player discovery, cleanup, harmless return/examination, native serialization and patrol state. CrimeSceneProbe separately passes the revised indoor warning, Trespassing surrender and real save/reload. Evidence: work/OutdoorSceneProbe-pass.log and work/CrimeSceneWarning-review-pass.log. Current core suite: 176 tests. Actual corpse inventory/loot is still absent. Citizens can notify nearby guards but do not yet make a dedicated trip to report an outdoor corpse. Full end-to-end outdoor patrol/save/reload and all witness combinations remain open.

## Latest approved policy

The user approved a revised policy after the initial amendments below: mere presence at discovery is not evidence of murder either. Examining a body is harmless; only witnessed taking of belongings triggers Theft. Later arrival remains innocent. Known murderers retain their existing witnessed-murder accusation. Unidentified visitors to an indoor scene receive ten seconds of unpaused real time to leave; refusal causes Trespassing and native surrender/arrest. This supersedes both the first-discovery-presence accusation and the earlier immediate indoor Murder accusation. Searching outdoor guards do not converse. Placeholder corpses currently have no inventory, so there is no actual item-taking action yet; do not charge for examining them.

Historical initial amendment (superseded on accusation and examination by the approved policy above): outdoor cleanup requires discovery followed by departure. The initial request treated presence at discovery as Murder and any corpse interaction as Theft; neither of those accusation rules remains approved. One-hour investigation and unseen-body persistence remain required.

Implementation sequence:

1. Add a persistent, validated first-discovery record to outdoor corpses, with observer identity and time. Explicit observation of player absence authorizes cleanup only after discovery. Keep existing cleared legacy bodies cleared, and never invent witnesses on restore. Cover unseen bodies, presence, departure, return, time rollback, observer validity and save/load with core tests.
2. Connect nearby living citizens and finite roster guards to actual line-of-sight detection. Use a documented local scene radius to determine player presence; entering an interior or leaving the settlement counts as leaving the outdoor area. Preserve discovered incidents after body cleanup so cleanup does not cancel the guard response.
3. Dispatch only existing available guards over surveyed collision-safe routes. Preserve responder identity, first arrival and a one-hour search deadline. Charge Murder only when the saved discovery/witness evidence identifies the player, then offer native surrender on physical contact. Later passersby are not suspects. Patrol reachable local points, block conversation during the search, then return; repeat observations and reloads must not renew the hour. Witness reporting must respect physical reporting rather than instantly informing the whole roster. Add witnessed-corpse-interaction Theft separately when corpse activation is connected; retain an existing more serious known crime.
4. Verify native serialization and actual-player discovery, departure cleanup, finite arrival, on-site surrender, absent-player patrol/expiry and reload. Keep automated tests mouse-unlocked. Update installed version and docs only after the runtime checks.

Household routine locks remain the next separate feature after this amendment. Do not describe this plan or core-only state as completed outdoor gameplay.

## Core checkpoint

The core now records the first known living observer, discovery time and whether the player was present at that first observation. Later observations cannot rewrite that evidence. Unseen outdoor corpses no longer use elapsed-day cleanup; explicit player absence after discovery clears them. Already-cleared legacy bodies remain cleared. Indoor cleanup remains unchanged. Restore rejects unknown observers, invalid chronology and incompatible indoor discovery records.

Finite rosters can retain a collision-surveyed approach and closed local patrol loop, repeat that loop for exactly one game hour after each responder arrives, then return to their original posts. Repeated reports and restore preserve deadlines and arrival evidence. The caller supplies surveyed geometry; core tests do not prove runtime collision safety. Native FullSerializer checks verify discovery, cleanup, position and patrol timing. Core suite: 170 tests.

CleanupVersion 1 marks newly confirmed bodies. Unseen version-1 exterior cleanup is rejected on restore. Version-0 bodies already cleared by 0.3.12 remain cleared; uncleared legacy bodies upgrade to the new policy. This preserves historical saves without permitting the old one-day rule for newly created outdoor bodies. Restore also checks approach endpoints against search destinations and rejects null guard observers through validation.

Migration validation is not tamper-proof provenance: deliberately relabeling an edited record as version 0 makes it a legacy record. This is the explicit compatibility branch, not a claim that arbitrary save edits can be detected. The current runtime always creates version-1 records.

Historical core-only checkpoint: runtime integration was pending at that stage. Use the current runtime evidence above for installed behavior and remaining limits.

Diagnostic routes were valid; readiness was delayed by scheduling (work/ApproachBudget-building-red.log, work/ApproachBudget-detail-red.log). ## Initial guard preparation slices, 16 October

Finer instrumentation identified initial guard setup as the population-refresh spike: 554.54 ms, including a 249.53 ms public-post survey and a 162.25 ms entrance survey (`work/GuardSetup-profile-pass.log`). New exterior guard setup prepares both surveys incrementally before creating the roster controller. Public-post selection and complete entrance dictionaries publish only when finished; changing settlement or clearing the controller cancels preparation. Existing synchronous survey callers remain supported.

Native comparison preserves 273 selected public posts and 617 usable entrances across 465 yielded batches, and cancellation leaves the public-post result unpublished. The complete gate positioning, facing, dusk recall, wall patrol and morning-return regression passes (`work/GuardSetupSlices-gates-pass.log`). Core 436/436 and Unity build pass (`work/GuardSetupSlices-core.log`, `work/GuardSetupSlices-build.log`).

The active timing sample reports 467 preparation advances totaling 450.36 ms with a 7.80 ms maximum advance. Initial guard setup falls to 130.96 ms; initial maximum frame is 211.92 ms. Steady frame p99 is 38.25 ms, max 45.36 ms; steady guards max 5.15 ms and gate duty max 0.02 ms (`work/GuardSetupSlices-performance-pass.log`). Remaining initial gate batches still reach 155.27 ms and inn slices 29.05 ms. Full-world load is not established. The saved-interior-to-exterior deferred post survey and urgent synchronous refuge fallbacks are still separate unsliced paths; this change specifically covers new exterior controller setup. Guard readiness now waits for complete surveys rather than exposing partial post/entrance data.

## Shared work-start spacing, 16 October

Road searches, unloaded settlement grid-route requests, exterior inn-survey starts and gate-duty batches now share `BackgroundWork`/`WorkStartGate`. Actual starts are separated by at least 100 ms of unscaled game time. The next slot is based on the actual admission time, so a stalled frame cannot release a backlog simultaneously. Waiting coroutines do no blocking sleep; cancelled requests do not acquire a slot. Existing sliced jobs continue across frames, so this is start spacing, not a global per-frame CPU budget or a guarantee that active jobs never overlap. Combat, actor motion and immediate crime reactions are not gated.

Verification: 436/436 core tests, Unity build, full resident-trip suite, Daggerfall gate/wall-shift suite and all three Gothway inn lifecycle/rental suites pass. Concurrent native starts were 101.43 ms apart. Evidence: `work/WorkStagger-core.log`, `work/WorkStagger-build.log`, `work/WorkStagger-native-pass.log`, `work/WorkStagger-gates-pass.log`, `work/WorkStagger-inns-pass.log`.

Active performance sample (`work/WorkStagger-performance-pass.log`): steady frame p99 37.18 ms, maximum 48.27 ms; steady gate duty maximum 0.02 ms; steady guard update maximum 5.82 ms. Initial inn slices maximum 29.53 ms and gate duty maximum 156.05 ms remain substantial. The initial frame maximum was 648.42 ms, with a 573.11 ms population-refresh call; that initialization path needs finer instrumentation and slicing. These are one-fixture measurements, not proof of a causal frame-time improvement or a finished-world maximum. Do not compare the initial maximum directly with older windows that excluded scene/population construction.

## Overland route planning, 16 October

The new road planning service starts after a 100 ms offset and advances at most 64 search work steps per frame by default. Queue pops (including stale entries) and reconstruction points yield; context invalidation suppresses completion callbacks. Native comparison matches the synchronous route while advancing over multiple frames. Core 406/406, Unity build and the resident trip native suite pass (`work/RoadSlices-native-pass.log`). This prevents future automatic departures from requiring a fully synchronous route search, but the producer is not yet connected. Work count is not a hard millisecond cap; initial provider import and final path reversal remain synchronous. No new world-wide frame-time claim is made.

## 16 October baseline and current work

The user reports recurring short freezes and requests settlement-wide coverage, sparse overland travelers, and staggered updates. Native city/town/village categories are already admitted by the population adapter. Full world-wide resident-owned journeys are not yet connected; a city sample is not a maximum-load benchmark for the finished system.

`TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -PerformanceProbe` samples a noon Daggerfall scene for initial and steady-state windows. Command-line-only `RuntimeTiming` measures population refresh, streets, guards, gate duty and inn inventory. Normal play does not record timings or allocate timing samples.

The first active baseline (`work/Performance-city-native.log`) found recurring guard calls around 140–160 ms. Initial inn inventories also took 55–251 ms. Steady-state frame p99 was about 174 ms. The focused second run confirms gate duty accounts for the guard stall: the same eight outside-to-inside routes were rebuilt every second. Its later window became paused and is invalid as a performance comparison (`work/Performance-gate-native-invalid-paused.log`); the probe now rejects paused samples.

Verified active comparison (`work/Performance-cache-native.log`): caching each fixed gate-pair return route reduced steady-state gate-duty maximum from approximately 150 ms to 0.04 ms. Frame p99 fell from approximately 174 ms to 41.04 ms; maximum steady-state frame time was 49.46 ms. Initial loading still reached 1137.95 ms. This is one fixture, not a world-wide performance guarantee. Gate shift behavior still needs its dedicated regression after caching.

The inn survey no longer builds an unused whole-inn navigation flood just to locate entrance/bed-adjacent floors. Door-separated bedroom surveys remain. Exterior inventories now start after a 100 ms offset and yield between rooms and navigation slices. Results are published only after the complete survey; entering a building or changing settlement cancels and disposes the temporary model. Native comparisons match synchronous room, rental-marker and occupant data in all three Gothway inns (23, 12 and 16 slices). Rental import, payment, availability and occupied-bed checks pass (`work/InnSlices-final-native.log`). Sleeping/waking passes for one supported occupant; two occupants use documented missing art (184/12 and 184/10). The probe reports those gaps explicitly rather than substituting another face. Its earlier failure was this existing art gap, not a partition discrepancy. Core 399/399 and Unity build pass.

The subsequent active Daggerfall run (`work/InnSlices-performance-native.log`) records 494 survey slices totaling 1108.83 ms, maximum 30.51 ms. The 2 ms flood-yield target is not a hard cap: model creation and other partition work remain synchronous. Initial frame p99 is 46.87 ms; steady-state p99 is 39.45 ms and maximum is 47.08 ms. Recurring gate duty remains at 0.04 ms maximum. Initial gate setup still costs 1126.96 ms (maximum initial frame 1141.84 ms) and needs separate incremental work. Garbage collections remain frequent (424 gen-0 collections in the 30-second steady window), so allocation reduction is still open.

The user's 100 ms staggering proposal is retained for periodic background work. Do not delay input, combat or actor rendering, and do not use blocking sleeps on Unity's main thread. Delaying an indivisible 150 ms job alone cannot remove its frame spike. Large jobs need bounded slices or cached results as well.

The dedicated gate regression also passes (`work/Performance-gate-native.log`): all four gates retain four finite guards, correct inner/outer posts and facing, physical recall before 18:00, eight wall shifts, indoor time progression, visible wall patrol and morning return. This supersedes the pending gate-behavior check above.

Next acceptance: reduce initial gate setup and remaining survey slices; then measure more settlements, growing persisted state, moving actors and actual road travelers. Track loading cost separately from steady-state cost and never interpret idle/paused frames as successful optimization.

## Gate setup staggering follow-up

Gate assignments now advance between posts/pairs with 100 ms spacing. The initial synchronous pass combined eight route assignments and eight wall plans: the measured diagnostic maximum was 1105.19 ms, including 480.46 ms in wall planning. The first staggered comparison reduced this to 295.22 ms. Approach and wall-refuge surveys now also share loaded settlement entrance geometry; `Clear()` invalidates that cache.

Each recall and its wall-duty plan still commit in one frame. An experimental yield inside that commit would let dusk consume an incomplete recall, or let entering an inn cancel it. Review caught this before acceptance; production drains that particular survey synchronously. The reusable refuge survey iterator supports sliced callers and deterministic disposal, but production wall assignment does not yet claim a hard per-frame budget. A persisted preparation stage is required before safely deferring that commit.

Native testing exposed and repaired another boundary: changing a gate post invalidates and stops an in-progress building-approach survey immediately, before it can publish routes from old posts. The first red run is `work/GateSlices-approach-red-native.log`. The final gate regression passes (`work/GateStagger-final-native.log`), including all previous positions/recall/wall-shift checks plus sliced-versus-synchronous wall route equivalence and canceled-model disposal. Core remains 399/399; Unity build passes (`work/GateStagger-arrival-build.log`).

The fixture now waits for assignment and advances to the latest authoritative initial arrival before checking stationed actors. Its old noon jump occurred before staggered assignments finished, so later guards correctly needed up to an hour of game time to arrive. Diagnostics confirmed an unpaused HUD with valid future arrivals (`work/GateStagger-wait-native.log`); the dusk test still observes physical walking. No production movement speed or arrival deadline was shortened to satisfy the test.

Final active comparison (`work/GateStagger-final-performance-native.log`): initial gate-duty maximum 159.65 ms, down from 1105.19 ms; initial measured frame maximum 170.95 ms, down from approximately 1142 ms. Steady-state gate maximum is 0.02 ms and frame p99 is 41.65 ms. The 250 ms gate-slice acceptance check fails on the baseline and passes on this build. This is a large reduction, not elimination of setup hitches; individual route/wall work and allocations still need improvement. The probe's sampling window does not include every part of initial scene construction.
# 17 October: current-layout Daggerfall restore

The fresh `People - Daggerfall day` save completed three 60-second timing samples with all 16 gate assignments and eight wall plans ready. Frame p99 was 23.58, 21.82 and 23.19 ms; maximum frames were 494.84, 541.84 and 629.99 ms. The prior multi-second guard-restore block did not recur. Travel setup still produced a 615.81 ms `ReadSteps` spike, so this is not a claim of stutter-free performance.

Evidence: `work/FreshDaggerfall-performance-20261017.log`. The timing probe passed, but the subsequent daily-itinerary probe failed because it required schema 19 while the installed save schema was 20. That assertion now compares against the current schema and the serialized source version; the full sequence still needs rerunning after the dialogue integration build.

## Original: QUEST-IDENTITY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Quest identity integration probe

Verified on 14 October 2026 against the installed DFU 1.1.1 player. This is baseline feasibility evidence, not a replacement quest system or complete quest compatibility.

## Current checkpoint: automatic local delivery pilot

Normal gameplay now installs the native parser hook and enables one explicit rule: unchanged vanilla `A0C00Y14` (The Delivery Boy), local recipient `_pickuplocal_`, Gothway Garden only. The native quest-selected house must already have a living adult of the recipient's gender. Existing resident identity and surname supply the pre-offer name; destination, quest UID, symbols and native tasks remain unchanged. Partial parses, fixed individuals, questors, other towns and other quests are excluded. A SHA-256 check over UTF-8 source lines joined with LF (`3423FFF5B75ABA8C8C8240C850A5096FAED612ACC0F3349E9241F586B2D69CAC`) rejects modified sources, including quest mods overriding the same name. Rule provenance is `vanilla/A0C00Y14/local-recipient/v1`.

The ninth native setting, `General/QuestHouseholds`, defaults true. Disabling blocks new selection and acceptance of a previously staged binding. Already accepted contracts retain published names and resident ownership, including after reload. Unaccepted plans do not alter persistent population; weak references avoid retaining abandoned quest previews indefinitely.

Actual-player combined verification passed native social offer acceptance, native package creation, SAVE20 reload, matching journal and recipient names, native hand-in and collection of 40 gold. Recipient: Elyzyssa Buckingham, resident `s:206728581:h:260:r:1`, quest UID 33554466. The fixture selects an eligible native quest and uses native offer/message handlers; it does not test random social-quest selection or mouse raycasting. It also verifies disabled selection, disabling between staging and acceptance, changed-source exclusion, partial previews and accepted-contract retention while disabled. Existing street/interior/death/family and synthetic offer/ledger tests pass in the same run. A test-camera helper now explicitly applies the native yaw/pitch transforms for unattended family visibility checks, as the sleep probe already does; normal input/camera behavior is unchanged.

Reproduce with `tools/Build.ps1 -QuestBindingProbe` followed by `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -QuestBindingProbe -DeliveryProbe`. Both exit 0. Evidence: `work/quest-auto-native-pass.log`, `work/quest-auto-runtime-pass.log`; ordinary startup without the temporary probe passes in `work/player-smoke.log`. The initial automatic-binding assertion and the staged-offer toggle regression each failed before their fixes (`work/quest-auto-binding-red.log`, `work/quest-toggle-staged-red.log`). Read-only Luna/high review found no remaining critical/important toggle defect. Core code remains unchanged from the 119/119 checkpoint.

This bounded quest pilot is closed. Per the user's priority amendment, return to sleeping, population activities and complete appearance-specific animation coverage now. Broad main/side/mod quest compatibility, rumors/building labels, remote recipients and native quest actor appearance/mobility/sleep remain open. The historical checkpoints below describe earlier stages and do not override this current status.

## Reproduce

Run `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -QuestProbe` from the Living Cities repository. The runner starts its own player, requires the disposable character naming convention, and refuses to interrupt an existing player. Normal gameplay never starts the probe. The harness disables live input only in this owned test process; native activation methods are invoked directly where needed.

The run first verifies existing population, street, interior, death and family behavior. It then enters a real Gothway Garden tavern through the native building-entry handler, binds an original adult StaticNPC to a minimal parsed quest, and checks:

- Original complete NPCData and display name remain unchanged after an in-place transform move.
- QuestResourceBehaviour retains quest UID, symbol and Person resource; ActiveQuestor recognizes the same NPC.
- The actual PlayerActivate.StaticNPCClick handler triggers a reset `clicked npc` quest task before and after movement.
- Changing any of hash, mapID, nameSeed or buildingKey fails native identity equality, establishing the matching contract's negative controls.
- Quest hide/show suppresses and restores the original actor without replacing identity.
- An actual save/load preserves quest UID and task state, reconstructs exactly one matching StaticNPC with a different Unity instance ID, restores binding/name/data, and accepts a new native quest click.

The passing fixture uses Vannolda Woodston in building 65542, quest UID 33554448. `SAVE16`, **Living Cities - quest identity probe**, belongs to disposable character `LivingCitiesTest-126494eb`; it intentionally contains the synthetic active quest. Normal family preview remains SAVE15. The prototype quest has no rewards or faction changes. Evidence: `work/quest-identity-pass.log`, native build log `../work/livingcities-build.log`, and SAVE16's original DFU quest/save data. The run also passed all previous family and street checks; core behavior tests remain 87/87.

## Integration consequences

Keep original StaticNPC and QuestResourceBehaviour identity when changing visuals or moving within the allowed interior. Native quest identity uses the original map/building/layout hash/name seed, not the current transform. A newly created generic mobile person with a matching appearance or name does not establish equivalent quest identity.

StaticNPC.Start derives runtime building information from player context. Cross-building/outdoor proxies therefore need a deliberate binding adapter and tests; this probe does not authorize recomputing identity from a new location. QuestResource.Tick also controls active state for bound people, so a future radius/pose controller cannot assume sole ownership of the same GameObject's active flag. Sleeping can change visual pose while retaining the original activation target, but actual bed placement, waking and service interaction still need gameplay acceptance.

## Harness correction found by this probe

Calling PlayerEnterExit.TransitionInterior directly bypassed PlayerActivate's building-discovery and shop/tavern/residence setup. With stale default building data, vanilla occupants were disabled as if the tavern were a closed shop; generated residents alone still passed earlier tests. A runtime census showed the original NPCs present but inactive, before StaticNPC.Start had filled location data. The shared test helper now calls PlayerGPS.DiscoverBuilding and the native PlayerActivate.TransitionInterior handler, then verifies the building key. This fixes test fidelity rather than changing normal game behavior. Failure evidence: `work/quest-probe-closed-npcs-red.log`.

The previous harness also accepted live desktop input during automated steps. A logged activation caused an unexpected exterior transition during an interior reload assertion. InputManager and PlayerActivate updates are now disabled only in the opt-in process; programmatic activation checks remain available. Evidence: `work/quest-probe-input-interference.log`. No normal gameplay input setting is changed.

## Remaining acceptance

### Quest destinations and household surnames

User requirement added 14 October: quests directing the player to a named person at a specific house must agree with the household simulation, for both side quests and main quests. Castles and palaces retain vanilla identities and arrangements.

The quest's authoritative person name, placement, destination and scripted relationships take precedence. Do not redirect a quest to a generated household merely because its surname matches; do not rename the quest person or rewrite journal/rumor/marker destinations to satisfy generated population data. Bind the original quest identity at its required location.

In an ordinary residence, establish whether the named person is a permanent resident before generating family ties. Generated relatives adopt that resident's surname, while retaining their own stable IDs, given names, relationships and death records. If a quest introduces an authoritative resident after initial allocation, reconcile the generated family surname through a persisted override with provenance; do not regenerate the family. A visitor, captive, enemy or temporary quest target must not rename the household. Explicit quest relationships, distinct fixed names and unrelated lodgers override the generated shared-surname convention. Do not guess a surname by blindly taking the last word of a title, mononym or unusual name. A quest ending must not randomly roll names again.

Keep castles, palaces and shared institutions outside this generated-family rename policy. Existing vanilla named NPCs remain authoritative. Ambiguous residency must not silently create a family or alter quest data; preserve native behavior until an explicit binding rule is established.

Required tests: a named home resident before initial population creation; a side quest starting after the house was visited; an actual main-quest home target; a temporary visitor/captive; conflicting fixed names; castle/palace NPCs; quest start/completion/cancellation and save/reload. Verify native person binding, journal/rumor destination, actual door/interior presence, conversation/task advancement, generated relatives' surname and unchanged resident IDs. This policy is recorded scope, not implemented behavior. The current synthetic tavern probe does not prove these cases.

### Broad destination and delivery integration

The user explicitly includes Fighters Guild and other quest givers whose procedurally selected destinations vary between offers. Cover destination-based main and side quests through a shared identity/location integration, not a list of individually rewritten quests. The recipient, household, offer, acceptance text, journal, directions/rumors, discovered building/map label, native activation and hand-in must refer to the same authoritative person and site. Use stable map/building/person/quest identifiers, not name-string matching. Cover delivery, retrieval, escort, rescue and other scripted destinations, including remote towns, previously visited houses, multiple concurrent quests using one building and destinations changed by later quest stages. Residency and relatedness still depend on role; a delivery recipient may be a guest.

Source inspection in the pinned DFU checkout establishes why a late rename is insufficient:

- `Person.AssignDisplayName()` independently sets a faction individual's fixed name or a generated full name.
- `Place.GetBuildingName()` generates a residence surname separately; non-residences use their fixed building name. A person's surname and the residence label must not be assumed equal in vanilla.
- `Person.ExpandMacro()` exposes person name and dialog-place building/town/region independently. The displayed destination can follow the current dialog place rather than always the home place.
- `DaggerfallGuildServicePopupWindow.OfferQuest()` creates the offer prompt before the quest is started. Its inherited `DaggerfallQuestPopupWindow.OfferQuest_OnButtonClick()` starts the quest only after acceptance. `QuestMachine.OnQuestStarted` alone is therefore too late for names already shown in an offer; partial quest-list previews must also remain free of persistent allocation side effects.
- `Place.SiteDetails` has a public setter. This is a possible integration boundary, not proof that changing it alone updates discovered labels, existing topic data or already rendered text.

Resolve procedural naming and household bindings before any player-visible offer is expanded, then keep those published bindings stable. Fixed story names and sites remain untouched. Do not silently rename an existing household every time another procedural quest chooses it or rewrite a previously issued destination. Conflicts need an explicit binding policy that preserves all active quest contracts. Rejection/cancellation must not leave an unaccepted preview's temporary family/name changes behind. Existing active quests loaded from saves require reconciliation without changing the directions the player already received. Tests must exercise actual guild offer timing and hand-in, plus distinct main-quest placement paths; the previous quest probe covers neither.

The breadth requirement includes every main/side quest family that supplies a destination, not only Fighters Guild deliveries. Before declaring compatibility, inventory the pinned quest scripts by person/place construction and macro/placement path. Use that inventory to select representative native end-to-end cases for each distinct path, and identify script-specific exceptions separately. Record which cases are tested, unsupported or still untested. A passing generic synthetic probe is not evidence that all shipped quests match. Include fixed story people, procedural household recipients, temporary visitors/captives, institutions/castles, cross-town destinations, chained or changing destinations and quests already active in an older save. The acceptance comparison is the exact offer/journal/directions/door/recipient/task contract, together with stable household identity and appropriate family surnames.

## Mod-added quests

The compatibility target includes quest packs using DFU's native person/place and quest-text pipeline. Bind a procedural recipient to the appropriate household before names are published; preserve the actual quest resource identity, task conditions and hand-in behavior. A display-only name replacement is insufficient. The earlier prohibition on renaming a quest person applies to fixed or already published contracts; a future pre-publication binding may select a generated recipient's name consistently through the native resource. Fixed named characters and explicit scripted relationships remain authoritative. Do not assign a temporary visitor the family name merely because the quest places them in that house.

Custom C# quest engines, private placement systems or bypassed macro expansion may require explicit adapters. Inventory and test actual mod-added quest cases before claiming support, including accepted quests loaded from older saves. No quest-pack compatibility or pre-publication interception is implemented or verified yet.

## Pre-publication interception checkpoint, 14 October 2026

Historical checkpoint: the subsequent persistent-ledger section below extends this probe with native quest startup and an actual save-slot reload. The original pre-publication checks and limitations remain relevant.

The preceding no-interception status is superseded only for a separate opt-in feasibility probe. A standard mod package successfully attaches a Harmony postfix to native `Parser.Parse` in the shipped player. For its synthetic procedural recipient, it selects an existing living male household resident at the native quest-selected local home and sets the native person display name and residence name before offer expansion. This is temporary probe behavior; normal Living Cities gameplay does not rewrite quests yet.

The expanded actual-player test passes:

- Partial header parses create no binding; fixed recipient Meridia and unrelated quests are excluded.
- Every non-name field in the person save data and site details remains unchanged. Quest UID survives native FullSerializer JSON round-trip and reconstruction.
- Native message macro output and `QuestMachine.CreateMessagePrompt` text labels contain both bound names before the quest starts.
- A subsequent explicit tavern assignment changes `GetDialogPlace` and message destination while retaining the person's bound name and original home name. That assigned destination survives serialization.
- The persistent Living Cities population remains byte-identical in its snapshot. No accepted quest, household allocation or new game save is created by the probe itself.
- Owner-specific unpatch removes interception; a subsequent parse no longer invokes it. The test runner removes the separate temporary `.dfmod` afterward. Base-game files and tracked engine source remain unchanged.

Reproduce: `tools/Build.ps1 -QuestBindingProbe`, then `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -QuestBindingProbe`. Both commands passed; initial red evidence is `work/quest-binding-red.log` (recipient unchanged without interception), final evidence `work/quest-binding-pass.log`. Core tests remain 97/97; native build checks pass. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed critical or important defect. The normal bundle contains only the dormant opt-in test bridge, not Harmony or the binding implementation.

The test constructs a native prompt but does not accept an actual guild quest, place or activate a renamed target, or perform hand-in. FullSerializer reconstruction is not an actual save-slot reload for this new probe. It does not establish native discovered map labels, rumors, concurrent quests, story relationships, semantic residency classification, mod-file/asset loading, or broad main/side quest compatibility. Native macro expansion logs an attempted dialog-link lookup before quest registration; the probe does not claim persisted talk-topic behavior.

The reproducible inventory now covers 265 shipped scripts and 125 explicit NPC placement actions. `QUEST-SOURCE-SURVEY.md` records counts, the merchant relocation counterexample and open coverage groups. `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-quest-binding.md` records the remaining persisted binding and actual actor/quest integration work.

This tests a synthetic questor binding on an actual original tavern NPC, using the native activation handler directly. It does not test mouse raycasting, door locks/greeting gates, every named individual/main quest, turn-in rewards, shops/services while relocated, scripted placement into another building, death policy, or cross-map actor transfer. M1 probes are complete; the corresponding M2/M3 integration work remains open. No engine changes were required for this probe. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed critical or important issue; runtime evidence remains the acceptance authority.

## Persistent binding ledger checkpoint, 14 October 2026

Schema 4 adds committed quest-to-resident contracts to the mod save data. Each contract records the native quest UID/name and person symbol, persistent settlement/home/resident IDs, published person and residence names, and the adapter rule that established eligibility. The caller must explicitly establish permanent residency; a generated quest home is not sufficient evidence. General gameplay quest rewriting remains disabled.

`PrepareQuestBindings` returns a detached, immutable offer plan without changing the world. Discarding that plan leaves no allocation or naming side effects. `CommitQuestBindings` validates the complete batch before assigning the ledger. A new binding to a dead resident, conflicting published person/home names, duplicate symbols, or mismatched source quest rejects the batch. Compatible concurrent quests can share a resident. Repeated commitment is idempotent. Committed contracts remain historical records after death; commitment does not replace the live resident references held by actors. Plans from another store or before a successful restore cannot commit. Snapshots and restores copy the ledger defensively.

Schemas 1–3 migrate to an empty ledger without regenerating residents, family links, surnames, deaths or existing routes. Native FullSerializer converts unsigned numbers through `Int64`; the first boundary test with `ulong.MaxValue` exposed an overflow. The ledger now rejects UIDs above `Int64.MaxValue` before saving. Native round-trip verification preserves `Int64.MaxValue` exactly, including precision beyond floating-point integer range. This changes no engine file or serializer.

Evidence:

- 118/118 core tests pass. Twenty initial behavioral tests failed against stubs, then passed; the native UID-range rejection separately failed before its fix. Logs: `work/quest-ledger-red.log`, `work/quest-ledger-uid-red.log`, `work/quest-ledger-green.log`.
- Unity serializer/adapter/build checks pass, including committed contracts, deceased residents and actual old JSON shapes without the ledger field. Logs: `work/quest-ledger-native-red.log` and `work/quest-ledger-native-pass.log`.
- The opt-in parser probe now hands a serialized bound quest to `QuestLedgerProbe`. The latter checks preparation does not modify the mod save, starts that synthetic quest through native `StartQuest`, commits the binding, saves and reloads a real slot, then compares the reconstructed native quest with its reconstructed mod contract and household resident.
- Actual-player run passed with UID `33554450`, resident `s:206728581:h:131598:r:0`, Gondane Hawkham, SAVE19 **Living Cities - quest binding ledger**, character `LivingCitiesTest-126494eb`. Log: `work/quest-ledger-runtime-pass.log`. The same run passes walking animation, interior continuity, death persistence and five-person family checks under schema 4. Temporary probe package was removed afterward.
- Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed correctness or migration defect in the ledger diff. Built and installed normal bundle SHA-256: `8092B85CFAEC8F57C23AB84647CB1F6EBB9A6CA27DD399B94F0529EF5D8CECC2`.

Reproduce with the same `Build.ps1 -QuestBindingProbe` and `TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -QuestBindingProbe` commands above. SAVE19 is a developer fixture containing a synthetic active quest, not the recommended normal preview.

At that checkpoint, the actual guild UI acceptance/rejection adapter, authoritative fixed-name/guest/captive classification, old accepted-quest reconciliation, household surname overrides, duplicate-free native target actors, actual activation/hand-in, discovered labels and rumors, quest completion/history retirement, and representative main/side/mod-added coverage remained pending. Current ledger entries retain references to deceased residents still stored in their households; later migration/repopulation must preserve historical identity rather than remove referenced records. This checkpoint establishes persistence, not complete quest compatibility.

## Native offer and recipient checkpoint, 14 October 2026

`QuestOfferBindings` stages explicitly eligible procedural residents before publishing offers. It changes only native person/home display names after validating the entire proposal. Refusal discards the pending plan. Acceptance revalidates the living resident and published resources before the native acceptance message, then commits after native quest startup. Native StartQuest also revalidates staged plans. Fixed individuals and questors are excluded. Automatic residency classification and general quest rewriting remain disabled.

The standard bundle now contains Harmony, its MIT notice and `LivingCities.QuestHooks.dll`. Hooks are owned by the mod, removed on reset/load/destruction, and reinstalled when restoring a nonempty ledger. `StaticNPC.DisplayName` needs a scoped getter hook: native Person.DisplayName and the spawned NPC's name derived from nameSeed otherwise disagree. Matching uses quest UID, source quest name and normalized person symbol; native NPC identity data is unchanged.

The actual-player synthetic fixture passed native Fighters Guild offer creation and real message button handlers for refusal, rejection after recipient death, and successful acceptance. Native accept/refuse text was checked. The recipient appeared in the published home, displayed the same name and triggered a native quest task through StaticNPCClick. SAVE19 reconstructed the quest, mod ledger, visible name and working activation. These are native handler tests, not mouse/raycast or item hand-in tests. Evidence: `work/quest-offer-runtime-pass.log`; initial failures are `work/quest-offer-native-red.log` and `work/quest-recipient-name-red.log`. Core tests pass 119/119, including read-only acceptance revalidation.

The Unity editor's virtual-mod loader only loads DLL assets listed in `ModInfo.Files`. Packaging now includes both libraries in that manifest as well as the player bundle, with verification through native `Mod.GetTypesInEditor`. Initial missing-manifest failure: `work/quest-editor-manifest-red.log`. This checks editor library discovery, not a full interactive editor play session.

The expanded nighttime test initially failed because a generated household actor and native recipient represented the same resident. Active native quest contracts now suppress that resident's scheduled actor both indoors and outdoors, including while native quest scripting hides or relocates the recipient. An index avoids scanning every contract for every unbound resident. Ending the quest releases ownership without deleting its historical ledger. Actual player verification passed no household duplicate at 23:00, no duplicate after SAVE19 reload, and restoration of the normal household actor after native EndQuest. Fixture recipient: Gondoryan Coppersmith, UID 33554449, resident `s:206728581:h:196609:r:0`. Evidence: `work/quest-duplicate-red.log` and `work/quest-ownership-runtime-pass.log`. This temporary quest placement priority does not yet provide radiant movement, appearance continuity or sleeping for the native quest actor.

Final native build, 119/119 core tests, and standalone startup with the temporary probe package removed passed. Built/installed bundle SHA-256: `F88187D8D12A1CF8C9F0526BF95B84DB8C3C9FE8924252B568033B777320ED78`. Read-only Luna/high reviews found no confirmed critical/important defect in the bounded offer/ownership integration. Upstream tracked engine source remains clean.

Still pending: representative shipped/main/side/mod quest eligibility, older accepted quest reconciliation, journals/rumors/discovered labels, actual delivery and rewards, shared quest-person arbitration, identity-preserving mobile/sleeping quest actors and quest-aware death propagation. The synthetic test does not establish broad quest compatibility.

## Shipped delivery quest checkpoint, 14 October 2026

The opt-in `VanillaDeliveryProbe` extends coverage to the unchanged shipped **The Delivery Boy** (`A0C00Y14`). It loads the quest through native `QuestListsManager.GetQuest`, selects a living same-gender adult from the quest-selected local household, and explicitly prepares a binding before native StartQuest. This is test-specific eligibility; it does not enable automatic production binding or exercise random social-quest offer selection/UI.

The actual player passed native package generation by the quest's own startup task, bound acceptance text, SAVE20 save/reload preserving the quest and carried package, persisted journal step 1010 expanding to the bound recipient, native recipient placement/name and StaticNPCClick hand-in, package removal, native success/reward popup, and gold collection through the native inventory transfer handler. Quest completion releases resident ownership while retaining the historical contract. The passing sample paid 42 gold to deliver to Dunoryan Kington, UID 33554448, resident `s:206728581:h:259:r:0`. The pre-delivery save remains available as **Living Cities - vanilla delivery**, character `LivingCitiesTest-126494eb`.

Reproduce: `tools/Build.ps1`, then `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -DeliveryProbe`. Both exited 0. Evidence: `work/vanilla-delivery-build-pass.log`, `work/vanilla-delivery-runtime-pass.log`. The selected quest file in the installed player and pinned upstream both have SHA-256 `9972F93494B5BDB21153D1A97C69D6F042257C89B0B9F976025FCA0DC446C238`. Built/installed mod bundles match `166232AD2FA66066EAACC0BC9F279C5AAF3DF2D382B68BD44A753D834F73AEC4`; tracked upstream source remains clean. Core code is unchanged from the 119/119 checkpoint.

The initial fixture expanded `%qdt` before a journal step existed, causing native QuestDate to dereference a missing date. The corrected probe waits for startup and save/reload, verifies the recorded step exists, sets CurrentLogMessageId as the journal does, then expands its text. This was a test setup error, not an engine change. Historical failure: `work/quest-delivery-journal-fixture-fail.log`.

Limits: one randomized package branch in one local quest, direct native start/activation/transfer handlers rather than mouse interaction, no claim of every package branch, failure/timeout/thief path, remote deliveries, social offer selection, mod-added quest assets or broader main/side-quest compatibility. This closes the absence of any real delivery/reward evidence, not the full quest compatibility milestone.

<a id="source-quest-pack-compatibility-md"></a>

## Original: QUEST-PACK-COMPATIBILITY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Quest Pack 1 and Quest Actions Extension audit

17 October 2026. These are separately installed third-party mods, not code or assets incorporated into People of the Iliac Bay.

## Installation and evidence

**Planning follow-up, 17 October 2026:** [quest-role design and representative cases](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design) and [delivery phase 6](ROADMAP.md#delivery-plan) consolidate mobility and spoiler-control options. Quest Pack mobility implementation is explicitly deferred. Review must eventually cover each quest's roles/stages, native bindings and disclosure gates; representative source analysis and installation tests are not certification of every quest. Existing native control remains the conservative default.

Installed from the user-supplied archives:

- `Quest Pack 1 dfmod-2-2025-11-20b-1763826811.zip`: Quest Pack 1 **2025.11.20b**.
- `Quest Actions Extension-677-2-0-0-1740947698.zip`: Quest Actions Extension **2.0.0**.

Both bundles target DFU 1.1.1. Player and editor copies match the archive-extracted SHA-256 values. Player load order is BasicRoads 18, Quest Actions Extension 19, Quest Pack 1 20, People of the Iliac Bay 21. Other enabled choices are preserved. The pre-install configuration is retained locally in `work/quest-mod-audit/Mods.before-install.json`.

Quest Pack 1 declares a nonoptional, nonpeer dependency on Quest Actions Extension version 1.12.0. DFU's `ModManager.CheckModDependencies` treats the declared version as a minimum (`IsVersionLowerOrEqual`), so installed 2.0.0 satisfies that version check. This is not proof that every script action is bug-free.

The owned-player startup test passed and registered all nine pack quest lists. The existing live dialogue regression also passed with both mods enabled. A data-only quest pack reporting “No mod loaders found” is expected; it contributes quest assets/lists rather than a runtime initializer. This audit does not claim all 302 quests have been played or that native quest integration is complete.

Local inspection evidence is under ignored `work/quest-mod-audit/`. The original bundles, extracted quest text and decompiled extension source are not redistributed in this repository. Existing NameGen/localization and DET material diagnostics still appear in startup logs; no new clean-whole-stack claim is made.

## Quest inventory

The bundle contains 303 QBN-bearing TextAssets: **302 quest scripts plus one literal `test` fixture**. Metadata exposes 298 loose resources, including the test fixture. Five additional `JHrepchange1`–`JHrepchange5` script assets are linked child quests, not missing orphan entries.

| Quest list | Active entries | Disabled entries |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Commoner | 23 | 0 |
| Dark Brotherhood | 25 | 0 |
| Fighters Guild | 41 | 2 |
| Knightly Orders | 47 | 0 |
| Mages Guild | 29 | 1 |
| Misc | 2 | 0 |
| Noble | 4 | 0 |
| Temples | 12 | 4 |
| Thieves Guild | 30 | 0 |
| Total | 213 | 7 |

These are list entries, not all directly offerable quest scripts. Loose quests include chained/supporting content. `QuestList-Misc:6` refers to `JHPCART`, while the available asset is `JHCART`; this is a source-level candidate defect requiring native lookup verification, not a reason to rewrite the downloaded pack silently.

There is no `A0C00Y14` or `S000...` asset/list reference in the pack. It does not override our narrow vanilla delivery pilot or the main-quest files inspected here. It still uses fixed main-world identities such as Gortwog.

Raw syntax census across all 303 scripts: 258 declare people, 199 declare questors, 117 contain `place npc`, 82 `hide npc`, 90 `place foe`, 134 `create foe`, 150 `killed`, 288 clocks and 85 daily time windows. These overlapping counts identify test coverage needs; they do not establish semantic compatibility.

## Representative NPC risks

| Case | Script and evidence | Required treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Human/monster swap | `JHCAPA01` The Werewolf Hunt, declarations 385–391, hide/place sequence 513–521, kill/reward 528–535 | Preserve distinct Person and Foe resources and scripted visibility. Do not let routine lifecycle resurrect the human or remove the foe |
| Fixed named people and disguised actor | `JHDB121a` To Kill an Ambassador, Br'itsa/Gortwog 326–331, alias/vampire sequence 390–439 | Retain individual identity, alias semantics, quest placement and hostile replacement |
| Captive child/parent | `JHDB109` Going Home, 95–113 and 129–154 | Quest dungeon/home assignment overrides normal family routine; timed hide and parent ambush must remain native |
| Hostage | `JHDB113` To the Wolves, 68–117 | Preserve target, hide/face/dialogue transitions and timer outcomes |
| Priest escort and timed combat | `JHKN119` Rite of Vampire Dispelling, 115–149 and 181–213 | Temple service affiliation does not make this an ordinary staff actor; preserve hidden escort and event clock |
| Relocated service-role people | `JHCAPA01`, 385–411 and 458–489; `JHCG003`, 84–99 | Merchant/apothecary/chemist faction alone is not proof of a permanent shopkeeper or resident |
| Kill target represented as foe | `JHFG106`, 157–170 and 220–224 | Native kill event and foe identity must survive simulation; do not substitute a generic citizen |

All source identifiers above refer to extracted user-installed bundle TextAssets. Representative tests must cover click, item hand-in, reward, scripted relocation, hiding/restoration, timing, foe injury/death and save/load, not merely whether an NPC appears.

## Extension inventory

Quest Actions Extension registers **28 actions/conditions** at game start plus four console commands. It adds no NPC sprite sheets or global household system. Quest Pack 1 adds quest resources, not a global resident census or new animation assets.

| Group | Registered classes |
| --- | --- |
| Player stats/status | `ReducePlayerHealth`, `ReducePlayerFatigue`, `ReducePlayerMagicka`, `IncreasePlayerHealth`, `IncreasePlayerFatigue`, `IncreasePlayerMagicka`, `InfectPlayerWith` |
| Items | `PlayerHandsover`, `PlayerPossesses`, `EquippedWithItem`, `UpdateQuestItemMaterial`, `UpdateQuestItemAddEnchantment`, `UpdateQuestItemApplyMagicTemplate` |
| Time/location/state | `RaiseTime`, `WithinUnits`, `CurrentMapPixel`, `CurrentInBlockPixel`, `PlayerCurrentRegionIs`, `CurrentStateIs` |
| Thresholds/progression | `WhenHealthLevel`, `WhenFatigueLevel`, `WhenMagickaLevel`, `GuildRankAtLeast`, `EnemyHealthLowerHigher`, `KilledEnemiesOfClass`, `MagicEfectKeyIsOn`, `PlayerLegalReputeIs`, `PlayerFactionReputeIs` |

Important interactions:

- `WithinUnits` reads the actual QuestResourceBehaviour transform and vertical difference. It can follow the original moving resource; it cannot locate an unrelated substitute resident. Example: `JHMG222:131`.
- `CurrentMapPixel`, `CurrentInBlockPixel` and region conditions remain player-position checks. Do not satisfy them using an unloaded NPC's scheduled position.
- `RaiseTime` directly advances the game clock. Our schedules, sleep, stays, reporting and guard duties must handle clock jumps. A future 2× clock must change elapsed-time progression without incorrectly scaling explicit quest time jumps or deadlines.
- `GuildRankAtLeast`, legal reputation and faction reputation read native player state. NPC disposition is a separate value; planned guild expulsion must update native membership coherently rather than imitate it only in dialogue.
- `EnemyHealthLowerHigher` and `KilledEnemiesOfClass` depend on native quest foe state. Background cleanup must not count as a kill or lose required injury/death events.

The decompiled `CurrentStateIs.Pattern` appears to omit alternative separators before vampire/wereboar/werewolf forms. This is a candidate upstream parsing defect, not a confirmed gameplay failure or a mod change made by this audit. Check affected syntax in a native parser fixture before considering a compatibility shim. No downloaded bundle was modified.

## Acceptance backlog

- [ ] Native parser/lookup check for every contributed list entry and extension syntax used by the pack, including the `JHPCART` discrepancy.
- [ ] Original-resource identity and no duplicate actor across sleep, return, streaming and save/load.
- [ ] Captive/escort, disguise/monster swap, fixed individual, relocated service actor and kill/reward fixtures.
- [ ] Timed meetings, explicit clock jumps and quest deadlines under the eventual clock-rate policy.
- [ ] Guild/reputation conditions after any future disposition-driven expulsion system.
- [ ] Confirm pending and completed quest cleanup releases simulation ownership without reviving dead people.

Use the [NPC integration policy](NPC-SYSTEMS.md#inventory) for implementation ordering. Installing these mods does not expand the current automatic household-binding whitelist.

<a id="source-quest-source-survey-md"></a>

## Original: QUEST-SOURCE-SURVEY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Quest source coverage inventory

Source: pinned official DFU `v1.1.1-cve-2025`, commit `fb2627082fabe473bf108c816263b71c6e26ace6`, `Assets/StreamingAssets/Quests`. Run `tools/QuestSurvey.ps1`; the detailed JSON is written to ignored `work/quest-source-survey.json`, including filenames, line numbers and exact declarations/actions. This is a syntactic census, not semantic residency detection or a compatibility pass.

The survey finds 265 quest scripts: 245 declare people, 215 declare places, and 195 contain explicit placement actions. Categories below count declarations, not distinct NPCs or quests.

| Construction path | Count |
| --- | ---: |
| Fixed named individual | 97 |
| Questor | 181 |
| Generated person, explicit local home scope | 70 |
| Generated person, explicit remote home scope | 193 |
| Generated person, unspecified home scope | 33 |
| Local place | 70 |
| Remote place | 252 |
| Fixed place | 36 |
| Random choice of fixed places | 1 |

There are 455 explicit placement commands: 125 NPC, 224 foe, and 106 item placements. NPC placement may replace the dialog destination; item and foe placement must not be treated as resident allocation.

## Concrete counterexample to home-only binding

`A0C01Y09.txt`, The Birthday Present, declares a questor, a local sister and a local merchant (lines 151–153), plus a separate general store at line 157. Line 168 places the merchant at the store. A generated home field does not establish that the currently discussed destination is that house, or that a character is related to arbitrary existing residents. The story also has an explicit sister relationship in its prose. Automatic house/name matching must preserve those semantics, not merely fill a matching-sex resident slot.

Native `Person.GetDialogPlace()` selects the assigned place when present and otherwise the home. `Person.Tick()` may place a person at home on interior entry only when no explicit assignment exists. `PlaceNpc` calls `SetAssignedPlaceSymbol`; both initial creation and later stage changes need coverage.

## Mod quest entry paths

`QuestListsManager.LoadQuest()` reads either an external quest file or a TextAsset supplied by ModManager. Both branches call `QuestMachine.ParseQuest`, which calls `Parser.Parse`. A shared parse boundary can therefore support native-format quest packs without changing base files. This does not cover a custom quest engine that constructs resources outside these paths. Actual external-file and bundled-mod quest loading still need separate acceptance tests.

## Acceptance groups still open

- Actual main-quest fixed identities and story relationships; castle/palace exclusions.
- Guild offer/accept/reject and hand-in, including generated home recipients.
- Non-guild deliveries, retrievals, escort/rescue, guests and captives.
- Explicit initial placement and later destination changes.
- Remote and previously visited towns, concurrent quests using one building.
- Existing accepted quests from older saves and rejected/partial previews.
- Native-format mod quest files, bundled mod quest assets, and explicit adapters for custom engines.

The opt-in binding probe is evidence for interception timing and native name/resource behavior only. It does not close these end-to-end cases. No general quest-rewriting policy is enabled in normal play yet.

## Verified shipped case, 14 October 2026

The subsequent production pilot automatically binds this unchanged source in Gothway Garden only, before native offer text. A normalized-source SHA-256 guard rejects modified scripts, including mod overrides with the same quest name. Automatic eligibility, native social acceptance, save/reload and delivery now pass for this one reviewed rule; this does not generalize the census into a role classifier. Further survey/adapter expansion is deferred while M2/M3 gameplay and animations resume. See the current checkpoint in `QUEST-IDENTITY.md`.

`A0C00Y14` (**The Delivery Boy**) now passes the dedicated actual-player `-DeliveryProbe`: unchanged source loaded through QuestListsManager, explicit local household binding, native package startup, real pre-delivery save/reload, journal recipient consistency, native hand-in consuming the package, and collection of the script's gold reward. This covers one randomized package branch. The probe bypasses random social offer selection, mouse raycasting and quest eligibility rules; thief/failure/timeout branches remain untested. Remote counterpart `A0C00Y15` remains untested. See `QUEST-IDENTITY.md` for exact evidence and reproduction.
