<a id="source-relationships-and-future-inventories-md"></a>

## Original: RELATIONSHIPS-AND-FUTURE-INVENTORIES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Relationships, memory, and future inventories

> Current amendment, 14 October 2026: one NPC per bed; maximum household residents equals actual bed count, with no separate five-person cap. Preserve old saves and use a fresh compliant test population. Shared-bed rendering, kill-all-bed logic and separate head/blanket layers are superseded. Keep complete green-blanket/white-pillow single-sleeper sprites. Children may inherit either parent's race; keep existing child sprites now. Gameplay takes priority over art. See [current citizen behavior and next guard work](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md). Earlier checkpoint descriptions below remain historical evidence.

> Planning amendment, 17 October 2026: the [consolidated design](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design) adds a **future, narrow exception** for two authored ordinary inn-room occupants sharing one suitable bed, with separate claims and proposed two-head/blanket composition. This does not double general household capacity or change today's one-per-bed implementation. It supersedes the 14 October rejection of shared-bed graphics only for that case. NPC invitations, human stats, persistent inventories, nonquest companions, skill progression and a validated world-action framework now belong to this mod's shelved backlog; older suggestions to split inventories into another mod are not the latest decision. No assets or gameplay are added by this amendment.

User additions on 13 October 2026. These extend the active mod goal without replacing its earlier requirements.

Verified 0.3.0 checkpoint, 14 October: new households now use soft bed/size preferences, up to five residents, shared surnames and persistent sex/age/parent/partner fields. Legacy adults remain unchanged. Home-only child flats and a brief greeting pass actual-player save/load tests; outdoor play, richer family dialogue, sleeping and relationships beyond family links remain unfinished. Sleeping sprites for all NPC flats are the first art production step when home/tavern sleeping is implemented. Complete animation and child-diversity requirements remain in `DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md`.

## Current mod: relationships and bereavement

Track persistent NPC-to-NPC relationships using stable resident IDs, separately from disposition toward the player. Household membership does not automatically define a marriage: model the actual bond so dialogue can identify a partner, relative, friend, or housemate correctly.

On 14 October the user required all permanent residents of a household to share a surname. Persist the household surname and use it consistently in displayed NPC names and dialogue context, while preserving distinct given names and stable resident IDs. Visitors retain their own names. Shared surname alone does not establish a marriage or parenthood.

The user requested male/female family households, sometimes with children. They explicitly superseded the original maximum of three: four was approved, then five for two adults and three children. Most households should remain smaller. Their subsequent clarification makes beds a weak indicator, not a strict occupancy cap: couples can share a bed and children can sleep with parents. Use actual interior size, room arrangement and beds as weighted preferences; larger homes favor larger families. Count the specific interior rather than assuming all buildings of a type have equal furnishing. Existing three-person implementation is historical foundation behavior and needs a versioned family-aware migration, not blind regeneration of saved residents.

Separate workplace from residence. A tiny shop without beds, such as an alchemist or pawn shop, does not force its owner to sleep there. After closing, the owner travels to the assigned home/family, then returns for opening. A shop with suitable living quarters can remain its owner's home. Preserve a single persistent owner identity across shop, street and house; maintain services and quest bindings. Determine bed-sharing sleep anchors separately from household membership so sleepers do not all occupy the identical sprite position.

Record explicit adult/child and parent/partner relationships rather than inferring them from appearance. DFU's `TextureReader.IsChildNPCTexture` identifies existing child flats, including archives 181, 182 and 184; full walking/action animation coverage remains unverified. Children stay home or play/socialize in nearby outdoor groups, facing one another. They do not join adult work, shopping, tavern or inter-settlement travel schedules. Schedule local play in daylight and return home before danger/nightfall; group membership must remain stable across streaming.

The user delegates whether children can be spoken to. The selected behavior is brief age-appropriate conversation about home, family, playmates and nearby places, with knowledge constrained to what the child knows. Pinned DFU `TalkManager.TalkToStaticNPC` already permits child dialogue while excluding children from ordinary quest-offer branches. This source check does not establish every interaction in the original DOS executable. Keep children out of adult service and quest-giver allocation.

Seed friendships, rivalries and familiarity from household ties, neighbouring homes, shared factions/guilds, political attitudes and social standing. These are tendencies rather than hard barriers: cross-class and distant friendships should occur. Shared appointments reserve both participants' time and a reachable destination, including houses, taverns, squares and parks where available.

Household deaths produce persistent bereavement memories. Grief lasts one to two game years, with diminishing probability of mentioning the loss as time passes. Use DFU's calendar, stable event timestamps, and a bounded salience model. Local dialogue receives verified relationship, deceased identity, elapsed time and current salience. Avoid inventing extra relatives, causes of death, or a culprit. Repeated conversations should not mention the death on every turn.

When a resident witnesses the player kill their partner, record that attributed witnessed event. The survivor becomes hostile, refuses ordinary conversation and attempts to attack the player. Witnessing requires actual perception at the event: distance, visibility, alertness and relevant awareness. Unwitnessed deaths do not reveal the player as killer. Later secondhand reports must retain their source and certainty. The 14 October amendment allows player kills of important quest NPCs, including broken quests, while preserving technical bindings and save integrity. Separate player-caused deaths from default protection against background events; see `CRIME-SCENES.md`.

Persistent events must be idempotent across death callbacks, save/load, and scene changes. Recruitment must not reuse a deceased resident's ID or erase survivors' memories. Ordinary household replacement does not silently create a replacement spouse.

## Future inventory extension, possibly a separate mod

The user is considering a later inventory/pickpocket mod. Do not silently expand current implementation into a complete equipment system. Research vanilla sneak/crouch and pickpocket behavior from the pinned source before choosing hooks; no crouch/sneak-combining mod is assumed installed.

Preserve extension points for resident inventory, ownership, home keys, and theft events. Keys should identify actual households/buildings and work with nighttime locks. Support mobile and stationary NPC identities. Equipped clothes and armor can be looted only after death and only when the NPC's visible sprite represents those items. Do not permit stealing equipped trousers or armor from a living resident. Other carried items can follow a separate pickpocket rule when implemented.

NPC sprite variation can benefit both mods: combinations corresponding to clothing items that exist in Daggerfall, with metadata mapping visible outfits to equipment. Keep canonical clothing appearance separate from transient animation state and inventory ownership.

The 14 October animation amendments make visual continuity an essential current requirement for every stationary and mobile human NPC appearance. Adults require walking, running, weapon-fighting and spellcasting from all viewing angles, plus a sleeping sprite lying in bed. Children require directional walking/running and a sleeping sprite, with no battle sets. Existing mobile walkers also require distinct running frames. Preserve recognizable face, clothing, proportions and palette across actions; never substitute a generic fighter when a civilian becomes hostile. A single camera-facing flat gliding to a door is not an acceptable completed implementation.

Use one source child and one source adult as validated asset pilots before scaling to every stationary and mobile human appearance. Verify all viewing directions, foot placement, frame timing, transitions between idle/movement/combat/sleep and stable appearance through doors and saves. Retain the same resident and service/quest identity when changing action or representation. Existing static child flats may support an explicitly incomplete home-only prototype, but cannot satisfy the mobile-child requirement. Spellcasting and sleeping are required; additional corpse coverage remains a separate expansion decision. See `DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md` for the per-appearance coverage inventory and acceptance gates.

<a id="source-repository-md"></a>

## Original: REPOSITORY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Repository setup and development baseline

Repository: https://github.com/Bl4ckh34d/People-of-the-Iliac-Bay

The existing local Git history predates the GitHub repository. Initialization preserves that history and the remote README history; it does not reset the project to a new empty repository. The initial publication is a development baseline containing unfinished work, not a release tag.

## Local layout

The current Windows scripts expect this sibling layout by default:

```text
DFU/
  LivingCities/       # this repository; retain this folder name for current scripts
  daggerfall-unity/   # separate DFU 1.1.1 source checkout
  Player/            # separately installed DFU player and portable app data
  GameFiles/         # user's game data
  downloads/         # external mod archives
  work/              # machine-local build evidence
```

Clone with an explicit folder name if setting up a new machine:

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Bl4ckh34d/People-of-the-Iliac-Bay.git LivingCities
```

Core tests require only the .NET 8 SDK and restored NuGet packages. The .NET Framework runtime/hook projects additionally require assemblies from the sibling player. `tools/Deploy.ps1` expects `work/Harmony/net472/0Harmony.dll` and the built hook assembly. This baseline does not yet provide a complete clean-machine dependency bootstrap. A fresh clone alone cannot package the Unity mod.

`tools/Build.ps1` uses Unity 2019.4.40f1 at its configured Windows Hub path, deploys into the separate DFU project's ignored mod directory, verifies and packages the mod. It refuses to overwrite a mod loaded by a running game. `tools/RuntimeCompile/RuntimeCompile.csproj` provides a faster source compilation check against installed assemblies.

## What is excluded

Runtime logs, local `work/`, `dist/`, `bin/`, `obj/`, downloaded bundles/archives, model weights, credentials and extracted native reference PNG directories are excluded. External mods remain separate installations. Decompiled external source and extracted quest scripts remain under ignored local audit directories; only findings and source identifiers are committed.

Generated mod artwork and its provenance metadata remain part of the project. Rejected pilots are identified explicitly in their art READMEs and must not be mistaken for accepted animation coverage. Historical commits are retained.

## Verification boundaries

The first GitHub baseline passes 647 core tests and runtime source compilation. It does not imply every included development change has a new Unity build or live acceptance. In particular, the saved-door survey and current/next destination changes require their remaining targeted native checks. Do not infer game readiness from the repository being clean.

Track future behavior changes in focused commits. Keep compatibility claims tied to named tests and distinguish source implementation, compilation, bundle installation and gameplay verification.

<a id="source-roadmap-md"></a>

## Original: ROADMAP.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Roadmap — TL;DR

Consolidated 17 October 2026. **Planning only. No new gameplay implementation is authorized by this document.** The user subsequently authorized the **2× game-clock fix and rebuild** as a separate bounded task. Other implementation or bug-fix slices will be chosen after review.

## Direction

Make one persistent person exist across home, work, streets, travel, conversation and combat. Preserve native quest/service ownership. Complete reliable physical movement and identity before adding more behaviors that depend on them.

The [current inventory](NPC-SYSTEMS.md#inventory) describes implemented coverage. The [future design and backlog](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design) records the latest requirements. The [delivery plan](ROADMAP.md#delivery-plan) gives ordered, testable work packages. Old milestone paragraphs remain historical evidence, not a competing current plan.

## Important answers

- Most side quests retain native quest control. They are **not automatically broken** because only one delivery quest has household binding. General quest integration remains unverified, and unrelated movement/combat/guard changes still need compatibility tests.
- Main-story people stay under native placement and quest control for now. Their authored personality, public story and disclosure permissions must override random personality generation. Mood can vary within those boundaries.
- Same-building bed movement can preserve identity, but staying in one building alone does not guarantee quest safety. Original native binding, activation, visibility, timing, service access and return position must also survive.
- Two authored NPCs in a one-bed inn room do not share that bed today. Excess occupants are logged as lacking a bed. The requested shared-bed exception is future work.
- Outdoor stall owners do not currently receive homes before generated families. This becomes a required allocation order.
- GitHub has source/build notes and a local-model launcher, not a complete end-user installer or a published release bundle. See [installation status and manual setup](INSTALLATION.md).

## Proposed sequence

| Order | Work package | Exit condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Stabilize observed movement, itinerary/dialogue agreement, door transitions and frame stalls | A tracked person reaches the stated destination, enters/exits physically, and remains the same person after reload; repeatable frame-time evidence |
| 1 | Incremental actor census and unified ownership | Every surveyed actor has stable provenance, identity, role, appearance, owner and a visible adoption/exclusion reason; no duplicate representation |
| 2 | Housing and bed allocation before generated population | Saved people and authored tenants keep capacity; local workers obtain homes before remaining families are generated; migration never evicts silently |
| 3 | Native locals and visitors become mobile by role | Stall workers commute; shopkeepers lock/unlock and commute when needed; shop tenants use keys; inn lodgers have origins, visits and checkout/return journeys |
| 4 | Bedside staff behavior and appearance coverage | Ordinary institutional staff retain services and use real reserved beds; movement/sleep art matches each actual vanilla or replacement appearance |
| 5 | Story-aware dialogue and persistent memory | Main NPCs keep authored character; stage-filtered facts do not leak secrets; encounters and shared activities persist with provenance and game dates |
| 6 | Broader vanilla quest adapters, then Quest Pack 1 | Per-quest/per-role capability records and native end-to-end tests; unsupported stages remain native rather than being moved speculatively |
| 7 | Modular packaging and public installation | No-LLM installation works without model/CUDA downloads; optional setup is reproducible on a clean machine with measured hardware requirements |
| Later | Invitations, durable human combat state, companions and action framework | Independent milestones after movement, ownership, memory and inventory foundations; explicitly shelved now |

Some work can be scoped independently: installation documentation and spoiler-protection design do not require full mobility. The order is a dependency guide, not permission to start a large combined implementation. Quest Pack 1 mobility implementation remains deferred despite its installation and source audit.

## Latest behavioral decisions

- Reserve housing for authored local stall owners, staff needing off-site homes and shop tenants before filling remaining homes. Keep traveler hometown reservations intact.
- Stall workers have slightly varied opening/closing commutes. Markets may stay open longer on weekends while demand remains, within finite closing and safety limits. Local residents can browse multiple stalls as well as indoor shops.
- Ordinary authored inn-bedroom occupants become visiting travelers with stable origins and a reason for visiting. They leave for daytime activities, return to lodging, and eventually check out and travel home. Quest actors and staff are classified separately.
- Allow two authored inn-room occupants to share one bed through an explicit capacity/claim exception and a proposed shared blanket with appearance-specific heads. Do not double all household capacity or infer a romance from co-occupancy.
- Ordinary guild/temple/castle staff keep their workplace duties; off-site daily routines are not required now. Reserve reachable beds if present and preserve each native service role.
- Upstairs shop-bedroom occupants are residential tenants. Their beds are unavailable to the shopkeeper. Tenants carry valid access keys and may return after closing; the same persistent key can be looted after death.
- Main and side quest actors keep original identity and script control. Only reviewed capabilities may be enabled per role/stage. Recruitment excludes main-quest and side-quest actors.

## Existing obligations retained

These additions do not replace earlier work: finite guards, crime/fleeing/reporting and pursuit, guard quarters, household recovery/house sales, corpse loot/property keys, human stats, skill-sensitive awareness, adult itineraries and sparse inter-settlement travel, spacing/facing/idle/running art, commerce and service disposition, rank-sensitive guild expulsion, noble insult consequences, optional quest tone, modular settings, context/memory/listener awareness, performance and versioned saves. See the design's backlog crosswalk and [milestones](HISTORY.md#source-milestones-md).

Current blockers and uncertain evidence remain visible: physical inn movement is not broadly accepted; the prior current/next destination and saved-door fixes still need targeted native checks; game time remains 12× and a safe 2× migration is pending. No percentage-complete estimate should hide these distinctions.

## Next decision

After the separately requested clock fix, recommended next selectable slice: finish the existing destination/door/movement regression, then implement the read-only actor census. Alternative: prioritize story-safe context if plot leakage is the immediate concern. Choose one bounded slice before code changes; companions and exhaustive Quest Pack adaptation are not that slice.

<a id="source-settings-and-compatibility-md"></a>

## Original: SETTINGS-AND-COMPATIBILITY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Settings and city-mod compatibility

> Current amendment, 14 October 2026: one NPC per bed; maximum household residents equals actual bed count, with no separate five-person cap. Preserve old saves and use a fresh compliant test population. Shared-bed rendering, kill-all-bed logic and separate head/blanket layers are superseded. Keep complete green-blanket/white-pillow single-sleeper sprites. Children may inherit either parent's race; keep existing child sprites now. Gameplay takes priority over art. See [current citizen behavior and next guard work](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md). Earlier checkpoint descriptions below remain historical evidence.

User requirement added 13 October 2026: provide broad, practical player control through a mod menu, and add city-overhaul patches after the vanilla implementation is stable. This is part of the active goal, not a separate project.

Use DFU's native mod settings menu and live-settings callback. Add controls only when their corresponding behavior exists. Describe whether a setting takes effect immediately, affects future allocation only, or needs a reload. Disabling a subsystem must preserve its saved identities/deaths. Household capacity now equals actual bed count, one person per bed, without a separate five-person cap. Preserve technical quest/save integrity while allowing the player's deliberate quest-NPC kills as later requested; separate background-event protection.

Version 0.3.3 removes `Population/HouseholdLimit` and adds `General/CivilianReactions` (default true), retaining nine controls. This pilot controls generated-citizen fight-or-flight and one-hop warnings; disabling it retains saved incidents. Native guard replacement is not included yet. The older setting counts and hashes below describe historical checkpoints.

Version 0.3.0 supersedes the earlier 1–3 limit described above: family allocation and schema-3 migration now support 1–5, default five. The isolated player's saved setting is also five; existing households are never rerolled.

First seven functional controls: simulation enable, Gothway Garden physical-population preview, initial household capacity (1–5; new settlements only), waking hour, sleeping hour, diagnostic refresh interval, and F8 diagnostics availability. The preview toggle clears generated actors and restores the vanilla civilian pool while retaining saved identities and routes. Validate settings even when configuration files are edited outside the menu. Existing residents must not disappear or resurrect when capacity changes. A live menu/toggle acceptance test remains pending; build checks cover native deserialization and defaults.

Later controls accompany implementation: crowd/activity weights and operating hours; stealth/detection/waking sensitivity; settlement guard counts, patrol/response/search settings and recruitment; nightlife/group behavior, event frequency/severity and haunting behavior; dialogue enable/model/response limits/timeouts; actor radius, simulation budgets and diagnostics. Add coherent presets and reset-to-defaults support when enough systems exist to make presets useful. Changes that affect saves require explicit descriptions and migration behavior.

Version 0.3.1 adds the eighth control, `DailyRoutine/SleepingPoses` (default true). It immediately enables/disables supported sleeping visuals while retaining identity and schedule state. Current coverage is explicitly one adult appearance on unshared home beds. Native deserialization/default checks cover this control; runtime tests exercise the underlying live transition. Clicking the actual settings menu remains a separate UI acceptance check.

Version 0.3.2 extends this same sleeping control to one male and one female appearance, each with its own green-blanket/white-pillow atlas. Actual-player toggle and save/reload checks pass for both; it still does not cover other appearances or shared beds. Latest built/installed bundle SHA-256 is `807DEAD3566DF7C6718D106C297FB4490E0E107ACFA0C9CAAA4007B933B67B06`; this supersedes the previous quest-checkpoint hash below.

## Distribution boundary

The ninth control is `General/QuestHouseholds` (default true). It enables the reviewed vanilla The Delivery Boy household-binding pilot in Gothway Garden. Changes take effect immediately for new bindings; turning it off also prevents an already staged binding from being accepted. Accepted quests keep their published names and targets while disabled. Actual-player tests invoke the real settings callback and verify these transitions across native offers and reload. Clicking the menu remains a separate UI check. Other quest types and modified quest sources keep native behavior.

The user requires a distributable standard mod, not a custom game installation. Living Cities is packaged as `livingcities.dfmod`; its runtime sources, settings and generated atlas are under its own mod directory. Editor-only build/survey tools are excluded from the package. Mod save data is separate (`mod_livingcities.txt`). No custom executable or replacement engine assembly is required. Any future inability to implement a feature through a standard mod must be reported rather than silently changing this distribution requirement.

Reverified 14 October 2026 after the automatic delivery checkpoint: the pinned upstream DFU checkout has no tracked changes, and all 598 files from the official `dfu_windows_64bit-v1.1.1.zip` match the installed player by SHA-256, with no missing or changed files. The archive's 642 entries also include directories. The current development build and installed Living Cities bundles match SHA-256 `51C146DA6454744422150FBD96BA981B0950CCD139418F6E21EECA2760E4916A`. The official security-patched player is the baseline release, not an engine patch made by Living Cities. Building inside the upstream ignored `Assets/Game/Mods/LivingCities` folder does not modify tracked engine source. The mod now packages Harmony and its quest hook library inside the `.dfmod`; hooks change behavior in memory and do not replace the original executable or engine assemblies. Distribution should contain the mod and required notices, not a copied game installation. Mod-specific save data remains a separate compatibility concern when removing the mod.

## City overhaul adapters, after vanilla acceptance

Keep settlement inventory, navigable routes, entrance/bed/activity anchors, vendor capabilities, and persistent identity mapping behind clear adapter boundaries. A patch can register additional market stalls, buildings, destinations, and actors. Stable IDs must include the supplying mod and object key so additions do not renumber existing residents.

Decorative seller NPCs should become interactable through patches only when valid stock, ownership, service hours, and dialogue rules can be supplied. Preserve vendor and quest identities from the source mod. Added stalls must affect crowd attraction, physical obstruction, ownership, and guard coverage together.

Detect supported mod/version combinations, apply patches only when installed, and document unsupported layouts. Test added/removed/updated city mods, interior transitions, path obstruction, quest access, save restoration, and duplicate vendors. Do not promise compatibility with unnamed city mods or guess which overhaul the user means. Identify target mods with the user once vanilla gameplay passes.

## Implementation sequence

- [x] Add behavior tests for bounded configuration and new-settlement-only household capacity. Five new tests observed failing before implementation, then passed; 57 total pass.
- [x] Implement configuration and wire current controls to the adapter.
- [x] Package native `modsettings.json`; validate it with DFU's actual settings deserializer.
- [x] Rebuild and smoke-test settings loading alongside Basic Roads. Unity build exit 0; settings-loaded and readiness markers present in a fresh player log.
- [ ] Inspect the live menu and exercise in-game changes when the gameplay harness is available.
- [ ] Expand controls alongside later milestones; vanilla-first city patches remain part of M7.

## Original: SHOP-VISITS.md

# Shop visitor activities

Later continuous checkpoint: both local non-buyer and buyer pass five observed shelf stops and physical doorway exits (`work/ShopPhysicalProgress-locals-pass-foreign-plan-fail.log`). Maximum frame steps are 0.0399 m and 0.0205 m; the buyer spends 5.00 seconds at the counter. The failure after these passes is foreign visitor activity-plan creation, not a passed full regression. A diagnostic run identified `Visit cannot fit travel and activities` for the short local fixture; the planner now tries bounded alternative shelf sequences before rejection, and the local fixture uses a 24-minute visit. The foreign fixture's equally short window is adjusted in source but not rerun yet. Cross-scene progress loss remains unresolved. `work/ShopPhysicalProgress-build.log` records the installed build; a later diagnostic cleanup and foreign test-window adjustment are source-only.

Continuous traversal checkpoint: one native buyer now completes four distinct shelf stops, 5.01 real seconds at the keeper and a physical doorway exit. Maximum rendered step is 0.0218 m; last observed doorway distance is 0.145 m (`work/ShopPhysicalProgress-buyer-pass-nonbuyer-plan-fail.log`). The same run fails the next non-buyer because no usable activity plan was created. This is not full acceptance. The revised test keeps the player away from the entrance; previously the fixture itself blocked initial entry. Core tests pass 543/543. Runtime now follows surveyed legs with `InteriorRoomFollower` and starts dwell only on arrival (`ShopPhysicalProgress`, `PhysicalShopVisit`); it no longer samples a future shelf directly from the clock. Saved cross-scene continuity remains pending in [the continuity plan](HISTORY.md#source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-16-observed-visit-continuity-md).

16 October user regression: a shopper remains far from shelves/counter, then disappears after the player exits and re-enters. Source audit confirms two independent defects: loaded bodies chase clock-sampled positions even when delayed at earlier corners, and scene changes clear transient physical activity/departure state. Existing phase-jump tests did not cover continuous browsing. Physical waypoint-based activity progression is now implemented and under live verification; the revised probe watches the full visit continuously. Saved cross-scene continuation is still missing and must preserve the next itinerary stop, not force every shopper home. `VisitDeparture` currently means cancellation-to-home and must not be reused unchanged for this purpose. Household history work is temporarily deferred while this gameplay regression is addressed; its draft test is retained as `HouseholdHistoryTests.cs.pending` and is not part of the compiled suite.

Later rerun limitation: physical shop departure is not yet robust across crowd arrangements. After the passing cases below, another local non-buyer stalls at route point 42/113 beside shelf 41046 with another resident 0.15 m from its target (`work/ShopExit-local-crowd-regression.log`). The actor stays visible, but the exit does not finish. Keep this defect open; the earlier passing run must not be generalized to all shoppers.

16 October physical-exit checkpoint: the latest native run passes local buyer and non-buyer exits plus the previously blocked foreign visitor exit. The foreign visitor travels 12.32 m to the doorway, with maximum frame displacement 0.167 m and no duplicate or early disappearance. Bounded detour checks now include managed crowd bodies as well as native colliders. Evidence: `work/ShopDeparture-pass-crime-redirect-fail.log`. The full regression is not passing: its later guarded-crime-scene fixture schedules the newcomer before their arrival time and must be corrected and rerun. This does not establish general inn room arrival.

## 16 October foreign departure and crowd follow-up

The next diagnostic identifies the bypass blocker as shelf mesh 41046; floor support is valid (`work/town-test.log`, foreign exit failure). A local detour around the live crowd is under implementation. It must retain supported, collision-checked routes and cannot solve the test by allowing passage through shelves or people.

Foreign shop/social visitors now retain their existing indoor actor while following the surveyed doorway exit after their authoritative visit ends, matching the local departure path. This is transient rendering continuity; it does not retime the world itinerary or persist the physical delay. Native acceptance is not complete: the new foreign exit probe retains the actor correctly but stalls behind a dense group of stationary shoppers (`work/ForeignShopExit-crowd-still-blocked.log`). Do not report this departure as fixed. Follow-up diagnostics (`work/ForeignShopExit-bypass-diagnostic.log`) show the next-waypoint bypass is rejected despite a 0.4264 m horizontal segment and zero height delta. The current-waypoint sweep is clear; the next segment's blocker/support reason still needs isolation. Do not remove physical collision or permit walking through other people to make this test pass.

Core investigation reproduced a separate crowd defect: a small initial overlap made every swept candidate invalid at its starting point. `CrowdSteering.Move` now permits non-closing separation from an existing overlap, while preserving ordinary swept collision checks and the static-clear callback. Core suite passes 520/520 (`work/InteriorMovement-core.log`); Unity build passes. This does not resolve every crowded doorway or the remaining foreign-exit stall.

## 15 October varied browsing and purchase choice

Current runtime requests three to five distinct reachable browsing anchors, usually checking two sections near a shelf before selecting another. Stops use the corrected resident clearance and distinct anchors within each visit. Where travel cannot fit the appointment, the itinerary reduces its browsing count rather than exceeding the schedule. Missing usable geometry retains the existing finite standing fallback.

Purchase probability is 50%, selected from a stable mixed appearance/building/day seed. Buyers walk to a separate keeper stop, face the keeper for three to seven real seconds (36–84 game seconds at the standard12x clock), then depart. Non-buyers skip both the counter route and haggling phase. This represents the activity visually; no item or money transfer exists yet. Browsing dwell shares vary deterministically between stops. Reservations conflict only while visits overlap; morning visitors no longer reserve anchors for the entire day. Selection may use either accessible side represented by the surveyed anchors; comprehensive shelf-side coverage remains unverified.

The core journey now accepts a variable number of browsing legs, with or without a purchase, and reconstructs every position from absolute time.364/364 tests pass (`work/ShopPurchase-final-core.log`). The live shop test selects both deterministic purchase outcomes on separate days: the buyer has five planned shelf stops and60 game seconds at the counter; the non-buyer has four planned stops and no counter phase. Both pass rendered approach, first browsing stop, task-facing, dialogue/restoration and departure checks (`work/ShopPurchase-both-runtime-pass.log`). Unity integration/build passes (`work/ShopPurchase-build-editor.log`). This is not uninterrupted traversal acceptance for every stop or every shop layout.

## 15 October directional-facing correction

Busy adult resident actors now retain task-facing despite small crowd corrections and use a frozen native directional walking frame instead of the forced frontal idle record. Actors still approaching their stop face their actual displacement; reaching the logical browsing phase alone does not make them walk backwards. Dialogue temporarily overrides the pose immediately, retains player-facing while the talk window is on the UI stack, and restores task-facing after closing. This affects managed resident actors; it does not add missing directional art to stationary flats or children.

The live regression exposed another cause of shelf jitter: resident navigation used a 0.24-metre radius while CrowdMotion used 0.4 metres, allowing a selected stop to overlap shelf mesh 41046. Resident interior surveys now use the same 0.4-metre body radius as movement. The test records this obstruction in `work/ShopStop-obstruction-runtime-red.log`.

Editor regressions failed before each corresponding fix (small lateral corrections, directional standing state, and approach-facing) and pass afterward. The actual shop probe passes stable task-facing/frozen frames across 60 rendered frames for both browsing and haggling, immediate conversation-facing, restoration on close, animated walking and scheduled exit. Evidence: `work/ConversationFacing-runtime-pass.log`, `work/ConversationFacing-build-editor.log`; core suite 362/362 in `work/DirectionalStanding-core.log`. These checks do not establish every camera angle, shop layout or street conversation behavior. Varied browsing and the 50% purchase choice below remain pending.

The native keeper upstairs-to-exit regression also passes (`work/ActivityFacing-stairs-runtime-pass.log`,124 reachable sampled routes and physical descent). That probe covers the keeper's existing escape routing; it is not broad acceptance of every resident route at the revised radius.

Remaining activity requirements: [activity follow-up](HISTORY.md#source-activity-and-gate-followup-md). The section below records the older mandatory-counter implementation; the checkpoints above supersede it.

Development version 0.3.32 replaces fixed standing positions for usable shop visits with timed interior activities.

## Behavior

A visitor enters at the surveyed interior entrance, walks to two shelf stops, spends time browsing, walks to a merchant stop, spends time haggling, and walks back to the entrance before the daily visit ends. Each position derives from absolute game time; movement does not accumulate frame history. Walking uses the existing 1.3 metres per real second and 12 game seconds per real second conversion.

The geometry adapter discovers native ShopShelves loot objects and a static merchant. It chooses supported standing anchors near those objects. Routes connect existing surveyed floor/stair paths through their common ancestor, preserving the checked geometry instead of drawing straight lines through furniture. The visitor logically faces the shelf or merchant while stopped. Native idle art still faces the player; directional idle artwork remains unfinished.

All scheduled visitors to that shop receive stopping positions in stable population order, including visitors currently elsewhere. Different visitors reserve different stopping anchors. The allocation reconstructs daily or when the interior reloads. It does not add save fields or menu settings. Deaths or layout changes can change which routes are available. Crime reactions take precedence over shop activities.

If a shop has no identified shelf/merchant, lacks a usable route, cannot fit its walking plus at least one minute of activities into the visit, or lacks free stopping anchors, the visitor retains the existing standing fallback and leaves when the visit ends. This fallback does not extend the appointment indefinitely. Stop reservations currently cover all daily visitors, so busy shops can exhaust anchors even when appointments do not overlap. Full moving-body avoidance and counter queues remain unfinished. Haggling is an activity pose; no inventory or money transaction is implemented.

## Verification

- 272/272 core tests pass. Tests cover fractional travel, browsing/haggling/departure, reconstruction without frame history, defensive path copying, exact daily appointment bounds, disconnected paths and insufficient duration. Evidence: `work/ShopJourney-{red,green}.log`.
- `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -ShopVisitProbe` passes in the actual Gothway Garden shop fixture. A controlled generated visitor uses normal StreetPopulation updates; the probe checks rendered walking with animation, phase positions and idle states, and removal after scheduled departure. It changes a runtime appointment in the dedicated test process and does not save it. Evidence: `work/ShopJourney-live-pass.log`.
- `-IndoorNoiseProbe` passes on the rebuilt bundle: bed-origin waking, actual save/reload, physical reporting, neutral finite dispatch and guard arrival remain intact. Evidence: `work/ShopJourney-noise-regression-pass.log`.
- Unity build passes. Installed 0.3.32 bundle SHA-256: `3940B55F421B1420932A2BC26F4FDC9BFD1DB4C3727C67FD22590EBC2AD7A188`.

The live activity check uses short continuous movement plus clock jumps to inspect later phases. It does not prove uninterrupted traversal of every shop layout, actual interior re-entry, or full crowd behavior. These remain gameplay acceptance work.

<a id="source-shopping-and-stationary-identities-md"></a>

## Original: SHOPPING-AND-STATIONARY-IDENTITIES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Shopping behavior and stationary NPC identities

Shopkeepers are normal persistent people with full names, not extra anonymous occupants. An on-site keeper is the same-ID head of the shop household and reserves a bed before family allocation. Family members share the keeper's surname and race metadata; each person requires one actual bed. One bed means only the keeper lives there. Extra staff bed reservations remain exclusive. The native service actor is retained instead of generating a second head. Bedless-shop owners' separate homes and commuting remain pending. Taverns remain excluded from residential family allocation.

0.3.21 removes cached-route and interior-graph prerequisites from concealment of an unwitnessed keeper death. Finite settlement ownership remains active in a supported shop before its exterior approach is cached; route absence must not create immediate guards or an automatic Murder charge. Physical response still requires surveyed travel. See GUARD-BUILDING-RESPONSE.md for the exact remaining queue limitation.

0.3.20 connects on-site native keeper schedules to their reserved physical beds. Wake/sleep minutes use stable identity variation and stay outside actual vanilla shop opening hours. The original StaticNPC remains the interaction identity; the sleeping composite replaces only its renderer, and a shallow bed-oriented BoxCollider replaces its standing bounds temporarily. Waking restores the original renderer, collider, rotation and service position. Sleeping keepers cannot converse/trade or witness theft/violence; active crime responses override normal sleep. The sleep-pose toggle controls presentation, while scheduled awareness still applies.

Matching native composite assets cover male flat182/0 and182/44 only, with green blankets and white pillows; other faces are not substituted. The Peryrick fixture is182/0 despite its native hash44. Atlas lookup uses TextureArchive/TextureRecord; the head-first generated rows are adapted to SleepView's foot-to-head order. See SPRITE-PROMPTS.md for saved assets and exact built-in generation prompts. More appearance/angle/bed visual acceptance remains necessary.

The actual-player probe verifies Peryrick's bed slot0, blocked nighttime dialogue, actual night save/reload, preserved name, shallow collider, morning position/renderer/collider restoration, all-sleeping theft awareness and a weapon death remaining at the bed position without resurrection. SAVE39, `Living Cities - keeper sleeping`, preserves the living sleeper; SAVE35 remains the living daytime couple and SAVE38 the separate death fixture. A live negative check caught native theft detection treating the sleeper as awake; it now reads the same native schedule/alarm state. Bedless commute, travel to/from bed, broader art coverage, combat/spell damage and bereavement remain unfinished.

Historical source foundation55accda: DailySchedule.Workday supports a home and workplace with the same building key, an empty exterior route and exactly zero commute. Work must fit within waking hours; invented routes/commutes and sleeping through business hours are rejected. Exact opening/closing/wake/sleep boundaries, fractional seconds and reconstruction thousands of days later have core coverage. Core196/196 and native Unity schedule checks pass. At that checkpoint the player remained0.3.19 and runtime sleep was not connected;0.3.20 supersedes that boundary for on-site keepers with matching art.

Runtime integration requirements: compute the keeper's work window from the actual native shop type, keep wake/sleep outside that window with stable per-person variation, reserve the assigned physical bed, restore the original service position after waking, and let crime responses override normal sleep/work. Matching complete sleeping art is required before displaying a native sleeping identity. Existing SleepingArt.ForResident covers only two mobile appearances and explicitly forbids borrowing another person's face; SleepingPose requires a ResidentActor and capsule collider. Native merchant flats retain StaticNPC and their original service identity, so pose/collider integration and matching composite sprites remain necessary. Preserve the green blanket, white pillow and single-person-per-bed rule; no separate head/blanket layers.

0.3.19 adds weapon-hit deaths for registered ordinary native merchant flats while civilian reactions are enabled. DFU's native WeaponDamage handles MobilePersonNPC but has no StaticNPC damage branch. A scoped completion handler supplies native civilian one-hit lethality only for owned active merchant flats without an entity, quest resource or action. It preserves the original service/body identity, mirrors a linked household head's death, records a positioned corpse and collects nearby awake living family witnesses. Finite guards receive reports through the existing physical witness/discovery pipeline; the handler does not report instantly when the finite controller owns the interior. With finite guards disabled, native reporting remains available. Dead native bodies stay hidden and both native service entry points remain blocked after reload.

The core ledger now admits both linked native heads and unlinked staff as victims. Unwitnessed native deaths can create a scene at their workplace as well as their assigned home. The scene remains undiscovered until player departure and actual investigation arrival. Native serializer checks cover both kinds of victim; transactional restore rejects missing victim identity and inconsistent linked life state. Core tests pass194/194. The actual-player test reproduces the previously ignored keeper hit, verifies a deliberately positioned partner as a direct witness, no immediate guard notification, a physical corpse and a real save/reload with disabled trade/dialogue. It does not yet prove an unrestricted natural encounter or physical partner escape from every floor.

SAVE38, `Living Cities - keeper death`, preserves the killed Peryrick Kinghart fixture. SAVE35 remains the living couple fixture. Weapon-only lethality is verified; spell damage, merchant health/combat, bereaved-partner hostility, individual corpse art/inventory, head sleep/work and commuting remain unfinished. These are not completed by this death integration.

0.3.15 household bugfix replaces unclaimed generic residential flats with the authoritative saved family representation, excluding active quest resources/actions/listeners and service/faction sites. This prevents extra residents over bed capacity. It is a temporary reconciliation boundary: original appearance/identity mapping and shopkeeper service/bed integration still need implementation. Do not classify these extra generic house flats as planned visitors. See HOUSEHOLD-RECONCILIATION.md.

Crime-response clarification: a shopkeeper who becomes mobile to attack or flee cannot be talked to or traded with until the crime response is cleared and normal behavior resumes. Preserving the service role means preserving identity and later service availability, not permitting interaction during combat or flight. Apply the restriction to native activation and any new dialogue entry point, including reloads and the stationary-to-mobile handoff.

Approved requirement, 14 October 2026: a shop visit is a finite sequence of activities, not standing at one generic interior anchor. Customers walk between accessible shelves, pause facing goods, approach the shopkeeper, spend a bounded interval haggling, then leave and resume their next scheduled activity. Use physical routes, occupied-space avoidance and distinct waiting positions. Customer browsing must stay in the sales area, not private bedrooms. A missing or unreachable shelf must have a bounded fallback; it must not leave a customer stuck indefinitely. Persist or deterministically reconstruct activity phase and deadlines across interior transitions and reloads.

All stationary NPCs except main-quest NPCs must participate as real persistent people, with names, identities, household/home ownership where appropriate, beds, relationships and schedules. They can become mobile to work, travel, flee or fight. Preserve an existing native name and identity rather than inventing a duplicate person. Retain trade, guild and other service roles when the actor changes presentation. Side-quest participation remains possible: maintain quest bindings to that same person and the correct location/name rather than treating all quest participants as permanently excluded.

A shopkeeper with one bed upstairs occupies that bed; do not allocate another permanent resident over the keeper's capacity. A bedless shopkeeper lives in a separate assigned home and returns there after closing. Visitors do not acquire bed ownership from being inside a shop. Main-quest NPCs remain outside this general conversion under this requirement; their behavior needs explicit quest-specific handling.

Household-head amendment, 14 October: the native shopkeeper is an ordinary persistent person with a full name and is the head of the shop's household. When additional beds exist, allocate a family rather than unrelated permanent residents; family members share the keeper's surname. Include the keeper in the total resident/bed capacity. Preserve the original native person and service role, with no additional generated keeper. A one-bed shop has only the keeper as an owner; shopping visitors remain separate. On-site linkage is implemented in 0.3.18 below; full native keeper behavior is still incomplete.

Checkpoint 0.3.18 adds an on-site household headed by the original registered keeper. The native metadata and head resident share one ID; no generated head actor is spawned. Preserve the keeper's full name and assign family members the same surname. Two free family beds permit an opposite-sex partner; larger homes use the existing deterministic family-size and child rules, including at most three children. All new family members inherit the keeper's race metadata. Existing child art remains unchanged. A one-bed shop contains only the native owner as a permanent household member.

Reserve existing native staff beds before generating a family. New members receive explicit saved bed slots in the remaining capacity, so a head in slot 0 and staff in slot 1 can have a partner in slot 2 without overlap. Staff keep their original identities and reservations; they are separate from the owner's family. Do not overwrite an existing unrelated household or create a new living family around a dead unlinked keeper. Saved families retain their identities, names and relationships when later survey data changes.

Native head lifetime has a nonserialized reference to its saved native metadata, rebuilt on restore; core death changes both records. This is a persistence prerequisite, not an implemented static NPC combat/death adapter. Family actors use persisted full names and race metadata, and receive the configured home wake/sleep schedule. Head sleep/work, bedless-home family integration, daytime family visits and broad stationary conversion remain pending. The existing quest pilot excludes these service households until service-aware identity/placement is reviewed, leaving their native quests intact.

Actual-player HousingProbe verifies Peryrick Kinghart and Vannara Kinghart in the two-bed general store: same surname, opposite sex, correct partner identity/race, no generated head duplicate, and real SAVE35 reload. Keeper response, native weapon witness capture and service recovery still pass with the family present. Core male/female heads, children/parent links, single-bed capacity, sparse staff reservations, contradictory head slots, death-link persistence and legacy preservation pass. Native FullSerializer verifies metadata/bed/head fields and reconstructs the lifetime reference without serializing it twice. 192 core tests and Unity build pass. Evidence: work/KeeperFamily-0318-pass.log.

Checkpoint 0.3.16 adds saved native merchant identities keyed by settlement, workplace and native hash, preserving native name, sex, race, faction and flat. Bed assignment reserves a free on-site bed first, then a free known residential bed for a bedless workplace. Existing assignments survive layout changes and reloads; invalid or overlapping reservations are rejected transactionally. No duplicate keeper actor is spawned, and native trade remains intact.

The actual-player fixture in The Superior General Store (131343) has two physical beds. Peryrick Kinghart reserves slot 0 and retains the same native identity and reservation after real SAVE35 reload. The user's reported tiny one-bed general store has not been identified conclusively. One-bed ownership and bedless-home allocation have core tests; commuting and sleeping do not yet use these reservations. Evidence: work/KeeperIdentity-0316-pass.log, native persistence verification and 185 passing core tests.

Checkpoint 0.3.17 connects registered merchants to generated-resident violence. Awake visible keepers become direct witnesses in the saved bounded alarm ledger. Native service activation and direct dialogue are blocked throughout their response, including reloads. Keepers follow surveyed interior routes at a stable 2.5–3.5x walking speed, leave through the actual exit and notify a finite posted guard only after the saved exterior journey reaches that guard. Missing routes retain a pending response and retry; they do not justify teleporting guards. When the incident expires and the player's crime clears, the original native keeper and service resume.

The store fixture exposed a closed ordinary interior door dividing the keeper room from the entrance hall. Navigation now treats unlocked, unscripted doors as openable, and moving civilians open such a door on their immediate path. Wall occlusion, locked doors and scripted actions remain barriers. Opening currently uses the native instant-open option; animated door waiting remains future work. Interior survey uses 40 cm cells with a bounded 3,600-node budget and 80 standing anchors. Saved native keeper escape has no exterior rendered body yet, and recovery restores the native service position rather than a physical return commute.

Actual-player HousingProbe and standalone KeeperResponseProbe verify saved sight response, both service hooks, interior movement/door exit, real SAVE36 reload, delayed finite reporting and service recovery. A controlled visitor placement followed by actual native WeaponDamage also captures the keeper witness and blocks service. Separate actual-player door checks retain a wall barrier, lock and scripted door and open an ordinary door. Evidence: work/KeeperResponse-0317-pass.log; 186 core tests and native Unity checks pass. This does not establish unrestricted natural shop crime, all layouts or native keeper death.

SAVE35, `Living Cities - shopkeeper identity`, and SAVE36, `Living Cities - keeper response`, belong to LivingCitiesTest-f238d317. `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -KeeperResponseProbe` starts from SAVE35 and overwrites only SAVE36. HousingProbe refreshes both. The probe's later native weapon kill is not saved. In 0.3.18 these fixtures include the keeper and named partner.

Current implementation remains incomplete: generated residents receive a 20–60 game-minute visit and stand at generic interior anchors. Bedless-home family integration, head home commuting/sleep, richer relationships, combat, spell damage and broader stationary conversion remain pending. Native weapon-hit death and real reload are verified in 0.3.19; broader natural encounters still need acceptance. These requirements must not be marked complete by identity registration or a visitor timer alone.

Acceptance: observed shelf-to-shelf travel and facing, bounded haggling and exit, crowded counter queues, no private-bedroom browsing, missing shelf/keeper fallback, closure during a visit, unloaded/reloaded visit continuity, one-bed keeper occupancy, bedless keeper commute, preserved buying/selling dialogue, named stationary-to-mobile identity continuity, flight/fight and death, side-quest binding continuity, and explicit main-quest exclusion.

<a id="source-sleeping-checkpoint-md"></a>

## Original: SLEEPING-CHECKPOINT.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Sleeping foundation checkpoint — 14 October 2026

> Current amendment, 14 October 2026: one NPC per bed; maximum household residents equals actual bed count, with no separate five-person cap. Preserve old saves and use a fresh compliant test population. Shared-bed rendering, kill-all-bed logic and separate head/blanket layers are superseded. Keep complete green-blanket/white-pillow single-sleeper sprites. Children may inherit either parent's race; keep existing child sprites now. Gameplay takes priority over art. See [current citizen behavior and next guard work](HISTORY.md#source-civilian-responses-md). Earlier checkpoint descriptions below remain historical evidence.

## Version 0.3.2: female appearance and green blankets

Sleeping now supports two generated adult appearances in unshared home beds: Breton male variant zero (archive 385) and female variant zero (archive 453). Each has a separately cached five-view atlas. Both atlases and the current flat-182/1 side study now use green blankets matching the original bed cover, with white pillows, as requested. Historical white studies are superseded. Full generation/edit prompts and source metadata are in `art/sleep/README.md`, `mobile-453-prompt.txt` and `green-blanket-edit-prompt.txt`. The female green PNG is 793×1984 pixels; this is still prototype art, not native-resolution production coverage.

The expanded actual-player `-SleepProbe` passes both appearances through bedtime, midnight, waking with original actor/name restored, sleep-toggle changes, raycast interaction, living save/reload, death and dead save/reload, then restoration of the living preview. Existing street/interior/family tests pass in the same run. The female fixture is Elyzyna Wickford (`s:206728581:h:66049:r:0`), building 66049, character `LivingCitiesTest-126494eb`. SAVE21 is **Living Cities - sleeping preview - female**; SAVE22 is the corresponding disposable death case. SAVE17/SAVE18 remain the male fixtures.

Visual inspection exposed an end-view sizing bug that lifecycle checks did not detect: the tall female atlas frame became a 1.8-metre-high billboard and its center was lifted 0.7 metres above the sleeping actor. The new whole-sprite viewport assertion failed in that exact foot-end view before the fix. The renderer now keeps the drawing centered at the mattress/body anchor and caps image-derived height using the projected bed/body footprint. The corrected foot view is about 1.3 metres high with no additional center offset, fits the bedside camera and was visually inspected alongside both side views. This fixes the oversize/offset defect, not every perspective/art fidelity issue. Near-overhead viewing remains untested; the fallback basis can change orientation at its threshold.

Verification: `tools/Build.ps1` exits 0 with separate five-row/transparency/footprint checks for both PNGs; `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -SleepProbe` exits 0; ordinary `tools/SmokeTest.ps1` startup passes. Evidence: `work/sleep-green-native-pass.log`, `work/sleep-green-runtime-pass.log`, `work/player-smoke.log`. Red evidence is `work/sleep-female-red.log` (unsupported female) and `work/sleep-footprint-red.log` (oversized view). Core code is unchanged from the 119/119 checkpoint. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed defect for normal bedside sizing or lifetime; screenshots, rather than structural checks alone, established the new face and blanket appearance.

Built/installed bundle SHA-256: `807DEAD3566DF7C6718D106C297FB4490E0E107ACFA0C9CAAA4007B933B67B06`. Other adult variants, children, shared-family compositions, original stationary NPC mapping, bed approach, tavern bookings and stealth waking remain open. No complete M2/M3 or all-sprite coverage is claimed. The sections below preserve earlier checkpoints.

Version 0.3.1 adds a limited sleeping prototype: generated adult men using mobile archive 385 can sleep in unshared home beds. Other appearances and shared beds still use standing presence. No M2/M3 completion is claimed.

## Runtime prototype

The renderer keeps the same ResidentActor and MobilePersonNPC, hides its standing asset, places a directional blanket/face visual at the native mattress, and changes the existing interaction capsule to a horizontal body. Morning and the live `DailyRoutine/SleepingPoses` toggle restore the original asset and capsule. Five atlas views are selected by camera direction, with mirrored opposite views. The first atlas is oversized generated prototype art, not completed classic-resolution production coverage; its provenance and exact prompt are in `art/sleep/README.md`.

Core tests pass 97/97, including three direction-selection cases. The Unity build checks the five separated atlas views, transparent borders against the native shader cutoff, end/side proportions, eight settings controls and the reopened bundle.

`tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -SleepProbe` passed in the actual player. Edwore Coppercroft (`s:206728581:h:263:r:0`, building 263) sleeps at 23:00 and midnight, wakes with the same native person/name and restored upright capsule at 07:00, and responds to the sleep toggle. Camera raycasts reach the sleeping interaction target. Actual SAVE17 reload restores the living pose; entity death removes the actor, SAVE18 reload retains death, and reloading SAVE17 restores the living preview. This tests the death callback, not melee damage, crime attribution or sleeping dialogue. Existing street/interior/family checks passed in the same run. Evidence: `work/sleep-runtime-pass.log`; earlier failing checks are retained under `work/sleep-runtime-red.log` and camera diagnostic logs. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed important production-code issue.

Actual side and foot-end screenshots were inspected: blanket/head are visible on the mattress with transparent surroundings. The waking screenshot points toward the downstairs standing destination but is occluded; the visible standing pose is not established by that screenshot. Waking identity, original asset activation and capsule restoration are established by runtime assertions. Physical stair/bed approach remains unimplemented.

The screenshot harness needed two corrections: native `SetFacing` changes fields but its Update may skip applying transforms in the unattended process; the probe now explicitly applies the native body-yaw/camera-pitch split and checks centered projection. Player positions are grounded by interior floor raycasts near the upstairs bed, excluding the player's own collider, with movement cleared before teleporting. The general standing-anchor survey deliberately does not traverse stairs and cannot supply this upstairs camera position. The corrected preview survives actual reload without the previous below-floor camera placement.

Remaining limits: no shared-bed blanket compositions, child or other adult appearance coverage, original stationary NPC binding, tavern bookings, stealth wake reactions, or walking to/from the bed. Entering/leaving the sleep pose currently snaps between the bed and the existing standing anchor. The native NPC/quest integration is still separate, and broad quest compatibility remains pending.

## Verified behavior

`HomeBeds.Assign` derives stable logical claims from permanent household IDs and stable interior bed record indices. Couples share, additional residents use spare beds, and a five-person family may share one bed without reducing population. Dead members retain their allocation in the derivation so survivors keep their slots; only living members receive active claims. New household identities will create new assignments. Enumeration order and serialization do not change the result. Foreign residents cannot use this household API as a substitute for tavern booking.

`BedGeometry` extracts the mattress from actual original model geometry rather than assuming the furniture bounding-box top is the mattress. This matters for canopy bed 41002. Placement follows DFU's rotation, scale and prop-bottom rules.

Evidence:

- `dotnet run --project tests/LivingCities.Tests -c Release`: 94/94 passed, including seven new bed tests. The seven cases first failed against an explicit unimplemented allocator; logs are `work/bed-assignment-red.log` and `work/bed-assignment-green.log`.
- `tools/Build.ps1`: Unity 2019.4.40f1 exit 0, native serialization/settings/adapter and bundle checks passed. Original bed models 41000–41002 passed mattress bounds, canopy exclusion, rotation, upstairs offset and prop placement checks. Geometry had a prior failing native test before implementation (`work/bed-geometry-red.log`).
- `tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-126494eb -BedProbe`: all three models matched loaded mattress colliders within 0.005 metres: 41001 in building 4/record 13, 41000 in building 16777216/record 21, and canopy bed 41002 in building 1/record 22. Five resident claims share the family home's one bed and reproduce after loading the real family save. The same run passed existing street animation, interior continuity, death/reload and family tests. Evidence: `work/bed-runtime-pass.log`. Read-only review identified the initial one-model scene coverage gap; the expanded run closes it.
- Source survey exported 226 FLATS.CFG records, 25 mobile appearance archives and three bed meshes. `art/sleep/source-appearances.csv` is uncurated metadata: it includes nonhuman candidates and does not prove complete human coverage or accepted assets.

## Current art direction

The latest user color amendment requires thick green blankets matching the classic bed cover, with white pillows. This supersedes the original white blankets for all current and future sleeping art. The blanket covers the body to the neck, with only the face/head visible. Reusable blanket poses reduce outfit work, while the face and adult/child proportions preserve identity. The camera usually sees a bed from the side and slightly above; directional side/angled/head/foot views are required. A horizontal overhead cutout is not the finished renderer.

`art/sleep/drafts/flat-182-1-blanket-side-v1.png` is the first side/angled study from original flat 182/1. Built-in image generation produced 2138×736 RGBA pixels with transparent corners, despite the native-scale prompt. It needs native-scale/style review, directional consistency and actual in-engine inspection. Two earlier overhead images are retained as explicitly superseded drafts. None is packed into the playable mod.

## Remaining implementation

Extend the limited runtime prototype across supported appearances and shared beds. Define family blanket compositions that physically fit shared beds and handle missing/unsupported geometry explicitly. Bind original home/shop NPCs without duplicates, including authoritative quest household surnames and destinations. Implement paid traveler/player room rights before tavern sleepers or occupied-bed enforcement. Expand approved sleeping faces/views across all required human appearances, including children. Verify sleep-specific exit/re-entry, native service/quest activation, waking reactions and physical bed approach before claiming the complete feature.

# Native sleeper sprite generation

Generated with the built-in imagegen tool on 14 October 2026. Final assets are copied into this repository unchanged, preserving alpha; no external paid API or CLI fallback was used. These are individual complete sleeper composites, not separated heads/blankets. Effective pixel scale and final in-game appearance still need broader visual acceptance.

## Native flat182/0

Reference: work/sleep-source/flat-182-0.png, extracted from the installed classic game files. Match by native TextureArchive/TextureRecord, not NativeHash. Peryrick Kinghart's fixture has hash44 but sprite182/0.

Saved asset: mod/Textures/sleep-flat-182-0.png.bytes. Original generated output: C:/Users/Daniel/.codex/generated_images/01a135ac-12fe-7041-9e19-3dbc601bd8cb/exec-5d6f22c9-26e5-4cd4-b430-ce8b711383c1.png. Inspection confirms real alpha and five separated opaque row groups. Canvas1145x1374; the sprite uses coarse pixel blocks. Imported without resizing.

Final prompt:

> Classic 1996 Daggerfall low-resolution limited-palette pixel-art sprite atlas of the EXACT male merchant in reference: middle-aged fair-skinned man, short brown/reddish hair, short reddish-brown beard and moustache. NO HOOD, NO HAT. Same face and classic sprite style. Asleep with closed eyes lying HORIZONTALLY, body fully covered to chin by thick DARK GREEN blanket, one WHITE pillow under head. No bed/frame/props. Five rows vertically with large TRANSPARENT gaps and margins, each row ONE contiguous complete sleeper composite including head+pillow+blanket. No separate head layers. Rows top to bottom: (1) head-end view, (2) three-quarter between head-end and broadside, (3) pure broadside head LEFT and feet RIGHT, (4) three-quarter from foot-end, (5) foot-end straight view. Consistent camera eye height from a person beside bed, never overhead. Broadside horizontal and widest; end views narrower and foreshortened. Single same merchant sleeping peacefully in each of FIVE frames. Hard crisp pixel edges, minimal classic colors, roughly120x32 effective pixels per broadside,50x35 end views. Transparent outside each sprite, absolutely NO background, glow, shadow aura, grids, text, labels, extra faces, standing people, props or frame. Head and blanket form ONE connected sprite per row. Green blanket and white pillow identical all rows.

## Native flat182/44

Reference: work/sleep-source/flat-182-44.png. This is the hooded elder appearance, not the Peryrick fixture. Saved asset: mod/Textures/sleep-flat-182-44.png.bytes. Original output: C:/Users/Daniel/.codex/generated_images/01a135ac-12fe-7041-9e19-3dbc601bd8cb/exec-99293d05-d54e-4bf7-909d-ab1edb0ddef0.png. Canvas1024x1536; alpha and five opaque row groups verified. This appearance has asset coverage but no dedicated live native-keeper fixture acceptance yet.

He is peacefully asleep lying horizontally on a bed, body entirely covered up to chin in a thick DARK GREEN blanket, white pillow under his head; only face/hood visible. Do NOT draw the bed or frame. Each sprite is a COMPLETE contiguous blanket+pillow+head composite, not separate pieces. Transparent background, no text, no grid, no labels, no extra props or staff. Five separated rows from top to bottom, each row contains exactly ONE complete sleeper: row1 view from head end; row2 three-quarter view between head end and broadside; row3 pure side profile broadside, head LEFT feet RIGHT; row4 three-quarter from foot end; row5 straight foot-end view with face foreshortened beyond blanket. All cameras at modest eye height like a person standing beside a bed, NOT overhead. Consistent single-person identity, blanket color, length, camera height and scale. Broadside is widest, end views narrower, shallow horizontal shapes, never standing figures. Plenty of transparent margins and vertical gaps between rows. Crisp limited-palette authentic low-resolution pixel art, each broadside around 120 pixels wide and 32 pixels high, end around 50 pixels wide and 35 pixels high. No smoothing or painted gradients. Match classic reference style precisely. Output vertical five-row atlas.

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-physical-town-slice.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Physical town slice

Continue the approved goal through the first playable population slice. Use the existing tested core, preserving static quest actors and saved identities. Do not infer gameplay success from a menu startup marker.

Checkpoint, 14 October: step 1 is verified, including real inside/outside saves and fresh-process reloads. Step 2 has an actual-town adapter (156 anchors, 17 rejected door candidates, 11 sampled routes). Step 3 includes fractional-second visits and persisted routes; 66 core tests pass. Steps 4–5 now have Gothway physical actors, walking animation, matching interior presence, re-entry deduplication, living/dead indoor save/load and schema migration verified in the actual player. Whole-town route traversal, origin shifts and cross-map continuity remain open. Review found and helped fix the House1 destination bug. Read the amended family, child, bed-sharing and tavern rules in `docs/MILESTONES.md`; the original three-person cap remains in code pending family allocation.

1. Add an explicit command-line-only test harness that creates a uniquely named disposable character and loads Gothway Garden using actual game data. Inspect real building doors and navgrid dimensions. Preserve all normal user saves and settings on disk.
2. Add a settlement adapter that maps stable building keys to valid exterior entrance anchors. Snap an entrance only through a short unobstructed segment to a walkable cell; unreachable entrances get a reported conservative fallback. Use sorted keys and stable selections.
3. Add route/schedule planning and smooth sub-minute evaluation with behavior tests first. Travel duration derives from distance and walking speed/time scale. Use deterministic work destinations and real opening hours; no-route residents stay home. Separate routes from rendered actors.
4. Add distance-based actor activation with hysteresis, stable identity bindings, classic sprites and guarded Unity random state. Keep actors keyed by resident ID. Disable the vanilla random civilian pool only while a supported settlement adapter owns it; restore it when disabled/unloaded. Retain stationary quest actors and vanilla guards until their dedicated replacement is ready.
5. Exercise actual town loading, actors, route boundaries and world-origin coordinates. Then save/load and leave/return with a real save slot, recording identity/death continuity and checking duplicate actors. Report limitations for interiors, anchors and quest actors explicitly.
6. Review changes, fix meaningful findings, update milestones and Obsidian with verified results. Keep the overall goal active.

Relationships and future inventory extensions use the same persistent identities; see `docs/RELATIONSHIPS-AND-FUTURE-INVENTORIES.md`. City-overhaul adapters follow vanilla acceptance.

## Immediate continuity extension, 14 October

The user observed a resident disappear on entering a house and a frozen walking sprite. Prioritize functional milestones over appearance; defer collision-checked curved paths and other visual polish. Fix the animation-state reset with an actual-frame regression check. Then render the same scheduled resident in the currently loaded destination/home interior, using collision-checked reachable floor anchors and the same identity/name/appearance/death record. Remove the street representation during interior transitions and never duplicate vanilla quest/service identities. Interior activity poses, full room navigation, furniture assignment and sleep placement follow after presence continuity works.

Acceptance: observe walking frame changes; advance a sampled resident to arrival; enter the matching real door; find that resident with unchanged name/appearance; exit and re-enter without duplicates; persist a death and verify save/load prevents respawn. Test fixtures must reproduce the game's load-button behavior by returning to the HUD; a loaded but menu-paused game cannot test actor updates.
