<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-13-population-foundation-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-population-foundation.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Population Foundation Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** Use the executing-plans skill to implement this plan task by task in the current session. Code review uses the requesting-code-review skill. Steps use checkboxes for tracking.

**Goal:** Build a deterministic, testable population core and load it as a standard DFU mod with persisted state and observable integration diagnostics.

**Architecture:** Keep identities, household allocation, paths, schedules, and save validation in a Unity-independent C# core. The DFU adapter reads layout and time, owns mod lifecycle and persistence, and initially reports simulated state without displacing quest-bound vanilla actors. Actor replacement is the next independently tested slice, not an implied consequence of loading this foundation.

**Tech Stack:** C# 7.3, .NET Standard 2.0, .NET 8 console behavior tests, DFU 1.1.1, Unity 2019.4.40f1, DFU source asset bundles.

**Spec:** `../specs/2026-10-13-living-cities-design.md`, approved by the user on 13 October 2026.

## Global constraints

- Standard DFU mod; preserve quests and saves. Engine changes require a demonstrated limitation.
- Maximum three permanent residents per household. Identity and appearance may not depend on enumeration or frame order.
- NPC records survive scene changes. Save schemas are versioned and invalid input may not partially replace live state.
- No Unity APIs or process-global random state in the simulation core.
- C# 7.3 and .NET Standard 2.0 compatibility. No inference, networking, or expensive work in frame loops.
- Deaths persist; protected actors cannot be killed through the civilian death API. Recruitment is a later bounded subsystem.
- Do not claim physical actor integration from a simulation-only milestone.

## Task 1 Household identities

**Files:** `src/LivingCities.Core/Population.cs`, `StableSeed.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests/PopulationTests.cs`, project files and `Program.cs`.

**Interfaces:** `Population.Create(uint seed, int settlementId, IEnumerable<int> homeKeys)` returns `SettlementPopulation`; records contain household keys, resident IDs, appearance seeds, and alive/protected state. `Population.MarkDead(SettlementPopulation, string)` updates only the matching non-protected resident.

- [x] Write behavior tests using a reversed building order, distinct settlements, duplicate building keys, and an empty layout. Mutation caught: allocation from loop index or process-global Random changes identities when input order changes.

```csharp
var first = Population.Create(42, 100, new[] { 20, 10 });
var second = Population.Create(42, 100, new[] { 10, 20 });
Check.Equal(Signature(first), Signature(second));
Check.True(first.Households.All(h => h.Residents.Count >= 1 && h.Residents.Count <= 3));
```

- [x] Run `dotnet run --project tests/LivingCities.Tests`; see explicit failing behavior results with the unimplemented contract.
- [x] Implement sorted unique home allocation with stable integer mixing and culture-independent IDs. Reject duplicate homes rather than silently creating duplicate residents.
- [x] Run all tests; add death idempotency and protected-person tests. Commit verified task.

## Task 2 Deterministic street routing

**Files:** `src/LivingCities.Core/GridPathfinder.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests/PathTests.cs`.

**Interfaces:** `IWalkGrid.Width`, `Height`, and `Cost(int x, int z)`; zero means blocked. `GridPathfinder.Find(IWalkGrid, GridCell, GridCell, int)` returns a list of cells or an empty list for unreachable routes or exhausted search budget.

- [x] Write tests for an obstacle detour, an unreachable destination, costly shortcuts, start=end, out-of-bounds inputs, and deterministic tie-breaking.

```csharp
var path = GridPathfinder.Find(new TestGrid("111", "001", "111"),
    new GridCell(0, 0), new GridCell(0, 2), 100);
Check.Equal(7, path.Count);
Check.Equal(new GridCell(0, 2), path[path.Count - 1]);
```

- [x] Run and observe failures. Implement deterministic A* with bounded work, positive costs, a Manhattan lower bound, and stable ties.
- [x] Run tests and verify no route crosses a blocked cell. Commit verified task.

## Task 3 Continuous schedules

**Files:** `src/LivingCities.Core/DailySchedule.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests/ScheduleTests.cs`.

**Interfaces:** `DailySchedule.Workday(homeKey, workKey, route, openMinute, closeMinute, commuteMinutes, wakeMinute, sleepMinute)`; `Evaluate(long absoluteMinute)` returns activity, interior building key or exterior route position, and normalized route progress. `DailySchedule.HomeDay(homeKey, wakeMinute, sleepMinute)` handles residents without a valid commute. Routes consist of immutable copied points, and reverse trips follow the same route backward.

- [x] Write boundary tests for sleep, outbound travel, arrival exactly at opening, departure exactly at closing, return, midnight, repeated days, and time jumps. Reject overlapping or impossible schedule windows and negative time.

```csharp
var plan = DailySchedule.Workday(10, 20,
    new[] { new RoutePoint(0, 0), new RoutePoint(100, 0) }, 480, 1020, 20, 360, 1320);
Check.Equal(Activity.Travel, plan.Evaluate(470).Activity);
Check.Near(50, plan.Evaluate(470).Position.X);
Check.Equal(20, plan.Evaluate(480).BuildingKey);
Check.Equal(Activity.Sleep, plan.Evaluate(1440 + 270).Activity);
```

- [x] Observe failures, implement a pure analytical schedule without frame-by-frame catch-up, then test all boundaries and route interpolation. Commit verified task.

## Task 4 Save validation and transactional restore

**Files:** `src/LivingCities.Core/WorldState.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests/SaveTests.cs`.

**Interfaces:** `WorldState` has `Version`, `WorldSeed`, and `Settlements`; `WorldStateStore.Snapshot()` and `Restore(WorldState)` deep-copy and validate the entire graph before replacement; `GetOrCreate(int settlementId, IEnumerable<int> homeKeys)` returns existing records without regenerating deaths.

- [x] Test round-trip identities/deaths, snapshot aliasing, future-version rejection, duplicate IDs, wrong household ownership, over-capacity homes, malformed null collections, and preservation of live state after rejected restore.

```csharp
var before = store.Snapshot();
var invalid = store.Snapshot();
invalid.Version = 999;
Check.Throws<ArgumentException>(() => store.Restore(invalid));
Check.Equal(Signature(before), Signature(store.Snapshot()));
```

- [x] Observe failures, implement deep copies and validation, run the suite, and commit verified task.

## Task 5 DFU host adapter and build

**Files:** `mod/Scripts/LivingCitiesMod.cs`, `mod/LivingCities.dfmod.json`, `tools/Deploy.ps1`, `tools/Editor/LivingCitiesBuild.cs`.

**Interfaces:** implement DFU's `IHasModSaveData`. Register at Start, wait for GameManager readiness, read current BuildingDirectory, use home types House1 through House6, and avoid for-sale homes. Track current settlement by MapID and report generated counts in a diagnostic panel toggled with F8. The panel is development-only and must identify the simulation as virtual until actor replacement is implemented.

- [x] Add a Unity integration verifier that constructs and serializes/restores real core data with FullSerializer, validates DFU type/method contracts, and fails when required hooks are absent.
- [x] Deploy source to `Assets/Game/Mods/LivingCities`, compile with Unity, then build the standard source bundle. The output must include all manifest assets and no editor/test code.
- [x] Start the isolated player, verify Living Cities initialization and Basic Roads coexistence, then stop only the owned test process. Record the difference between startup verification and gameplay verification.
- [x] Commit source, tests, plan status, and evidence references.

## Task 6 Review and integration risk report

**Files:** `docs/INTEGRATION.md`, `docs/MILESTONES.md`.

- [x] Record direct non-virtual guard spawn call sites and available quest, save, population, and UI hooks from the pinned source. Do not label runtime interception successful before testing it.
- [x] Use a read-only Luna/high reviewer for the completed foundation. Fix critical and important findings, rerun affected checks, and capture verified reusable results in Obsidian.
- [x] Keep the overall goal active. M2 remains incomplete until physical actors and in-world continuity pass testing; guard interception feasibility passed a separate temporary standard-mod probe; roster and quest behavior remain pending.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-14-family-households-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-family-households.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Family households implementation plan

> Use executing-plans to implement the following tasks and requesting-code-review before the checkpoint. Existing implementation authorization covers this refinement.

**Goal:** Generate persistent family households of up to five, influenced weakly by real housing size/beds, while preserving existing residents and preventing children from joining adult visits.

**Architecture:** Keep demographic allocation and relationships in the Unity-independent core. Read vanilla interior bed counts and exterior size hints in a DFU adapter. Migrate old saves without rerolling names, appearance, deaths or household membership; legacy adults remain adults and housemates unless an actual relationship already exists. Newly generated families explicitly identify parents, partners and children. Runtime visuals use existing classic child flats where available.

**Tech stack:** DFU 1.1.1, Unity 2019.4.40f1, C# 7.3/netstandard2.0 core, net8 behavior harness.

**Spec:** `docs/RELATIONSHIPS-AND-FUTURE-INVENTORIES.md`, `docs/MILESTONES.md` and latest user amendments.

## Constraints

- Maximum five: up to two adult parents and three children; most households smaller.
- House size and beds are preferences, never one-person-per-bed limits. Sharing is allowed.
- Shared household surname; names/roles persist. Existing saved appearances and identities are preserved.
- Children have no shopping, tavern, job or inter-settlement travel routines. Local play is a later extension of this demographic slice, not adult visits disguised as play.
- Existing quest/service bindings and physical test-town scope remain unchanged by demographic generation.
- Functional milestones take priority over curved paths and visual polish.
- Directional child movement and appearance-preserving civilian movement/combat are now essential functional requirements; see `docs/DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md`. Static child flats in this demographic slice are an incomplete home-only prototype, not completion of mobile children.

## Tasks

1. [x] Core allocation and tests. Add `HomeProfile`, persistent sex/age-group/parent/partner fields and household surname seed. Generate weighted deterministic families with test coverage for five-member bounds, mostly smaller homes, bed sharing, larger-house preference, order independence and valid relationships.
2. [x] Save migration and validation. Schema 3 annotates legacy adults using their existing appearance-seed gender and historical surname seed. Preserve all identities, deaths and routes. Validate parent/partner references, household membership, reciprocal partners and child schedule restrictions. Core tests and actual Unity FullSerializer passed, including raw legacy field shapes. Actual-player fixture migration remains in task 5.
3. [x] DFU housing adapter and settings. Read actual interior bed models using stable building keys and an exterior model size hint, without opening every interior. Increase the bounded household menu limit to five for new allocations only. Ownership/residence suitability remains a separate task. Native housing checks and actual-player allocation passed.
4. [x] Runtime child distinction. Use saved sex for adult graphics/names; child schedules stay home until local play is implemented. Render existing child flats with a persistent identity and brief greeting rather than adult bodies. Adult fixture behavior passes. This completes only the home-only prototype, not required directional child movement.
5. [x] Actual-player validation. New family fixture and legacy fixture passed. Child sprites were inspected; children have no adult visits and family fields survive reload. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed critical/important issues. Documentation records evidence and limits; the full goal remains active.

## Foundation checkpoint

Subsequent runtime checkpoint: 0.3.0 is built and installed, with 87/87 core tests and passing actual-player family/legacy checks. Character `LivingCitiesTest-126494eb`, SAVE11–SAVE15, has 402 residents including 118 children and a verified five-person home. The earlier source-only checkpoint below is retained as history. Sleeping sprites are the first art production task when actual home/tavern sleeping is implemented; directional movement, child play and full appearance coverage remain open.

86/86 core behavior tests pass. `LivingCitiesBuild.VerifySaveState` passed in Unity with exit 0, including FullSerializer family round trips, v1/v2 migration and adapter restore isolation. Read-only Luna/high review found no confirmed critical/important issues. The installed portable 0.2.0 bundle remains unchanged; family runtime integration has not shipped. The latest animation amendment also requires every stationary and mobile adult human appearance to have directional walking/running/weapon combat/spellcasting plus sleeping in bed; children need directional walking/running and sleeping, without battle sets.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-14-guard-enemy-defense-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-guard-enemy-defense.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Guard defense against hostile enemies

**Goal:** Finite settlement guards physically defend the settlement from hostile creatures and attackers, independently of player crime.

**Architecture:** Reuse the saved roster, actual-position dispatch, native combat and physical return. Add an optional enemy target identity to a report; absence preserves existing crime and investigation behavior. Resolve native persistent targets after loading; a missing transient target must never turn into a player accusation. Engine scripts remain unchanged.

**Approved scope:** M4 enemy defense. This plan implements the user's existing authorization to continue implementing the complete mod. M4 remains open after the exterior pilot.

## Requirements

- Use existing finite guards; never create additional responders.
- Ignore dead, inactive, pacified or friendly enemies. Respect native quest AI attackability.
- Detect visible threats locally; use actual guard position and physical navigation.
- Save the distinction between enemy defense and player criminal response.
- Preserve player crimes. Defense alone never creates Assault or Murder.
- Enemy combat must not target the innocent player or other guards, including with native enemy infighting disabled.
- Stop after death/pacification, loss of target or bounded search expiry. Return by a surveyed route from actual engagement position.
- Existing guard health/death, native enemy damage, animation and reload ownership remain authoritative.
- Expose a defense toggle; toggling off ends defense through physical return.

## Implementation sequence

1. **Saved defense reports:** Add `GuardReport.ThreatId`, validated copy/save handling and `GuardRoster.DispatchDefense`. Write failing core cases for actual-position dispatch, repeated reports, target mismatch, ordinary-report collisions, missing routes, patrol interruption, save migration and corrupt defense reports. Reuse normal guard engagement and return rather than inventing another position owner.
2. **Native target adapter:** Identify native enemies by nonzero LoadID, otherwise use a session identity which cannot accidentally bind after reload. Survey routes from available guards that see a living hostile enemy. Scope selection to supported town/exterior and a bounded responder count. Persist the report before actor combat.
3. **Native fighting and return:** Add explicit enemy-target combat to GuardActor. Temporarily use native ally combat filtering so guard defense does not depend on EnemyInfighting; restore original team and callbacks on exit. Capture enemy observations without reading player location as suspect. Resolve saved target and stand down physically when invalid. Player assault and existing bounties must still reach native criminal response.
4. **Live acceptance:** Spawn a controlled hostile enemy near a finite guard. Observe physical response, enemy target and actual native enemy damage; innocent player crime stays None. Check pacified/blocked candidates, no extra guards, wounded guard and real reload, target death, physical return and prior crime preservation. Run existing patrol, indoor surrender and housing regressions after native integration.

Core foundation is not proof of playable defense. Mark each stage only after its own evidence passes; preserve broader interior/civilian/event requirements in M4/M5.

## Verified exterior checkpoint

Stages1–3 and the controlled exterior portion of stage4 pass in0.3.25. Core222/222, native WorldState serialization and GuardDefenseProbe verify finite actual-position response, enemy damage with EnemyInfighting off, pacified/inactive/wall-obscured rejection, native enemy and wounded guard real reload, target death/physical return, prior Theft retention and player-assault takeover. SAVE46 is the living defense encounter. Native MakeEnemyHostileToAttacker can retain an adjacent visible enemy when the player attacks; the expanded red fixture reproduced ignored assault, and takeover now uses the player-hostility flag independently of retained target. Evidence: work/GuardDefense-assault-red.log and work/GuardDefense-0325-pass.log. The assault probe calls the native reaction method; it does not simulate an actual player weapon swing. Interior defense, missing-target live reload acceptance, wider layouts, events and large-town profiling remain open.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-14-lawful-defense-and-welfare-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-lawful-defense-and-welfare.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Lawful defense, corpse rights and situational guard welfare

**Goal:** Killing a genuine hostile attacker grants immediate persistent loot rights without Murder, and witnessed fights receive a short-lived guard welfare exchange followed by existing crime enforcement.

**Spec:** `docs/LEGITIMATE-DEFENSE.md`, reflecting the user's approved indoor/outdoor defense, immediate loot, bounded gifts, situational timing and outstanding-bounty requirements.

**Architecture:** A saved combat-provenance ledger records original hostility, player provocation, participation, actual fight witnesses and death. The native adapter supplies authoritative hit/death/loot observations. Physical guard approaches use the existing finite roster and surveyed routes. Courtesy never clears player crime. Native enemy corpses remain the actual inventory; no duplicate loot is generated.

**Constraints:** Standard DFU1.1.1 mod, Unity2019.4.40f1, no tracked engine changes; one existing WorldState save owner; immutable target identity, finite responders, no teleport paths, no imaginary witnesses. Existing families, quests and guard states survive migration. The five-in-game-minute courtesy window starts at death, expires sooner on leaving the local scene, and is never renewed by loading. Gift offers are decided once with stable seed and saved probability; one successful exchange can award at most one potion. Native stationary/mobile civilians and guards cannot become lawful victims merely because the player provoked them.

## Stage1: persistent provenance and courtesy contract

Files: `src/LivingCities.Core/DefenseIncidents.cs`, `WorldState.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests/DefenseIncidentTests.cs`, existing schema assertions and `tools/Editor/LivingCitiesBuild.cs`.

Types: `CombatOrigin` (HostileAttacker, Civilian, PassiveEnemy), `DefenseCombatant`, `DefenseWitness`, `DefenseDeath`, `WelfareResult`. Add `WorldState.DefenseCombatants` and migrate schema1–6 into schema7 with an empty ledger.

API: `RegisterDefenseCombatant(id, settlementId, origin, now)` preserves first observation; `RecordDefensePlayerHit(id, now, wasHostile)` permanently records provocation when appropriate. `RecordDefenseWitness(id, observerId, isGuard, now)` records actual sight supplied by the adapter and rejects dead/unknown observers. `ConfirmDefenseDeath(id, now, buildingKey, x,y,z, killedByPlayer, welfareSeconds, potionPercent)` confirms location once, grants immediate loot only to an unprovoked hostile attacker killed by the player, and fixes courtesy expiry/offer. `InformDefenseGuard(id, witnessId, guardId, now)` requires an actual recorded witness and a living known finite guard. `ObserveDefenseScene(id,onSite,now)` expires courtesy after departure/time. `CompleteDefenseWelfare(id,guardId,now,acceptPotion)` consumes at most one eligible informed-guard exchange and returns its bounded gift decision.

Test first: genuine attacker loot before guard arrival; provoked passive enemy and civilian hostility cannot qualify; duplicate death cannot move expiry/re-roll gifts; unwitnessed death has no welfare; stale witnesses do not qualify; exact expiry/departure survives reload; prior participation can qualify for witnessed welfare even when a guard finishes the enemy; death of witnesses never revokes earned rights; gift decline/accept cannot be farmed; unknown identity/time/coordinates and mixed state reject restore transactionally; old saves gain no invented rights. Then native WorldState serialization verifies target provenance, immutable deadlines and consumed gifts. This stage alone is not playable welfare.

## Stage2: native combat and corpse binding

Progress,15 October: native weapon/sourced-health and Disintegrate hooks, actual melee/sourced aggression evidence, immutable passive/ally provocation, invisible-witness exclusion, real corpse binding and immediate native inventory pickup pass. A real dedicated game-slot roundtrip retains corpse, remaining inventory, loot entitlement and prior crime. Scope and evidence are in `docs/LEGITIMATE-DEFENSE.md`. Remaining before Stage2 completion: target-owned scene context and transitions, broader outdoor/mod paths and indirect group provocation. Stage3 remains pending.

Scene follow-up: target-owned interior/exterior coordinates and same-context interior corpse parenting now pass controlled relocation tests, including restored rooms without DoorOwner and inactive exterior parents. Cross-world-context corpse tracking, cross-settlement death, unparented mod actors, observer scene ownership and indirect group provocation remain. Extended testing also fixed resident escape planning to use saved alarm position rather than the rendered position.

Files: native combat hooks under `src/LivingCities.QuestHooks`, `StreetPopulation.BeginWeaponHit`, a focused native defense adapter and live probe under `mod/Scripts`.

Register original native enemy hostility before a player hit can change it. Preserve provocation on later hits and reload. Track player weapon and spell sources separately from guard/environment damage. Observe fight witnesses while the hit occurs; body discovery cannot fabricate a hero. Bind native EnemyDeath's actual corpse marker/LoadID and inventory to the saved resolution. Existing native enemy corpse transfer is already free; confirm actual item transfer and no Murder/Theft both before guard arrival and after reload. Never apply this exemption to managed civilian crime corpses or City Watch deaths.

## Stage3: physical scene security and guard courtesy

Partial runtime checkpoint: native controller now approaches, opens the welfare message, awards a bounded native potion and preserves crime handoff. A controlled indoor fixture verifies these paths and assault cancellation; core310/310, native build and citizen-defense regression pass. Full natural encounter, outdoor/mid-approach reload acceptance, civilian witness relay and scene-security policy remain unfinished. See the current checkpoint in `docs/LEGITIMATE-DEFENSE.md` for exact evidence and limits.

User correction, 15 October: gate the entire welfare exchange on current player health strictly below 66% (not 75%) and above zero. Recheck health and the responding guard's specific threat-report membership before dialogue and completion. Loot entitlement and existing bounty are independent of this health gate.

Extend outdoor/indoor guard reports with lawful-scene policy rather than using guilty-suspect investigation behavior. Welfare uses only guards who responded to fight this particular threat, as clarified by the user on 15 October. Do not dispatch separate courtesy responders or choose unrelated nearby guards. Retain the threat-response identity after the enemy dies. Approach the player only within the live local courtesy window, then show the brief welfare/loot dialogue and optionally add one native healing potion. Consume the saved exchange once; restore normal native bounty surrender afterward. Departure or expiry prevents late greetings/gifts while preserving loot rights and continuing legitimate scene security.

## Stage4: live acceptance and release

Integrated shop acceptance now passes through keeper flight/reporting, finite guard travel and combat, native player kill and witness capture, automatic welfare and the actual native surrender popup for prior Theft (`work/WelfareChain-pass.log`). The dedicated `-WelfareProbe` fixture controls setup and travel time but uses the running world store/controller. Remaining acceptance includes outdoor and multiple-responder encounters, unavailable/dynamically blocked routes, mid-approach reload and broader quest/mod attackers.

Test unprovoked hostile and player-provoked civilian/enemy, indoor/outdoor scenes, witnessed/unwitnessed kills, real weapon/spell provenance, immediate native loot transfer, real guard travel, prior bounty after welfare, one gift across reload, expiry/departure and unavailable routes. Re-run civilian murder/theft, native surrender, keeper and patrol regressions. Update milestones, README and Obsidian from verified scope. The overall goal and remaining broader event/quest/settlement requirements stay active.

# Paired guard night patrols

Current checkpoint 0.3.24 implements steps 3/4 and the first live acceptance portion of step 5. Two native surveyed circuits pass in Gothway Garden, both guard sprites animate, reload preserves phase, and disabling patrols followed by a native crime dispatch starts at the actual returning position. Core216/216 pass. Evidence: `work/GuardPatrol-0324-pass.log`; SAVE45 is the living pair fixture. Native timestamp reload uses a 0.001 game-second tolerance. Runtime packaging uses the official DFU compiled mod assembly after source-text compilation produced native navigation failures; the exact compiler cause is unproven. Terrain/crowd coverage beyond these circuits, wall patrols, other settlements and profiling remain open. Earlier source-only checkpoints below are historical.

The approved end state is two finite roster guards physically joining a surveyed circuit at night, carrying torches, answering crimes from their actual positions, and returning without teleportation. Existing posts, guard IDs, deaths, health, native surrender and saved crimes remain authoritative.

1. Add a serializable core patrol plan containing two identities, individually surveyed join routes, a closed circuit, walking speed/time scale, spacing and exact start/end times. Both members wait for a shared circuit start; the follower uses a shifted circuit entry. Derive positions from game time and support physical return to individual posts using only traversed circuit/join segments. Test joins, waiting, multiple laps, dawn return, explicit interruption, defensive copies and save reconstruction.
2. Persist plans in GuardRoster with transactional validation and migration defaults. Define patrol participation and interruption explicitly. Keep responses eligible from actual patrol positions; cached building approaches must join those positions through validated travel, and return must ultimately reach a real post. Test death, one unavailable partner, crime interruption and resumes without position jumps.
3. Survey valid circuits and both joins incrementally in the live exterior. Reject disconnected routes and formations that collide at sharp reversals; never invent straight paths. Keep guards posted when no usable circuit exists. Prefer a few short routes over expensive all-city routing.
4. Integrate night start/stop, paired walking animation and existing torch controls. Add a patrol toggle and bounded pair allocation while retaining stationary posts. Direct indoor loads reconstruct patrol progress from saved time; offscreen travel does not depend on actors.
5. Verify actual-player paired spacing, both moving sprites, night/day transition, crime travel from the current position, native surrender, pursuit/return, death/reload and no extra roster identities. Profile route work and visible actors. Wall patrols require separate wall navigation acceptance and remain part of M4.

Step 1 is a source foundation, not proof of a playable patrol. The installed0.3.23 preview remains authoritative until runtime steps and live verification pass. M4 and the overall goal remain open.

Step 1 implemented: `src/LivingCities.Core/PatrolPairPlan.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests/PatrolPairTests.cs` and native validation in `tools/Editor/LivingCitiesBuild.cs`. Core208/208 pass. DFU's serializer preserves the standalone plan, exact phase at fractional absolute game timestamps and physical return. The circuit validator computes minimum separation over each linear interval between corner events and rejects circuits where separation falls below one metre; circuit count is bounded at512 points. This does not validate terrain clearance, simultaneous joining collisions or wall navigation, which require step3. Source-only validation uses `tools/VerifyGuards.ps1`; it stages source but does not replace the installed player bundle. Next is step2 roster persistence and interruption/response ownership.

Step 2 implemented in core: `GuardPatrols.cs` adds saved pair assignments and optional interruption time. Available guards retain `GuardDuty.Posted` while their separately saved patrol plan determines actual position; no new identity or enum renumbering is introduced. Start validates living members at their posts and exclusive participation. Stop, crime dispatch and death interrupt the pair; a surviving unassigned member physically returns along traversed paths. Repeated stop requests do not renew the interruption time. Completed assignments are removed. A later patrol must start from a real post again, rather than snapping back to its former circuit phase.

Dispatch validates the actual source position and saves `ResponseOrigin`, `ReturnPath` and `ReturnRate`. Building dispatch prepends physical patrol-to-post travel to the surveyed post-to-door route. Return stops at the original post when the reversed response already passes through it, or appends the traversed patrol return otherwise. The same return helper supports search expiry and interior exits; live engagement returns continue requiring a surveyed route from actual engagement position to the post. Legacy saves default new fields to null/zero and missing patrol lists to empty. Invalid partners, origins, endpoints and overflowing return rates reject restore.

Core215/215 and native WorldState serialization/response-return checks pass; `work/GuardPatrolRoster-native-pass.log` includes `LIVING_CITIES_GUARD_PATROL_ROSTER_PASS`. Installed0.3.23 remains unchanged. Next is step3/4: survey real circuits and joins, then adapt exterior reports, witnesses and self-defense to actual patrol positions, animate posted-but-moving guards, start/stop night plans through the menu, and verify playable pairs. Current runtime callers that assume posted guards stand at `Post` must be updated before enabling patrols.

# Quest-to-household binding implementation plan

Use executing-plans and test-driven-development within the existing isolated mod repository. The user has authorized implementation and testing of this compatibility requirement.

**Goal:** Keep native and mod-added destination quests consistent with persistent households before player-visible names are published, while preserving fixed identities and existing quest contracts.

**Architecture:** A standard-mod adapter at native quest parsing supplies stable person/site bindings before macro expansion. A persistent binding policy must distinguish procedural residents, fixed names, guests, captives and changed quest destinations. Native resource IDs and tasks remain authoritative. No engine edits or replacement game files.

**Current evidence:** QuestListsManager.LoadQuest calls QuestMachine.ParseQuest for both disk quests and mod TextAssets. Parser.Parse returns fully constructed resources before guild offer text is expanded. Partial parses create only header metadata. Person serializes displayName separately from nameSeed; Place serializes site details. This is source evidence, not an implemented binding system.

## Sequence

Native `QuestHouseholds` settings callbacks block new and staged bindings while preserving accepted contracts. The combined runtime suite and standalone startup pass; the bounded pilot is complete. Earlier checkpoint wording is historical, not a claim that the pilot is still disabled.

Scope the probe to its synthetic quest, use an existing household resident name at the quest-selected home, preserve UID/symbol/nameSeed/site coordinates, skip partial previews and fixed individuals, and verify native message macros plus quest resource serialization. Remove all patches and the temporary package after verification. Files: probes/quest-binding, editor build helper, opt-in town harness, build/test scripts.

- [ ] Implement persisted binding policy with provenance, pre-publication reservation versus accepted commitment, concurrent quest contracts, explicit role/adaptor support, and old-save reconciliation. Write behavior tests before implementation. Never infer permanent residency solely from a generated home field or rename fixed/already-published contracts.
  - Verified partial implementation: schema-4 committed ledger, immutable side-effect-free offer plans, transactional/idempotent commit, published-name conflicts, death races, restore/store isolation, provenance and v1–3 migration. Core 118/118 and native serializer/build pass; actual synthetic quest startup plus sidecar/native quest reload passes in SAVE19. Core 119/119. Editor manifest library discovery also passes. General role classification and item hand-in remain open; these bounded results do not complete this parent item.
  - Native active quest ownership suppresses generated indoor/outdoor duplicates; actual nighttime/reload checks and return to the normal household actor after native quest end pass. Appearance continuity, mobile/sleeping quest actors and representative quest hand-in remain open.

# Superseded sleeping plan

This plan is historical. Latest rule: one NPC per bed, household capacity equals actual beds, no independent five-person cap. Shared sleepers and modular heads/blankets are rejected. Further art is deferred; continue citizen and guard behavior per docs/CIVILIAN-RESPONSES.md.

# Sleeping implementation plan

Use executing-plans and test-driven-development for this approved milestone. Use imagegen for generated sprite art and requesting-code-review before the implementation checkpoint.

**Goal:** Make residents visibly sleep at real beds, retain identity when waking, and extend this to original NPCs and paid tavern occupants.

**Architecture:** Read stable bed instances and their mattress planes from original interior records and model geometry. Keep bed assignment separate from household size: parents and children may share, with deterministic distinct logical slots. Directional blanket/face art is anchored to the bed and selected for the camera view; a pose renderer changes visuals without replacing resident, service or quest identity. Logical sharing slots do not prove that individual full-body sprites physically fit. Tavern reservation rights remain a separate persisted booking policy and must govern traveler/player occupancy.

**Tech stack:** DFU 1.1.1, Unity 2019.4.40f1, C# 7.3/netstandard2.0 core, net8 behavior tests, standard mod assets.

**Spec:** `docs/MILESTONES.md`, `DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md`, `TAVERN-LODGING.md`, `QUEST-IDENTITY.md` and the approved living-cities design.

## Constraints

- Up to five per generated family; beds are soft demographic preferences, never a hard one-person-per-bed cap.
- Sleeping sprites for all NPC flats come before directional movement/combat production. Begin with one fully tested identity-matched pose, then expand the inventory; a pilot does not complete all-appearance coverage.
- No generic appearance substitution, rotated standing sprites or cloned quest identities as the finished behavior.
- Preserve original quest components, hidden state and activation identity. Cross-building service moves need separate binding tests.
- A tavern may be fully booked; no guaranteed player bed. Do not assign residents to guest rooms without booking authority.
- Unknown/modded geometry must be reported as unsupported until validated; never put a sleeper in guessed furniture or through a wall.

## Tasks

1. [ ] Export source references and inventory. `tools/Editor/SleepAssetSurvey.cs` reads FLATS.CFG plus mobile appearance archives; writes metadata and original PNG references under ignored `work/sleep-source`. Inspect source sprites and mattress geometry for bed models 41000–41002. Track source IDs, dimensions, child/adult status, generated status and validation separately.
2. [x] Bed geometry and assignment. `mod/Scripts/BedGeometry.cs` produces stable record-index keys, mattress positions, long/short axes and extents using the same interior transforms as DFU, including prop offsets and scaling. Test asymmetric rotations, upstairs heights and real model surfaces in Unity. Core assignment tests cover parents sharing, children sharing, enumeration order, no beds, dead residents, and distinct logical slots. Native runtime tests compare placements with actual loaded geometry. Multiple logical slots do not mean separate physical blankets fit one mattress.
3. [ ] Sleeping art pilot and catalog. Latest user refinement supersedes the overhead/full-body draft approach: use a thick green blanket covering neck down, leaving only the face/head visible, with white pillows. Generate side/angled views suitable for a standing observer, including head/foot viewpoints, using shared blanket poses and identity-specific faces. Preserve classic pixel scale and adult/child proportions. Define shared-bed compositions instead of overlapping individual blankets. Persist provenance and prompt, inspect alpha/style/dimensions, and review in-engine before accepting. Catalog lookup must return no pose for unmatched identities. Expand all source appearances after the pilot.
4. [ ] Runtime pose transitions. Add an appearance-specific sleeping renderer and connect schedule sleep/wake to assigned beds. Preserve the same actor ID and restore original renderer/collider on waking. Expose an implemented sleep toggle through native settings. Test night entry, midnight/rest jumps, morning waking, death, disabling, interior exit/re-entry and save/load. Keep unsupported appearances explicitly incomplete.
5. [ ] Original residents and taverns. Bind original home/shop identities without duplicates, respect quest obligations and closed-business schedules, and test sleeping questors without losing native activation. Add persisted paid room reservations before traveler sleep or player occupied-bed enforcement. Test full tavern/earliest checkout and traveler departure.
6. [ ] Review and capture. Run core/native/player checks, inspect bed and waking screenshots, record exact coverage and unresolved limitations, update milestone and Obsidian evidence, and commit. The full goal remains active until all requirements pass their own acceptance checks.

## Current checkpoint, 14 October

Version 0.3.2 adds female-453 alongside male-385, green blankets with white pillows, and actual male/female lifecycle checks. The old white-blanket requirement below is superseded. The next shared-bed implementation uses one common green blanket mesh per occupied bed, with separate appearance-specific head/pillow layers and preserved resident actors/colliders. Normalized deterministic slots arrange up to five residents in one or two rows, with smaller child heads; dead residents retain reserved slots. Original child flats 182/43 and 182/38 plus adult archives 385, 391 and 453 provide the first modular head references. A shared composition must clear on waking/toggle/interior unload, preserve identities through save/load and death, and pass actual five-person family rendering/interaction checks. Unsupported appearances remain explicitly incomplete rather than receiving substitute faces.

- Version 0.3.1 implements the first archive-385 adult sleeping pose on unshared home beds and an immediate settings toggle. Five atlas directions are selected by the camera; waking restores the same actor's original visual/collider. Core tests pass 97/97 and native build checks validate the atlas and eight settings. Tasks 3/4 remain partial: shared beds, other appearances, native quest/service actors, sleep-specific exit/re-entry and bed approach are still open. See the runtime evidence in `docs/SLEEPING-CHECKPOINT.md`.

- Source export ran: 226 FLATS.CFG entries and 25 mobile archives, plus geometry for three classic bed models. The CSV is a candidate inventory and still includes nonhuman entries; it is not a completed human-appearance catalog.
- Core bed assignments pass seven new behavior cases (94 total tests). Partners share; unused beds serve other members; a one-bed family may contain five; ordering/reload is deterministic; deaths do not shift survivors; visitors and duplicate identities/keys are rejected. Slots reserve deceased membership until the household is replaced. This is not tavern booking or completed multi-person rendering.
- Unity checks pass real model extraction, canopy exclusion, rotations and prop offsets for 41000–41002. The expanded actual-player bed probe verifies all three mattress models against loaded colliders in real interiors and preserves five assignments to one bed across a real save/load. Physical shared-bed pose composition and sleep/wake rendering remain open.
- Two overhead drafts are superseded. A side/angled blanket draft for flat 182/1 exists, but is 2138×736 rather than native sprite resolution and is not yet an accepted runtime asset. See `art/sleep/README.md` and `docs/SLEEPING-CHECKPOINT.md`.

# Indoor hearing and sleeper waking

Runtime checkpoint 0.3.27: generated and native observers now use saved same-building hearing checks, including native Stealth and actual distance. Native sleepers wake and block services. Heard recipients can own physical escape/report routes and dispatch neutral finite investigations. The controlled live probe passes blocked sound, visible native awakening, no false Murder, actual SAVE47 reload, awake-origin escape/report and actual guard arrival (`work/IndoorNoise-0327-pass.log`). Core244/244 and native build pass. Step1 and neutral report foundation are verified; step2/4 remain partial because bed-to-floor transitions, overlapping-floor/stair routes, broader actor coverage and natural multi-room acceptance are still open. Nonlethal/step/theft stimuli remain later work. Historical source-only progress below is superseded.

Sight-promotion regression now passes: a capable fighter initially reacting to unidentified noise regains its stable fight-or-flight capability when it actually witnesses the attack, provided a physical escape has not already started. Hearing itself never authorizes attacking an unidentified player. Core244/244 pass after this additional red/green case; native report serialization evidence below remains applicable to the unchanged report schema.

Verified source progress: step1 passes237 core tests and native serialization. Neutral reporting foundation now adds saved hearing-reporter/guard contact, permits hearing escape plans without visual identity, requires actual living posted guard contact, and allows scene investigation before player departure. Actual guard arrival still starts one-hour occupation and corpse cleanup. Core243/243 pass, including ordinary scenes, hearing-only reports, visited-building victims, missing/dead guards, duplicate reports and corrupt saved contact. Native report serialization passes with fractional flight timestamps and a scene still occupied by the player (`work/HearingReport-native-pass.log`). Actor noise/waking and the GuardPopulation adapter are not connected yet; installed0.3.26 remains unchanged.

The player authorizes awake indoor occupants to notice noisy violence through walls and sleeping occupants to wake probabilistically based on player skill and disturbance. Preserve quests/services, ordinary identities, physical reporting, finite guard arrival, crime-scene fairness, saved reactions and bounded outdoor relay. This extends the current behavior; it does not replace sight with omniscient attacker identification.

1. Add a pure Stealth/distance/noise wake policy and saved per-incident/per-observer checks in `LivingCities.Core/IndoorNoise.cs`. Save positive and negative decisions. Awake hearers and awakened sleepers join the incident with an explicit hearing flag, not `DirectWitness`. Hearing-only reaction is flight until visual identification or investigation supplies stronger evidence. Validation must reject invented recipients, duplicate checks, invalid probability inputs and impossible hearing records; legacy schema6 saves lacking the optional checks must load without invented responses. Add focused red/green tests to `NoiseTests.cs` and Program registration.
2. Integrate generated residents and native keepers in `CivilianResponses`, `StreetPopulation` and `KeeperIdentities`. Use actual building membership and physical distance, native live Stealth skill, one attack incident roll, and saved state. Wake reactive sleepers, preserve identity/service blocking and obtain exit routes from actual positions. Test sleeping/awake occupants behind walls, visitors, dead occupants, distinct floors and toggle fallback. No empty-building auto-witnesses.
3. Extend witness/report ownership carefully. A hearer may physically report a disturbance and discovered corpse but must not identify an unseen player as murderer. Direct witnesses still report known attacks. Scene-only reports use the existing unidentified investigation/ten-second leave policy. Hearing does not permit an unlimited civilian relay or extra guard spawning. Preserve lawful-defense distinction and previous bounties.
4. Verify native FullSerializer round-trip for failed/successful wake decisions and legacy saves. Live multi-room tests must show visible awakening and flight, eventual guard reporting/arrival, no instant guards downstairs, no replayed rolls after reload, no automatic murderer identification from hearing alone, and correct visual witness promotion. Then rebuild/install and document actual verified scope. Keep goal active.

Current attack interception is lethal for managed civilians; one check per victim's incident matches that boundary. When nonlethal civilian health is introduced, use separate saved noise-event IDs for separate swings rather than rerolling this incident on each frame. Quiet movement/theft wake stimuli are a later integration step using the same policy, not claimed delivered by a violence-only adapter.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-15-travelers-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-travelers.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Nonlocal travelers implementation plan

16 October final incoming-discovery build passes the complete ResidentTripProbe/TownTest regression (38 runtime pass markers, exit 0; work/IncomingDiscovery-full-pass.log). Coverage includes original hometown reservation, incoming production planning, three-day keeper-bound lodging without prepayment, cancellation, native serialization, same-body road/street transfers, destination actors, crime identity, and scheduled interior entry/exit. This verifies the broad traveler suite after shared inn NonRentable normalization; it does not prove every settlement layout, historical destination occupants, or overnight stops along long roads. Existing unmatched inn occupant and missing sprite warnings remain separate limitations.

Final incoming-discovery build regression also passes after the inn classification fix: work/IncomingDiscovery-final-native-pass.log verifies production historical discovery, no past inn payment, native restore, and the 78-point cross-region trip with five visits and original home absence/return.

16 October production incoming discovery now checks at most two historical departure dates derived from actual road duration, only for unobserved/partial hometowns. It commits only travelers currently outbound, retaining original reserved household identity and refusing past inn admissions or observed history. Core 464/464 and Unity build pass. Extended normal-production timing discovers and commits a traveler (work/IncomingDiscovery-performance-pass.log); the earlier dedicated native runner/serialization check passes in work/IncomingDiscovery-native-pass.log. Roadside overnight rests and discovery of visitors already inside destination buildings remain unfinished.

16 October outbound discovery boundary implemented: WorldStateStore.DiscoverOutboundResidentTrip accepts a validated original adult currently in the Outbound road phase only when the source town remains partial and that resident has no recorded trip. It requires home/destination connectors and rejects completed hometowns, deaths, children, protected/native identities, local/future/visiting/returning/finished phases, prior trip history, service exits and inn admissions. Normal ResidentTripPlanner.Commit still rejects late/stale work. Four red missing-API cases preceded implementation; core 463/463, Unity build and native cross-region discovery/serializer checks pass (work/ResidentDiscovery-red.log, work/ResidentDiscovery-core.log, work/ResidentDiscovery-build.log, work/ResidentDiscovery-native-pass.log). Native fixture takes the real region-18-to-Daggerfall plan, discovers its original reserved resident mid-road in a fresh fixture ledger, rejects duplication and preserves absence/return after native serialization. Production incoming selection does not call this method yet. Next: derive bounded historical candidate departure days from actual road duration and wire discovery without backdating observed residents or inn transactions.

## Next: discover original residents already on the road

Current incoming discovery creates future departures, which delays distant arrivals after the first visit to a settlement. Add a separate, explicitly constrained discovery path instead of relaxing normal commit-time rejection. Initially accept only never-observed generated adults from still-partial hometowns, without any existing trip history, whose validated journey is currently outbound and reaches the destination in the future. Retain the same household reservation and original identity. Do not backdate an already observed resident, resurrect anyone, overlap another trip, invent a prior inn payment, or create a second body. Derive candidate departure days from the real route duration; keep daily demand and origin weighting deterministic. Normal late/stale work must remain rejected. Test rejection boundaries and native serialization before wiring incoming discovery. Travelers already inside destination services and roadside overnight rests remain separate follow-up work, not implicit consequences of this first discovery path.

16 October broad traveler verification after the soft route CPU budget: the complete ResidentTripProbe and TownTest marker checks pass (work/TravelBudget-full-pass.log). Coverage includes catalog/origin reservation, automatic overnight planning, keeper admission and capacity, road joins/actor ownership transfer, home absence, native serialization, destination interiors and service exits. This supplements the dedicated cross-region/performance checks; it does not prove historical discovery, road overnight rests or full-world worst-case load.

16 October cross-region journey verification: the dedicated CrossRegionTripProbe uses the production frame runner and real source/destination surveys with guaranteed fixture demand (1000/1000, maximum one). After one skipped origin, resident Vannyn Kingfield from settlement 430060351 in region 18 reaches Daggerfall 1291010263 in region 17 through a 78-point regional road route and five scheduled service visits. Native serialization preserves the original named resident, one partial reserved source household, outbound/returning phases, destination building presence, and home absence until return. Unity build and TownTest -CrossRegionTripProbe pass (work/CrossRegion-build.log and work/CrossRegion-native-pass.log). This verifies planning and evaluated lifecycle, not a rendered end-to-end journey or guaranteed feasibility for every foreign origin. The run also reports the existing bounded interior survey limit at building 132622; expanded-world performance and road rests remain unverified/unfinished.

16 October weighted origin selection: incoming discovery now uses deterministic 60/35/5 default weights for nearby (nearest sixteen supported settlements in the destination region), distant (other settlements in that region), and foreign (other regions). Travel menu sliders accept relative weights 0–100; all zero disables new incoming-origin discovery, while existing trips/local outbound planning remain. Settings changes cancel pending preparation. A sliced cached map-only catalog covers 3,444 supported settlements without creating their households. Each selected origin must have a regional road route before any source household is reserved; that route is reused by the traveler batch. Missing/excluded/unreachable origins are skipped without replacing the category with nearby traffic. At most sixteen origin attempts occur per destination/day; normal sparse source demand and trip ownership still apply. These weights govern origin attempts, not guaranteed arrival headcounts: population, demand, prior planning, road-search limits and journey duration affect observed arrivals. Two red selector cases preceded implementation; 459/459 core tests, Unity build and full native traveler suite pass (`work/VisitorOrigins-red.log`, `work/VisitorOrigins-core.log`, `work/VisitorOrigins-build.log`, `work/VisitorOrigins-native-pass.log`). Native coverage verifies the complete catalog, frame slicing, all three region/index references and hometown identities, plus existing near-origin travel lifecycle. A full distant/cross-region physical journey has not yet been independently exercised; global arrival history, long-road rests and expanded performance profiling remain open.

16 October sparse unvisited-origin integration: ReserveResidentTravelProposals previews deterministic household identities against saved partial households, honors deaths and overlapping trips, and persists only households of selected travelers. Selection matches a fully generated town for the same seed/day; zero demand creates no settlement. Housing metadata is read in batches of eight, and source navigation is reused by the sliced route planner. Normal planning now considers the loaded origin plus its nearest sixteen same-region settlement origins, each retaining its own nearest-sixteen destination pool and default 10/1000 adult demand capped at eight proposals. Planning remains sequential/frame-sliced and context-cancellable; this is not a full-world scan or the requested 60/35/5 weighted selector. The native production frame runner schedules a Gothway-origin visitor to loaded Daggerfall while saving one household, without loading the origin scene. Reservation/save/full-population completion and original absence/return pass. Review found missing names/race before departure; a native red check reproduced it, and RegionalIdentityAdapter now accepts explicit hometown metadata, used when reserving households/demand while preserving saved identity and DFRandom state. Core 457/457, Unity build and full traveler suite pass (`work/SparseOriginDemand-red.log`, `work/SparseOriginDemand-core.log`, `work/SparseOriginDemand-build.log`, `work/SparseOriginDemand-native-pass.log`, `work/ReservedIdentity-native-red.log`). The native incoming-demand fixture deliberately uses 1000/1000 with cap one to guarantee an exercised trip; normal play retains 10/1000. Expanded neighborhood performance has not yet been profiled. Remaining: 60/35/5 weighted distant/cross-region origins and settings, road rests, historical arrival discovery, global indexing and performance.

Visitor origin distribution requested on 16 October: use weighted defaults of 60% nearby settlements, 35% distant settlements in the same political region, and 5% settlements in another region. These are probabilities, not exact quotas; another region does not imply another race. Expose the weights in mod settings when the selector is connected. Origins require usable routes and physical travel time; distant visits should generally support longer stays. Current nearby-only discovery does not implement these weights. Add weighted distant/cross-region selection using reserved hometown households, with explicit handling of unavailable routes rather than pretending a nearby-only pool matches the requested distribution.

16 October reserved hometown households (user-requested direction): WorldStateStore.ReserveHousehold creates only a bed-surveyed household in an explicitly partial settlement. Stable building/slot identities and the household's names, relatives and death state survive snapshot/native JSON. GetOrCreateFamilies later builds the remaining town and retains the reserved household objects; missing homes or insufficient new capacity fail before mutation. Existing completed populations remain unchanged, and reservation never revives an existing person. UnloadedPopulation.ReserveHome reads only the selected house's beds/footprint without changing scenes. Two red core cases preceded implementation; 455/455 core tests, Unity build and full native traveler suite pass (`work/ReservedHouseholds-red.log`, `work/ReservedHouseholds-core.log`, `work/ReservedHouseholds-build.log`, `work/ReservedHouseholds-native-pass.log`). Native check reserves one Gothway household while Daggerfall remains loaded, serializes, then completes Gothway while retaining identity/name/family. This supersedes the attempted full-origin initialization approach (`work/RemoteOrigins-native-red.log`). The reservation mechanism is ready; automatic demand from unvisited origins is not yet wired. Next: derive sparse candidates from deterministic home/slot metadata, reserve only selected households, and route their original residents without filling entire hometowns in advance.

16 October late day-visit promotion: automatic planning now promotes a one-day request to a two-day lodging plan when its first service requires an overnight opening wait. Short opening waits and outside-the-gate waits remain unchanged; explicitly requested two-to-four-day stays retain their duration. The promoted plan uses the same capacity, keeper, reception and no-prepayment checks as other overnight trips. Core regression failed before the policy change; 453/453 core tests, Unity build and full native traveler suite pass (`work/DayVisitLodging-red.log`, `work/DayVisitLodging-core.log`, `work/DayVisitLodging-build.log`, `work/DayVisitLodging-native-pass.log`). Native fixture verifies one keeper-bound night followed by the original service, no paid stay before arrival, and home absence/return after serialization. The existing production three-day runner check also passes. This does not yet cover overnight rests along roads or all-day services that finish too late to return home.

16 October automatic multi-day activation: the daily controller no longer drops two-to-four-day proposals. It prepares the first service route, then requires capacity-aware lodging, saved keeper receptions and a complete return itinerary before committing the original resident's trip. No feasible lodging means no trip, not a shortened day visit. Stable resident/destination/day seeds choose overnight timing; existing sparse demand, frame slicing, cancellation and future-departure checks remain. A native red run committed zero trips for a three-day request. Core 452/452, Unity build and full traveler suite now pass (`work/AutomaticOvernight-native-red.log`, `work/AutomaticOvernight-core.log`, `work/AutomaticOvernight-build.log`, `work/AutomaticOvernight-native-pass.log`). The production Update runner schedules two nights without prepayment; native serialization preserves original identity, absence and return, and cancellation commits nothing. Generation still starts from loaded settlements and nearest same-region destinations; this is not all-world demand generation. One-day requests that arrive too late for a service still need lodging promotion; global indexing, road rest stops and legacy reception migration remain open.

16 October lodging-first routing: travelers reaching a closed service with an overnight wait now route to an inn first, then return to the original service after morning checkout. Short opening waits and waits outside closed city gates retain their existing behavior. Core 452/452, Unity build and the full native traveler suite pass (`work/LodgingFirst-core.log`, `work/LodgingFirst-build.log`, `work/LodgingFirst-native-pass.log`). The native fixture initially assumed a universal 20:00 closing time; it now uses the actual service closing time plus fifteen minutes. Native save/load preserves the arrival night and original service appointment. Automatic multi-day proposal activation remains pending; this checkpoint verifies planning, not worldwide generation.

16 October same-inn social stay: a social/inn destination that is itself a tavern can now host the two-to-four-day plan directly. StayAtInn preserves the original approach/exit geometry, converts the single stop to lodging, extends the room hold through the requested nights and keeps morning checkout. ExtendOvernight prefers that inn unless capacity selection has excluded it; other-inn fallback remains available. The native fixture excludes every other inn and verifies two nights at inn 65542 with original approach and serialized home absence. Core red reproduced the missing lodging conversion; 451/451 core tests, Unity build and full traveler suite pass (`work/SameInnStay-red.log`, `work/SameInnStay-core.log`, `work/SameInnStay-build.log`, `work/SameInnStay-native-pass.log`). This adds planning support, not automatic multi-day activation or new daytime tavern animations. Late-arrival inn-first routing, legacy keeper-binding migration and automatic multi-day generation remain pending.

16 October keeper-bound admission: new loaded and remote reception plans persist KeeperId, validated against the destination inn's native identity. Remote inventory registers that existing record without reviving it or assigning a new name; dead keepers are excluded from new plans. Arrival-time payment checks keeper death and active alarm participation; refusal saves the physical return route. Loaded reconciliation processes already prepared refusals even when no available keeper can prepare new routes. Core tests cover dead/alarmed keepers, zero booking, escape, identity re-registration, invalid keeper references and exit-reference rounding; 450/450 pass. Native cross-town fixture kills the bound keeper after planning, verifies rejection/exit and persistent death through JSON. Unity build, full traveler suite and all three inn lifecycle tests pass (`work/KeeperAdmission-red.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-core.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-build.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-native-pass.log`, `work/KeeperAdmission-inn-pass.log`). Native restore exposed another exact timestamp reference in service exits; those now use the same bounded 1ms comparison, with larger mismatches rejected (`KeeperAdmission-native-red`, `-exit-red`). Older receptions with null KeeperId retain legacy behavior and still need migration; this does not claim keeper checks for every legacy generated-traveler path. Automatic multi-day activation, late-arrival inn-first routing and same-inn social plans remain open.

16 October interrupted-visit retiming: saving a non-refusal service exit now recalculates future planned reception times and itinerary room-hold bounds from the revised journey. Existing paid bookings retain their original expiry; already processed future admissions/exits cannot be rewritten. Restore first validates exit timing against a provisional schedule, then validates reservation bounds against the final interrupted schedule. The red regression reproduced invalid room-hold bounds before reception matching; cases with an unpaid upcoming inn and an already paid multi-night room now pass restore, revised admission timing and unchanged booking expiry. Core 449/449, Unity build and full three-inn suite pass (`work/ReceptionRetiming-red.log`, `work/ReceptionRetiming-core.log`, `work/ReceptionRetiming-build.log`, `work/ReceptionRetiming-native-pass.log`). Native suite covers existing paid-room excursions, serialization and refusal lifecycle; the new retiming scenarios are core-level tests. Loaded reception caching now uses the same 1ms reference tolerance, avoiding exact-comparison cache misses after native timestamp rounding. Keeper availability/death, late-arrival inn-first routing, same-inn social plans and automatic multi-day activation remain pending.

16 October planned remote reception: capacity-prepared overnight trips now carry copied keeper approach routes for every inn stop, beginning at each authoritative arrival. Forecast capacity starts after counter service; no payment occurs during planning. Refusal discards future reception plans while preserving completed admission/exit records. Native Daggerfall-to-Gothway capacity fallback verifies the planned route survives JSON, payment is rejected before arrival at the counter, payment succeeds after service, and the paid bed survives another native restore. Core 448/448, Unity build and full resident-trip suite pass (`work/PlannedReception-red.log`, `work/PlannedReception-core.log`, `work/PlannedReception-build.log`, `work/PlannedReception-native-pass.log`). Native testing exposed timestamp rounding: reception references now use a bounded 1ms comparison, and persisted booking IDs retain their original timestamp text while validating against the rounded visit reference and matching ledger. Regressions cover both rounding directions, refusal, retained current reception, no duplicate payment and rejection of a one-second mismatch (`PlannedReception-native-red`, `-precision-red`, `-rounding-red`, `-booking-red`). Still required before automatic multi-day activation: keeper availability/death, reception retiming after non-refusal early service exits, late-arrival inn-first routing and same-inn social visits. Review the runtime reception cache's exact timestamp comparison when connecting these plans to loaded actors.

16 October unloaded reception geometry: remote inn surveys now retain the native keeper hash and doorway-to-counter path from passive person records and a sliced InteriorGeometry flood. No NPC is spawned; results publish only after the complete remote operation. Regular inventory-only surveys do not add this flood. A native red check reproduced missing routes. Core 447/447, Unity build and full three-inn suite pass: routes of 65, 40 and 58 points match actual loaded entrance/keeper positions and clear loaded geometry; existing scene-preservation, cancellation, room ownership and refusal checks still pass (`work/RemoteReception-native-red.log`, `work/RemoteReception-core.log`, `work/RemoteReception-build.log`, `work/RemoteReception-native-pass.log`). TownTest requires all three reception markers. Routes are not yet attached to planned trips; keeper availability/death must be checked before payment, and the added survey cost has not been profiled. Automatic multi-day activation remains pending.

16 October saved-reception offscreen refusal: room refusal now reverses the persisted doorway-to-counter route at the authoritative admission time, saves the physical exit and cancels future appointments immediately, including during late admission catch-up. Queued admission candidates recheck the current itinerary, preventing a later night from booking after the first refusal replaced the trip. Red regression reproduced three retained stops; 447/447 core tests, Unity build and full three-inn native suite pass (`work/OffscreenRefusal-red.log`, `work/OffscreenRefusal-core.log`, `work/OffscreenRefusal-build.log`, `work/OffscreenRefusal-native-pass.log`). The core test covers late catch-up and restore without actors; native checks cover loaded physical exits and serialization. This requires a saved reception route. Route-less offscreen inns, existing refusal migration and automatic multi-day activation remain open.

16 October refused-inn return: saving a physical exit after room refusal now cancels remaining appointments and reverses the already surveyed street legs to the regional road, preserving gate timing and interior exit continuity. Core regression reproduced the retained-appointments bug (445/446), then passes 446/446. Unity build and all three native inns pass physical exit, cancelled appointments and native serialization/home-return checks (`work/InnRefusalReturn-red.log`, `work/InnRefusalReturn-core.log`, `work/InnRefusalReturn-build.log`, `work/InnRefusalReturn-native-pass.log`). This covers newly saved loaded-interior exits; offscreen refusal without an exit plan, migration of already saved exits, late-arrival inn-first routing and automatic multi-day activation remain pending.

## 16 October priority and sparse travel requirements

`PrepareLodgedTrip` joins route selection to actual destination capacity. It initializes missing households from HousingSurvey metadata in batches of eight (never empty placeholders), registers remote inventory/paid receipts/permanent claims, reconciles due admissions and forecasts the entire inn hold. Full candidates are excluded and another reachable inn is tried, bounded at eight attempts. It does not reserve or pay for future rooms. Native fixture creates 162 Gothway households, fills inn 65542 and selects 131590; scheduled original identity survives serialization. Core 445/445, build/full traveler suite pass (`work/OvernightCapacity-core.log`, `work/OvernightCapacity-build.log`, `work/OvernightCapacity-native-pass.log`).

Important correction to earlier RemoteInn claims: same-town tests did not prove cross-town independence. DFU DoLayoutAutomap privately calls AssignBlockData using PlayerGPS.CurrentLocation; the cross-town fallback failed on RMB index 561 (`work/OvernightCapacity-native-red.log`). Unloaded entrances now retain their actual source DFBlock, and the mod initializes the private fields of its isolated DaggerfallInterior then calls the existing model-only AddModels method via reflection. No player-location mutation or engine source change. The full three-inn parity/receipt/room-hold suite passes again (`work/RemoteBlock-inn-pass.log`). This is tied to current DFU private field/method names and fails explicitly if they change. Layout creation remains synchronous. Automatic multi-day proposals are still filtered until arrival refusal can replan or return home without corrupting later nights; late-arrival inn-first routing remains pending.

`ResidentTripPlanner.ExtendOvernight` now creates continuous two-to-four-day geometry from a prepared single-service trip. It identifies the exact first doorway, tries up to eight connected inn entrances, adds service/inn legs with sliced model-cleared routing, prefers unvisited buildings matching the original purpose, and joins the final service to the original regional exit with adjusted gate timing. Excluded inn keys allow a future capacity caller to try another candidate. Native fixture: original `s:1291010263:h:67073:r:0` travels Daggerfall -> Gothway, shops 196624 and 196622, inn 65542. Temporary-store schedule/native serialization retain absence/return; cancelled extension publishes nothing. Build/full resident-trip suite pass (`work/OvernightRoutes-build.log`, `work/OvernightRoutes-native-pass.log`). No room is booked and the automatic controller still filters multi-day proposals. Remaining integration: destination family/inventory setup, capacity selection and receipt/claim reconciliation, runtime inn refusal/replanning, late-arrival inn-first routing, remote terrain/non-model clearance. Single-inn social trips whose first service is that same inn currently have no distinct inn leg; handle without fabricating a zero-length walk.

`ResidentOvernightPlan.Schedule` now converts a complete routed skeleton into morning-checkout inn stops without mutating its input. It honors preceding service opening windows, requires one inn across one-to-seven nights, derives deterministic checkout between 09:00 and 11:00 with at least two hours' rest, and holds the first room through the final night. Core fixture exercises distinct shop/guild visits across two nights; native fixture uses actual Daggerfall service/inn geometry and verifies night presence, morning checkout and return through JSON serialization. Core 445/445, build and full resident-trip suite pass (`work/OvernightTiming-core.log`, `work/OvernightTiming-build.log`, `work/OvernightTiming-native-pass.log`). No automatic multi-day enablement yet. Next: prepare actual service/inn legs, initialize unseen destination households from HousingSurvey metadata (never an empty permanent population), register inventory/claims/receipts, forecast capacity, and handle refusal by choosing another inn or returning home without shifting later nights into wrong hours. Current timing stage also does not reroute a late arrival to an inn before a closed first service.

Multi-night reservation continuity is implemented through optional `ResidentVisitStop.KeepRoomUntil` (zero preserves prior single-visit behavior). Scheduling rejects holds ending before the inn visit, beyond the itinerary or on non-inn stops. Admission books through checkout, later covered visits reuse the same stay, and daytime outings retain the bed. Interior room plans use current visit entry/leave, not the reservation's entire duration, so guests actually exit and return. Core 444/444; native three-inn fixtures exercise a synthetic guild excursion, real JSON serialization, same-bed return without duplicate booking, second-night sleep and final checkout. Build/full inn suite pass (`work/ResidentRoomHold-core.log`, `work/ResidentRoomHold-build.log`, `work/ResidentRoomHold-native-pass.log`). Red evidence: room was formerly released during excursion (`work/ResidentRoomHold-red.log`); native JSON rounded admission 12597552218.162523 to 12597552218.1625 (`work/ResidentRoomHold-precision-red.log`). Restore counter comparison permits 1ms only; `work/InnPrecision-red.log` and core regression verify the issue and retain rejection one second early. Automatic overnight selection, service/inn routing and full-inn fallback remain pending; this is not a real intersettlement multi-day trip demonstration.

`NativeTavernRentals.RegisterInventory` now provides the remote/exterior registration boundary: require complete rooms/markers/occupants, validate an active receipt's destination and marker before mutation, register inventory, reconcile paid player room, then register native occupants. Exterior reconciliation uses this shared boundary before mixed guest admission. Three native fixtures verify repeated registration creates one player booking, native claims cannot displace that room, competing guests cannot book it, and an invalid marker leaves a fresh destination without lodging or people. Build/full inn suite pass (`work/RemoteReceipt-build.log`, `work/RemoteReceipt-native-pass.log`). This supplies the receipt prerequisite; automatic overnight planning/service/fallback and global admission updates are still not connected. Future multi-night itineraries must retain a paid room across daytime excursions, not accidentally release it on every morning's service visit.

Destination claim adapter now accepts settlement plus DFLocation explicitly. It derives native name seed from destination LocationIndex, regional name bank and race fallback from destination RegionIndex, preserves explicit faction race and DFRandom state, and claims only living ordinary native occupants after receipts are reconciled. Local Claim delegates to the same adapter. Native tests compare local/remote identities and claimed beds in all three inns, check foreign-region fallback and random-state preservation. Remote survey hidden layouts now serialize through a shared active slot plus the 100ms start gate; an active and waiting survey cancellation probe publishes nothing and removes the active owner. Build/full inn suite pass (`work/RemoteClaims-build.log`, `work/RemoteClaims-native-pass.log`). This supersedes missing destination identity and in-progress cancellation support below. The cancellation fixture queues the second survey immediately; long-running concurrent throughput remains unmeasured. Next integration must reconcile destination receipts, register claims before arrivals, handle unavailable keepers/full inns, and attach overnight stops to automatic trips. Model layout remains synchronous.

Remote inventory adapter: original unloaded entrances now retain StaticDoor block/record/building metadata. `TavernRoomSurvey.ReadRemoteSteps` takes that entrance plus destination climate, spaces starts, creates a hidden model-only layout with a temporary owner away from the player, partitions across frames, publishes only completed results and disposes temporary objects. Three Gothway inns match native-owner surveys exactly for room IDs/beds, rental markers and native occupants; pre-start cancellation publishes no inventory and the existing probe verifies no player movement or NPC spawning. Build/full inn suite pass (`work/RemoteInn-build.log`, `work/RemoteInn-native-pass.log`); TownTest now requires all three remote markers. This is inventory preparation, not automatic overnight admission.
