Still required: remote native-person claim registration with destination identity, existing receipt reconciliation, keeper/service availability, full-inn fallback and itinerary integration. Multiple concurrent remote surveys share isolation coordinates and must be serialized before production integration; in-progress cancellation needs a native test. DoLayoutAutomap creation remains synchronous and is not claimed spike-free.

Shared admission ordering now connects to every NativeTavernRentals catch-up path. `AdvanceInnAdmissions` merges generated travelers and original residents by check-in/reception-completion timestamp, with deterministic identity tie-breaking and a shared bounded budget. Loaded reception preparation precedes the merged queue; unresolved due resident service prevents later guests bypassing it. A red test reproduced generated-first room theft; both arrival orders, counter delay, bounded processing and restore now pass. Evidence: `work/MixedInn-red.log`, `work/MixedInn-core.log` (442/442), `work/MixedInn-build.log`, `work/MixedInn-native-pass.log` (three native inns). This supersedes the mixed-source ordering gap below. Admission still requires reconciled inventory and registered demand; global unloaded discovery and keeper-unavailable fallback are not solved.

Inn catch-up correction: pending original-resident admissions now enumerate all scheduled inn visits rather than only the visit active at reconciliation time. They sort by arrival or saved reception completion, attempt admission at that timestamp, and retain completed booking history. Regression cases reproduce skipped expired stays and incorrect later admission; both now pass across bounded batches and save/restore. Direct explicit admission retains its supplied actual payment time. Evidence: `work/InnCatchup-red.log` (439/441), `work/InnCatchup-core.log` (441/441), `work/InnCatchup-build.log`, `work/InnCatchup-native-pass.log` (three inn lifecycle suite). This supersedes the catch-up defect noted below. Still pending: mixed legacy/original traveler chronology, unloaded global reconciliation, replaced-trip history and automatic overnight integration.

Building-to-building routes now retain exact external doorway endpoints, use sliced model-cleared street paths and reject disconnected/cancelled results. Road/building results retain their selected entrance so a subsequent leg starts at the same door. Native Daggerfall route to inn 196879 passes 134 collision-checked segments over 212.29 m; a chained destination/inn/return itinerary reaches the inn continuously. Build/full resident-trip suite pass (`work/ServiceInnRoute-build.log`, `work/ServiceInnRoute-native-pass.log`). This is route/timing preparation only: no bed is booked and automatic multi-day proposals remain deferred. Remaining integration: real inn inventory/native claims and receipts, reception/admission timing, multi-day opening-hour planning and full-inn fallback. Before enabling overnight catch-up, test missed earlier stays: `PendingResidentInnAdmissions` currently evaluates only the visit at `now`; late reconciliation must not retroactively admit a guest whose room was occupied on arrival.

Adjacent-pixel route support: both persisted road endpoints may now be trimmed inside the same unit cardinal/diagonal grid edge. Off-grid lines and hidden intermediate-node crossings remain rejected. The planner/controller accept two-node provider routes instead of skipping them. Red regression reproduced the former rejection; all 439 core tests, Unity build and full native resident-trip suite pass (`work/AdjacentTrips-red.log`, `work/AdjacentTrips-core.log`, `work/AdjacentTrips-build.log`, `work/AdjacentTrips-native-pass.log`). Native serialization checks a synthetic two-trimmed-endpoint route, midpoint motion and return; this does not claim a physically observed trip between two actual adjacent settlements. Existing long-route/daily-batch probes also pass. This supersedes the two-node deferral noted below.

Automatic daily planning is now connected in normal play. Once street setup is ready, the current settlement considers tomorrow's deterministic proposals against its nearest 16 same-region supported destinations. Existing demand remains 10 per thousand eligible adults, capped at eight proposals; only requested one-day visits currently proceed. A main-thread iterator stack advances one step/frame, honors pause, shares background start spacing and cancels on source/store/setting/load invalidation. Reset/new-game/load clear pending work. Complete plans commit only before departure and while ownership remains valid; death/overlap invalidation cannot publish partial absence. Planning attempts are bounded once per settlement/planning day in memory; cancellation currently retries on a later day or save/load, not immediately on same-day return.

Build and full resident-trip regression pass (`work/AutomaticTravel-build.log`, `work/AutomaticTravel-native-pass.log`). The same frame-driven production batch schedules one Daggerfall-to-Gothway original resident; an invalidated batch schedules none. Native commit checks reject stale, late and duplicate overlapping requests; save/load preserves absence and return. Scripted fixtures suppress unsolicited background demand and explicitly exercise the production batch against a separate store; the harness must unpause before testing the pause-aware runner. Natural daily population yield across multiple settlements has not been measured. Geometry planning still uses classic models; remote terrain/billboards/replacement prefabs, overnight/multi-stop planning, road rests, adjacent two-node roads, unseen-settlement demand and cross-region destinations remain unfinished. No full-world performance claim is made.

`ResidentTripPlanner.Prepare` now builds a complete single-day service visit for an eligible original adult: checks ownership, prepares both street/road connections, routes from the real home to a service matching the requested purpose, trims both road ends, derives walking/gate times and validates the complete ResidentTrip before publication. No store mutation occurs during preparation. Multi-day requests are explicitly deferred for lodging planning; they are not shortened. Adjacent-pixel routes with fewer than three regional nodes are deferred under the current trimmed-endpoint contract. Native Daggerfall resident `s:1291010263:h:67073:r:0` successfully plans a Gothway shop visit using 123 road nodes; temporary-store scheduling and native save/load preserve original-resident absence/return. Build and full resident-trip regression pass (`work/ResidentPlanner-build.log`, `work/ResidentPlanner-native-pass.log`). Earlier fixture used homes near the wrong road exit and exhausted their path searches; selecting fixture homes near the actual regional departure resolves it. Automatic daily controller, overnight/multi-stop planning, remote terrain validation and road rest behavior remain unfinished. This does not enable automatic departures yet.

Road-to-building composition now joins reversed road access, model-cleared street routing and an external doorway approach, without duplicate adjoining points. Complete results include route length and gate offset; `Visit` creates a validated one-stop destination with the reversed return path and gate timings. Cancellation/unreachable routes publish no path. Native Daggerfall fixture reaches building 131072 via 12 points over 196.68 m; all segments pass terrain-following collision sweeps and the generated schedule reaches/returns from the building. Build and full resident-trip suite pass (`work/RoadBuildingRoute-build.log`, `work/RoadBuildingRoute-native-pass.log`). Initial flat-height probe struck terrain outside the city; corrected verification samples actual ground along the route, as actors do. Automatic selection/commit, multi-stop overnight planning, unloaded terrain validation and wider settlement coverage remain unfinished.

Street-to-regional-road candidate preparation now runs in sliced, cancellation-aware steps after the shared 100 ms start gate. Walled settlements use street-connected open gates; unwalled settlements use boundary road-run midpoints. Candidates must end outside the native location rectangle, remain within 512 m of the exit and clear static models. Map/local conversion is derived from native map pixels and terrain dimensions. Daggerfall's southern-road fixture yields one candidate; it also passes loaded terrain slope and non-actor collision checks through the existing bridge validator. Build and full resident-trip regression pass (`work/UnloadedRoadAccess-build.log`, `work/UnloadedRoadAccess-native-pass.log`). Initial live failure was test gate state: native Update reclosed gates at night while the survey yielded; probe now explicitly opens gates for each open-plan comparison and restores clock-correct state. Candidate results still require terrain/non-model validation before actor use; unwalled branch and other settlement layouts need native coverage. Automatic resident trip production remains unfinished.

Unloaded surveys now retain the native terrain location rectangle, excluding blend tiles (texture records >=56) and adding the native city/non-city clearance margin. Full RMB dimensions are unsuitable for classifying road joins as wilderness. Daggerfall City matches the loaded terrain rectangle exactly (11,11,105,105); Unity build and full resident-trip regression pass (`work/UnloadedBoundary-build.log`, `work/UnloadedBoundary-native-pass.log`). The probe requires this comparison. This prepares road-join classification; actual regional bridge selection, terrain clearance and automatic departures remain unfinished.

Unloaded city gates now retain both native models (446 open, 447 closed), separate from ordinary static geometry. Model transforms and bounds determine inward/outward crossing points; an open crossing must clear both walking bands and connect to an inner street cell. Collision and route queries default to closed gates; a caller can explicitly request open geometry when planning a permitted time window. Native comparison verifies all four Daggerfall crossings against actual open gate colliders and checks that closed model geometry blocks each crossing. Core 437/437, Unity build and the full resident-trip suite pass (`work/UnloadedGates-core.log`, `work/UnloadedGates-build.log`, `work/UnloadedGates-native-pass.log`). This is route preparation, not automatic traveler scheduling; producer code must still bind the existing gate-time rules and join settlement approaches to regional roads. Non-model/terrain/replacement collision remains outside this snapshot. For the next road-join adapter, native `TerrainHelper.SetLocationTiles` derives the terrain location rectangle from texture records below 56 plus city/other clearance; this differs from the full RMB rectangle.

Unloaded grid routing now checks model collision at both cell positions and connecting edges. The optional `IWalkEdgeGrid` contract preserves existing grids while allowing obstacles between two individually walkable cells; a regression first reproduced the invalid three-cell shortcut and now requires a five-cell detour in both directions, including sliced search. The model adapter caches cell and undirected-edge results per request and uses the same 0.4 m/1 m height bands and 0.2 m radius as the existing loaded-town route checker. Door approaches also use both bands. Native comparison passes 758 approaches and all 79 segments of a nontrivial service-area route at both heights, followed by the full resident-trip save/load and actor regression. Core 437/437 and Unity build pass (`work/ModelRoutes-red.log`, `work/ModelRoutes-core.log`, `work/ModelRoutes-build.log`, `work/ModelRoutes-native-pass.log`). Terrain, billboards, replacement prefabs, dynamic gates and settlement-to-road joins remain separate requirements before automatic trip scheduling. The native trip fixture timeout is now 360 seconds because it includes exhaustive city geometry checks and the existing lifecycle suite.

Unloaded destination entrance clearance now uses classic exterior/subrecord and miscellaneous model triangles, indexed in spatial buckets without constructing Unity scene objects. Sphere-segment distance checks include wall crossings, starting overlaps, triangle edges and degenerate triangles. `TryApproach` selects a nearby walkable cell only after model clearance at the native entrance-check height (1 m, radius 0.2 m), rejecting elevated doors. All 758 accepted Daggerfall City doorway approaches also pass the live collider overlap/sweep checks. Synthetic wall/corner/overlap/height fixtures, full resident-trip native regression, core 436/436 and Unity build pass (`work/MeshClearance-native-pass.log`, `work/MeshClearance-core.log`, `work/MeshClearance-build.log`). This does not yet validate every route segment at multiple body heights, incorporate terrain/billboard/replacement-prefab collision or handle dynamic gates. Grid routing still uses native navigation costs, so physical path clearance and road joins remain required before automatic departures.

Initial exterior guard setup now prepares public posts and entrances across frames before publishing a complete controller. Measured guard setup drops from 554.54 to 130.96 ms; the 467 preparation advances peak at 7.80 ms. Core 436/436, build and full gate/wall regression pass; selected posts and entrances match synchronous surveys. See `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` and `work/GuardSetupSlices-*-pass.log`. Remaining: deferred post survey after an interior save, urgent synchronous refuge fallbacks, initial gate batches, and inn slice maxima. Sparse automatic traveler production remains the gameplay priority after these profiling fixes.

Shared background start spacing is now connected across road/grid route requests, inn-survey starts and gate-duty batches. Native concurrent starts measured 101.43 ms apart, with cancelled work omitted. Core 436/436, build, travel, gate/wall and three-inn suites pass; see `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` and `work/WorkStagger-*-pass.log`. This supersedes the per-request-only delay limitation below. Ongoing sliced jobs can still overlap; this is not a global CPU budget. Initial population refresh remains a measured 573.11 ms spike and requires further work. Physical destination entrance clearance, road joins and automatic resident departures remain unfinished.

Unloaded route coordinates now match all Daggerfall City navigation cells and all 817 entrances in settlement-local space. The native comparison caught the exterior block elevation of two classic units; the adapter now includes it for entrances while grid points remain horizontal. `GridPathfinder.Begin/Step` bounds frontier work and parent tracing, publishes no partial path, and preserves existing synchronous callers. Unloaded grid-route requests wait 100 ms, process 128 work items per frame by default, slice coordinate conversion, and cancel without publication when ownership expires. This is per-request delay, not a global stagger scheduler. Core 435/435, Unity build and the full native resident-trip suite pass (`work/SlicedGrid-core.log`, `work/UnloadedRoutes-build.log`, `work/UnloadedRoutes-native-pass.log`); the earlier coordinate failure is retained in `work/UnloadedCoordinates-native-red.log`. Grid routes alone do not prove physical entrance clearance or terrain/road continuity. Those adapters and automatic trip production remain required. No guard-stall improvement is claimed from this API change.

Unloaded building entrances now derive block-local position and outward normal from classic model door data using the native subrecord/model transforms and building keys. The coroutine yields between building subrecords to spread model reads across frames. Native comparison matches all 817 Daggerfall City building doors, in addition to the existing full navigation-grid and building-directory checks. Core 433/433, Unity build and the full resident-trip native regression pass (`work/UnloadedDoors-core.log`, `work/UnloadedDoors-build.log`, `work/UnloadedDoors-native-pass.log`). This does not yet provide destination road joins, walkable door approaches or automatic trip scheduling. Custom replacement prefabs with changed doors require a separate adapter; no prefab scene objects are instantiated by this survey. Initial block import and individual model decoding remain synchronous, so yielding is not a hard per-frame time guarantee.

Unloaded settlement navigation foundation: `UnloadedSettlementSurvey` reads RMB navigation cells and native building summaries directly through game data without constructing Unity scene objects or moving the player. It yields after each block and publishes only the completed grid/service list. Native Daggerfall City comparison matches every navigation cost cell and every building key/type; the full resident-trip regression also passes (`work/UnloadedSettlement-native-pass.log`). Core 433/433 and Unity build pass (`work/UnloadedSettlement-core.log`, `work/UnloadedSettlement-final-build.log`). A first regression timed out after a clock jump because a native message box paused gameplay; diagnostic output proved playing=False/paused=True. The disposable fixture now restores HUD/unpaused state before waiting for population readiness (`work/UnloadedSettlement-paused-native.log`); production pause behavior is unchanged. Limits: initial RMB import remains synchronous, grid equality does not prove door/road joins or collision-safe destination routes, and automatic departure scheduling remains unconnected. Next derive destination entrances and road joins from actual layout data rather than using synthetic fixture endpoints.

Sparse resident travel demand foundation: `ResidentTravelDemand` proposes trips for existing adult resident IDs using stable world/town/day/identity seeds. Defaults are a 10-per-thousand daily candidate chance and at most eight proposals per town/day; departures vary between 06:00 and 14:00, target settlements are canonically sorted, visits request one to four days and shop/temple/guild/social/castle purposes. Children, dead residents, quest-protected residents and native service staff are excluded. Store proposals remove overlaps with current/future trips and create neither a new resident nor a synthetic traveler. Tests cover 10,000 candidates, sparse selection/caps, order independence, timing variation, eligibility, restore and overlap. Core 433/433 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentDemand-core.log`, `work/ResidentDemand-build.log`). This only proposes trips; runtime route/service feasibility and scheduling are not connected. No autonomous departure is claimed yet. Service staff exclusions are a current coverage limit until staffed absence can be handled.

Resident room allocation catch-up now orders due guests by counter service completion, falling back to doorway arrival only for legacy trips without reception records. The red test gave the last room to the earlier doorway arrival despite another guest finishing reception first; after repair, the earlier counter completion wins across restore and one-admission batches. Candidate due times are computed once rather than reevaluating itineraries inside sort comparisons. Core 430/430 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentAdmissionOrder-red.log`, `work/ResidentAdmissionOrder-core.log`, `work/ResidentAdmissionOrder-build.log`). This does not implement physical counter queues or unify the older synthetic-traveler admission queue. Sparse autonomous resident trip production remains a main unfinished requirement, not replaced by inn refinements.

Loaded-inn reception is connected. Native rental reconciliation asks the existing interior survey for a reachable approach to a living merchant-service NPC (matching vanilla tavern service selection), stores its timing, and defers resident admission until service completion. Pending visitors follow the saved approach and face the keeper; admitted visitors use a continuous counter-to-bed route plus the normal exit. Scene clearing invalidates reception caches. The native test reproduced entry-time booking before the change; it now passes all three inns with no admission before reception exists, followed by room/sleep/wake/checkout and refusal-exit checks (`work/ResidentKeeper-native-red.log`, `work/ResidentKeeper-native-pass.log`). Core 429/429 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentKeeper-core.log`, `work/ResidentKeeper-final-build.log`). The refusal fixture now advances past saved service completion before expecting departure. Remaining: direct facing/intermediate-route assertions, simultaneous counter queues, dead/busy-keeper departure, first-time unloaded reception surveys, and candidate admission ordering by counter completion rather than original doorway arrival. Existing saved receptions continue to constrain exterior admission, but that transition lacks dedicated native acceptance. Unloaded arrivals without reception still use the prior admission behavior.

Saved reception gate: optional `ResidentTrip.InnReceptions` stores copied approach geometry, visit binding, start time, walking rate and service duration. Admission APIs and due-batch queries defer while its counter service is unfinished. Restore validates itinerary ownership and rejects admissions predating service completion. Core tests verify stair interpolation, source-copy isolation, reload continuity, exact completion boundary and atomic rejection of corrupted timing; 429/429 pass, as does Unity build (`work/ResidentReceptionSave-core.log`, `work/ResidentReceptionSave-build.log`). Old schema-15 trips with no receptions retain existing admission behavior. Runtime still does not create reception records; living keeper survey, actor approach/facing and unloaded reception creation remain next. No new native keeper-interaction claim is made.

Inn reception journey foundation: `ResidentInnJourney` models absolute-time entrance-to-keeper movement, a service pause, keeper-to-bed movement, room occupancy and a separate physical exit route. It copies geometry, validates route joins/finite timing/stay capacity, and exposes service completion for future admission scheduling. Tests cover stairs, phase boundaries, reconstruction without restarting service, mutation isolation and invalid/disconnected routes. Core 428/428 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentReception-core.log`, `work/ResidentReception-build.log`). This new journey is not yet connected to actors. Next integrate actual living keeper geometry and delay admission until counter service completes; do not describe the current entry-time reservation as a completed keeper transaction. Existing room renderer and admission timing remain unchanged.

Resident room lifecycle acceptance now checks the actor against the actual booked bed center, verifies waking by midday, observes checkout movement, and confirms interior removal after checkout. It passes in all three inns before and after caching (`work/ResidentRoomLifecycle-native-pass.log`, `work/ResidentRoomCache-native-pass.log`). Resident room plans now cache paid interval data by interior and visit entry, eliminating the per-frame copied stay query; scene/save clearing and new visit keys invalidate plans. Core 425/425 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentRoomCache-core.log`, `work/ResidentRoomCache-build.log`). This is an allocation removal verified by code and behavior regression, not a measured FPS gain. Keeper choreography, detailed inbound walking samples and crime wake acceptance remain open.

Booked resident inn rooms now render the original resident using a surveyed entrance/bed route, the shared booked bed and matching sleeping art. Room walking uses admission/check-out timestamps; nighttime sleep has a stable small per-resident time offset and only starts when room walking is complete. Destination schedule queries expose Sleep, and reactions use existing wake handling. Native three-inn tests failed before rendering was connected and now verify sleep after scheduled room-arrival time, restoration of sleeping actors, and the existing refusal/rental regressions (`work/ResidentRoom-native-red.log`, `work/ResidentRoom-native-pass.log`). Core 425/425 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentRoom-final-core.log`, `work/ResidentRoom-final-build.log`). Live intermediate walking samples, exact bed-position assertions, morning waking/checkout, crime wake regression and keeper interaction are not yet covered by this new fixture. Missing route/art retains standing fallback. The room-render path still copies the stay query each frame; caching that lookup is a follow-up performance task.

Resident full-inn departure now uses the saved physical service-exit route and resumes the retimed onward itinerary. A new native fixture fills actual lodging inventory, introduces an original foreign resident, waits for native refusal and an automatic exit, restores that exit, and verifies interior removal after departure. The test failed before the refusal trigger was connected (`work/ResidentInnExit-native-red.log`) and passes in all three Gothway inns afterward (`work/ResidentInnExit-native-pass.log`). Core 425/425 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentInnExit-core.log`, `work/ResidentInnExit-build.log`). This covers loaded inn refusal; unloaded refusal routing, keeper approach and admitted resident bed walking/sleeping remain unfinished. Unsupported physical exit geometry still retains the ordinary itinerary.

Resident admission scheduling: native rental reconciliation now advances at most eight original-resident admissions for the reconciled inn, after importing player receipts and claiming permanent occupants. Pending resident arrivals also block a new player rental until bounded catch-up finishes. Resident batches order by visit entry time and identity, persist refusal, and leave unsurveyed inns pending. Core 425/425, Unity build and the existing three-inn rental/occupancy regression pass (`work/ResidentAdmissionBatch-core.log`, `work/ResidentAdmissionBatch-build.log`, `work/ResidentAdmissionBatch-native.log`). Native sleeping verified one supported occupant; two known missing-art cases remain. This supersedes the API-only status below. Physical keeper approach, booked-bed movement/sleeping, refusal departure and a dedicated native resident-arrival fixture remain unfinished. Candidate collection still scans saved trips; admission count is not a strict frame-time bound. Legacy generated travelers advance before resident batches, so cross-system arrival fairness remains open.

Resident-owned inn booking foundation: optional trip stays record admitted/refused attempts and reference normal lodging bookings under the original resident ID. Admission shares player/native occupancy rules, rejects investigated inns, waits for missing inventory, and does not retry a recorded full-inn refusal after a room becomes free. Paid intervals begin at the actual attempt; bed lookup respects death/expiry. Restore and scheduling validate booking ownership, home settlement, interval and party size; ordinary booking extensions cannot silently change resident itinerary bookings. Core 424/424 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentInn-final-core.log`, `work/ResidentInn-build.log`). This API is not yet called by runtime destination actors; keeper approach/payment choreography, bed walking/sleeping, refusal exits and native resident-inn tests remain next. No duplicate `TravelerPerson` is created and no NPC currency inventory/debit is implemented here.

Guarded-shop automatic acceptance: the native chain now waits for the reported guards to physically occupy the shop, introduces another foreign shopper, verifies an automatically saved service exit without a redundant witness report, and checks removal from the interior at exit. The red run exposed corpse discovery taking priority over existing guards; guard occupancy now wins for newcomers. Build and full native shop suite pass (`work/GuardedVisitor-native-red.log`, `work/GuardedVisitor-native-pass.log`, `work/GuardedVisitor-build.log`). This closes the dedicated automatic trigger acceptance for the tested shop. Paid lodging, unsupported exit geometry and autonomous trip production remain pending.

Service-exit timing correction supersedes the prior outdoor-wait/late-window limitations: each physical exit now determines that stop's end, so subsequent walks start at the exit time and later services reapply their opening windows. Late cancellation may finish beyond the old visit deadline rather than snapping away. Core 422/422 and build pass (`work/ServiceExitTiming-final-core.log`, `work/ServiceExitNative-build.log`). Native trip suite verifies a surveyed cancellation route survives restore, retains interior presence before exit and removes the actor afterward (`work/ServiceExitNative-pass.log`). The probe supplies cancellation directly; automatic guard-occupancy detection still needs its dedicated live acceptance. Unusable exit geometry still falls back to the existing schedule and should be addressed separately.

Guarded-service cancellation implementation: resident trips now carry optional validated service-exit routes, including interior height, visit ownership and timing. A guarded destination service can replace browsing with physical exit movement; the saved cancellation keeps the resident outside until the next planned leg and preserves original household ownership. Missing exit data remains compatible with existing schema-15 saves. Core 422/422 and Unity build pass (`work/ServiceExit-core.log`, `work/ServiceExit-build.log`), including stairs, copy isolation, itinerary binding, restore and malformed-save rejection. Native guarded-shop exit acceptance is pending. Current limits: the visitor waits outside for its original next-leg time; an exit that cannot fit the remaining visit window falls back to ordinary visit expiry. Neither limitation is the desired final interruption behavior.

Foreign physical reporting acceptance: the shop fixture now disables other civilian reporters in its disposable population, asserts no dispatch before the visitor exits, advances the simulation while the player stays indoors, and verifies a neutral investigation dispatch attributable only to the foreign corpse observer. Native suite and build pass (`work/ForeignReport-native-pass.log`, `work/ForeignReport-build.log`). This closes foreign-only delivery acceptance. Guard-occupied-shop cancellation still needs a destination-aware departure: existing `VisitDeparture` requires a local home and must not be reused by pretending the foreign home belongs to this town.

Foreign corpse response checkpoint: destination visitors now discover interior corpses, interrupt browsing and enter the existing saved escape/report planner. New actors receive their reachable initial ground position before escape preparation. The corpse list is shared across destination visitors within an update. Native red/green proves the previously ignored corpse now creates a foreign corpse observer with a physical escape route (`work/VisitorCorpse-native-red.log`, `work/VisitorCorpse-native-pass.log`); build passes (`work/VisitorCorpse-final-build.log`). This test does not yet isolate delivery by the foreign witness from other local witnesses, nor cover already-cleaned guard-occupied shops or reactions outlasting the itinerary.

Foreign shop activity checkpoint: derived visit snapshots expose service purpose and exact entry/exit times. Destination shoppers now reuse surveyed `ShopVisitGeometry` and `PlaceShopVisit`, including multiple shelf stops, task-facing, existing deterministic 50% purchase/haggling choice and a route back to the entrance within the visit window. Local reservations are prepared before foreign reservations; cached foreign journeys invalidate when their window changes. Core 420/420, Unity build and native shop suite pass (`work/VisitorShopping-final-core.log`, `work/VisitorShopping-final-build.log`, `work/VisitorShopping-native-pass.log`). Native checks retain local purchase/browse-only/facing/talk regressions and verify an original foreign resident following a real shop browsing route. This does not yet prove both purchase outcomes for foreign residents, crime-scene rejection for foreign shoppers, admission to lodging, or autonomous trip generation.

Navigation-readiness regression acceptance: the full native gate suite passes after guard-only deferral (`work/DestinationReadiness-gates-native-pass.log`). Checks cover four gates, finite sentry assignment/facing, physical dusk return, recall while inside an inn, wall duty/morning return and sliced wall survey equivalence/cancellation. This closes the pending gate-specific regression from the destination actor change.

Destination interior acceptance: the native resident-trip fixture now enters the scheduled house, verifies the original foreign visitor's name and interior parent, restores through DFU serialization while inside, and advances past departure to verify removal. All checks pass (`work/DestinationInterior-native-pass.log`, `work/DestinationInterior-build.log`). This verifies presence continuity and scheduled absence, not browsing, paid beds or a physically animated walk from the entrance to the standing anchor. Those remain implementation work.

Destination actor checkpoint: `ResidentDestinationActors` renders original household visitors along destination itineraries, with shared actor ownership, death references, nearby culling and basic reachable interior standing positions. Road transfers now admit destination residents as well as returning locals. Native fixture verifies the foreign resident's name/identity, recognized living status, travel state and movement in Daggerfall (`work/DestinationActor-native-pass.log`); prior home/road/serializer checks also pass. Core 420/420 and Unity build pass. This fixture creates a disposable synthetic visit, not an automatic departure. Interior browsing, admission/paid beds, physical foreign crime responses, destination road-boundary health continuity and automatic production still require acceptance. Active reactions outlasting an itinerary also need explicit schedule/presence handling before broad rollout.

The same native round trip exposed a guard setup race: navigation formatting can be visible before `CityNavigation.Start` sets its peer. Only guard route preparation now defers on a newly observed navigation component. Deferring the entire population prevented the initial load fixture from becoming ready; that experiment was removed. Final native trip suite passes initial load and return to Daggerfall. Broader gate regression remains to run after this readiness change.

Native foreign incident checkpoint: original resident death and destination corpse survive DFU FullSerializer round-trip in the native trip suite (`work/ResidentCrimeNative-pass.log`). This is serializer validation, not an on-screen destination combat test. Entry-report validation now accepts living resident visitors during their visit, rejects pre-arrival reports, and preserves evidence after trip replacement. Core 420/420 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentEntry-core.log`, `work/ResidentEntry-build.log`). The report resolver retains original identity for later messenger delivery. Visible destination actors, physical foreign witness delivery and automatic travel production remain next.

Foreign resident incident checkpoint: civilian incident resolution now uses original household residents for destination victims/witnesses and retains historical participant resolution after trip replacement. New foreign victim incidents require current destination presence. Core 419/419 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentCrime-core.log`, `work/ResidentCrime-build.log`), covering pre-arrival rejection, death/corpse restore and witness restore after a subsequent trip replaces the original. Native destination crime interactions, actor rendering, broader entry-report validation and automatic departures remain pending; this does not claim the complete foreign visitor crime integration is finished.

Destination identity checkpoint: `DestinationResidents` borrows the original living resident only during an itinerary-backed destination visit. `TryDestinationResidentState` and the street schedule adapter return the destination building and position before home-absence suppression. Core 417/417, Unity build and the existing native trip regression pass (`work/DestinationState-core.log`, `work/DestinationState-build.log`, `work/DestinationState-native-pass.log`). Destination actors are not yet rendered. Next integrate foreign resident identities with civilian incidents and their save validation: current `AlarmResident` rejects visitors belonging to another household settlement. Preserve the original identity rather than creating a second traveler record. Then connect destination actors, route surveying, automatic departures and authoritative inn admission.

The user has promoted resident-owned inter-settlement travel to the next main implementation priority. Eligible adults leave their actual households for pilgrimage to temples or visits to settlements, shops, guild services, castles and other services. Visits respect opening times and can span multiple days when the itinerary needs it. Late travelers seek first-come inn accommodation before continuing or returning home. Children do not travel. The same resident must be absent from home throughout the trip, including home occupancy/lock and witness checks; there must be no destination-created duplicate.

Only a small fraction of settlement pedestrians should be visitors. Generate sparse departures with varied timing and occasional small groups, not continuous streams. Nearby road actors need usable lateral spacing and collision avoidance rather than a razor-thin shared lane. Visitor frequency should be configurable with a low default. The user's village-edge screenshot shows the current gap: walking near a settlement boundary does not prove a connected overland trip.

Settlement coverage already accepts native city/town/village categories. This is separate from world-wide travel continuity, which is unfinished. Performance validation must measure nearby active actors and growth in persistent multi-settlement state. Do not simulate every distant actor every frame or describe current city measurements as the finished system's maximum load.

15 October refusal checkpoint: a fully booked inn now sends a refused traveler back along the surveyed exterior route without reserving a bed. Closed gates delay departure; admission changes invalidate cached journeys so the proposed multi-day stay cannot keep a refused actor alive.352 core tests and the native Daggerfall full-inn test pass, including visible return movement and actual save-slot reload (`work/TravelerRefusal-core.log`, `work/TravelerRefusal-runtime-pass.log`, `work/TravelerRefusal-build-editor.log`). The test runner now requires `LIVING_CITIES_TRAVELER_REFUSAL_PASS`. Automatic demand, keeper interaction, daytime visits, historical retention and broader layout acceptance remain open.

**Goal:** Nonlocal adult NPCs physically arrive, obtain first-come lodging, visit town, sleep in their assigned beds, and leave after their stay. Preserve identities, death and journey progress across save/load and unloading.

## Resident-owned trip foundation, 16 October 2026

Schema 12 now stores one latest trip per existing adult resident, with absolute outbound/visit/return times and validated adjacent map-pixel routes. It rejects overlapping trips, preserves household membership and bed ownership, and does not create a duplicate TravelerPerson. Generated home actors and household door checks respect absence; the same identity returns after the trip. Legacy saves migrate to an empty trip ledger.

403/403 core tests and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentTrip-green2.log`, `work/ResidentTrip-native2-build.log`). Native Daggerfall checks verify pre-departure presence, departure absence/locking, DFU FullSerializer round-trip while away, and same-ID/name return (`work/ResidentTrip-native-pass.log`). The disposable probe uses a synthetic adjacent-pixel route and a generated single-adult household. It does not prove actual Basic Roads connectivity, disk save/load or native-owned resident absence.

Basic Roads API follow-up: `BasicRoadsNetwork` lazily imports road and track arrays through `getPathData`, validates dimensions and caches an immutable combined network. Missing/disabled/not-ready providers or malformed layers do not produce routes. The native probe verifies the installed provider supplies a connected three-point route from (207,213) to (207,211), rejects the disconnected adjacent pixel (208,213), and reuses the cached network. Core 404/404, Unity build and native resident departure/return regression pass (`work/RoadNetwork-green.log`, `work/RoadNetwork-final-build.log`, `work/RoadNetwork-final-native.log`). This verifies provider integration, not settlement-to-settlement destinations. The home absence test still uses its explicitly synthetic itinerary.

Incremental planning follow-up: road searches now expose bounded `Advance` steps, including stale queue pops and path reconstruction. Results remain unavailable until completion; cancellation publishes an empty result. The runtime `FindRoute` starts after 100 ms, advances at most 64 work steps per frame by default, and suppresses publication when the owning context is invalidated. The synchronous convenience wrapper remains for diagnostics. Native comparison verifies the installed provider's route over multiple frames plus invalidated-request suppression; resident absence/return still passes. Core 406/406 and Unity build pass (`work/RoadSlices-green.log`, `work/RoadSlices-build.log`, `work/RoadSlices-native-pass.log`). This is a work-count bound, not a measured hard millisecond bound. Initial provider import and final list reversal remain synchronous.

Wilderness actor checkpoint: `RoadTravelActors` now renders the actual living household resident during saved outbound/return transit, using loaded terrain transforms and fractional game time. Map nodes represent terrain centres; northward map motion maps to positive scene Z. Actors enter within 140 m, leave beyond 165 m, and are capped at 16 nearby bodies. They reuse normal identity/appearance and crowd collision handling. They clear during interiors, load/restore and disabled simulation. Settlement-containing map pixels are deliberately excluded until local connectors exist, preventing straight routes through buildings.

Core 408/408 and Unity build pass. Native Daggerfall-to-adjacent-wilderness probe verifies the same named resident, visible walking animation and movement, distance culling and reconstruction (`work/RoadActors-core.log`, `work/RoadActors-clock-build.log`, `work/RoadActors-native-pass.log`). Native testing found that vanilla `MobilePersonMotor.ClearMotor` requires a navigation object even when disabled; road actors use an inactive zero-sized navigation owner for cleanup. Fractional `Now.Second` supplements truncated `ToSeconds()` for continuous movement. The fixture schedules a disposable trip explicitly; normal automatic departures are not enabled by this checkpoint.

Home connector checkpoint: schema 13 adds a persisted surveyed home-to-exit route, duration and walled flag. Departure waits at home until its exit arrival falls in the 06:00–18:00 gate window. Returning travelers wait at the outside endpoint when gates are closed, then reverse the route to their home door. Home absence spans the complete local/road/visit/return interval. Street actors render the local phases; road actors render only regional transit. Schema 12 trips without connectors retain their original timing.

Core 411/411 and Unity build pass (`work/HomeConnector-final-core.log`, `work/HomeConnector-build.log`). Native Daggerfall verifies movement along an actual surveyed home-to-gate route, locked home, FullSerializer continuation mid-walk and removal from the town after exit; the road actor suite also remains green (`work/HomeConnector-native-pass.log`). Native returning-home motion and closed-gate waiting are not yet separately tested; their timing and reverse positions are core-tested. The probe explicitly supplies the route and chooses an exit; it is not an automatic departure producer.

Regional join checkpoint: schema 14 accepts trimmed endpoints on a cardinal/diagonal road segment and separately stores gate-crossing walk time before the road join. `ResidentDepartureSurvey` extends a surveyed exit to a clear point on the first regional segment, rejecting static obstacles, steep steps and bridges beyond its current 512 m search bound. It trims the regional start to exactly that endpoint. Road rendering now excludes the actual terrain location rectangle rather than the whole settlement map pixel, allowing actors outside town on the same tile.

Core 412/412 and Unity build pass (`work/RoadJoin-core.log`, `work/RoadJoin-south-build.log`). Native Daggerfall verifies the shared endpoint within 0.02 m, a same-identity road actor after exit, and the existing home/serializer/wilderness regressions (`work/RoadJoin-native-pass.log`). The selected home's only successful surveyed exit was south; forcing the northbound road failed correctly because it would cross back through town (`work/RoadJoin-direction-red.log`). The fixture now uses the actual connected southbound edge. The future producer must choose exit and regional route together. The bridge is collision-surveyed terrain, not proof that every bridge point follows painted road textures.

Live ownership transfer now supersedes the recreation limitation above. Street and road controllers transfer the existing `ResidentActor`, its entity and components, changing only parent/navigation ownership. Both update orders request a transfer before creating a replacement; the losing dictionary releases ownership without destruction. The road spawn scan remains limited to 16 newly managed road bodies, but existing visible transfers are retained rather than dropped merely to satisfy that spawn cap.

Native regression first failed on actor recreation/health reset (`work/RoadTransfer-native-red.log`), then passed with the exact same body and health retained across both outbound and returning-home handoffs (`work/RoadTransfer-native-pass.log`). Core 412/412 and Unity build pass (`work/RoadTransfer-core.log`, `work/RoadTransfer-build.log`). This preserves live components at the boundary; it does not establish persistence of every effect across distant actor unloading or disk reload. Return verification advances the clock to the home phase; exact native closed-gate crossing geometry remains untested.

Destination timing checkpoint: schema 15 optionally stores a chained multi-stop destination itinerary with purpose, building key, opening hours, walking duration, dwell duration and exit route. Absolute-time evaluation distinguishes gate waiting, walking, waiting outside a closed service, visiting inside, departure waiting and walking back to the same regional join. Service windows can cross midnight; a visit must fit before closing. Entry/exit gate timing is separate from walk completion. The complete itinerary determines return departure and home absence; legacy trips retain their fixed visit duration.

Core 416/416 and Unity build pass (`work/ResidentVisit-final-core.log`, `work/ResidentVisit-build.log`). Native DFU serialization preserves a synthetic two-stop shop/guild itinerary and calculated home return, while all earlier resident/road transfer checks pass (`work/ResidentVisit-native-pass.log`). Destination service rendering is not tested or connected yet. An Inn purpose is timing metadata only and cannot reserve a bed; the producer must consult authoritative lodging admission. Likewise, the core can represent overnight waits outside closed services, but the final producer must arrange an appropriate inn stay instead of scheduling such waits as a normal daily routine.

Remaining: automatic compatible exit/destination selection and surveyed destination-local connectors; destination exterior/interior actors and actual service activities; sparse automatic departures and lodging-backed multi-day plans; native-owned residents; road-specific crime/corpse, death/save reload and floating-origin boundary acceptance; varied lateral spacing. Candidate discovery currently scans the trip ledger twice per second and active lookup resolves the home resident; larger-world indexing/performance work remains. The automatic producer must use incremental regional searches, slice the currently synchronous local join survey, invalidate outstanding requests on load/settlement changes, and handle crime/haunting interruptions. Complete autonomous inter-settlement travel is not finished.

## Cross-settlement continuity requirement, 15 October 2026

World-wide identity is required, not a special-case traveler illusion: all managed NPCs must have authoritative identity, life state, destination and determinable position across the game world. Absolute-time reconstruction and bounded local simulation are acceptable; rendering every NPC or updating every NPC every frame is unnecessary. A traveling resident cannot simultaneously render, witness, occupy their home for lock checks or follow a home routine there. Their household membership and owned bed remain reserved. Every scene resolves the same death and journey state. This must work regardless of which settlement the player visits first.

Before automatic lodging demand, implement the existing inn-room occupant claims and sleep behavior specified in `docs/TAVERN-LODGING.md`; otherwise generated travelers can reserve beds belonging to vanilla room occupants.

Traveler production must connect to a real source household, not merely attach a hometown label to a destination-generated person. The current `TravelerDemand` IDs are destination/tavern/day/slot based and `RegisterTraveler` rejects resident IDs; these are existing limitations to replace before claiming household-linked travel. Preserve existing saves and their historical traveler identities during migration.

The next integration sequence is: read Basic Roads connectivity through its `getPathData` mod API; find bounded reciprocal road routes; connect source/destination gates to surveyed local routes; persist one resident-owned trip with absolute outbound, visit and return times; suppress that resident's home routine during the entire absence; resolve death and crime against the same identity in every scene; render nearby road travelers from world coordinates. Children must remain in their home settlement. Household occupancy and locks must use absence, not delete the resident or release their home bed. Failed routes must not create cross-country straight-line travelers. Only then enable recurring demand from eligible adult residents.

The pure road planner is implemented in `OverlandRoads.cs`, using Basic Roads' eight direction bits and row-major map-pixel coordinates. It requires reciprocal edges, uses geometric edge costs with deterministic tie breaking, and has an expansion budget. Synthetic bend, reverse, disconnected, diagonal, boundary and budget tests pass as part of355 core tests (`work/OverlandRoads-core.log`). This does not yet read the mod API at runtime, prove routes in the shipped map, create trips, suppress home residents or render road actors.

15 October route continuation: `TravelerRoomWalk` supplies absolute-time 3D arrival and checkout movement over the actual bed/exit route. Runtime opens ordinary doors and keeps walking guests awake; gate waits use the indoor entrance landing after the bed is released.350 core tests and native Daggerfall checkout movement plus sleep/lifecycle regression pass. Missing routes retain prior static placement with an explicit warning. Broad layout coverage, live arrival/gate-wait acceptance, daytime visits, refused departure, demand and history work remain open (`docs/TAVERN-LODGING.md`).

15 October physical guest checkpoint: admitted travelers render inside their booked inn, sleep in a stable paid-room bed, reload sleeping and wake with the same identity.349 core tests and native Daggerfall sleep/lifecycle checks pass (`work/TravelerInn-runtime-pass.log`). Awake guests remain beside their bed for now. Door-to-room movement, daytime visits, checkout/refusal routing, automatic demand and broader layout/crime acceptance keep the actor milestone open.

15 October admission continuation: registered arrivals now enter paid lodging automatically after per-inn native receipt reconciliation. Player rental requests process earlier due arrivals first, with bounded work and explicit remaining-queue detection. Daggerfall automatic admission/lifecycle and Gothway rental/rest suites pass;348 core tests and Unity build pass. Native fractional calendar serialization regression fixed with sub-ms validation tolerance. Full demand-window generation must precede this admission when automatic demand is added; physical inn rendering, rejected departures and historical indexing remain unfinished (`docs/TAVERN-LODGING.md`).

15 October exterior actor checkpoint: registered arrival/admitted departure actors are connected. Daggerfall runtime verifies movement/name, arrival save/reload, later visit replacement, native death/corpse and death save/reload (`work/TravelerActor-runtime-pass.log`);345 core tests and Unity build pass. Remaining actor milestone includes inn interiors/sleep, rejected arrival departure, automatic demand/admission orchestration and bounded history. Review specifically found retained historical plans and repeated history scans; fix before recurring production demand. Later checkpoint supersedes actor-absent text below without completing the full milestone.

**Primary acceptance location:** Daggerfall City (user direction,15 October2026); Gothway is the unwalled comparison. Native city gate route fixture now verifies4 gates,20/20 daytime inn routes, all-inn night rejection and representative dawn recovery (`work/CityTraveler-final-pass.log`). Physical traveler actors/waiting and haunting-aware itinerary behavior still require integration.

**Architecture:** Travelers are separate from residential households and garrison population. A deterministic core itinerary uses surveyed entrance-to-inn routes and absolute game time. Persisted visit identities and booking outcomes drive the same civilian actor, collision, crime and sleeping systems used by residents; they must not become decorative invulnerable crowds. Lodging remains authoritative even while a traveler is outside.

Current authority: `docs/TAVERN-LODGING.md`, native `DaggerfallCityGate.Update` (06:00 open, 18:00 closed), existing `Lodging.cs`, `StreetPopulation.cs`, and civilian/death persistence. No random enemy generator is part of this work.

15 October continuation: `AdvanceTravelerAdmissions` now processes registered pending arrivals by check-in time with ordinal-ID tie breaking and a bounded commit budget. Tests cover reverse registration, reload between batches, paid player priority, long time skips and unsurveyed inns.342 core tests and Unity build pass. Caller must register the complete demand window and reconcile native receipts before advancing; no production controller invokes this yet. User-reported stationary invulnerability and witness flavor text took priority before runtime integration. Demand generation/identity adapter and native Daggerfall identity probe remain in the working tree; visible travelers and bounded historical retention remain open.

- [x] Implement and test absolute-time itinerary in `TravelerJourney.cs`: deterministic checkout/sleep variation, one/multiple nights, supplied-route interpolation, gate-aware entry/exit, checkout ending the stay before any gate wait, and reconstruction after time skips.327/327 core tests and Unity build pass (`work/TravelerJourney-green.log`, `work/TravelerJourney-build.log`). This accepts a proposed arrival time; generation of staggered arrival demand belongs to admission below. Runtime actors remain pending.
- [ ] Add versioned traveler visit persistence and atomic admission with lodging. Import native paid receipts before allocating NPC rooms. Persist rejected arrivals so reloading does not retry until success. Use actual nonlocal settlement IDs, distinct adult identities, bounded active visits and explicit historical retention; preserve deaths without respawning a departed identity.

  Partial completion: schema9 identity/visit/death/admission persistence and atomic validation pass333 core tests and native Unity serialization; all3 inn rental/payment/rest regressions pass. See `TravelerState.cs` and `work/TravelerSave-regression-pass.log`. Runtime origin selection, demand, receipt ordering and bounded history remain open, so this task is not complete.
- [ ] Survey inn inventory and usable settlement entry/exit routes without requiring player rental. Keep private guest rooms, staff beds and unclassified areas distinct. Identify physical gates rather than assuming every settlement is walled. Preserve unsupported-layout compatibility and report missing geometry.

  Partial completion: automatic exterior inventory now surveys one inn per refresh, caches native geometry and reconciles paid player receipts. All3 Gothway inns match loaded layouts and pass no-entry/payment/rest regression (`work/ExteriorInventory-pass.log`). Gate/road routes, staff allocation, replacement-layout compatibility and large-town survey timing remain open.
- [ ] Integrate traveler actors with `StreetPopulation` and resident resolution in crime/defense/death systems. Reuse path collision and speed handling, same-person interior/exterior transitions, consistent portraits/names and bed poses. A rejected booking sends the traveler onward rather than making them a permanent lobby resident.

  Partial completion15 October: civilian alarm/corpse core resolves traveler identities through destination visits. Traveler victim/witness/death/discovery round-trips, dead witness rejection and atomic death chronology checks pass345 core tests plus native Unity serialization (`work/TravelerCrime-final-core.log`, `work/TravelerCrime-build-editor.log`). Actor death synchronization and rendering/controller integration remain open; do not claim visible travelers from ledger tests.
- [ ] Add deterministic local visits during a paid stay, gate and haunting constraints, staggered sleep and departure. Travelers and local tavern patrons remain separate; neither inherits the keeper's surname.
- [ ] Add meaningful settings for demand/stay ranges and profiler counters. Test native slots mid-arrival, sleeping, away from paid room, rejected booking, death and departure; test closed walled gates versus unwalled night arrivals and bounded large time skips. Never interrupt the user's game or capture their mouse.

Each step uses failing behavioral tests before implementation, then the relevant core/native build and live checks. Core-only itinerary results do not establish that travelers are visible in gameplay. Keep incomplete integration explicit until native arrival, sleep, visit and exit are observed.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-16-household-recovery-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-household-recovery.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Persistent household recovery

Latest checkpoint, 16 October: history, migration, persisted reservations, runtime activation and native bank listing/purchase adapters are implemented and pass the combined 573-test core suite and Unity build. Earlier foundation-only status below is historical. Native gameplay acceptance, release of deceased native bed/worksite claims and smooth crowd refresh during replacement remain open.

This implements the approved vacancy requirements in `../../VACANCY-AND-RECOVERY.md`. It does not redefine an empty rendered interior as an extinct household. Latest user rules override the old goal summary: one occupant per bed; player-caused deaths may break quests; no artificial random enemy spawner.

## Contract

Every permanent household member, including a native keeper whose home is elsewhere than their work, must be confirmed dead before recovery is scheduled. Living travelers and absent residents retain ownership. Preserve old names, relationships, corpses, quest references and journey history. A replacement is a new family with new persistent IDs, never resurrection or silent reassignment.

Choose a fixed deterministic deadline once: dense cities 3–7 game days; ordinary settlements 7–30; isolated hamlets bias late. Initial isolated policy is 24–30 days, an implementation default within the user's approximately 30-day requirement. Runtime classification must use actual settlement/isolation evidence. Settings or reloading must not reroll an existing decision.

## Implementation sequence

1. Add and test a pure recovery policy with explicit eligibility facts, fixed deadline calculation, and activation gates. This is a foundation only; it must not spawn families until identity history and persistence are ready.
2. Add generation-aware IDs and a historical household ledger. Keep exactly one current household per building. Identity resolution for alarms, relationships, quest bindings and trips must continue finding retired people. Preserve generation-zero IDs and legacy saves unchanged.
3. Persist extinction/recovery decisions and native bed reservation lifetime. Dead native keepers retain historical identity but no longer reserve a bed for current occupants. Missing legacy death timestamps leave recovery dormant rather than guessing.
4. Integrate DFU property eligibility: surveyed residential buildings only, positive known bed count, complete population, no player ownership, faction claim, active quest placement or native keeper worksite. Recheck at activation; do not overwrite newly purchased/protected property.
5. Atomically archive the old family and create one new generation after the deadline, with the player outside and corpses cleared. Refresh schedules and identity caches without touching historical quest contracts. Retain vacancy when blocked.
6. Verify save/load during delay and after replacement, no duplicate generations, new IDs/surnames, one resident per bed, living absent-owner exclusion, corpse/player/quest/property gates and different settlement delays in core and native tests.

## Related work and limits

16 October generation factory checkpoint: `HouseholdGeneration.Create` preserves generation-zero identities and appearance exactly. Positive generations use deterministic fresh appearance/surname seeds and generation-qualified resident IDs, with partner/parent links remapped within the new family. It requires surveyed beds and respects one resident per bed. Core tests pass 542/542 (`work/HouseholdGeneration-core.log`). This is not the historical ledger or replacement transaction; no runtime caller activates replacements yet. Next implement the historical ledger and validated save migration before connecting the factory to vacancy recovery.

Bank listings must eventually use real DFU ownership APIs; a fake listing is not an acceptable substitute. This initial implementation sequence covers family replacement and safe vacancy only. Player-caused quest deaths versus ambient protection need their own verified policy change; they are not a reason to rebind an active quest to a replacement family.

Current runtime has individual deaths and corpse/crime-scene cleanup. It does not yet have household generations, replacement immigration or bank listings. Core-policy tests alone will not establish runtime completion.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-16-observed-visit-continuity-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-observed-visit-continuity.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Observed visit continuity

## Reproduced problem

Loaded shop movement previously sampled absolute schedule time, while collision avoidance moved the body separately. A delayed body could target a later shelf through an intervening obstacle. On scene changes, `physicalShopVisits`, `shopJourneys` and `shopDepartures` are cleared, and bodies are reconstructed from the original clock. A visible delay therefore disappears from the simulation.

## Required invariant

A person has one authoritative location and remaining journey. A player observing a delayed person, leaving the building, then immediately returning must find the person at a physically reachable point along that remaining journey. Outside representation cannot begin until the interior exit has been reached. Reloading must preserve the same rule. Offscreen movement may advance analytically along surveyed routes, without simulating every frame.

## Work sequence

1. Replace loaded clock sampling with actual waypoint arrival, followed by arrival-based browsing/service dwell. Share this with local and visiting shoppers and common-room patrons. Verify entire rendered visits without phase jumps.
2. Persist observed activity state: resident identity, settlement/building, original visit identifier/window, copied validated remaining routes, active waypoint, actual local ground position, remaining dwell, observation timestamp and activity target. Include both ordinary visits and physical departure. Capture before native scene destruction and before save; do not rely only on the subsequent controller update.
3. Analytically advance that checkpoint while unloaded. Preserve interior-local coordinates until the doorway, then switch to the saved exterior route. Resolve the next actual itinerary stop, or the return-home route for the final stop. Existing cancellation-to-home `VisitDeparture` cannot represent this unchanged.
4. Make local and foreign activity queries consult the continuation before original schedule time. Apply the delayed handoff consistently to destination selection, presence, arrival and later visits; do not merely keep a rendered body while the authoritative person is elsewhere. Reconcile closing hours and missed appointments by explicit cancellation/rerouting, never teleportation.
5. Rehydrate the physical follower from the analytically advanced checkpoint. Reject duplicate bodies and conflicting continuations transactionally. Death, crime response and explicit service rejection supersede shopping, but retain physical exit progress.
6. Test blocked movement, interruption during browsing/payment/departure, immediate exit/re-entry, time elapsed outside, save/load, multi-stop outings, foreign visitors, doorway handoff and cancellation. Each test must check actual identity and reachable position, not merely actor count or a passing schedule query.

The loaded movement fix alone does not complete this requirement. Household-history work remains deferred until this regression has been addressed.

## Implementation checkpoint

ObservedVisit now stores copied remaining legs, dwell, original completion time and return-home deadline. Version 19 saves accept the additive optional ledger. The runtime captures before native transitions and saves, restores at the analytically advanced interior position, and removes the body only after physical doorway completion. Local schedules and foreign trip queries consult the continuation. Displacement from a reached activity pauses its dwell and requests a route back.

Core suite passes 553/553 and Unity build/integration succeeds. Native serialization and same-door exit/reentry tests are now running; these changes are not yet accepted as a complete fix. Missing activity routes, missed opening windows after delays, and overnight-room movement remain separate unresolved cases. The native test must also verify that no stale scene objects or fixture schedules invalidate its evidence.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-16-road-inn-stops-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-road-inn-stops.md
