# Intermediate road inn stops

**Goal:** Long journeys stop at real intermediate inns, then continue to the original destination and finally home with the same resident identity.

**Architecture:** Keep one resident-owned trip. Add ordered stops along its surveyed road, each with a settlement and a normal local inn visit. Derive absolute road/visit timing together. A planned visit does not pay for a room: existing keeper, inventory, crime and first-come admission rules remain authoritative.

**Constraints:** One resident per bed; children do not travel. No duplicate home resident, invented beds, teleporting or road shortcuts. Existing saves retain their original timing when no road stops exist. Use the normal mod API and existing native serializer. Implement in this worktree without agents or unrelated cleanup.

## Evidence and affected boundaries

16 October remote intermediate inn reception preparation now surveys actual keeper/counter geometry, registers real room inventory and permanent occupants, reconciles existing rentals, rejects unavailable keepers, and binds outbound/return receptions without advance payment. Unity build and CrossRegionTripProbe pass (`work/RoadKeeperPlan-build.log`, `work/RoadKeeperPlan-native-pass.log`), including real inn 65792 in settlement 135420502 and native serialization. This verifies planning and inventory, not rendered intermediate admission or death/refusal in this new helper. Automatic stop selection remains disabled. User requested shifting effort from prolonged inn detail work toward major unfinished gameplay systems; prioritize complete playable features over further isolated travel refinements.

16 October scoped reception planning added in ResidentOvernightPlan.WithSettlementReceptions. It binds every matching outbound/return inn visit to one keeper and copied counter route while retaining existing destination receptions. Duplicate preparation, absent inn scopes and processed itineraries are rejected; planning creates no paid stay. Core 497/497 and Unity build/integration checks pass (`work/RoadReceptionPlan-core.log`, `work/RoadReceptionPlan-build.log`; failing pre-implementation fixture in `work/RoadReceptionPlan-red.log`). This is a core planning API only; remote keeper/inventory preparation and native rendered acceptance are still required before automatic intermediate stop activation.

16 October intermediate visitor crime identity corrected. AlarmResident now recognizes every settlement in the resident trip, while new violence still checks actual presence; entry witnesses use evaluated settlement/phase instead of the primary destination window. Core 496/496, Unity build and native real-passage crime/serialization checks pass (`work/RoadStopCrime-red.log`, `work/RoadStopCrime-core.log`, `work/RoadStopCrime-build.log`, `work/RoadStopCrime-native-pass.log`). Tests preserve the original resident death at home, the intermediate incident location and historical entry evidence, and reject new entry reports outside the visit. Runtime destination actors/inn placement already query evaluated settlement in the inspected paths. This does not prove rendered intermediate crime/pursuit; automatic stop selection, terrain checks and visual acceptance remain.

16 October ResidentRoadPassage.Attach inserts real surveyed entry/exit joins into an unprocessed trip, adds paired outbound/return inn visits, remaps existing stop indices while retaining IDs, reverses local/gate geometry for return, and retimes planned destination room holds/receptions. Joins must lie on the original adjacent road edges; overlapping passages and already processed trips are rejected. Fractional interior road points require matching forward/reverse passage ranges and integer-grid exterior legs. Core 494/494, Unity build and native real-route insertion pass (`work/RoadPassagePlan-red.log`, `work/RoadPassagePlan-core.log`, `work/RoadPassagePlan-build.log`, `work/RoadPassagePlan-native-pass.log`). The native fixture uses the surveyed intermediate inn on the 78-node cross-region journey, preserves original resident/home absence and existing destination appointments through serialization, and leaves the source plan untouched. Return dwell is explicitly supplied by the caller. Automatic time-of-day selection, terrain/obstacle checks, intermediate actor/crime ownership, keeper preparation and rendered movement remain before activation.

16 October real intermediate inn geometry survey implemented in ResidentRoadInnSurvey. It surveys incoming/outgoing accesses along the actual neighboring road edges, tries at most eight inn doors and four accesses per side, and joins two model-clear routes at the same inn door. The resulting ThroughRoute carries distinct fractional road joins and correct entry/exit gate timing without creating people or booking rooms. Core 492/492, Unity build and native cross-region probe pass (`work/RoadInnSurvey-red.log`, `work/RoadInnSurvey-core.log`, `work/RoadInnSurvey-build.log`, `work/RoadInnSurvey-native-pass.log`). Native evidence: settlement 135420502, inn 65792, 45 incoming points and 158 outgoing points. This proves model geometry only; terrain/obstacle validation, road-list insertion and reverse mapping, interior reception preparation, rendered actor transitions and automatic planning remain pending.

16 October schema 17 adds through-town road passages: a stop can carry ExitRoadNode and a ThroughRoute local visit. Its street/inn duration replaces the covered regional segments; travel resumes at the exit join instead of crossing the settlement center on the raw regional road. Ranges cannot overlap, primary destinations must still return to their join, and schema 16 single-join trips retain timing. Core 492/492, Unity build/integration checks and native passage serialization/cross-region regression pass (`work/RoadPassage-red.log`, `work/RoadPassage-core.log`, `work/RoadPassage-build.log`, `work/RoadPassage-native-pass.log`). Native passage coverage is synthetic geometry; the real 78-node route still supplies eight surveyed inn settlements as candidates only. Production activation still requires generating/validating both joins, local routes and rendered actor transitions, including reverse trips and refusal.

16 October real-road settlement lookup verified: RoadSettlementIndex indexes map metadata once per instance and returns copied candidates in actual road-node order, excluding home/destination and off-road towns without rounding fractional points. Core 490/490 and Unity build pass. Native CrossRegionTripProbe surveyed eight intermediate settlements on its 78-node route; all eight contain inns, and surveyed map pixels match the route (`work/RoadSettlementIndex-red.log`, `work/RoadSettlementIndex-core.log`, `work/RoadSettlementIndex-build.log`, `work/RoadSettlementIndex-native-pass.log`). This is candidate discovery only, not inn selection/admission activation. Important next geometry requirement: existing local inn routes join fractional points outside town bounds, while intermediate timelines currently use one road node/same return join. Production must replace center-crossing regional segments with surveyed entry/exit/transit routes rather than sending actors through buildings.

16 October scoped road-stop exits are now persisted and consumed by ResidentTrip. Outbound and return exits retain interior coordinates/height, finish their local exit before road travel and recompute onward timings. Prepared-counter refusal at a full intermediate inn creates the exit and retimes future destination/return receptions by stable IDs. Primary exits filter road exits and retime return receptions too. Invalid rewrites of completed future visits are rejected; provisional refusal records are rolled back. Core 488/488, Unity build and native isolated full-inn refusal/serialization plus cross-region regression pass (`work/RoadStopExitSave-red.log`, `work/RoadStopRefusal-red.log`, `work/RoadStopExitSave-core.log`, `work/RoadStopExitSave-build.log`, `work/RoadStopExitSave-native-pass.log`). Production intermediate selection remains inactive. Runtime crime/actor ownership, actual surveyed intermediate inn rendering, extended room holds and performance still require acceptance.

16 October road inn timing now accepts scoped service-exit routes. It keeps the traveler at the road join while exposing the actual interior exit position/height, resumes local walking only after the exit completes, then derives later road/inn times. Tests cover copied route isolation, equal building keys, mismatched/duplicate scopes and an early departure that avoids the next overnight gate wait. Core 484/484 and Unity build/integration checks pass (`work/RoadStopExitTimeline-core.log`, `work/RoadStopExitTimeline-build.log`; missing-overload red fixture in `work/RoadStopExitTimeline-red.log`). This is the timing prerequisite only: ResidentTrip copying, service-exit persistence, reception retiming and prepared-keeper refusal still need to pass scoped exits into it before production activation.

16 October intermediate keeper approaches now resolve against the full outbound/primary/return itinerary using stable visit IDs and settlement identity. Equal building keys in three towns remain distinct; mismatched IDs/settlements are rejected before duplicate writes or restore, and absent metadata is inferred from matching itinerary entries. Core 480/480, Unity build/integration checks, isolated native reception serialization and cross-region historical-discovery regression pass (`work/RoadStopReception-red.log`, `work/RoadStopReception-core.log`, `work/RoadStopReception-build.log`, `work/RoadStopReception-native-pass.log`). This verifies planned reception persistence, not physical intermediate admission. Payment enumeration/stay validation and scoped refusal/exit retiming remain pending; existing primary-only retiming must be generalized before production stop activation.

16 October persisted road-stop timeline: schema 16 stores ordered outbound and return inn stops with stable outbound/<index> and return/<index> IDs. ResidentTrip evaluates their local visits, extends onward/return arrival, and exposes the original resident through destination lookup without a simultaneous road actor. Schema 15 trips without stops retain their previous timing; older schemas containing stops are rejected. Historical discovery currently rejects stop-bearing trips rather than inventing past admission. Core 479/479 and Unity build/integration checks pass (`work/RoadStopSave-core.log`, `work/RoadStopSave-build.log`). The isolated native serializer fixture verifies both stop scopes at year-scale game timestamps; it does not exercise real intermediate inn admission. Stop-specific reception, payment, refusal/exit retiming, crime ownership and production selection still require integration before activation.

16 October stable visit IDs implemented: evaluated/scheduled primary-destination visits use destination/<itinerary index>, scoped to the resident trip. Inn stays, receptions and service exits retain VisitId; legacy null IDs are inferred from a validated matching itinerary entry. Explicit conflicting IDs are rejected on restore and before duplicate-write shortcuts. Admission/reception lookup uses the stable ID plus settlement/building, while paid booking identifiers remain unchanged. A core test verifies an earlier exit moves a later inn visit from 430 to 345 seconds without changing destination/2. Core 475/475, Unity build, six native paid/refused ID migration checks across three inn layouts and all 38 broad traveler runtime checkpoints pass (work/VisitIds-red.log, work/VisitIds-core.log, work/VisitIds-build.log, work/VisitIds-native-pass.log, work/VisitIds-full-pass.log). IDs assume committed primary itinerary order remains fixed apart from truncating future visits; intermediate stop scopes, persisted road-stop lists and production activation remain pending.

16 October visit settlement identity prerequisite implemented: ResidentInnStay, ResidentInnReception and ResidentServiceExit now carry optional SettlementId. Trip validation resolves absent legacy fields to the existing destination and rejects a conflicting explicit settlement before authoritative state changes, including duplicate writes. New admissions and runtime reception/exit records bind the evaluated settlement; keeper, paid-bed and booking validation use that identity. Core 472/472, Unity build and all three native inn layouts pass six paid/refused settlement serialization and legacy-inference checks, alongside room sleeping, physical checkout, refusal exits and return-home behavior. Booking IDs are preserved. Evidence: work/VisitSettlement-red.log, work/VisitSettlement-core.log, work/VisitSettlement-build.log, work/VisitSettlement-native-pass.log. This is additive single-destination migration support, not activation of intermediate stops. Stable stop identity, persisted stop lists, timeline integration and production planning remain pending.

`ResidentTrips.cs` now composes optional intermediate stops into outbound and return timing. Stays, receptions and exits carry settlement identity, but their validation and admission enumeration still target the primary destination. Primary visit IDs use `destination/<itinerary index>`; intermediate snapshots use `<direction>/<stop index>/<visit index>`. Later primary visit IDs remain stable when earlier exits retime appointments. Payment identifiers retain their original timestamp text. Intermediate scoped admission and interruption must be connected before enabling road inns.

## Implementation sequence

Through-town representation added: `ResidentRoadStop.ExitRoadNode` optionally marks the regional exit join, and `ResidentDestination.ThroughRoute` allows the local exit to end there instead of returning to its entry. The road timeline replaces the covered regional segments with the local visit duration. Schema 17 is required for these stops; schema 16 single-join trips migrate unchanged. Stop ranges cannot overlap, primary destinations still return to their join, and this representation does not itself certify street or wilderness clearance. Next have the sliced survey generate and verify both joins and the local route.

Physical transit finding: `UnloadedSettlementSurvey.ReadRoadAccessSteps` derives a fractional road join outside a location rectangle from a road beginning at the settlement's integer map pixel. Current `ResidentRoadStop` timing instead stops at an existing node and requires a local return to the same join. Do not attach an inn visit to the center pixel and assume the following road segment is walkable: it may cross buildings. Production planning needs validated entry/exit joins and a through-settlement street route (or a surveyed bypass), with corresponding regional segment replacement and reverse-trip mapping. Candidate lookup alone does not solve this geometry.

Admission integration enumerates all scoped inn visits, validates stays against those visits and uses the matching room checkout. Prepared-reception refusal now persists an intermediate exit and recomputes future appointments by stable visit ID. Primary exits filter out road exits and also retime return-stop receptions. Completed future admissions/exits cannot be rewritten; failed refusal updates remove their provisional refusal record. Native isolated coverage passes, but actual surveyed intermediate inn actors, crime handling and route selection still need acceptance before production activation.

Next integration detail: review runtime actor/crime and survey ownership for intermediate settlements, then select actual road inns and prove rendered entry, counter service, sleep, rejection/exit and onward travel. Timing now recomputes gate waits rather than applying a constant offset. Primary refusal still truncates its abandoned primary itinerary; intermediate refusal continues along the already surveyed road after reaching the local road join. Extended room holds on intermediate stops and already-completed historical admissions need explicit coverage before those cases are generated.

- [x] Build and test a pure road/inn timeline in `ResidentRoadJourney.cs`. At each stop, keep world-map position at its road join while exposing local visit position separately. Derive later road times from actual visit completion, including gates. Test multiple towns, boundaries, copy isolation and reconstruction. This timing component alone does not admit guests or activate stops. Verified by core 469/469 and Unity build (`work/RoadInnTimeline-core.log`, `work/RoadInnTimeline-build.log`); missing API red evidence is `work/RoadInnTimeline-red.log`.
- [x] Extend persisted trip stops and their visit identity. Scope receptions, stays, exits and booking validation by settlement and stable stop identity. Missing fields in legacy single-destination saves resolve to the existing destination. Validate the complete candidate before changing authoritative state. Test equal building keys in different towns and native JSON precision. Verified incrementally through `RoadStopExitSave`; production route selection remains separate.
- [ ] Integrate the timeline into outbound/return evaluation and original-home absence. Update destination resident lookup, actor ownership, crime identity, bed lookup and all admission queues to use the evaluated stop's settlement. Preserve existing single-destination tests.
- [ ] Generalize refusal/interruption: leave the actual inn through its surveyed exit; continue or return over surveyed road segments. Never retain invalid future appointments or replay paid historical admission. Test deaths, capacity loss, unavailable keepers and save/load during a stop.
- [ ] Select intermediate inn settlements from the actual road network before dusk. Prepare real road joins, local entrance/counter/bed/exit routes in slices. Do not silently substitute an unreachable inn or book in advance. Preserve sparse demand and original visit purpose.
- [ ] Enable production planning only after native tests show the original traveler entering an intermediate inn, paying, sleeping, leaving, reaching the intended city and returning home. Repeat with an unavailable inn, closed gates and a loaded home settlement. Profile the additional survey work with automatic planning enabled.

## Acceptance

The shared admission changes also pass the complete ResidentTripProbe regression: 38 runtime pass markers, terminal exit 0 (`work/RoadStopAdmission-full-pass.log`). Coverage includes keeper rejection, overnight capacity, original identity, road actor reconstruction/transfers, destination save continuity and surveyed interior service exit. This is regression evidence for existing trips, not proof of physical intermediate stop refusal.

Latest native result: `work/RoadStopSave-native-pass.log` passes the isolated schema-16 stop fixture and existing cross-region/historical-discovery regression, terminal exit 0. This verifies serialization and logical presence only, not intermediate inn payment or rendered entry.

Use actual Daggerfall-area road connections, one original reserved resident, at least two settlement visits and a home return. Verify identity, no duplicate body/home presence, first-come beds, gate timing, arrival/checkout continuity and native save/load in each phase. Keep broad traveler, guard pursuit and performance regressions green. Do not describe intermediate stops as implemented until production planning and native actors use them.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-16-unresolved-murder-witnesses-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-unresolved-murder-witnesses.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Persistent witness fear until crime resolution

Requirement: witnesses of the player's killing of an innocent resident flee and refuse dialogue until that crime resolves. Witnessed partner/child killing retains the separate lasting grudge. Do not attribute hostile or NPC-caused killings to the player.

Verified gap: generated resident alarms expire with their temporary incident/report window, allowing ordinary witnesses to talk again while Murder remains outstanding. Adult reaction selection can also choose Fight. Existing dialogue gates use the active alarm or family grudge. Generic `RecordViolence` has neither explicit offender attribution nor a resolution marker.

Implementation plan:

1. Add explicit saved player attribution from player-hit paths only (`StreetPopulation.BeforeWeaponHit`, keeper hit handling). Leave neutral `RecordViolence` callers neutral; `ThreatId == null` is not evidence of player guilt.
2. Persist an accusation-issued marker per incident when that incident dispatches the Murder charge. Keep direct witness fear before reporting and past temporary alarm expiry.
3. Clear this response only after accusation was issued and native crime state clears. `CrimeCommitted == None` before a delayed witness reaches a guard must never clear fear. Pursuit/search expiry is not crime resolution. Review fine, prison, save/load and native-charge paths.
4. Gate player dialogue and ambient chatting consistently. Ordinary direct witnesses flee; lasting family hostility remains separate. Preserve bounded warning spread.
5. Test neutral/hostile deaths, delayed reporting with no current charge, report and persistent Murder, reload in each phase, and post-resolution return to normal. Add a native test including actual reporting and legal resolution where feasible.

Implementation checkpoint: schema 19 now saves explicit player attribution, accusation and resolution state. Direct and warned ordinary witnesses retain fear beyond the temporary alarm window. A missing native crime before reporting does not clear fear; clearing an issued accusation does. Native keeper responses clear immediately after resolution. Core regression suite passes 518/518. Unity build and native isolated-store adapter/save-load checks pass (`work/WitnessPersistence-performance-pass.log`).

Local DFU court code and the existing guard integration remain authoritative for event wiring. No universal fine/prison completion callback is used: runtime checks the native crime state only after game readiness and only resolves incidents whose accusation was issued. The planned native probe checks adapter state and FullSerializer roundtrips on an isolated store; it does not enact a courtroom sentence or prove every regional crime transition.

<a id="source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-17-consolidated-delivery-plan-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-17-consolidated-delivery-plan.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# NPC lifecycle and quest compatibility delivery plan

> **For agentic workers:** Use the `executing-plans` skill for a selected implementation slice. This is a planning backlog, not authorization to execute all phases. Delegation requires user authorization and the configured Luna/high ceiling.

**Goal:** Turn the NPC inventory and accumulated requests into independently reviewable deliveries without losing native quests, identity or physical continuity.

**Architecture:** Stable identity and native ownership underpin role-specific mobility. Housing reservations precede generated population; quest capabilities and information disclosure are explicit. Unloaded simulation remains bounded and deterministic where possible.

**Tech stack:** DFU 1.1.1, Unity 2019.4.40f1, C# 7.3 runtime adapters; .NET 8 core tests; existing PowerShell build and owned-player probes; optional local llama.cpp server.

**Spec:** [NPC lifecycle and quest-dialogue design](ROADMAP.md#detailed-design). Read alongside the [TL;DR roadmap](ROADMAP.md) and [current inventory](NPC-SYSTEMS.md#inventory).

## Scope and execution gates

17 October 2026: all new role, companion and quest designs remain future work. The user subsequently authorized **only the 2× game-clock fix and rebuild** as immediate code work. That bounded exception does not authorize the other phases.

This document selects deliverables and acceptance criteria. Before implementing a phase, write its narrow technical plan against then-current code, including concrete tests and interfaces. Do not invent a universal actor API now or turn the whole backlog into one patch.

For each selected slice:

- [ ] Record current behavior, a failing regression or reproducible native case, and the exact save/mod combination.
- [ ] Implement only that slice, preserving original identifiers and native quest/service ownership.
- [ ] Run relevant core checks and runtime compilation, then an owned-player probe where physical/native behavior is involved.
- [ ] Compare save/load continuity and frame-time cost where relevant; state what was not tested.
- [ ] Update evidence/status documents and make a reviewable commit. Separate planning acceptance from gameplay acceptance.

## 0. Stabilization before expanding actor coverage — B01–B04

**Existing touchpoints:** `mod/Scripts/StreetPopulation.DailyItinerary.cs`, `mod/Scripts/InteriorRoomFollower.cs`, `mod/Scripts/LivingCitiesMod.DialogueLife.cs`, `mod/Scripts/RuntimeTiming.cs`, `src/LivingCities.Core/AdultDailyItinerary.cs`, `mod/Scripts/DailyItineraryProbe.cs`, `tests/LivingCities.Tests`.

- [ ] Deliver the separately authorized 2× clock: normal elapsed game time advances two seconds per unpaused real second, including new/load game lifecycle. Preserve pause, rest/wait and fast-travel intentional time advances. Audit schedule conversion constants and saved timers; validate native elapsed time, not just a configuration value.
- [ ] Trace one resident across home → shop → inn → home with stable ID, actual location, current activity and next destination. Confirm dialogue uses actual progress rather than a nominal schedule that already moved ahead.
- [ ] Complete the existing door/arrival regression: navigate physically to a valid entrance, transition once, leave via the real exit, resume outside without teleport or duplicated actor after reload.
- [ ] Reproduce and correct blocked inn room routes and unintended shelf/staff-area destinations. Verify standing pose while idle, stable facing and no door oscillation.
- [ ] Measure guard updates and inn surveys separately; bound per-frame work and stagger independent work without changing deadlines or freezing identities. Save before/after frame-time samples.

**Acceptance:** same tracked person, right named destination, real doorway arrival, accurate dialogue, no observed freeze/disappearance; explicit timing evidence. Passing a clock probe alone does not accept all movement work.

## 1. Read-only actor census — I01

**Existing touchpoints:** `mod/Scripts/HouseholdOccupants.cs`, `mod/Scripts/TavernRoomSurvey.cs`, `mod/Scripts/QuestOfferBindings.cs`, `mod/Scripts/KnownQuestBindings.cs`, `src/LivingCities.Core/QuestBindings.cs`; evidence in `docs/NPC-INTEGRATION-AUDIT.md`.

- [ ] Survey vanilla and installed-mod exterior/interior actors incrementally after providers finish creating them.
- [ ] Produce a diagnostic inventory containing stable source/identity, appearance provider, role, building, native quest binding, service role, current bed/home claim and adoption/exclusion reason.
- [ ] Compare Daggerfall market/castle/guild/temple/shop/inn examples with a village and a walled/non-walled location. Include Quest Pack actors when active.
- [ ] Re-enter/reload/re-survey and prove inventory entries remain stable with no duplicate identities or gameplay mutation.

**Acceptance:** inspectable actor census and explicit unknown cases. No movement, replacement, mortality or housing assignment in this slice. Its report determines adapter work instead of assuming all flats are interchangeable.

## 2. Reservation and identity foundations — I02–I03, H01, H04

**Existing touchpoints:** `src/LivingCities.Core/HouseholdGeneration.cs`, `src/LivingCities.Core/HouseholdRecoveryReservation.cs`, `mod/Scripts/HouseholdOccupants.cs`, `mod/Scripts/TavernRoomSurvey.cs` and existing traveler/save models located through the census.

- [ ] Define a versioned claim ledger keyed by persistent person and actual dwelling/bed identity. Preserve saved residents and traveler hometown claims before assigning new capacity.
- [ ] Reserve authored upstairs tenants and their real bedrooms; reserve local stall workers and off-site shopkeepers before generating remaining households.
- [ ] Handle already-full saved locations explicitly: retain people and their homes, record unmet capacity, and use a reviewed relocation/expansion policy. Never silently evict or regenerate saved families.
- [ ] Test migration, provider load order, duplicate appearances with different identities, absent/dead owners, changed interior layouts and repeat surveys.

**Acceptance:** one identity across representations; one valid claim per slot; every unassigned person has a recorded reason; generation respects existing claims. No forced home invented where capacity is absent.

## 3. Separate mobility deliveries by role — H02–H08

**Existing touchpoints:** `src/LivingCities.Core/AdultDailyItinerary.cs`, `mod/Scripts/StreetPopulation.DailyItinerary.cs`, `mod/Scripts/HouseholdDoors.cs`, `mod/Scripts/HouseholdPropertyKeyItem.cs`, `mod/Scripts/NativeTavernRentals.cs`, `src/LivingCities.Core/TravelerRoomWalk.cs`; native service hooks in `src/LivingCities.QuestHooks`.

Deliver each row separately, with save/load and player-observed route tests:

| Slice | Deliverable | Required native acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Local stall workers | Assigned home, slightly varied work commute and native stall service | Home → stall → home; no service loss, clone, or independent duplicate at home |
| Market demand | Residents visit real active stalls and still visit indoor shops; bounded late weekend opening | Multiple stalls attract natural spread; finite closing despite lingering shoppers; correct game-day calendar |
| Shop tenants | Upstairs residents retain beds, leave/return independently and carry scoped keys | Return after closing; key permits correct door; looted key persists; no access to unrelated shop |
| Shopkeepers | Free shop bed or assigned off-site home; closing/locking and opening/unlocking | Return before service hours, usual shop post, tenant claims untouched; native services/quest hooks still work |
| Inn visitors | Authored nonquest bedroom occupants gain stable outside origin/home, reason, itinerary and return journey | Day outing, evening room return, checkout and physical departure; no simultaneous home/inn clone |

**Acceptance:** observed physical travel and actual schedule state agree. Do not convert quest actors through generic role inference. Recheck quest ownership before a journey starts.

## 4. Bed capacity, staff and appearance — H09–H10, A01–A02

**Existing touchpoints:** `mod/Scripts/TavernRoomSurvey.cs`, `mod/Scripts/HouseholdOccupants.cs`, `docs/DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md`, `mod/Textures`, `art`.

- [ ] Keep one-per-bed as the general rule. Add explicit two-slot capacity only for suitable beds serving two authored ordinary inn-room occupants; retain both identities and claims.
- [ ] Inspect source/replacement sprites first. Prototype a shared blanket with two appearance-specific heads and validate bed alignment, one occupant waking/leaving/dying and correct selection/collision.
- [ ] Inventory actual idle/walk/run/sleep coverage by provider before generating missing art. Never substitute an unrelated face or imply all variants already exist.
- [ ] Assign ordinary institutional staff reachable beds where available; return to native work posts. No new off-site staff routine is required. Quest-owned staff use the later capability gate even within one building.

**Acceptance:** no doubled global housing capacity, no stacked single-person sleepers, no loss of services, no invisible/incorrect animation. More than two occupants or unsuitable beds remain explicit capacity exceptions requiring another decision.

## 5. Story-aware context and persistent memory — Q02, Q04–Q05, M01–M03

**Existing touchpoints:** `mod/Scripts/LivingCitiesMod.Dialogue.cs`, `mod/Scripts/LivingCitiesMod.DialogueLife.cs`, `src/LivingCities.Core/LocalDialogue.cs`, `src/LivingCities.Core/DialogueHistoryWindow.cs`, `mod/Scripts/LocalDialogueClient.cs`, native conversation hooks.

- [ ] Separate authored story persona from randomized ordinary personality. Mood changes delivery without changing hidden intent, allegiance or reveal permissions.
- [ ] Create a speaker/stage disclosure projection using native quest events and permitted leads. Never submit complete raw quest scripts, hidden identity facts or future outcomes to the social model.
- [ ] Test questions before/after a reveal, direct guesses, indirect requests, another speaker's knowledge, save rollback and remembered false claims. Sensitive quest answers must come from native/approved stage content or a validated rewrite because models may already know the plot.
- [ ] Add per-person dated memory with participants, locations, actual actions/results, certainty and source. Retrieve relevant memories after disclosure filtering; retain recent exact conversation and current authoritative context.
- [ ] Verify promised versus completed trips, past-season recollections, social history and perceived same-room events. Avoid global omniscience and broad canned refusals that destroy ordinary roleplay.

**Acceptance:** new legitimate leads remain available when native gates allow them; unrevealed facts stay protected; ordinary chat remains free-form and in character. Memory must not resurrect withheld facts through summaries, caches or other speakers.

## 6. Native quest capabilities; Quest Pack implementation deferred — Q01, Q03, Q06–Q07

**Existing touchpoints:** `src/LivingCities.Core/QuestBindings.cs`, `mod/Scripts/QuestOfferBindings.cs`, `mod/Scripts/KnownQuestBindings.cs`, `src/LivingCities.QuestHooks/NativeQuestOfferHooks.cs`, `tools/QuestSurvey.ps1`.

- [ ] Build one review record per quest-role-stage: source version/hash, original person/foe identity, placement/hide/kill/service/timer actions, permitted capabilities, bed/return anchor, public cover story, allowed new leads, withheld facts, reveal gates and acceptance saves.
- [ ] Start with an ordinary vanilla quest giver and recipient. Prove native accept → progress → reward/failure behavior remains authoritative before enabling optional same-building sleep.
- [ ] Cover dynamic hostility/transformation, aliases/captives, repeated timed placement and multi-actor scenes. Same-building movement alone is not sufficient evidence.
- [ ] Expand vanilla coverage incrementally. Unsupported actors keep native placement/actions; restrict sensitive plot discussion without disabling all harmless social chat.
- [ ] Keep Quest Pack 1 mobility in the backlog. Use `JHDB121a`, `JHDB109`, `JHCG003` and `JHKN119` as initial representative review records, then classify the remaining scripts individually. Source sampling is not certification of all 302 quests.

**Acceptance:** quest success, failure, rewards, staged hostility and reload match native semantics. Each adapter lists precisely enabled capabilities and tested versions. User-requested mortality policy stays distinct from accidental quest breakage.

## 7. Player installation and modular packaging — P01–P04

**Existing touchpoints:** `README.md`, `docs/INSTALLATION.md`, `tools/StartLocalDialogue.ps1`, `mod/Scripts/LivingCitiesMod.Dialogue.cs`, mod settings and existing feature switches.

- [ ] Publish a separately verified playable bundle with exact tested DFU/dependency versions and file hashes; source checkout alone is not an installer.
- [ ] Make no-LLM installation complete without model/runtime/CUDA downloads or background launch. Verify settings and save compatibility across module toggles.
- [ ] Add an optional prebuilt llama.cpp bootstrap with explicit download consent, pinned official assets/checksums, partial-download recovery, health checks and useful errors. Prefer PowerShell/native packaging over an unnecessary Python dependency.
- [ ] Measure combined game/model VRAM and RAM on supported configurations. Publish a tested minimum only after measurement; CPU/AMD/other platforms remain untested until verified.

**Acceptance:** clean-machine installation, optional dialogue works, no-LLM path works, update/removal does not damage saves or unrelated files. Broader guard/recovery/commerce modules each require their own toggle lifecycle tests.

## Shelved extension: invitations and companions — F01–F06

After foundations, split into: social invitation/temporary schedule override; durable human stats/effects; inventory/equipment/gold; nonquest recruitment and payment; used-skill progression; validated world-action adapters. Each gets a separate technical plan and acceptance boundary.

Initial action examples remain thief lockpicking and fighter door bashing, followed by combat/buff support, stealing, climbing, ranged cover and item carrying/exchange. Commands must resolve a real target, check consent/resources/quest restrictions, execute physically, and record actual results. Quest NPCs remain excluded. Shared activity and travel memories are prerequisites for convincing long-term companions.

No companion, sprite-generation or exhaustive quest implementation is authorized by this planning document.

## Original: superpowers/plans/2026-10-17-grounded-conversation.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Grounded conversation implementation plan

**Goal:** Finish the approved free-text conversation flow with native topic knowledge, validated map requests and persistent individual disposition.

**Architecture:** A bounded local-model classifier selects a supplied native topic ID, conversational intent and tone. The host resolves only real topics through TalkManager, which retains knowledge and quest rules. Model text cannot supply map coordinates or numeric disposition changes. Unknown/ambiguous facts produce clarification. Facts use native text; social chat uses the existing local model. No vector database or engine-source modifications.

**Tech stack:** C# 7.3, DFU native UI/TalkManager, llama.cpp localhost, existing world-state persistence.

User authorized implementation on 17 October after reviewing native retrieval/map-action approach. Routine choices proceed without another approval round.

- [x] Reproduce shortcut propagation, enable focused-text hotkey suppression, verify native dispatch restores after blur.
- [x] Add bounded topic selection/classification and regressions for invalid IDs, unknown topics, injection and explicit-only map intent.
- [x] Persist per-NPC disposition with neutral=0, polite=+1, rude=-2, insult=-8. Cap goodwill and repeated-line farming; migrate older saves without changing identities.
- [x] Connect native topic trees, unknown/group clarification, current-subject map follow-up and native knowledge/reveal checks. Keep exact factual answers canonical.
- [x] Add user settings for conversation effects and map requests; document limits and supersede display-only rule only for these validated actions.
- [x] Build, run core regressions and targeted live conversation tests covering focus, native locations, map discovery, disposition and model response.
- [x] Record evidence and hand back a restart/test instruction.

Evidence: core 605/605; `work/GroundedConversation-native-pass.log`; `work/GroundedConversation-build-pass.log`. Native checks include a map follow-up after an intervening left-hand topic response, quoted-insult neutrality and reaction-tier changes. No player save was overwritten.

## Original: superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-living-cities-design.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Daggerfall Unity NPC mod workspace

Approved by the user on 13 October 2026. This design covers a standard Daggerfall Unity mod with persistent residents, daily activities, finite guards, civilian stealth, and optional local language-model dialogue. The baseline sections preserve setup evidence at design time. Current implementation status and acceptance evidence live in `docs/MILESTONES.md` in the LivingCities repository.

User addition on 13 October 2026: provide a configurable mod menu with meaningful controls for implemented subsystems. After stable vanilla gameplay, add compatibility patches for city overhauls, added market stalls/buildings, and currently non-interactable vendor NPCs. Detailed constraints and sequencing are in `docs/SETTINGS-AND-COMPATIBILITY.md`.

## Setup as of 13 October 2026

- Workspace: `C:/Users/Daniel/Desktop/Code_Experiments/DFU`.
- `daggerfall-unity/`: official source at `v1.1.1-cve-2025`, commit `fb2627082fabe473bf108c816263b71c6e26ace6`.
- The pinned release declares Unity `2019.4.40f1`, matching `C:/Program Files/Unity/Hub/Editor/2019.4.40f1/Editor/Unity.exe`. Current upstream master declares a newer editor; do not accidentally upgrade this checkout by following master instructions.
- `GameFiles/arena2/`: original game assets from the archive linked by the official installation guide. ZIP CRC verification passed for all 1,688 entries. There are 472 texture files, 17 videos, and the required ARCH3D.BSA, BLOCKS.BSA, MAPS.BSA, DAGGER.SND, and WOODS.WLD files.
- Game archive SHA-256: `8be67a4ebb76ed684968f49d29cbbe57b0c10dc4554d111d64356a2355be5083`. This is a locally measured fingerprint, not an independently published checksum.
- `references/dfunity-mods/`: Hazelnut's source, commit `0af2ec99b5ad7e6722ddc9ce284987d4d6fa262d`. Basic Roads 1.3.1 is copied into `daggerfall-unity/Assets/Game/Mods/BasicRoads/`. This folder is ignored by the upstream repository. The mod was built locally and installed as `Player/DaggerfallUnity_Data/StreamingAssets/Mods/basicroads.dfmod` (112,355 bytes). Unity reopened the bundle, confirmed all 17 assets, and checked all four 500,000-byte path maps.
- `Player/`: official patched Windows x64 DFU 1.1.1 release. The archive matched GitHub's published SHA-256 `b4eb2620cdf31f973ed1105d06be8a31f21796915d65c253a93eeb02cb1d64bd` and passed CRC verification for all 642 entries.
- Unity activation initially blocked batch import. After the user activated the license, import and compilation succeeded with exit code 0. The Basic Roads build also succeeded with exit code 0. Evidence: `work/unity-import-activated.log`, `work/basicroads-build.log`, and `work/baseline-build-result.json`.
- A standalone headless startup loaded Basic Roads, logged `Finished mod init: BasicRoads`, and reached `GameManager ready`. Evidence: `work/player-startup-verified.log`. This validates startup and mod initialization, not visible road rendering or an actual playthrough. The temporary test process was stopped after verification.
- Existing installation `D:/Code_Experiments/DFU` is preserved. It contains source, a player, and original assets. Unity Hub instead references the nonexistent path `D:/Code Experiments/DFU/daggerfall-unity` with a space. Old global editor-mod settings mention a Persistent NPCs mod, but no matching source or package was found in the inspected old mod directories.

The old and new editors ordinarily share DFU's user settings and saves. Use a separate test save. The new standalone player uses DFU's supported `Portable.txt` mode, and its settings point to the new `GameFiles` folder. Its settings and saves remain in `Player/PortableAppdata`. `Open Unity Project.lnk` opens the new source project with the matching editor; `Play DFU.lnk` opens the new standalone player.

The local setup helper is preserved at `tools/DFUBaselineSetup.cs` and copied into `Assets/Game/Mods/DFUSetup/Editor/DFUBaselineSetup.cs`. It packages unchanged upstream Basic Roads source using DFU's source-as-text convention and does not implement any NPC gameplay. To rebuild, run the installed editor in batch mode with `-projectPath` pointing to this source checkout and `-executeMethod DFUBaselineSetup.BuildBasicRoads`. The upstream repository ignores the Mods folder. Unity-generated localization changes were recorded in `work/unity-import-generated.patch` and reverted to retain a clean upstream baseline.

## Confirmed preferences

Use the standard mod format and preserve technical quest bindings and save integrity. Engine changes are allowed only when a concrete integration limitation proves them necessary. Keep classic sprites and add action poses or animations where needed. Ordinary resident and guard deaths persist, with bounded, gradual recruitment and migration. The 14 October amendment allows the player to kill important quest NPCs and break their quests; separate that from default protection against background events. Very small hamlets have no garrison; small towns have a handful of guards; cities have larger rosters. Later crime, recovery, public-warning and family refinements are authoritative in `docs/MILESTONES.md` and its linked specifications.

## Proposed architecture

### Persistent people and households

Give each resident a stable identity derived from settlement, building, and source identity or a deterministic generated slot. Separate that identity from Unity GameObjects and pooled renderers. Amendment on 14 October: the original maximum of three is superseded by up to five for family households (two adults and three children), with most households smaller. Beds and house size are soft allocation preferences: couples and children may share beds. Shopkeepers without living quarters commute to their assigned homes after closing. Household residents share a persistent surname and have explicit family roles. Children stay home or play locally with other children; they do not work, shop, visit taverns, or travel between settlements. See `docs/RELATIONSHIPS-AND-FUTURE-INVENTORIES.md` for current decisions. Visitors do not become household members. Shop staff and existing static NPCs retain their identities, services, factions, and quest bindings. Barracks are a separate garrison facility rather than a residential household.

Use versioned mod save data for the world seed, identity assignments, deaths, recruitment, witnessed incidents, relationships, schedule interruptions, and migrations. Never resurrect or duplicate a resident when a cell or scene reloads. Keep future migrations deterministic and capped by household capacity; recruit guards against roster vacancies rather than spawning them in response to crimes.

### Schedules and continuous time

Construct daily schedules from game time, occupation, service hours, sleep, and seeded preferences. Plans include destination reservations and travel time. Reconstruct unobserved progress analytically from absolute game time and saved interruptions instead of simulating every distant NPC every frame. Rest, fast travel, save/load, midnight, and leaving and returning to a town must produce consistent results.

Priority is quest obligations and scripted events, then immediate threats, then service duties, then ordinary activities. If a quest needs a person at a particular place, preserve that contract. Mobile quest NPCs can follow compatible schedules. Stationary quest NPCs can sleep within their allowed location only when activation and quest behavior remain intact; use a conservative fallback otherwise.

### Movement and activity selection

Start with CPU pathfinding over DFU's city navigation data, augmented by a portal graph connecting streets, doors, interiors, stairs, and validated wall walkways. Cache routes and batch expensive work. Basic Roads provides the wilderness road graph. Its map-pixel connections are not sufficient for precise obstacle avoidance near the player.

Use player distance and visibility for actor activation, with hysteresis and nearby-cell lookahead. Continue virtual schedules beyond the physical actor radius. Geometry availability still constrains where an actor can physically walk. Preserve global coordinates across DFU's world-origin shifts and keep exterior/interior spaces separate.

Choose destinations using attraction weights and actual capacity: markets, shops, guilds, temples, castles, authorities, taverns, houses, gates, and walking routes. Quiet wall corners attract fewer people. The heatmap influences choices; it does not create extra people. Meetings reserve compatible times for both participants. Activities include work, shopping, visiting, conversation, eating, drinking, sitting, walking, and sleeping, with suitable sprites and anchors.

Do not assume every shop has an upstairs bedroom or every wall has a usable walkway. Discover valid anchors from actual layouts, author corrections for exceptions, and report missing anchors. Never place a sleeping sprite through furniture or invent an inaccessible wall patrol.

### Civilian awareness and burglary

Extend DFU's existing crouch and Stealth skill rather than creating a competing skill. Civilian detection combines distance, line of sight, movement/noise, light, skill, and alertness. Sleeping NPCs require stronger stimuli and wake through an explicit state transition. Walls and floors block sight; sound uses a separate bounded model. Detection accumulates over time without frame-rate-dependent random rolls. Shop schedules and door locks agree with existing opening hours and quest requirements.

### Finite guards and crime response

Build a bounded roster per settlement from settlement class, resident population, and validated garrison capacity. Tiny hamlets have zero guards. Small towns have a small contingent. Cities allocate their larger roster among rest, reserve, posts, and patrols. Final counts remain configurable and should be tuned against actual layouts.

Night patrols operate in pairs with torches. Posts cover markets, shops, gates, and accessible wall positions. A reported theft dispatches available nearby guards; each guard travels from its real or reconstructed position. Entry into a shop occurs at the actual arrival time. Off-duty guards take time to mobilize. An exhausted roster produces no instant reinforcements.

Use explicit states: duty, investigating, pursuing, searching the last known area, arrest/combat, returning, resting, fleeing, and dead. Losing line of sight stops exact player tracking. Searches expire and guards return to duty, but a regional criminal record is distinct from immediate pursuit. Preserve DFU's arrest, surrender, punishment, and quest semantics. Guards defend citizens and the player against actually hostile actors according to crime and faction rules.

Vanilla `PlayerEntity.SpawnCityGuards` and `SpawnCityGuard` are called directly from several systems. A feasibility test must prove that a normal mod can intercept all relevant requests, including quest calls, before this subsystem is promised as fully compatible. If it cannot, propose the smallest necessary engine hook. Spawning and immediately deleting vanilla guards is not an acceptable substitute.

### Nightlife and dangerous events

Fewer ordinary residents travel at night, often in small groups around taverns. Evening and nighttime tavern occupancy comes from scheduled residents, not a separate duplicate crowd. The later threat-response clarification uses attackers from vanilla quests and installed mods; this mod does not add a separate random-incursion spawner. Guards intervene against active hostile assassins, thieves, creatures and other attackers, including quest-owned enemies. Nearby citizens flee to safe interiors and report the threat. Lawful defense does not accuse the player of murder; existing crimes remain independently enforceable. Official scripted placement and technical quest bindings retain ownership.

While Daggerfall's haunting is active, civilians overwhelmingly stay indoors. Guards still patrol but flee recognized haunting threats toward reachable unlocked shelters or safe wall access. Do not treat every building or wall as a valid refuge. Read the relevant quest state so ordinary nightlife can return after the haunting is resolved. Do not replace or multiply the main quest's supernatural encounters.

### Optional local dialogue

Use an on-demand, loopback-only llama.cpp process with a quantized 7B instruction model as the initial candidate. Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct Q4_K_M is a reproducible starting point, subject to latency, memory, and dialogue-quality tests on the RTX 4070 SUPER. No model has been downloaded or benchmarked for this project yet.

DFU supplies the authoritative answer, known facts, disposition, social role, lore context, race/culture, gender, current activity, and a stable personality. The model changes wording and tone. It cannot grant rewards, create quests, alter schedules, change disposition, invent knowledge, or execute commands. Free-text input resolves supported topics or grounded small talk. Use bounded context, output checks, response caching, cancellation, and a timeout with the original DFU answer as fallback. Keep the existing topic list. Dialogue must work with the model disabled. Text only is the initial scope; voice is not required.

## Alternatives and tradeoffs

1. **Recommended: standard mod with deterministic simulation and CPU routes.** Best fit for compatibility, testing, and sharing GPU memory with local dialogue. Add a minimal engine hook only after demonstrating a missing interception point.
2. **GPU-first population simulation.** Potentially useful for very large active crowds, but introduces synchronization and tooling costs before a bottleneck is known. Defer until profiling demonstrates a need.
3. **Custom DFU fork.** Gives direct control over guard spawning and engine internals but adds ongoing engine-merge and distribution work. Keep as a fallback, consistent with the user's chosen compatibility target.

## Proposed milestones and acceptance checks

1. **Working baseline and integration probes:** import and compile pinned DFU, build and load Basic Roads, validate game startup, inspect quest-bound NPC movement and guard interception, and prepare an isolated test save.
2. **One-town population slice:** assign households; implement work, travel, and sleep; render persistent residents; save/load state. Verify household caps, no duplicates, no visible spawning, plausible arrival times, and identical progress after leave/return and time skips.
3. **Civilian awareness and activities:** burglary, wake-up behavior, doors, meetings, tavern groups, and sprite poses. Test reachable anchors, closed shops, quest accessibility, and sleeping detection.
4. **Finite guard service:** settlement-specific rosters, posts, patrol pairs, reinforcement travel, pursuit/search/return, hostility response, and bounded replacements. Test repeated crimes without roster growth, wall and interior routes, surrender, deaths, and escaped pursuit.
5. **Threat responses and haunting:** guard intervention and citizen flight for existing vanilla/quest/mod attackers, nightlife distribution, refuges, and the main-quest override. Test threat ownership, innocent-player handling, fear behavior, and haunting resolution.
6. **Local dialogue and broader rollout:** add free-text UI and model service, then test mixed towns and nearby settlements, profiler budgets, save migration, other mods, and fallback behavior.

Implementation is authorized across the seven milestones. Each subsystem receives focused design and integration tests. Current acceptance evidence lives in `docs/MILESTONES.md`; this approved design is not a claim that all gameplay or engine hooks are complete.

## Primary sources

- [DFU patched release](https://github.com/Interkarma/daggerfall-unity/releases/tag/v1.1.1-cve-2025)
- [Official installation guide and game archive](https://github.com/Interkarma/daggerfall-unity/wiki/Installing-Daggerfall-Unity-Cross-Platform)
- [Pinned DFU source](https://github.com/Interkarma/daggerfall-unity/tree/fb2627082fabe473bf108c816263b71c6e26ace6)
- [Basic Roads source](https://github.com/ajrb/dfunity-mods/tree/0af2ec99b5ad7e6722ddc9ce284987d4d6fa262d/BasicRoads)
- [Basic Roads release page](https://www.nexusmods.com/daggerfallunity/mods/134)
- [llama.cpp](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp)
- [Qwen2.5 7B GGUF model](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-GGUF)

Useful source entry points include `PopulationManager`, `CityNavigation`, `MobilePersonMotor`, `StaticNPC`, `IHasModSaveData`, `UIWindowFactory.RegisterCustomUIWindow`, `DaggerfallTalkWindow`, `FormulaHelper.CalculateStealthChance`, `PlayerEntity.SpawnCityGuards`, and Basic Roads' `getPathData`, `getRoadPoint`, `getTrackPoint`, and `getPathsPoint` messages.

<a id="source-superpowers-specs-2026-10-17-npc-lifecycle-and-quest-dialogue-design-md"></a>

## Original: superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-npc-lifecycle-and-quest-dialogue-design.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# NPC lifecycle, story-aware dialogue and future companions

Date: 17 October 2026. Status: **user requirements captured; architecture proposed for review; implementation deferred**.

This consolidates the user's latest decisions with [current NPC evidence](NPC-SYSTEMS.md#inventory), [additional quest evidence](COMPATIBILITY.md#quests), [relationships](HISTORY.md#source-relationships-and-future-inventories-md), and [dialogue/memory](HISTORY.md#source-future-dialogue-and-voice-md). The short navigation entry is [Roadmap](ROADMAP.md). Nothing below establishes that the feature already works.

## 1. Alternatives and recommendation

1. **Replace every NPC with a generic mobile citizen.** Fast apparent coverage, but loses quest/service identity, authored appearance and native visibility ownership. Reject.
2. **Keep every authored NPC static indefinitely.** Safest immediate quest behavior, but cannot deliver worker commutes, active lodgers or consistent housing. Use only as the fallback for unsupported roles/stages.
3. **One persistent identity with role-specific capabilities and native ownership adapters.** More foundation work, but allows ordinary citizens to move while quest/service controls remain authoritative. Recommended. Enable capabilities incrementally after tests; do not require every quest to be rewritten first.

The invariant is one person, one persistent life state, one physical representation when loaded. The same name or sprite is not sufficient proof of identity. Scene absence, unloaded simulation, quest hiding, sleep and death are distinct states.

## 2. Identity, roles and allocation

The census records effective mod/world-data provider, map/block/building/record identity, native hash/nameSeed, faction/service role, quest UID/symbol/individual identity, appearance provider/archive/record, workplace, known home, room/bed, activity and ownership. Include inactive authored objects and replacements; distinguish decorations from people. Never use Unity instance IDs for persistence.

Roles can change. A citizen can become quest-owned after accepting a quest; a quest can finish while the citizen is sleeping. Ownership is re-evaluated at lifecycle boundaries. Existing saved names, deaths, relationships, keys, possessions and traveler reservations survive adoption.

Allocation order for a new or completing settlement:

1. Preserve existing saved identities, occupied homes, player property, active rentals and quest placement constraints.
2. Survey authored bedroom tenants/lodgers and real beds; reserve explicit room occupancy before keeper allocation.
3. Reserve on-site staff/keeper beds where actually free and suitable.
4. Assign local workers who need off-site homes to remaining residential capacity, before generated families fill it.
5. Preserve reserved traveler hometown households and merge them by stable identity rather than generating another copy.
6. Generate remaining households only in remaining capacity.

Existing towns require a versioned migration, not a destructive re-roll. Lack of space is an explicit unresolved housing assignment; it must not evict a saved family or invent a bed. Proposed fallback is to retain native placement until a legitimate vacancy or reviewed migration policy provides housing. Never label a local worker a foreign visitor merely to avoid the capacity problem.

### Market workers and destinations — requirements H01–H03

Stall owners are locals with a home in the stall's settlement. Separate home/work keys and retain the authored service. They walk to work before opening and home after closing, with stable small variations by person/day. Use loaded geometry to locate market stalls and browsing/transaction positions; detection is capability-based for active city/village improvement mods, not a hardcoded town list.

Residents' multi-stop schedules may select multiple outdoor stalls, browse, buy and socialize around markets while retaining indoor shop visits. Crowd generation must use existing people, not duplicate visitors. Demand and navigable capacity influence destination choice.

Weekend market extensions depend on the in-game calendar, customers still present, weather/danger and worker availability. Use a bounded latest closing time and a sustained-demand window to prevent a single late NPC from keeping the market open forever. Exact calendar-day mapping and balance values remain explicit design choices for that implementation slice; do not silently import a real-world Saturday/Sunday convention. Dialogue must report actual opening/closing state.

### Shopkeepers and upstairs tenants — H04–H06

Classify authored upstairs bedroom occupants as tenants unless quest/staff evidence overrides that classification. Reserve their actual beds first. Tenants receive household membership, full identity, daily activities and a key granting access through the shop after closing. They are not automatically the keeper's relatives or employees.

A keeper with a legitimate unoccupied on-site bed can live at the shop. Otherwise assign an off-site household. Closing behavior walks to the exit, locks the appropriate public entrance, returns home, sleeps, returns before opening, unlocks and resumes the native service position. No teleporting when the schedule deadline passes.

Tenant access must not reopen public commerce or unlock all internal rooms. Key permissions distinguish public business entrance, tenant residence and private areas where applicable. A killed tenant's genuine key becomes corpse loot once, retains building scope across saves, and permits the player's nighttime entry without granting ownership of merchandise. Native crime/quest door overrides retain priority. No permanent dead-person duplicate may deliver another key.

### Authored inn occupants — H07–H09

Ordinary non-quest bedroom occupants become travelers staying in that inn, not permanent immobile decorations. Assign a stable home outside the destination settlement and reserve that person in the origin population before it is fully generated. Prefer actual supported settlements; a proposed origin beyond the simulated map must be an explicit abstract origin, not a fabricated visitable house.

Give each traveler a reason for visiting: official business, services, pilgrimage, trade, or visiting established relatives. A matching surname may support a family visit but does not prove a relationship; create or validate an explicit relationship. Preserve authored/fixed names. Travel parties can share a booking without being spouses.

Plan local daytime visits, evening return, room use, morning checkout when the visit ends, and physical travel home. Multi-day stays are allowed. Staff/innkeepers and quest actors are not converted into transient tourists by this rule. Common-room NPCs require classification rather than blanket bedroom treatment.

**Shared-bed amendment:** when an authored inn room contains two ordinary occupants but one suitable bed, support one bed with two explicit occupant claims. This narrowly supersedes the earlier one-NPC-per-bed rule for this case. Normal generated households and other beds remain one-per-bed unless a later explicit policy changes them. More than two occupants are a capacity conflict, not permission for unlimited sharing.

Proposed art uses one shared blanket/pillow composition with two independently identified head layers, avoiding every possible paired sprite sheet. Preserve individual faces, bed-axis alignment, pixel proportions, wake/death state, hit selection and room booking. One occupant waking or dying must not remove or kill the other. Shared-bed occupancy does not create a relationship. This is an art/renderer proposal, not an accepted generated asset.

### Guild, temple and castle staff — H10

Ordinary staff keep their work inside the institution; new external daily routines are not required now. Native service/faction purpose supplies background. Add a stable personality and context-sensitive mood consistent with that role. If reachable beds exist, reserve them and implement bedside sleep/return without removing service identity. Do not assume every guild, temple or castle has beds. Quest-owned staff follow section 3 first.

## 3. Quest mobility and dialogue — Q01–Q07

### Preservation, not universal household binding

The current narrow household pilot does not mean all other side quests break. They mostly retain native quest actors and script behavior. Broad mod compatibility is unverified; deliberate player killing can still make quests impossible under the user's established policy.

Main-story NPCs remain in their native locations for now. A bed in the same building may be reserved without moving the actor. Actual bed travel/sleep requires preserving native identity fields, components, activation collider, quest visibility/mute/hostility, timers and services. An inaccessible bed or a quest-required active scene disables that capability. Return to the original authored anchor, not a newly inferred spot.

Maintain a capability record per role and, where necessary, quest stage: native placement only; local movement; sleep; cross-building movement; reactive fleeing/combat; service interaction; generated social chat; quest tone rewriting. Capability denial has a documented reason. Main/side labels alone do not determine safety. Quest Pack 1 implementation is deferred and uses the same framework later.

### Character and spoiler boundaries

Named story NPCs use authored character profiles: public role, voice, outward temperament, obligations, declared story and relationship posture. Do not randomly turn a calculating noble into an indiscreet cheerful stranger. Weather, injury and disposition can influence delivery within the authored profile; they cannot change motives, reveal gates or canonical facts. Ordinary citizens keep mixed generated traits.

Do **not** put entire quest scripts, future stages, hidden identities or true intentions into the generation prompt and rely on “do not spoil” instructions. Store full quest data outside the LLM context. Build a per-speaker, per-stage projection with:

- Public/current scene facts the speaker can observe.
- Facts the speaker knows and is permitted to tell this player at this stage, including newly available native leads when their conditions are satisfied.
- The speaker's authored public account or cover story; the model need not know the secret it conceals.
- Explicit native topic/action identifiers that are currently available, with outcomes still executed by native handlers.
- Relevant relationship/memory entries that pass the same disclosure gates.

Player knowledge and NPC knowledge are separate. A player's guess is not confirmation; overheard rumors retain source/uncertainty. Another NPC's private memory never enters this speaker's context automatically. High disposition or a compliment must not unlock a plot reveal unless native quest state authorizes it.

A model may know Daggerfall lore from training. Context filtering alone therefore cannot guarantee secrecy. For sensitive quest questions, use approved native-stage facts or authored responses and validate any allowed tone rewrite. Unsupported quest questions remain under native dialogue; spoiler-neutral social chat may continue. Do not replace all roleplay with generic rejection templates. Generated text cannot start, finish, reward, fail or relocate a quest on its own.

Retrieval applies authorization **before** ranking/context assembly. Summaries, persisted memories, caches and conversation history retain speaker/save/quest-stage provenance and cannot reintroduce blocked secrets. Invalidate stage-sensitive caches after native quest transitions. A spoiler typed by the player remains a player claim, not model authority. Native text already disclosed in this save can be recalled where the speaker legitimately knows it.

### Representative stage analysis

This is a planning sample, not an individual review of all 302 pack quests:

| Case | Native ownership requirements | Dialogue projection |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `JHDB121a`, named Gortwog/Br'itsa and alias/vampire sequence (326–331, 431–446) | Keep named/alias/foe resources distinct; script controls placement/hiding | Public persona before reveal; hidden identity and future encounter absent; expose only native-authorized information afterward |
| `JHDB109`, child/parent rescue and ambush (95–154) | Captive does not independently commute home; preserve parent and child hide/placement | Rescue-stage observations; no parent ambush details before disclosure; memory records actual shared rescue afterward |
| `S0000007`, Cyndassa and separate werewolf resources (334–355, 406–501) | Preserve Person/Foe, face, restraint and kill events | Authored character and current story; hidden monster information gated by actual stage, not friendliness |
| `JHCG003`, chemist giver/placement/hide/reward (84–106) | Original service/quest binding remains; routine cannot undo hide | Normal role-based social context; native offer/hand-in/reward handled separately |
| `JHKN119`, priest/ritual and timed foes (115–149, 181–213) | Script controls priest availability and event clocks; no independent bedtime catch-up | Current ritual instructions when available; future waves and concealed outcomes excluded |

The individual quest backlog record must include source version/hash, speaker identities, native stages/actions, public/known-private/disclosable facts, capability gates, reveal conditions and representative before/after/save-load tests. Generated analysis can propose records; manual source review and native tests establish acceptance. Keep unreviewed scripts on conservative native ownership. Candidate pack/extension issues already identified in the audit remain separate upstream compatibility tasks.

## 4. Memory, invitations and companions — M01–M03, F01–F06

These are explicitly future features, shelved while foundations stabilize.

**Memory foundation:** each persistent NPC remembers dated encounters, claims, promises, conflicts, activities and places genuinely shared with the player. Store save/NPC identity, game date/time/location, participants, source, certainty, event outcome and salience. Retrieve compact relevant memories plus recent exact turns; do not load every NPC's entire transcript. Keep current authoritative context present. Distinguish a promised trip from a completed one and a player claim from witnessed fact. Memory retention/summary work is bounded and asynchronous. Same-room listeners only remember what they actually perceive.

**Social invitations:** the player may invite an NPC to a specified inn/tavern for drinks and a few hours together. NPC chooses using familiarity, disposition, memory, personality/mood, current duties, danger, travel time and availability. Strangers usually decline; friendship creates opportunity, not guaranteed compliance. Acceptance reserves time, follows the player physically to that named venue, creates a joint activity, then releases the temporary schedule override and resumes a feasible plan. Cancellation, player abandonment, closing, full venue, combat, death and reload must not strand or duplicate the NPC. Record the actual experience in memory. Quest actors cannot be recruited through this route.

**Human combat state:** ordinary NPCs should not be guaranteed one-punch deaths. Assign persistent, modestly varied human attributes, skills, level, health, magicka, fatigue, resistances and abilities. Store the initial roll once; do not reroll on streaming. Some know spells, can protect/heal themselves or the player, and attack enemies. Use native combat/effect semantics where possible; sustain damage, diseases/status effects, cooldowns and resource use across transitions. Stats should fit role and experience rather than making every civilian a combat expert. Exact balance remains a later design task.

**Companion recruitment:** non-quest NPCs may join for money or earned trust/help. Preserve their identity, home, family, duties, possessions and ability to refuse/leave. Re-check quest eligibility at recruitment and subsequent quest ownership changes. Main and side quest NPCs are excluded, including fixed story identities and currently bound actors. Hiring cost/payment is authoritative; dialogue cannot duplicate money or bypass consent.

**Inventory and progression:** companions own persistent equipment, items, gold and keys; support carrying, trading and giving/receiving items with capacity and ownership checks. Used skills gain experience and improve; level progression follows a deliberate DFU-compatible skill model, not an unreviewed generic kill-XP system. Persist growth without streaming duplicates or time-skip training exploits. Visible equipment and sprite compatibility remain constraints from the earlier inventory plan.

**Action framework:** interpret chat as a proposed task, resolve real targets, check capability/consent/resources/law/quest ownership, then execute through bounded native adapters. Initial examples: a thief attempts a real lock using lockpicking skill; a fighter attempts native door bashing; neither guarantees success or bypasses a quest-sealed door. Further actions include melee, stealing, climbing, bow/spell cover, beneficial spells, inventory carrying and exchange. Every action has approach, start, progress, success/failure, interruption and cancellation states; report actual results back to dialogue and memory. The model never directly edits save state, grants rewards, removes locks or invents success.

Crimes committed on request need a deliberate attribution policy for actor/player, witnesses, reputation and guard response. Do not silently make companion theft consequence-free or assume player liability without that decision. Pathfinding, climbing, friendly fire, line of sight, ammunition, spell cost, limits, equipment art and unloaded execution all require distinct tested adapters.

## 5. Modular delivery and installation — P01–P04

Retain the existing request for independent chapters: LLM/free-text/server/model setup, finite guards, patrols/posts, household recovery/house sale, disposition pricing/services and other compatible subsystems. Dependencies must be explicit; toggles cannot produce duplicate guards, lost identities or corrupt saves. Simulation and NPC action logic should remain usable without an LLM; future non-chat commands can provide equivalent access.

Current launcher uses prebuilt Windows llama.cpp and Qwen GGUF, so Python is not required for player use. Recommended optional installer direction is a PowerShell bootstrap or packaged launcher consistent with current tooling, rather than adding Python solely to download files. A Python installer is an alternative only if it provides a concrete benefit; it would require a pinned tested interpreter/dependency environment documented before release.

Public setup must document mod installation, external mod order, tested DFU/runtime/model revisions, official downloads and checksums, required DLL placement, LLM opt-out, model consent/download size, local-only endpoint, startup/prewarm distinction, health checks, error recovery, removal/update behavior and minimum/recommended hardware based on measurement. Tested Windows/NVIDIA/CUDA configuration is not evidence for AMD/Intel/CPU/macOS/Linux support. Do not invent minimum VRAM from GGUF file size.

## 6. Backlog crosswalk and acceptance

All rows remain open unless a linked evidence document explicitly proves a narrower existing behavior. “Recorded” is not “implemented.”

| IDs | Requirement | Existing track / dependency |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B01–B03 | Movement/door continuity, factual next destination and safe 2× time migration | M2/M3; current itinerary and observed-visit probes; complete before more schedule overrides |
| B04 | Guard/inn survey stalls and bounded background work | M7; PERFORMANCE.md |
| I01–I03 | Census, identity/ownership, versioned adoption/migration | M2/M7; NPC-INTEGRATION-AUDIT.md; prerequisite for new roles |
| H01–H03 | Housing-first local market workers, varied commute, weekend/demand market visits | M2/M3/M7; identity and navigation |
| H04–H06 | Tenant bed priority, keeper commute/locks, tenant keys and after-hours access/loot | M2/M3; housing, native services, corpse keys |
| H07–H09 | Inn traveler origins/kin visits, daytime itinerary/checkout/home return, explicit shared beds | M2/M3; travel reservations, room inventory, art and persistence |
| H10 | Institutional roles/personality and actual bed reservations; no new external workday routine | M2/M3; service and quest capability checks |
| A01–A02 | Actual-provider walking/running/idle/sleep coverage; shared-bed composition | M3/M7; DIRECTIONAL-CIVILIAN-ANIMATION.md; inspect before generation |
| Q01–Q03 | Native quest preservation, authored story personality, capability-limited bed movement | M1/M6/M7; original identities and native lifecycle |
| Q04–Q07 | Speaker/stage knowledge projection, spoiler tests, vanilla adapter matrix, deferred pack review | M6/M7; quest audit and no whole-script prompt |
| M01–M03 | Dated persistent memory, relevant retrieval, shared activities/listeners and disclosure filtering | M3/M6; FUTURE-DIALOGUE-AND-VOICE.md |
| F01–F06 | Invitations; human stats/effects; recruitment; inventories; progression; world-action framework | Later M3/M6 extension; shelved; identity/memory/combat/action dependencies |
| P01–P04 | Player install guide, optional bootstrap, measured support matrix, modular packaging | M6/M7; INSTALLATION.md; no mandatory LLM dependencies |
| E01 | Existing crime/guard/haunting, household recovery, visits/travel and visual behavior | M2–M5/M7; existing domain documents remain binding |
| E02 | All paid services/pricing/refusal; rank-sensitive expulsion and castle-noble consequences | M3/M6; existing MILESTONES.md requests retained |
| E03 | Native quest dialogue with disposition-aware tone without semantic/action changes | M6/M7; disclosure/ownership policy above |
| E04 | English voices and broader NPC appearance work | Later M6/M7; preserve existing voice backlog, do not prioritize over broken movement |

Acceptance is per slice: core behavior, native bindings, save/load, player-observed result and performance evidence where applicable. Publishing plans or passing core tests does not certify all quests or layouts. No “everything active everywhere” per-frame simulation is required; unloaded positions/actions must be determinable consistently with bounded cost.
