<a id="source-tavern-lodging-md"></a>

## Original: TAVERN-LODGING.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Tavern lodging and travelers

16 October rendered local social patron acceptance now passes in actual inn 65542. A real local resident with a controlled visit schedule is observed continuously without clock jumps: keeper approach and dwell, four distinct social stops, then physical doorway exit. The largest rendered frame displacement is 0.489 m, with about 49.4 m of movement between social stops and 10.9 m of exit travel. The full three-inn survey/rental/room/sleep regression also passes (`work/InnSocialMovement-native-pass.log`, terminal exit 0). This verifies one rendered local patron/layout; other inn layouts retain geometry/inventory coverage, not a claim of identical rendered acceptance everywhere. Two existing sleeping-art gaps remain. No new social-route production change was needed for this test.

Follow-up regression passes after the crime response change: the full Daggerfall City traveler/gates suite, including paid room use, arrival/death persistence and full-inn walking refusal with disk reload (`work/InnCrimeArrival-traveler-regression-pass.log`). This is normal/full-capacity behavior coverage, not a live guarded-crime refusal test.

16 October crime-scene traveler handling: admission checks the saved guard investigation at the due arrival time, using a copied roster so catch-up never rewinds the live guards. An arrived, still-occupied investigation refuses the traveler without creating a paid booking; the existing refused journey returns toward the settlement exit and respects gate times. Core tests verify refusal, no bed reservation, restore and later admission after the one-hour occupation (371/371). Loaded admitted travelers now detect an uncleared corpse and use a neutral saved escape/report route instead of room activities. The actual inn fixture passes arrival discovery and doorway departure (`work/InnCrimeArrival-runtime-pass.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_INN_TRAVELER_CORPSE_PASS`); Unity build passes (`work/InnCrimeArrival-build-editor.log`). This test uses a registered synthetic arrival to exercise the actual actor/admission path, not a rendered inter-settlement approach. Guarded inn refusal is core-tested, not yet live-tested. Already paid guests retain their booking while fleeing; temporary displacement is not a cancellation/refund system. Witnessed-accusation guard occupancy, full exterior evacuation/contact, moving patrol interception and route failure recovery remain unfinished.

15 October native inn cleanup: existing indoor/outdoor corpse cleanup now removes the dead native occupant's lodging claim only after the corpse is cleared. Death alone keeps the room occupied; surviving roommates keep their own claims. Non-rental eligibility is unchanged.361 core tests and Unity build pass (`work/InnCleanup-core.log`, `work/InnCleanup-build-editor.log`); failing pre-fix test is `work/InnCleanup-red.log`. Core coverage verifies unattended-day cleanup, on-site retention, subsequent paid booking, surviving roommate exclusion and restore without resurrection. Live inn sleep/rental/cleanup acceptance remains deferred by user request. This does not yet shorten paid traveler bookings on death.

15 October non-rental bedroom integration: schema11 adds explicit NonRentable room metadata. Runtime inn inventory includes all surveyed bedrooms; only rest-marker-backed rooms are offered for rent. Existing inventories can gain strictly non-rental rooms without removing/reassigning old beds, changing eligibility or losing bookings/claims. Ordinary native occupants in those rooms now enter the same claim/sleep path.359 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/NonRentalRooms-core.log`, `work/NonRentalRooms-build-editor.log`). Live tests remain deferred by user request. Pending live validation must cover occupied-room claims, night/midday poses, save/reload and updated rental fixtures; older fixtures assume fixed available markers, which may now belong to native occupants and require deliberate setup reconciliation. Excess occupants and corpse-cleanup claim release remain open.

15 October inn claim runtime integration: exterior inventory and loaded-inn rental refresh now register ordinary native room identities from original records and commit surveyed bed claims after native player receipts reconcile, before traveler admission. Name generation preserves DFRandom state. Existing paid-room conflicts defer the claim instead of displacing payment. Claimed native occupants use seeded sleep/wake times and appearance-matched bed poses through the existing native wake/crime path.358 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/InnClaimRuntime-core.log`, `work/InnClaimRuntime-build-editor.log`). Live automatic-claim and night/midday assertions were added to TavernSurveyProbe but have NOT run: the user asked to keep working without live tests while their game is open. Integration currently covers marker-backed managed rental rooms; non-rentable bedroom occupants, excess occupants, cleanup release, actual sleep/reload and broader layouts remain pending. Do not report native gameplay acceptance from this build.

15 October inn occupant survey checkpoint: loaded and model-only unloaded surveys now classify original people records using door-separated room regions, excluding the entrance-connected common room. Assignment is deterministic by native position hash and original bed record. The actual three-Gothway-inn test matches three assignments (65542:372/bed30 and -1016/bed45;131590:102/bed15), with all rental/payment/rest regressions passing (`work/InnOccupantSurvey-runtime-pass.log`).358 core tests and Unity build pass. One bedroom has more original NPCs than beds; excess occupants log `LIVING_CITIES_INN_OCCUPANT_NO_BED` without assigning a shared bed or breaking the rental survey. Initial whole-inn survey failure is preserved in `work/InnOccupantSurvey-runtime-red.log`. Claim creation, native sleep integration, excess-occupant behavior, corpse cleanup and wider layouts remain unfinished; room classification alone does not reserve beds.

15 October native inn claim foundation: schema10 persists named native occupants with a physical bed and claim start time. A claim excludes its entire private room from offers, bookings, extensions and rest checks; conflicting paid intervals prevent the claim rather than being cancelled. Schema9 migrates empty claims while preserving bookings.358 core tests and Unity native serialization/build pass (`work/InnOccupants-core.log`, `work/InnOccupants-build-editor.log`). This is ledger infrastructure only: actual loaded/unloaded NPC room classification, assignment, sleeping, corpse-cleanup release and live inn acceptance remain pending. No runtime controller creates these claims yet.

## Existing room occupants, 15 October 2026

User requirement: original stationary NPCs placed in inn guest rooms occupy that room's beds. Their occupied rooms must not be rented to the player or generated travelers. Assign stable native identities to physical beds using the same door-separated room survey as rental allocation, not nearest-bed distance through walls. One occupant per bed; a reserved private room is unavailable as a whole. Common-room patrons and the service keeper must not consume arbitrary guest beds simply because they are in the inn. Assigned room occupants sleep in their beds at night with appearance-matched sleeping art and retain the existing awareness, wake, death and crime behavior.

Current gaps verified in code: `TavernRoomSurvey.Partition` considers beds, doors and rental markers but not NPC room membership. `SurveyKeeperIdentities` registers ordinary tavern people but only shop merchants receive bed assignment. `KeeperSchedule` excludes taverns. Implement matching loaded/unloaded room classification, persistent occupant claims, rental/rest exclusion and native sleep integration together. Existing paid player receipts need explicit reconciliation; do not silently cancel or charge again. Test rental refusal for occupied rooms, free-room availability, awake/sleep transitions, saved identity/bed continuity and room availability after occupant cleanup.

15 October refusal checkpoint: a fully booked inn now sends a refused traveler back along the surveyed exterior route without reserving a bed. Closed gates delay departure; admission changes invalidate cached journeys so the proposed multi-day stay cannot keep a refused actor alive.352 core tests and the native Daggerfall full-inn test pass, including visible return movement and actual save-slot reload (`work/TravelerRefusal-core.log`, `work/TravelerRefusal-runtime-pass.log`, `work/TravelerRefusal-build-editor.log`). The test runner now requires `LIVING_CITIES_TRAVELER_REFUSAL_PASS`. Automatic demand, keeper interaction, daytime visits, historical retention and broader layout acceptance remain open.

## Inn room walking, 15 October 2026

`TravelerRoomWalk` reconstructs 3D route progress from absolute check-in/checkout times. The runtime reverses the collision-surveyed bed-to-exit route for arrival, walks inward after check-in and starts returning early enough to reach the indoor entrance landing at checkout. Walking uses existing crowd placement/animation and opens ordinary doors. Walking guests count as awake even during scheduled sleeping hours. If a gate wait is required, the guest remains at the indoor landing without retaining bed occupancy until the exterior itinerary's `LeaveInnAt`; an unloaded interior does not create a duplicate exterior actor.

350 core tests pass, including stair-height interpolation, inbound/outbound timing, copied route isolation and impossible-duration rejection (`work/TravelerWalk-core.log`). The real Daggerfall fixture failed on the old stationary checkout guest, then passed physical checkout movement along with sleeping/reload/waking and later-visit/death/corpse persistence (`work/TravelerWalk-runtime-red.log`, `work/TravelerWalk-runtime-pass.log`). Unity build passes. Focused review found no confirmed issue after clarifying that gate waiting is indoors.

Missing room routes are explicitly logged as `LIVING_CITIES_TRAVELER_ROOM_ROUTE_MISSING`. Such layouts retain the prior room placement during the paid stay and hide the guest at checkout; they are not certified for walking. This checkpoint verifies one Daggerfall inn's checkout motion, not every layout, arrival observation, gate-wait scene transition or staircase. Route geometry is reconstructed on interior load. Automatic demand, daytime activities, refused-arrival departure, death-room release and full history indexing remain open.

## Physical paid guests, 15 October 2026

Admitted travelers now appear inside their booked inn during their stay. A stable bed is selected only from the paid room, and only while the guest is alive and the booking is current. Sleeping uses the existing appearance-matched, bed-aligned art. Awake guests stand on checked floor beside that bed. Interior changes clear and reconstruct actors under the correct parent; reload retains the same identity and bed. Indoor alarm handling reuses sleep waking, escape and civilian reactions instead of leaving a separate invulnerable decoration.

349 core tests verify payment/time/death/restore bed eligibility (`work/TravelerBed-core.log`). The native Daggerfall fixture first failed because the paid guest was absent (`work/TravelerInn-runtime-red.log`), then passed named awake presence, sleeping in the assigned bed, a real sleeping save/reload and morning waking, followed by the existing later-visit/death/corpse reload sequence (`work/TravelerInn-runtime-pass.log`). Unity build passes; focused review found no confirmed issues. Disposable test save: `Living Cities - traveler sleeping` for `LivingCitiesTest-f238d317`.

This supersedes the absent-indoor-guest limitation below. It is not yet entrance-to-room walking, daytime town visiting, tavern partying or automatic demand generation. Awake guests currently remain beside their bed. Refused-arrival departure, physical checkout routing and death-to-room-release remain open. The new fixture verifies ordinary sleep/lifecycle, not every indoor crime/escape combination or every inn layout.

## Automatic admission for registered travelers, 15 October 2026

Native rental refresh now advances up to eight due registered travelers for the inn whose inventory and native player receipt were successfully reconciled. Other inns remain untouched until their own reconciliation succeeds. Player rental actions also process earlier arrivals first; a remaining queue yields a keeper-busy message without charging the player. Exactly exhausting the batch does not falsely report a remaining queue. This connects registered arrivals to paid first-come room allocation; automatic demand generation is still pending.

The actual Daggerfall actor fixture now waits past check-in and verifies automatic admitted status and its matching booking before testing later visits, death and disk reload (`work/TravelerLiveAdmission-runtime-pass.log`).348 core tests, native FullSerializer checks and build pass; all three Gothway tavern inventory/payment/rest regressions also pass (`work/TravelerLiveAdmission-core.log`, `work/TravelerLiveAdmission-build-editor.log`, `work/TravelerLiveAdmission-rental-pass.log`). Review found no remaining confirmed defect in this delta.

Live paid-room testing exposed an exact-double validation bug at real calendar epochs: JSON saved12597580692.4037 while recomputing12597573702 +6990.40364950249 produced a slightly different check-in. Restore now permits less than0.001 seconds of booking timestamp drift and the same small admission-boundary tolerance, retaining strict identity/room ownership checks. A one-second alteration still fails. Core G15 reproduction and native FullSerializer large-epoch fixture cover this; failure evidence is `work/TravelerAdmissionPrecision-red.log` and `work/TravelerLiveAdmission-precision-runtime-red.log`. No save schema change or deletion is required.

Physical inn entry, guest-room sleeping, refusal departure, death/room release, automatic demand and history indexing remain unfinished. Registered visitors currently disappear from the exterior at check-in rather than appearing in their room; do not describe paid admission alone as a finished inn stay.

## Traveler runtime retention follow-up, 15 October 2026

Runtime now caches only visits overlapping the current game day, invalidated by day changes, new visit registration and state restoration. Dead/completed actor plans are removed; a living traveler with an active civilian reaction retains its plan until that reaction ends. Persisted visit and death evidence is not deleted. The half-open window includes entry and excludes exit, allowing a later visit by the same person to replace the completed one.

346 core tests pass, including window boundaries, destination filtering and cache revision after registration/restoration (`work/TravelerWindow-core.log`). Native Daggerfall arrival/reload/return/death/corpse regression also passes and asserts zero retained plans after the traveler dies (`work/TravelerWindow-runtime-pass.log`); Unity build passes. This removes historical plans from per-frame iteration and the half-second full-history scan. Window refresh still scans saved history on day/revision changes; indexed history, bounded generation and retained-save policy remain required before large-scale recurring demand.

## Exterior traveler actor checkpoint, 15 October 2026

Registered visits now render named adult travelers during exterior arrival and admitted departure, using the resident actor, collision and crime hooks. Native weapon death updates the persistent traveler identity and creates a corpse. Arrival reload reconstructs the actor; a later visit replaces the completed journey for the same person. Dead travelers do not reappear after disk reload.

Actual Daggerfall `CityTravelerProbe` passes arrival movement/name, arrival save/reload, a subsequent visit, native weapon death, corpse rendering and death save/reload (`work/TravelerActor-runtime-pass.log`).345/345 core tests and Unity integration/build pass (`work/TravelerActor-core.log`, `work/TravelerActor-build-editor.log`). The stronger fixture initially checked corpse rendering while vanilla gameplay was paused after its multi-day time skip; the corpse ledger was correct. The fixture now closes unrelated dialogue before observing rendering; diagnostic evidence is retained in `work/TravelerActor-return-diagnostic.log`.

This supersedes the actor-absent statements below, but is not automatic ambient traveler generation. Production demand/admission orchestration, inn interiors, sleeping, refused-arrival departure, paid-room cleanup and haunting integration remain unfinished. Review identified historical plan retention and repeated full-history scans: bound these before enabling recurring production demand. Current actor rendering is distance-limited and shares the resident actor cap, but historical simulation work is not yet bounded.

## Traveler civilian ledger integration, 15 October 2026

Registered nonlocal visitors with a visit to the incident settlement can now be civilian victims, witnesses and outdoor corpse discoverers without joining a residential household. Hostile-enemy incidents make them flee; dead travelers cannot witness new crimes. Alarm/corpse snapshot validation recognizes the same identity and rejects removal of the destination visit. Traveler death cannot precede its recorded attack through either mutation or save restoration. Ordinary native staff corpse observers are also accepted consistently with the runtime notice API.

Tests:345/345 core (`work/TravelerCrime-final-core.log`), expected failures in `work/TravelerCrime-red.log` and `work/TravelerCrime-chronology-red.log`, Unity native WorldState serialization/build (`work/TravelerCrime-build-editor.log`). Native serialization fixture now includes admitted traveler death, direct visitor witness and outdoor corpse discovery while retaining paid lodging. This supplies the core integration prerequisite; no traveler render controller or native weapon-hit traveler fixture is installed yet. Current identity association validates a stored destination visit, not the visitor's exact physical position at the incident time. Runtime actors must enforce active journey/location membership. Paid-room crime scene ownership, death-to-actor synchronization, guard/corpse rendering, room release and bounded visit history remain pending.

## Daggerfall gate routes, 15 October 2026

Daggerfall City is now the primary traveler acceptance location; Gothway remains the unwalled comparison. Its20 inns exposed a failure hidden by Gothway: navigation-boundary road starts yielded no usable route through the walls. Walled surveys now derive outside approaches from actual gate mesh bounds and connect to the inner street grid, retaining physical waypoint/segment sweeps across the full gate opening. This bridges the native grid discontinuity without bypassing collision. Unwalled road-boundary behavior is unchanged.

`TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -CityTravelerProbe` passes actual Daggerfall geometry:4 gates, all20 inns reachable at noon, every inn rejects physical routes after20:00 closure, a representative route recovers at06:00, and the core itinerary delays a proposed20:00 arrival until06:00. Evidence: `work/CityTraveler-runtime-red.log`, `work/CityTraveler-gates-red.log`, `work/CityTraveler-final-pass.log`;333 core tests and final Unity build pass. This validates routes and scheduled gating, not visible NPC gate crossing or a rendered waiting queue. Traveler demand/actors, paid city lodging and haunting-aware visits remain pending.

## Traveler approach routes, 15 October 2026

`TownGeometry.SurveyTravelerRoutes` identifies contiguous native road runs at the navigation-grid boundary, chooses stable midpoint approaches, and finds up to4 collision-checked routes to a surveyed inn door. Candidate attempts are bounded to32 per inn. The shared A* and physical waypoint/segment checks reject blocked paths. Walled status is read from actual `DaggerfallCityGate` components. These are local town approaches; regional Roads of Daggerfall routes remain unconnected.

Live Gothway testing finds4 routes to each of its3 inns and correctly classifies the settlement as unwalled (`work/TravelerRoutes-pass.log`). Routes terminate at the surveyed inn door, and the full inventory/payment/rest suite remains green.333 core tests and Unity build pass. This helper is not yet called by a traveler population controller. Walled-city gate crossings, closed-gate retry/persistence, selected-route reconstruction, physical travelers and large-city timing remain unverified; do not treat this unwalled fixture as proof of those cases.

## Automatic exterior inventory checkpoint, 15 October 2026

The running rental controller now surveys one tavern per refresh while the player is outside. Results are cached for the current `BuildingDirectory`; each cycle re-registers the cached layout against the current world state, so save restoration does not leave a stale cache without ledger entries. Native paid receipts are reconciled by their allocated marker and expiry without charging again. Geometry failures are logged once and retried when the settlement layout reloads; reconciliation conflicts retain existing bookings and receipts. No traveler admission is called yet.

The native fixture clears the town lodging ledger, supplies one paid receipt and stays outdoors. All3 inns appear with guest capacity, exactly that paid reservation, and no movement/entry/gold change. Loaded inventory, passive-vs-loaded geometry, rental/payment/rest checks also pass (`work/ExteriorInventory-pass.log`); the preceding build reproduces missing exterior inventory (`work/ExteriorInventory-runtime-red.log`).333/333 core and Unity build/install pass. First survey remains synchronous within its refresh; large/modded settlement profiling and background time slicing are not proven. Traveler origins, gate/road routes, arrival demand and actors remain pending.

## Unloaded survey checkpoint, 15 October 2026

`TavernRoomSurvey.ReadUnloaded` builds temporary model-only interior geometry above the scene, adds passive closed-door bounds from block records and reads native rest-marker positions. It shares the same partition algorithm as loaded interiors. It does not call native NPC/quest/loot layout or action-door registration. Temporary geometry is disabled and destroyed in `finally`.

All3 Gothway taverns match their actual loaded room partitions, bed membership and marker mappings, while player position/interior state and active native NPC count remain unchanged (`work/UnloadedTavern-pass.log`). Existing unrented inventory, paid import, payment and rest regressions pass in the same run;333 core tests and Unity build pass. This validates the helper, not automatic background inventory: the running controller still registers only loaded inns. Third-party model replacements and their script side effects are not covered; passive door bounds currently use native models. Settlement-wide registration, gate/road routes and traveler actors remain next.

## Unrented inventory checkpoint, 15 October 2026

Entering a supported tavern now registers its marker-backed guest rooms even when the player has no rental receipt. No booking or payment is invented. Existing paid player reservations are still reconciled immediately afterward. Survey results remain cached for the loaded interior, and room-layout validation still runs after save restoration. This removes a rental prerequisite for future traveler admission, but does not yet survey inns the player has never entered.

Native red test reproduced the missing inventory (`work/UnrentedInventory-runtime-red.log`). The fixed live test passes all3 Gothway inns with3/2/2 guest rooms and no receipt, booking or gold change, followed by all existing import/payment/rest cases (`work/UnrentedInventory-pass.log`).333/333 core tests and Unity build/install pass. Offscreen surveys require more than vanilla `DoLayoutAutomap`: that method adds models only, omitting rest markers and action doors needed for room partitioning. Do not reuse it unmodified as a complete lodging survey.

## Traveler persistence checkpoint, 15 October 2026

Schema9 stores traveler identities separately from households, proposed visits, admission outcomes and deaths. Admission reserves the itinerary's exact paid interval once; refusals remain refused after reload or later vacancy. A traveler cannot originate in the destination settlement, overlap their own visits, revive through repeated registration, or have a death timestamp before an admitted visit. Restore rejects malformed booking links and chronology atomically. Existing schemas1–8 gain empty traveler ledgers. Direct lodging extension cannot desynchronize a linked traveler itinerary.

Verification:333/333 core tests (`work/TravelerChronology-green.log`, chronology regression red in `TravelerChronology-red.log`), Unity native serializer (`work/TravelerSave-final-unity.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_TRAVELER_SAVE_PASS`), and all3 Gothway inn rental/payment/rest regressions (`work/TravelerSave-regression-pass.log`). Review identified the backdated-death gap; both mutation and restore now reject it before changing state.

This is persistence and admission infrastructure, not visible traveler gameplay. Actual origin selection, arrival generation, bounded history retention, importing native receipts before NPC demand, route survey and actor/crime/sleep integration remain pending. Refused visits still retain their proposed itinerary for overlap validation; early physical departure is not implemented. Death preserves the paid room until checkout; early corpse-cleanup release is not implemented.

## Implementation checkpoint, 15 October 2026

Traveler itinerary core now models a proposed visit separately from residential households and payment (`src/LivingCities.Core/TravelerJourney.cs`). Given an arrival, night count and surveyed entrance-to-inn route, it evaluates absolute-time arrival, inn stay/sleep, gate wait, return journey and departure. Walled entry/exit obey vanilla06:00–18:00 gates; unwalled arrivals can enter at night. Early check-in before06:00 uses that morning's checkout; later check-in uses the next morning. Seeded checkout09:00–10:59 and sleep/wake times remain stable after reconstruction. The stay interval ends at checkout even if a closed outbound gate delays departure. Route input is copied and interpolation follows segment lengths in both directions.

Verification:327/327 core tests (`work/TravelerJourney-green.log`), missing-type red (`work/TravelerJourney-red.log`), Unity build/integration (`work/TravelerJourney-build.log`), and read-only review with no confirmed defect. This is not traveler spawning or paid admission: generated nonlocal identities, persisted attempts/deaths, room admission, routes surveyed at runtime, visible actors and town visits still need integration. The complete next-step plan is [nonlocal travelers](HISTORY.md#source-superpowers-plans-2026-10-15-travelers-md). `WithinStay` is an itinerary interval, not evidence of payment; only the lodging ledger grants a room.

Managed tavern beds now gate native `DaggerfallRestWindow.CanRest`. Ordinary resting checks the native allocated marker's room before allowing vanilla to move the player there. A conflicting guest reservation shows an occupied-bed message; an invalid marker no longer silently falls back to marker0. `ignoreAllocatedBed` requests (rest in place) check the player's local mattress footprint and height against reserved beds. Standing at another guest's bed does not prevent ordinary rest from taking the player to their own valid allocated room. No-ledger, disabled and non-tavern contexts remain native; loitering is not intercepted. This does not add a direct click-on-bed interaction or prevent resting on arbitrary floor space through other mods.

Verification:322/322 core tests (`work/TavernRest-core-green.log`), Unity build (`work/TavernRest-build.log`) and native rest checks in all3 Gothway inns (`work/TavernRest-pass.log`). The pre-fix test accepted a receipt targeting a guest's reserved room (`work/TavernRest-runtime-red.log`). Final cases reject that conflict, occupied rest-in-place and invalid markers; allow ordinary rest to the unoccupied paid room even while standing at another bed; and avoid a false block after a controlled four-unit vertical offset. These exercise native `CanRest`, not full timed sleep or another mod's custom rest implementation. Rental import, payment, room census and door-state mapping regressions also pass. Read-only review found no confirmed rest-hook defect. Actual traveler generation, full disk-slot lodging reload and broader layout/compatibility tests remain pending.

Native rental payment is now capacity-aware. Scoped Harmony prefixes handle the accepted paid confirmation before native gold deduction and the direct free-room path. Managed inns select an available marker-backed room for the complete stay, commit that room to the ledger, create/update the native receipt, retain the permanent scene and deduct the native quoted price. Extensions keep the same room and reject future booking conflicts without charging. Full inns report the earliest available room for the requested duration using the game calendar. Native food/drink, quote calculation and privilege eligibility are unchanged. Declined offers remain native; disabled/non-tavern requests fall through. Unsupported surveys with no managed ledger fall through to vanilla; changed layouts with saved reservations refuse rather than risk double-selling rooms.

Verification:322/322 core tests (`work/RentalPayment-core-green.log`), final Unity build/integration (`work/RentalPayment-build.log`), and native callback tests in all3 Gothway inns (`work/RentalPayment-pass.log`). Tests cover full paid/free requests, sole vacancy selection, exact price and marker, valid extension, conflicting extension, insufficient funds, declined offers and available free rooms. The original live red case charged for a full ledger (`work/RentalPayment-runtime-red.log`). Test cleanup restores receipts, gold, ledger and prior permanent-scene membership. Review identified unsupported-layout fallback and it was corrected; that fallback and visual layout of vacancy text still lack dedicated live fixtures. The synchronous transaction uses native receipt/scene/gold APIs after ledger commit; exceptional failures in those native writes do not yet have a rollback transaction. No ordinary failure was observed. Occupied-bed/rest enforcement and travelers remain unfinished; normal gameplay currently has no generated traveler demand.

Native paid rental import is now connected to the running population controller. In a loaded tavern with an unexpired native receipt, `NativeTavernRentals` surveys marker-backed guest rooms once per interior instance, registers their layout and reconciles the exact allocated marker's room. Repeated observations preserve booking identity/start; extensions retain the room. No gold, native allocation or receipt expiry is changed. Conflicting rooms, shortened entitlements, unsupported markers and overlapping reservations are reported without evicting bookings. Layout changes still require explicit reconciliation. Expired receipts do not create new bookings; explicit cancellation/early native receipt removal is not yet reconciled.

Evidence:321/321 core tests (`work/NativeRental-core-green.log`), Unity build/integration checks (`work/NativeRental-build.log`), and all three Gothway inns under the running controller (`work/NativeRental-pass.log`). The live regression first failed because no importer existed (`work/NativeRental-runtime-red.log`); it now preserves marker1 in each inn and imports a native expiry extension without additional charge or duplicate booking. Core restore is covered; this new import path has not yet received a full disk-slot reload test. Native rental availability/payment/rest interception, staff allocation, historical pruning and traveler generation remain unfinished. Vanilla deducts gold in `ConfirmRenting_OnButtonClick` before `RentRoom`; availability hooks must preflight before deduction, not reject after payment.

Player reservations now permit simultaneous paid rooms in different inns, matching vanilla's `RentedRooms` list. The same inn still rejects duplicate overlapping player bookings, and travelers cannot reserve overlapping stays across inns. Booking and restore reject changing a guest's player/traveler role. Extension and save/load preserve these rules. Regression tests failed before the fix and pass afterward:319/319 core tests (`work/LodgingConcurrent-green.log`); Unity build and integration checks passed and the bundle was installed (`work/LodgingConcurrent-build.log`). This is ledger compatibility, not automatic native rental import or traveler spawning.

Native rental-marker mapping now passes for all7 Gothway markers, invariant with doors open or closed (`work/TavernMarkers-pass.log`, final build `work/TavernMarkers-final-build.log`). Mapping uses nearby visible surveyed floor in a single closed-door region on the same storey; markers over mattresses do not need a walkable route onto the bed. Missing or ambiguous mappings reject the survey.65542 markers0/1/2 map to rooms31/38/43;66061 markers0/1 map to rooms47/54;131590 markers0/1 map to rooms12/16. These7 marker-backed groups contain9 beds. The remaining4 groups lack native rental markers and are not yet designated guest or staff rooms.

The booking API now accepts an explicit allocated room and never falls back to a different vacant room when it is unavailable. This allows future reconciliation to preserve vanilla `allocatedBedIndex` ownership. New tests verify exact-room selection, occupied-room refusal, incompatible repeated booking rejection and unknown-room rejection;317/317 core tests pass (`work/TavernMarkers-core-green.log`). Existing native player rentals are not imported automatically yet; native payment/rest hooks, staff ownership and traveler generation remain next steps.

Room partition implementation now groups bed records by reachable floor regions while treating each action door's canonical closed mesh as a boundary (`mod/Scripts/TavernRoomSurvey.cs`). Optional seeded/boundary navigation leaves ordinary traversal defaults unchanged. Production survey does not move doors; truncated/unreachable room surveys fail explicitly instead of accepting guessed capacity. Room IDs derive from the lowest original bed record index. No reservations or staff classification are enabled yet.

Native open-versus-closed tests pass on all three Gothway taverns (`work/TavernRooms-pass.log`, build `work/TavernRooms-build.log`):65542 produces6 bed groups;66061 produces2 (47/49 together,54 separate);131590 produces3 (12/13 together,15 and16 separate). All13 beds are assigned exactly once and partitions remain identical after every action door is opened. These are floor-connectivity groups; rental-marker mapping, public/staff-room eligibility and broader layout acceptance are still required before calling them allocatable guest rooms. Read-only review found no confirmed defects. The first unrelated shop guard regression reached combat but timed out without native damage (`work/TavernRooms-navigation-first-fail.log`); an unchanged rerun passed the full report/arrival/combat/welfare/real-reload/surrender chain (`work/TavernRooms-navigation-retry-pass.log`). Keep that intermittent combat failure visible rather than treating it as resolved.

Loaded Gothway census (`tools/TownTest.ps1 -FixtureCharacter LivingCitiesTest-f238d317 -TavernSurveyProbe`, evidence `work/TavernSurvey-pass.log`, build `work/TavernSurvey-build.log`) visits all three real taverns. Building65542 has6 beds,3 rest markers,9 action doors and4 beds reachable by current navigation;66061 has3 beds,2 markers,6 doors and3 reachable beds;131590 has4 beds,2 markers,6 doors and4 reachable beds. Total13 beds/7 markers: marker count is not bed count. The probe records stable original bed record indices, local bed/marker/door coordinates, closed door rotations and native occupant positions without creating reservations. Do not infer private rooms or staff bed ownership from proximity alone. Investigate the two unreachable beds in65542 and partition geometry at closed doorway boundaries before enabling allocation. The census establishes inputs, not a completed room-grouping algorithm or staff classification.

Schema8 adds a persisted room/booking ledger in `src/LivingCities.Core/Lodging.cs`. An accepted reservation exclusively reserves every bed in its room for a half-open check-in/check-out interval, even while the guest is away. Requests are first-come, deterministic by room ID, idempotent by booking ID and cannot overlap another booking for the room or the same guest. Player reservations receive the same occupancy protection; no reserved vacancy is invented. Non-player travelers must have a home settlement different from the tavern's. Party size cannot exceed room bed count.

Extensions fail atomically when they conflict with another accepted booking. Earliest vacancy considers requested stay duration, including future bookings and exact checkout boundaries. Unknown beds fail explicitly. Room layout changes are rejected until explicit reconciliation exists, preserving old entitlements instead of silently deleting them. Snapshots validate ownership, unique room/bed/booking IDs and overlapping intervals and deep-copy the ledger. Schemas1–7 migrate with no invented reservations.

Verification:316/316 core tests (`work/Lodging-green.log`), Unity build/native WorldState serialization (`work/Lodging-build.log`, `LIVING_CITIES_LODGING_SAVE_PASS` in the Unity build log), and actual existing-fixture save/load plus welfare/surrender regression (`work/Lodging-save-regression-pass.log`). Read-only review found no confirmed defects; its migration-coverage gap was addressed with explicit schema1–7 tests.

This is a persistence foundation, not active tavern occupancy. Next: survey stable private-room groups and staff bed exclusions; reconcile native `RoomRental_v1` before allocating any traveler; intercept rental payment/extension atomically; enforce bed/rest access and earliest-vacancy dialogue; then add deterministic nonlocal traveler journeys and reservations. Correction from native source inspection: vanilla rental stores `allocatedBedIndex` as well as tavern and expiry. `DaggerfallTavernWindow.RentRoom()` chooses a rest-marker index; `DaggerfallRestWindow` resolves that index when sleeping. Preserve this assigned marker during reconciliation, then map it to the physical bed and exclusive room. Earlier descriptions of only tavern-wide entitlement omitted this field. Cancellation/death/layout reconciliation, bounded historical retention, traveler generation and local social patrons remain unfinished. No runtime fake guests or arbitrary room assignments are enabled.

User amendment on 14 October 2026. The initial suggestion to keep one bed free was explicitly withdrawn. Do not reserve a guaranteed player bed.

Taverns are commercial establishments, not residential households. Separate the keeper/owner, employees, local social visitors and overnight travelers. These people do not inherit one household surname merely because they share the tavern. Preserve the keeper's food, drink and rental services. Town locals may drink, eat, socialize and gather immediately outside the entrance without renting a bed. Visits increase from late afternoon through evening and early night; varied departures continue into late night, approximately 03:00. Do not place every patron on the same arrival/departure schedule.

Travelers can arrive and depart throughout the day. In walled settlements, travel across city gates must respect actual gate opening/closing: no arrival through a closed gate at night. Unwalled villages, hamlets and towns allow arrivals and departures at any hour. A closed gate constrains the route across the wall, not movement already inside town.

All routine times need small deterministic variation per NPC/day, including sleep, shopping, travel, tavern visits, walks and social visits. Variation must preserve activity constraints and survive save/load or unloading. Account for route duration: a shopper may leave before opening if arrival is at or after opening. Early arrivals wait outside until access is available. Use distinct reachable waiting positions and spacing so waiting NPCs do not overlap or obstruct the doorway. Late plans must respect closing times rather than entering a closed shop. Sleeping and waking must be consistent with the occupancy-based residential lock policy in HOUSEHOLD-LOCKS.md.

Travelers have homes outside the current settlement. They travel into town, pay for one-night or multi-day lodging, visit local destinations during their stay, and return to the road when the visit ends. Local residents may visit taverns socially, but should not be generated as ordinary overnight travelers merely to fill rooms. Keep traveler identities, destinations and progress deterministic across unloaded regions.

Paid bookings reserve a room or its allocated sleeping places for a defined interval, including when the guest is outside. Physical NPC presence alone is not the booking record. First come, first served: check availability and commit the reservation atomically so player and NPC requests cannot double-book. Do not evict an existing paid player booking to make room for simulated guests.

Rooms occupied/reserved by another guest cannot be used by the player for sleeping. Show a clear occupied-room/bed message. Exclude all beds belonging to an exclusively reserved room from other bookings; model actual room grouping rather than treating every bed in one room as a separate private room. Household bed sharing does not automatically authorize strangers sharing a paid private room.

When fully booked, the keeper refuses the new rental and reports the earliest time an eligible room becomes free, calculated from active reservation checkout times. Use the game's calendar and check requested stay duration, not merely whether a room is empty at this instant. Future extensions of stays must respect already accepted reservations. Checkout releases occupancy; cancellation, death, missing/inaccessible room and mod-layout changes need explicit reconciliation without silently erasing payment or player entitlement.

Persist traveler ID, tavern/room identity, check-in/out times, payment/booking state, party membership where applicable and current visit schedule. Save/load and time skips must not duplicate travelers or leave permanent stale reservations. Reconcile vanilla player room rental, rest markers, quest tavern NPCs and room allocation before replacing those behaviors.

Delivery remains future work within civilian schedules/nightlife and travel integration. Expose settings for traveler demand, stay lengths and lodging behavior when implemented. Test no vacancies, competing requests, day rollover, multi-day stay, guest out during paid stay, player rental, early/late checkout, unloading/reloading and earliest-vacancy dialogue.

<a id="source-theft-witnesses-md"></a>

## Original: THEFT-WITNESSES.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Theft requires a possible witness

Menu controls: Awareness / TheftWitnesses enables the witness override (default true); disabling restores native probability. Awareness / WitnessRange sets sight distance from 5 to 40 metres (default 20), clamped on load/use. These settings do not affect combat detection. Existing-save HousingProbe passes both transfers and awareness checks with the new defaults; work/AwarenessSettings-pass.log. Build/native settings validation passes.

Private-property transfer acceptance now passes as well: in the residential fixture, a controlled house-owned loot container supplies a dagger through the native inventory's Remove click path. The player receives it, the container loses it, and closing the inventory adds no crime with all NPCs temporarily hidden. Cleanup removes the item/container and restores NPCs. Both private-property and shop transfer checks pass in the same actual-player run; work/PrivateTheftTransfer-pass.log. These controlled absence tests do not verify native keeper death or detection-triggered finite guard delivery.

Shop transfer acceptance now passes: TheftTransferProbe enters The Superior General Store (131343), temporarily hides its active NPCs, opens the actual native Buy window, adds a controlled dagger to its basket and invokes the native DoSteal handler. The dagger arrives in player inventory, leaves the basket and preserves prior crime state. Test cleanup removes the item and restores NPC visibility. Log work/TheftTransfer-pass.log; build/native checks pass. This is controlled empty-shop transfer evidence, not proof of killed native keeper handling or private-property transfer. Those cases and witnessed guard-response integration remain open.

Latest runtime checkpoint: the hook is installed for the supported town's loaded building interiors. It enumerates native stationary NPCs, mobile citizens and finite guard actors, rejects inactive/dead actors and sleeping generated residents, and requires sight within 20 metres with physics obstruction checks. Queries run at the theft roll even while inventory pauses time. Unknown/out-of-scope coverage retains native behavior. Unity physics tests pass for range, walls, death and sleep. Actual-player HousingProbe passes empty-room zero detection, a nearby living resident restoring the native skill roll, and the same resident's dead ledger state excluding it. Evidence work/TheftAwareness-pass.log; build/native checks and 176 core tests pass. Installed development bundle remains labelled 0.3.12. These tests do not yet exercise actual shop basket/private-property item transfers, a killed native shopkeeper, or waking sleepers from theft noise; those acceptance cases remain open. Earlier unconnected-hook notes below are historical.

Implementation checkpoint: NativeTheftHooks now intercepts the shared native formula. A confirmed no-witness result returns zero detection; a witness or unknown coverage leaves the native calculation intact. Unity integration tests exercise the actual patched formula for all three states and 1,000 native Dice100 rolls at zero detection, all successful. The hook is not yet installed by the runtime: live stationary/mobile/guard witness enumeration, death/sleep filtering and visibility must be connected and tested first. Playable bundle unchanged by VerifyGuards.ps1.

Approved requirement, 14 October 2026: taking goods from shop shelves or private property in houses and other buildings succeeds with 100% probability when no living NPC or guard is nearby and able to witness the act. Dead NPCs never count. Merely belonging to the building, being upstairs behind an opaque floor, or existing in the population ledger does not make an NPC a witness. Use actual nearby position and unobstructed sight to the theft/player. Retain the applicable skill-based detection check when a living potential witness exists; do not replace witnessed theft with automatic success or automatic failure.

Sleeping residents must follow the sleep/awareness system: do not treat an unaware sleeper as an awake eyewitness. They may become witnesses if the act wakes/alerts them. This integrates with the planned distance/skill detection model and must be tested explicitly.

Source inspection: both DaggerfallTradeWindow.Steal_OnMouseClick (shop baskets) and DaggerfallInventoryWindow.AttemptPrivatePropertyTheft (private property) call FormulaHelper.CalculateShopliftingChance. Vanilla clamps detection probability to 5–95 percent without consulting actual occupants. The adapter must provide zero detection chance only when the absence of potential witnesses is known, while retaining native skill/item/quality calculations otherwise. Verify Dice100 zero semantics and both inventory transfer paths. Do not globally suppress existing crimes or erase outstanding bounty.

Integration remains pending. Acceptance includes empty shop/house, dead keeper, dead customer, living keeper out of sight, living nearby customer or guard, walls/floors, awake versus sleeping NPCs, generated and native stationary identities, inventory open across a state change, real item transfer, and finite guard reporting after detection. Lawful corpse loot rights remain a separate exemption.

<a id="source-threat-response-md"></a>

## Original: THREAT-RESPONSE.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Existing hostile attackers and civilian flight

Installed development version: 0.3.33. Bundle SHA-256: `BAAC0B339EB36574DBBCF1E73BC0560263C8A1DF8CF8EBF3E1FC647CF02B1932`.

Living Cities reacts to existing vanilla, quest and mod attackers. It does not create a separate random-incursion spawner. This supersedes the original random-event generation proposal.

Guards now accept active hostile quest-owned enemy bodies even when the native quest's `IsAttackableByAI` flag is false. The scoped defender explicitly targets that enemy without changing the quest flag. Dead, pacified, player-allied and City Watch bodies remain excluded. Existing exterior finite dispatch, native combat, search and return behavior apply; this is not full interior enemy-defense integration.

Awake generated residents and registered native merchants/staff react to a hostile enemy visible within 20 metres. They flee using existing escape/home routes, independently of whether guard enemy defense is enabled. A direct witness can warn at most two other residents; those recipients cannot relay further. Quest-protected residents and sleeping residents are excluded from this visual threat scan. Not every vanilla stationary service NPC is registered yet.

Threat incidents have a separate persisted `ThreatId`, with no invented murder victim, corpse or crime scene. Citizen danger reports can alert nearby available exterior guards without accusing the player or erasing an existing crime. Native merchant reaction identities survive serialization. Expired repeated threats clear old native reaction links before creating a new episode. Indoor enemy reports do not yet dispatch guards into that building.

## Verification

- `dotnet run --project tests/LivingCities.Tests -c Release`: 279/279 pass, including threat flight, bounded warnings, malformed restore rejection, serialization and renewed native response links.
- Native `GuardDefenseProbe`: actual damage to a quest-tagged hostile human with the quest AI flag preserved, creature combat, save/reload, existing crime retention and player-assault takeover. Evidence: `work/Threat-defense-regression-pass.log`.
- Native `CitizenThreatProbe`: a registered shopkeeper and a generated street resident physically flee a controlled hostile enemy while guard defense is disabled. Player remains uncharged; the generated threat survives native serialization and validated restore. Evidence: `work/CitizenThreat-live-pass.log`.
- Native `IndoorNoiseProbe`: existing hearing, waking, escape, saved state and finite guard arrival regression. Evidence: `work/Threat-indoor-regression-pass.log`.
- Native `RefugeProbe`: haunting flight from combat and patrol, three wall interior models, city population/save/reload/movement and regional fixtures. Evidence: `work/Threat-refuge-city-regression-pass.log`.
- Native `CrowdProbe`: opposing resident bodies retain at least 0.82 metres separation; inserted-wall checks verify no capsule penetration, without claiming route replanning. Evidence: `work/Crowd-safety-pass.log`.

The hostile actors in these checks are disposable test fixtures. A quest-tagged fixture does not prove every shipped quest or third-party quest's progression semantics.

## Movement limits

Rendered resident placement now checks local crowd spacing and swept physical obstacles. Opposing walkers pass with at least 0.82 metres between managed body centers in the native street fixture. Local steering remains outside inserted walls; it does not guarantee a replacement route around a newly inserted obstruction. Waiting or oscillation at a blocked route remains possible. Static merchant flight still uses its existing checked route rather than the new crowd body registry. Guard integration was deferred after it prevented a wall-post combat fixture from activating; guards retain their existing tested placement and native combat movement.

Dense-city profiling, narrow-door queues, moving attackers across interior transitions, complete stationary NPC coverage, sleeping threat awareness, indoor guard enemy response and lawful-defense welfare/loot dialogue remain unfinished.

## Indoor combat adapter checkpoint, 15 October 2026

The guard actor now supports native enemy combat inside buildings and captures the enemy in interior coordinates, including vertical position and the return trail. A controlled `CitizenThreatProbe` creates a disposable indoor defender and verifies actual damage to a hostile thief, a saved interior target, and no player charge or player hostility. Expected pre-fix failure: `work/IndoorDefender-red.log`; passing run: `work/IndoorDefender-green.log`. This validates the actor adapter, not automatic witness dispatch or a complete indoor defense encounter.

Building defense now tolerates an unavailable roster without dereferencing a missing report. Regression covers zero allowed responders, busy guards, and a successful later retry. All 287 core tests pass (`work/IndoorDefender-core.log`), as do the Unity build and exterior enemy-defense regression (`work/IndoorDefender-exterior-pass.log`). Installed development0.3.34 bundle hash: `E270863B7B0252FC9525DD2B11720A13D7B5E62FE7195E40449052B5626299AC`.

The automatic dispatch gap is superseded by the checkpoint below.

## Automatic indoor enemy defense, 15 October 2026

An escaped registered keeper now contacts an available finite guard at its actual post. The report retains the hostile enemy identity and dispatches only contacted guards, without charging the player. The guard takes its surveyed exterior travel time, enters the reported building, searches toward the witness location, and uses native combat once it sees the attacker. Disabled defense, an invalid target, or search expiry ends the response; existing player crimes remain actionable.

The native shop fixture verifies keeper flight, guard contact, delayed arrival, actual enemy damage, no innocent-player hostility, and standing down when defense is disabled. Evidence: `work/IndoorResponse-clearance-pass.log` and final-bundle `work/IndoorResponse-final-native.log`. The latter combined invocation completed the citizen fixture but rejected the unexecuted doorway fixture: this harness branch returns early, so doorway regression must run separately.

Two routing defects were reproduced: alarm ground coordinates were treated as actor-origin coordinates, placing the destination one metre below the floor; then civilian clearance admitted a route too narrow for the native guard's 0.4-metre capsule radius. Alarm conversion now preserves the actor-origin convention. The interior survey accepts actor dimensions, and guard movement uses its actual ground offset and CharacterController. Default civilian survey dimensions are unchanged.

Core tests: 289/289 (`work/IndoorResponse-core-final.log`). Unity build: `work/IndoorResponse-final-build.log`. Installed development0.3.34 SHA-256: `9EFE142996512076627218F79A852B02DDBD9D738FEF280EEA4B8A78350ABA91`.

Separate native doorway regression passes (`work/IndoorResponse-door-regression-pass.log`): existing crime response, same wounded guard physically pursuing outside, player spell retained across exit, and actual effect save/reload. Tests use only the dedicated fixture character and hidden owned processes.

Broader interiors, dynamic obstruction replanning, enemies crossing scene boundaries, and welfare/loot dialogue still need dedicated acceptance tests or implementation. This checkpoint does not establish complete indoor defense coverage.

### Indoor assault and enemy-death follow-up

The native assault probe exposed a handoff gap: ending defense cleared native attacker knowledge, then ordinary indoor response required a new sighting. The controller now records the known player's position and starts native crime response immediately, matching exterior behavior. It retains an existing charge and assigns Assault only when no other crime is recorded.

The expanded fixture uses a surveyed visible player position and native `MakeEnemyHostileToAttacker` notification. It verifies the enemy target is released, the report switches to player crime handling, and Assault remains. After restoring the disposable encounter, setting the enemy's health to zero verifies combat ends and interior return begins without a player charge. This tests target death handling, not attribution of a player kill or loot rights. Existing isolated combat/effect and citizen-flight checks also pass.

Evidence: `work/IndoorAssault-visible-red.log`, `work/IndoorDefense-outcomes-pass.log`, `work/IndoorDefense-outcomes-build.log`;289/289 core (`work/IndoorAssault-core.log`). Installed development0.3.34 hash: `7F2F8E3AA82EC431FFD2922CC707C0D67F0307B44367341880968CB5445F99D1`.

## Guard spell effects across transitions

Superseded by the verified [guard effect persistence checkpoint](HISTORY.md#source-guard-effects-md). The paragraph below records the original gap.

Doorway transitions recreate guard actors and retain health, identity and pursuit, but do not capture or restore EntityEffectManager bundles. Active spell effects can be lost. Required follow-up: preserve native effect state and remaining duration across interior/exterior transitions and save/load, verified with real cast-effect probes. Offscreen time must not restart or indefinitely preserve timed effects. This is an acknowledged gap, not implemented behavior.

## Source-only indoor defense foundation (earlier checkpoint)

Building dispatch and queued building reports now accept a separate enemy target. Validated copy retains that target through exterior travel, delayed doorway entry, interior observations and physical return. A report cannot switch between criminal response and enemy defense or combine enemy defense with crime-scene investigation.281/281 core tests pass (`work/IndoorDefense-green.log`). This is not yet wired into the runtime or installed bundle; indoor guard enemy response remains unfinished.

## Daggerfall doorway-post report

The placement and shop doorway-pursuit corrections are now in development0.3.34; see [verified post and doorway behavior](HISTORY.md#source-guard-posts-and-doorways-md). The following paragraph preserves the original diagnosis.

The player reported a guard standing facing a house door, then observed the guard run to city-wall shelter when a ghost appeared. SAVE50 stores guard `g:1291010263:n:4` as `Posted`, with no refuge/interior and post `(139.824996948242, 703.400024414063)`. The current player log later records that guard taking refuge in building67329. This supports a stationary doorway post followed by working ghost flight, rather than proving a stuck patrol. Current post selection samples resident commute starts, which are building entrances. Post selection needs an offset to reachable clear space; existing saved rosters need safe migration without renaming guards or rewriting active journeys. That placement correction is not implemented yet.

<a id="source-vacancy-and-recovery-md"></a>

## Original: VACANCY-AND-RECOVERY.md

Historical source; implementation claims retain their original date and limitations.

# Household deaths, cleanup and new occupants

16 October later checkpoint supersedes the historical status below: schema 20 household history, persisted exclusive replacement/bank-sale reservations, runtime recovery scanning and native bank listing/purchase hooks are implemented. The combined suite reached 573 passing core tests and a successful Unity build. Native gameplay acceptance remains pending. Assigned native bed/worksite claims conservatively block recovery; releasing those claims remains unfinished. Replacement currently rebuilds the town's actor plans and needs visual continuity validation.

User requirement, 14 October 2026. This refines gradual replacement rather than resurrecting killed NPCs.

Later same-day refinement: `CRIME-SCENES.md` governs silent single-occupant murders and guard discovery. In that case cleanup occurs when dispatched guards arrive after player departure, and guards occupy the scene for one hour. This is a specific override of the general cleanup timing below. Other occupants may fight or flee to raise an alarm, including previously stationary NPCs.

When every permanent resident of a household dies, preserve their death records and mark the dwelling vacant. Living residents who are at work or traveling still own the household; an empty room is not an extinct household. Offscreen death processing must not erase relationships or bereavement memories elsewhere.

Corpses remain initially. Normal cleanup is eligible after about one game day. If guards were informed, cleanup can occur after the player has left and a day/night period has passed. The exact informed cleanup boundary is an implementation default to document and expose where useful; use the next half-day boundary with an elapsed half-day minimum to avoid immediate cleanup near dawn/dusk. Never remove bodies visibly while the player remains in the interior. Player departure and elapsed time are separate conditions; re-entry before cleanup must not duplicate or reset bodies. Discovery/notification and criminal attribution remain separate: clearing a corpse does not make guards know an unseen killer.

Dense large cities can reoccupy a vacant dwelling after 3–7 game days, or offer it for sale through the local bank. Other settlements generally take 7–30 days. Small isolated hamlets, particularly after a population wipeout, bias toward the long end, approximately 30 days. The dense-city exception supersedes a universal seven-day minimum. Pick the outcome and delay deterministically once, persist them, and do not reroll on travel or loading. Keep homes vacant during the delay. Guards have their separate bounded recruitment process.

Replacement residents are newly created immigrants, not recycled dead residents and not existing town NPCs reassigned secretly. Give the incoming family new persistent IDs and a new household generation, respecting family/surname rules and the later hard capacity rule: at most one living occupant per surveyed bed, with resident count no greater than bed count. House size can influence occupancy below that cap. Preserve references to the deceased generation in memories, estates and death records. A whole-town wipeout must not trigger an instant population reset or force all replacement families to arrive together.

Vacancy outcomes are mutually exclusive: occupied by a new family, listed for sale, or still vacant. Integrate bank listings with actual DFU ownership rather than displaying a fake sale option. Respect purchased homes, existing quests and protected NPCs; never sell a player-owned house or create replacements inside a quest-owned dwelling without a validated compatibility rule. When sold, cancel the migration reservation for that property. Settings changes do not silently undo deaths or active ownership.

Persist household generation, extinction time, death/corpse IDs, notification time, cleanup eligibility/completion, outcome, recovery deadline and ownership/listing state. Tests must cover save/load/time jumps, an absent surviving household member, player staying/re-entering, informed versus undiscovered deaths, dense-city 3–7 days, isolated-hamlet delay, no resurrection/ID reuse, family replacement versus sale exclusivity and player property protection.

16 October status correction: corpse/crime-scene persistence and cleanup now have implementations and separate native checks documented in CRIME-SCENE-DISCOVERY.md and GUARD-BUILDING-RESPONSE.md. Household extinction generations, deterministic immigration, and bank-listing integration remain unimplemented. Do not infer extinction from an empty rendered room or from travelers being absent.

16 October policy foundation: `HouseholdRecoveryPolicy` now computes immutable, generation-specific deadlines and requires confirmed extinction of a previously occupied home. Dense-city delays are 3–7 days, ordinary settlements 7–30, and the initial isolated-hamlet default is 24–30. Activation rechecks current property/living-owner eligibility, player departure from the dwelling, corpse cleanup and deadline without rerolling it. Core tests pass 533/533 (`work/HouseholdRecoveryPolicy-core.log`). This has no WorldState persistence or runtime activation yet; no families are created by this foundation. The integration sequence is in `superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-household-recovery.md`.
